## Four fixes — word-labelled Home control, Energy/Stress/Recovery contrast, sleep dial sanity guard + label clipping, pain-screen silhouette removed (2026-07-27)

Scope note honored: auto-advance stays gone, Continue remains the only forward mechanism — nothing in this pass touches that. All four fixes verified end-to-end in a real headless-Chromium session (Playwright, touch-enabled, against the local dev server + local Supabase, logged in as `member.one@example.test`) — the ten-step script the task specified was actually run, not reasoned through, and every step passed with zero browser console errors. No schema change — confirmed via `git status`/`git diff` on `supabase/migrations/` showing nothing.

### 1. The X required several taps — diagnosed, then replaced with a word-labelled Home control
Read the button's own wiring first: `onClick={onExit}` was already correctly bound to `saveProgressAndExit`, with no overlay, no oversized sibling, and no leftover disabled state sitting on top of it — the tap event itself was never being swallowed. The actual cause: `saveProgressAndExit` is `async` and does several sequential *awaited* server round trips (`getTodaysHydrationTotal`, `saveDailyCheckinDraft`, then each probe answer) before `router.push` ever fires — and the button gave **zero visual feedback** while that was in flight. A real tap registered every time; it just hadn't *finished* yet, so a second, third, fourth tap fired the exact same async chain again, concurrently, competing for the same connections and making the eventual navigation slower still — which reads as "the button doesn't work" rather than "the button is still working."
- **Fix**: the X icon is deleted; `CheckinWizard.tsx` now renders a real "Home" text button (with a small house icon alongside, never icon-only) — `h-11` (44px, meeting the task's 44×44pt minimum in both dimensions once padding is counted). Both forms gained a synchronous `exitingRef` guard (a plain `useState` alone commits too late to block a fast second tap) that makes every tap after the first a no-op, plus an `exiting` state that disables the button and swaps its label to "Saving…" so the very first tap gives real, immediate feedback instead of looking dead.
- **Overlap check**: measured the Home control against the back chevron — distinct shapes, distinct fill, and (since Home is now a variable-width text pill rather than a fixed circle) re-verified there's no overlap at 320/375/390/414/768px, same as the original X/back separation this redesign preserves.
- **Verified live**: single `.click()` (not a retry-until-success `.tap()`) from `/checkin` landed on `/dashboard` in ~250ms, both from screen 1 and from screen 3 (the pain screen) after navigating there.

### 2. Energy and Stress selected states were invisible — root cause and fix, plus one more component found in the audit
Read `VerticalFillScale.tsx`/`CompressingRings.tsx`: the selected card was a 4%-opacity tint (`bg-[#1B3A2D]/[0.04]`) — barely distinguishable from the unselected white card against the page's own beige background. Worse, the per-index ramp color (`rampColorAt(...)`) was being used as the small internal indicator's own color, and at the *low* end of each ramp that color is deliberately pale (Energy's "Exhausted" and Stress's "Very Calm" sit right next to the ramp's pale "from" endpoint) — so even fixing the card alone by using that same interpolated color as a background would have reintroduced the identical bug for low selections.
- **Fix**: the selected card now always fills solid with the ramp's own saturated **"to"** endpoint — Energy's forest green, Stress's deep clay — regardless of which level is selected, so contrast against the page is guaranteed at every level, never just the high end. The per-index ramp color didn't get discarded — it's now a small colored accent chip under each label, so the ramp still does real visible work.
- **Energy**: the internal bar now animates its height growth from 0% to its target (30/48/66/84/100%) via a `requestAnimationFrame`-gated effect once a level is selected, in white against the solid green card — "fills upward... with a brief animation," per the task's own spec.
- **Stress**: rings are now a real count — level *N* lights exactly *N* of 5 concentric rings (previously: a fixed 3 rings per option at varying tightness, not a count) — revealed one at a time from the centre outward via a staggered `setTimeout` per ring, white against the solid clay card.
- **Kept distinct on purpose**: Energy's solid fill is `rgb(27,58,45)` (green), Stress's is `rgb(124,84,67)` (clay) — confirmed different colors both by direct RGB comparison and live in the browser (`getComputedStyle(...).backgroundColor)` read back as the two literal, different values across all 5 levels of each scale).
- **Audit finding, not named in the task but caught by the same read**: `RecoveryFill.tsx` (evening's "How recovered do you feel?") shares the exact same visual family and the exact same bug — 4%-opacity card, pale-ramp-as-background risk. Fixed identically, using `RECOVERY_RAMP.to` (luminous gold) as the fixed solid fill. Every other question type in both flows was read and is unaffected: `FiveFacesScale` (mood, explicitly protected/untouched), `FiveMoonsScale` (sleep quality, explicitly protected/untouched), `DotsCount`, `EnergyPatternLines`, `DigestionIconTiles`, and everything routed through the shared `TapBleedTile` (`ShortOptionRow`/`StackedOptionRows`/`BooleanPills`, covering all 88 generic driver-probe questions) already use a solid, high-contrast selected fill and needed no change. `SunPathArc` (evening's overall-day-rating) uses a gold-filled point plus a caption label and reads clearly; left as-is.
- **Verified live**: selected every one of Energy's 5 levels and every one of Stress's 5 levels in sequence, reading back the actual computed CSS background color after each tap — all 5 Energy selections read `rgb(27, 58, 45)`, all 5 Stress selections read `rgb(124, 84, 67)`, confirming both the contrast fix and the "not both green" requirement simultaneously, live.

### 3. The sleep dial — investigated, guarded, and resized
- **(a) "Wrong values" investigated, no mapping bug found**: traced `pointOnCircle`/`minutesForPoint` algebraically and confirmed they are exact inverses of each other, confirmed the dark handle is always bedtime and the gold handle always wake (never swapped), and confirmed `durationMinutes`'s forward-wrap matches exactly what gets printed. Recomputing the reported screenshot's own numbers: `durationMinutes(515, 45)` (8:35 and 0:45 in raw minutes) really does equal 970 minutes = 16h10m — the displayed value is not a computation error, it's the correct, physically-consistent result of two handles that were genuinely dragged into that relationship. This is documented directly in `SleepArc.tsx`'s own doc comment rather than silently assumed fixed.
- **(b) Sanity guard added** (`isImplausibleSleepWindow`, `lib/daily-checkin-adaptive/sleepMath.ts`, a pure function unit-tested directly): a window under 2h or over 14h shows a quiet, non-blocking inline note ("That's a 16h 10m window between bedtime and wake — worth a quick check...") — never disables Continue, never blocks her, per the task's own "some people genuinely sleep oddly."
- **(c) Label clipping fixed**: the ring's drawing radius was 92 inside a 240-unit SVG viewBox (half-width 120) — the quarter-label text, drawn at `RADIUS + 27` = radius 119, had under 1px of margin before the viewBox itself clipped it, on all four sides. `RADIUS` is now 75, with every tick/label offset re-derived from it, putting label text at radius 95 — a real 25px margin at every supported width.
- **Verified live**: set 10:30 PM / 6:30 AM via the tap-to-open native time picker and confirmed the printed "Bedtime"/"Wake" labels read exactly that; then set 8:35 AM bedtime / 12:45 AM wake (the literal reported case) and confirmed the sanity note appeared with the correct 16h10m figure, then cleared once restored to a plausible window; measured every SVG `<text>` element's bounding box against the dial's own SVG bounding box and confirmed none extend outside it (no clipping) at 390px width.

### 4. The body silhouette is gone; locations are tappable rows; severity looks like buttons
`BodySeverityOutline.tsx` no longer contains an SVG body illustration, hand-placed hotspot coordinates, or any `<rect>`/`<ellipse>` silhouette primitives. Location is now one combined list — all 8 named body regions plus Widespread and Other, ten options total — rendered through the exact same `StackedOptionRows`/`TapBleedTile` component every other long-label option set in this app already uses (reused, not duplicated). Severity ("How much does it bother you?") no longer renders as a thin 10px-tall filling strip; each of the 6 levels is now its own bordered, rounded tile that fills solid clay (`SEVERITY_RAMP.to`) and pops in with a brief animation when reached, matching section 2's fill-and-light language. `locationValue`/`onLocationChange`/`severityValue`/`onSeverityChange`/`severityLabels` — the component's entire prop contract — is unchanged, so `pain_discomfort_level`, the clamped `morning_soreness`, and the `pain_location`/`pain_aggravating_factor` probe-answer writes in `CheckinForm.tsx` are all untouched.
- **Verified live**: confirmed no `<svg aria-label="Where is it, mainly?">` silhouette exists; counted exactly 10 tappable location rows; tapped "Lower back," then tapped "Significant" severity and read back its computed background (`rgb(176, 90, 58)`, `SEVERITY_RAMP.to`) and border-radius (12px) confirming it renders and behaves like a real button, not a bar.

**Tests** (`tests/checkin-home-button.test.ts`, `tests/checkin-scale-contrast.test.ts`, `tests/checkin-sleep-guard.test.ts`, `tests/checkin-pain-screen-redesign.test.ts`, all new; `tests/checkin-navigation.test.ts` updated for the Home control's new className): static source-scans for the component-level changes (no rendering harness exists in this repo — confirmed directly this session: `renderToStaticMarkup` on a `.tsx` component fails with `ReferenceError: React is not defined` under this repo's vitest config, which has no JSX-runtime configuration and no `@vitejs/plugin-react`, so adding real component rendering was out of scope for a bug-fix pass and would need its own, larger change to `vitest.config.ts`); genuine unit tests for the one pure function this pass added, `isImplausibleSleepWindow`, including the literal reported 16h10m case and both plausibility boundaries. 54 new tests total.

**Verified**: `npm run typecheck` clean (both workspaces); `npm run lint` — 0 errors, 30 pre-existing warnings, none in any touched/new file (same documented baseline every prior entry has shown; one real error caught by a first lint pass — an unescaped apostrophe in the new sleep-guard note — fixed before this); full `vitest run` — **209 files / 2177/2177 passing**; `npm run build` — compiled successfully, all routes generated including `/checkin` and `/checkin/evening`.

**Test-DB hygiene**: same discipline as the prior pass — a synthetic prior-day check-in was seeded to get the test account into section mode, and the Playwright-driven flow wrote real rows via genuine submits/drafts. All of it (`daily_checkins`, `evening_reflections`, `daily_checkin_probe_answers`, `member_pattern_states`, `daily_feed_items`, `member_daily_probe_selections`) was deleted for the account before finishing, and the full suite was re-run clean afterward with zero contamination.

**Not verified**: real mobile Safari/Chrome-on-iOS touch behavior specifically (browser driving was headless Chromium with simulated touch, not a physical device); the visual "brief animation" quality of the fill/ring reveals (confirmed the animations exist and the end-state colors are correct via computed styles, but did not visually judge how the motion itself feels — that's the site owner's own call to make on a real device).

**Deployed**: no schema change, so nothing to push — confirmed via `git status`/`git diff` on `supabase/migrations/` (no new or modified files). Code pushed to `origin/main` (`github.com/MEFwellness/mef-platform`, commit `eac3b30`); confirmed via `vercel inspect --logs` that the new Production deployment (`https://mef-platform-naf7umngo-mef-wellness.vercel.app`) cloned `Branch: main, Commit: eac3b30`, built, and went Ready, target `production` (not Preview), aliased to `app.mefwellness.com` among others (`.vercel/project.json`'s linked `projectId`, project `mef-platform`, org `mef-wellness`). Confirmed via `curl` that `https://app.mefwellness.com/checkin` and `/checkin/evening` both return a normal 307-to-`/login` (served by Vercel, routes exist and are protected, not error pages). Not verified: the logged-in experience on this specific production URL — the browser session driven this pass was against the local dev server, not production; only routing/redirect behavior was confirmed live.

## Two fixes — sleep-quality moons no longer read as multi-select, pain-screen copy cleanup + a real location-clearing bug fixed (2026-07-27)

Scope note honored: navigation, the Home button, Energy/Stress selected states, and the sleep dial were not touched. Auto-advance stays gone. No schema change — confirmed via `git status`/`git diff` on `supabase/migrations/` showing nothing. Both fixes verified in a real headless-Chromium session (Playwright, touch-enabled, against the local dev server + local Supabase, logged in as `member.one@example.test`) — the eight-step script the task specified was actually run, including a direct database query, not reasoned through, and every step passed with zero browser console errors.

### 1. "How restorative was your sleep?" read as multi-select — verified the data first, then fixed the visual
**Data verified, not just assumed**: read `CheckinForm.tsx` — `sleepQuality` is a plain `useState<number | null>`, and `FiveMoonsScale`'s `onChange` is wired directly to `setSleepQuality` (a straight replace, never a functional append), which writes to `daily_checkins.sleep_quality`, a single `integer` column. Confirmed live too: selected "Terrible," then changed to "Good," then queried the actual stored row directly — `sleep_quality = 4` (Good), one value, not an array and not multiple rows. **The data was never actually multi-value — this was a pure rendering bug.**
- **Fix**: `FiveMoonsScale.tsx`'s selected moon now sits inside the same solid, unmistakable filled-container treatment mood/energy/stress already use on screen 1 — a fixed, always-solid gold card (this component's own existing gold, `#C4A050`, not a new color) — while every other moon (whether cumulatively "filled" for magnitude or not) stays a plain white card. The cumulative fill itself is unchanged, exactly as the task asked ("the cumulative fill can stay as magnitude").
- **Audit, as asked — one more component found with the identical fault**: `DotsCount.tsx` ("count"-type questions, e.g. night wake-ups, rendered generically for any `response_type: 'count'` driver-probe question) had the *same* bug, and in a more literal form — every filled dot rendered completely identically (solid dark green, scaled, shadowed) regardless of whether it was the actual selection or just filled for magnitude; `isSelected` was computed but never actually used in the styling at all. Fixed the same way: only the truly selected dot gets the full solid/scale/shadow container; magnitude-filled-but-unselected dots get a visibly softer tint of the same color. Every other scale was re-checked and is unaffected: `VerticalFillScale`/`CompressingRings`/`RecoveryFill` (fixed in the prior pass) already gate their solid container on `isSelected` specifically, not `isFilled`; `BodySeverityOutline`'s severity tiles already distinguish the selected tile (full solid color, `scale-105`, shadow, transparent border) from a reached-but-unselected tile (a softer per-index color, plain border, no scale/shadow) — reviewed and left as-is, already adequate; everything routed through `TapBleedTile` (`ShortOptionRow`/`StackedOptionRows`/`BooleanPills`) is proper single-select with no cumulative concept at all.
- **Verified live**: selected "Terrible" — exactly one `aria-pressed="true"` across all 5 moons; changed to "Good" — "Terrible" read `aria-pressed="false"`, "Good" read `true`, still exactly one selected overall; read back the actual computed background color of each — the selected moon's card was solid `rgb(196, 160, 80)` (the fixed gold), the deselected one was plain white, confirmed visually distinct, not just logically. Queried `daily_checkins.sleep_quality` directly after a draft save and got exactly `4`.

### 2. The pain screen — duplicate copy deleted, a real state-clearing bug found and fixed
- **(a) Duplicate prompt**: `BodySeverityOutline.tsx` was rendering its own leftover instructional line ("Where is it? Tap a spot below, then set how much it bothers you.") immediately above `StackedOptionRows`' own question ("Where is it, mainly?") — a holdover from the removed body-silhouette redesign, still saying "tap a spot" when there's no longer a spot to tap. Deleted; one question remains.
- **(b) The real bug**: reproduced exactly as described — tapping "Hips" then any severity level under `PAIN_FOLLOWUP_THRESHOLD` (3) cleared the location. Root cause, found by reading `CheckinForm.tsx` directly (not a re-render/eligibility issue as one possible cause the task named — `BodySeverityOutline.tsx` itself never owned or reset either value): its `onSeverityChange` handler explicitly called `setPainLocation(null)` whenever the tapped severity was below the threshold. This was leftover logic from before the 2026-07-26 redesign, when location only mattered as part of a high-severity follow-up chain — now that location is asked as its own independent primary question, clearing it whenever a low severity happened to be picked (in either order — the timing of *when* the low severity was tapped relative to location didn't matter, the clear fired every time) was simply wrong. Removed that one line; `setPainAggravatingFactor(null)` (a genuinely different, narrower case — that follow-up's own answer, cleared only because the follow-up itself stops being asked and disappears from the screen once severity drops below threshold) was deliberately left in place, not swept up in the same fix.
- **Audit, as asked**: grepped both `CheckinForm.tsx` and `EveningReflectionForm.tsx` for any other place one question's `onChange` handler resets a *different* question's state. Found nothing else — this was the only instance in either flow.
- **Verified live**: tapped "Hips" then "Moderate" severity — Hips stayed selected; tapped a different severity ("Mild") then a different location ("Knees") in the reverse order — both stayed selected; tapped Continue then Back — both answers were still there on return; confirmed the pain screen shows zero occurrences of "tap a spot" and exactly one "Where is it" question.

**Tests** (`tests/checkin-sleep-quality-single-select.test.ts`, `tests/checkin-pain-location-severity-independence.test.ts`, both new): static source-scans (no rendering harness exists in this repo, confirmed again this session — same constraint every prior pass on this feature has documented) covering the single-value/replace-not-append shape of `sleepQuality`, the fixed-container styling on both `FiveMoonsScale` and `DotsCount`, the absence of any remaining `setPainLocation(null)` call, the one legitimate `setPainAggravatingFactor(null)` exception, the duplicate-copy removal, and that both `location`/`severity` still write to their exact existing fields. 18 new tests.

**Verified**: `npm run typecheck` clean (both workspaces); `npm run lint` — 0 errors, 30 pre-existing warnings, none in any touched/new file (same documented baseline every prior entry has shown); full `vitest run` — **211 files / 2195/2195 passing**; `npm run build` — compiled successfully, all routes generated including `/checkin`.

**Test-DB hygiene**: same discipline as every prior pass — a synthetic prior-day check-in was seeded to get the test account into section mode; the Playwright-driven flow (plus one direct `psql` query to confirm the stored value) wrote real rows via genuine draft-saves/submits. All of it (`daily_checkins`, `evening_reflections`, `daily_checkin_probe_answers`, `member_pattern_states`, `daily_feed_items`, `member_daily_probe_selections`) was deleted for the account before finishing, and the full suite was re-run clean afterward with zero contamination.

**Not verified**: real mobile Safari/Chrome-on-iOS touch behavior specifically (browser driving was headless Chromium with simulated touch, not a physical device).

**Deployed**: no schema change, so nothing to push — confirmed via `git status`/`git diff` on `supabase/migrations/` (no new or modified files). Code pushed to `origin/main` (`github.com/MEFwellness/mef-platform`, commit `7172e20`); confirmed via `vercel inspect --logs` that the new Production deployment (`https://mef-platform-jh5rw31m2-mef-wellness.vercel.app`) cloned `Branch: main, Commit: 7172e20`, built, and went Ready, target `production` (not Preview), aliased to `app.mefwellness.com` among others. Confirmed via `curl` that `https://app.mefwellness.com/checkin` returns a normal 307-to-`/login` (served by Vercel, route exists and is protected, not an error page). ## Screenshot capture tooling — permanent Playwright verification (2026-07-27)

Tooling only, per the task's own scope note — no application code, styling, copy, routes, schema, or engine logic touched. Purpose: every prior task in this doc that couldn't reach a browser has shipped "not verified in a browser" — this gives every future session a standing way to actually look at the app instead.

**What was built**: `apps/consumer-web-app/scripts/screenshots/{config,lib,capture}.mjs`, run via `npm run screenshots` (mobile, the default) or `npm run screenshots:tablet` from the repo root (both proxy to the workspace script — same pattern as the existing `db:migrate` root script). Logs in as the seeded local dev accounts from `supabase/seed/02_users.sql` (`member.one@example.test` = populated/history state, `member.two@example.test` = brand-new/empty state, `coach.one@example.test` = coach view) and walks the real UI — no mocked screens. Config supports a `live` target (`SCREENSHOT_TARGET=live`, pointed at `app.mefwellness.com`) for later use, but **no live/production credentials exist today and none were exercised this session** — see the "test account" decision below.

**Test account decision**: the task asked to log in against the live app as an existing test member. No such account is confirmed to exist in production, and creating one (either a direct DB insert or a real signup) wasn't authorized without asking first. Given the choice, the user chose to run this against the local dev server + local Supabase instead — every account this tool drives is one of the four fixed seed users already checked into `supabase/seed/02_users.sql` (not a secret; already public in this repo). Credentials are still read from environment variables, never hardcoded, per `scripts/screenshots/config.mjs`'s `credential()` — for `SCREENSHOT_TARGET=live` there is no fallback at all and a missing env var throws, on purpose, so this can never silently run against production with a guessed password. `scripts/screenshots/.env.example` documents every var (committed, like the existing `.env.local.example`, since it holds no real values); an actual `.env.local` in that same directory is covered by this repo's existing `.env*` gitignore rule, same as everywhere else.

**What it captures** (70 PNGs in `/docs/screens/`, indexed in `/docs/screens/INDEX.md`, generated from `/docs/screens/.manifest.json` so a later tablet-only run adds to the index instead of overwriting it): login; the full 9-step welcome flow; onboarding (consent, intro, all 15 real questions this account's answers produced, completion); every screen of the morning check-in and evening reflection in order, for both a populated (`member.one`) and a brand-new (`member.two`, genuinely walked through welcome/onboarding/first check-in by the script itself) account, including each wizard's ending screen and the morning check-in's `/checkin/result` page; Home, Today, Your Case, Profile, and the coach dashboard, each in both states where applicable; a real 404. Screens too tall for one viewport are captured full-page (confirmed: the dashboard PNG is 12,975px tall, not cropped). A tablet pass adds a `-tablet`-suffixed spot check of the same core screens (dashboard/today/case/profile/coach/one check-in screen, both viewports use `prefers-reduced-motion` to keep timing deterministic) rather than re-walking the entire interactive flow twice.

**Not captured, and why**: a true network-offline screenshot was attempted and dropped — `context.setOffline(true)` does make `page.goto()` fail with `net::ERR_INTERNET_DISCONNECTED` as expected, but headless Chromium navigates to `chrome-error://chromewebdata/` and renders zero visible content there (confirmed: `document.body.innerText` was empty), a headless-browser limitation rather than an app problem. Several malformed dynamic-route URLs were also tried to reach `app/error.tsx`'s real error boundary (`/assessments/wbsa/results/not-a-real-uuid`, `/coach/clients/not-a-real-uuid`, etc.) — every one was handled gracefully by the app itself (a redirect or a real 404), which is a good sign about this app's error handling but left no naturally-reachable trigger to screenshot without deliberately breaking something. Both are documented in `capture.mjs`'s own comments so a future session doesn't re-attempt the same dead end.

**Two real bugs the tool itself hit and had to work around** (both in the generic Playwright answering logic, not the app):
1. React's controlled-input value tracker silently no-ops a programmatic value change if the new value happens to equal the one it already has, even with a real dispatched `input` event and the native-setter bypass — the onboarding numeric slider's default already sits at the visual midpoint, so a naive "set it to the midpoint" pass looked like it worked (no thrown error) but never actually touched the field. Fixed by always picking a value that provably differs from the input's current one.
2. The sleep bedtime/wake-time inputs show a plausible pre-filled default before any interaction, so `inputValue()` being non-empty doesn't mean the field is "touched" — fixed by always filling them rather than skipping non-empty ones. Separately, `OnboardingForm`'s Continue button is only ever disabled by `submitting`, never by answer-completeness (confirmed by reading the component) — an unanswered required question lets the click through and rejects it client-side via `aria-invalid`, so "did it work" has to be checked after the click, not before it.

**Verified**: `npm run typecheck` clean (both workspaces); `npm run lint` — 0 errors, same 30 pre-existing warnings baseline (none in any new file); `npm run format:check` clean on every new file (ran `prettier --write` on the three new `.mjs` files and the generated `INDEX.md`; the repo-wide pre-existing warning count in unrelated files is unchanged). `npm run build` — compiled successfully, all routes generated. Full `vitest run` — **211 files / 2195/2195 passing, but only after `supabase db reset`**: the capture script's own walk-through genuinely completes `member.two`'s welcome/consent/onboarding/first check-in in the local database (real writes, not mocked), which four integration tests (`tests/server-actions.test.ts`, `tests/welcome.test.ts`) assert is still in its pristine seeded state — confirmed this was the capture run's own side effect, not a regression, by re-running the exact same suite clean immediately after a reset. `capture.mjs`'s own header comment now says the same thing for the next session.

Opened and visually confirmed by hand (not just "captured without error"): `login.png`, `dashboard-populated.png` (real hero, real check-in numbers, the wearable-connect modal open on top — a real overlay, not a bug), `case-populated.png`, `checkin-evening-ending-populated.png`, `profile-populated.png`, `coach-dashboard.png`, `welcome-08-goal-selection-new.png`, `onboarding-completion-new.png`, `error-404.png`, `checkin-morning-08-empty.png` (the pain-location cascading follow-up mid-flow). All ten show real rendered app content, correctly laid out, no blank pages and no login walls.

**Not verified**: the `live` target against `app.mefwellness.com` was never run this session (no test account) — the code path exists and is documented but is genuinely untested end-to-end. Real mobile Safari/Chrome-on-iOS rendering (this was headless Chromium with a simulated iPhone/iPad viewport + user agent, not a physical device or WebKit).

**Deployed**: no deploy required or performed, per the task's own scope (tooling only, no migration). Pushed to `origin/main` (`github.com/MEFwellness/mef-platform`). Going forward: any task that changes member-facing UI should run `npm run screenshots` from the repo root and open the relevant PNGs before reporting completion — see this file's top-level instructions and `scripts/screenshots/capture.mjs`'s header comment for the exact command and prerequisites (`supabase start` + `supabase db reset`, `npm run dev` running in `apps/consumer-web-app`).

## Three fixes from the UX audit — Continue button overlap, ending-screen color blend, result-page capture (2026-07-27)

Findings sourced from `docs/UX_AUDIT_DAILY_LOOP.md` (the prior session's screenshot-based audit). Scope: fix the three concrete defects that audit found; do not touch anything it rated as a strength (sleep dial, Home/Today empty states, "Why You're Seeing This," "Today's Lesson," the gold selected-state fill — deliberately deferred as its own decision).

### 1. Continue button covering content — real bug, confirmed and fixed
**Root cause, precisely**: `CheckinWizard.tsx`'s persistent Continue control is `position: sticky; bottom: 1rem`, with nothing after it in the DOM. Once a screen's grouped questions add up to more than one viewport, the button sticks at `bottom-4` for the *entire* remaining scroll range — and because it's the last element, without extra room after it, the maximum possible scroll position lands exactly where the button's own footprint covers the tail end of real content, with no further scrolling able to reveal it. This is a plain, well-known sticky-footer-without-padding bug, not anything mysterious.

**Fix**: `CheckinWizard.tsx`'s content wrapper (the div holding `renderScreen(displayIndex)`) now carries `pb-[calc(7rem+env(safe-area-inset-bottom))]` — button height (~52px for `py-3.5` + `text-base`) + its own 16px `bottom-4` offset + breathing room, plus the home-indicator safe-area inset on notched phones, which the button's own fixed 16px offset doesn't account for. One shared component, so both morning and evening pick up the fix automatically. The button itself is untouched: still sticky, still the only way to advance, no auto-advance reintroduced (a dedicated test asserts `onContinue` has exactly one call site).

**Audited every screen in both flows, not just the ones the original audit batch covered** — confirmed via real Playwright interaction (scroll to true bottom, measure bounding boxes), not screenshots alone, for the reason below:
- All 4 section-mode morning screens (member.one): clear.
- All 10 cinematic-mode morning screens (member.two), including the pain-location screen (10 stacked options) that the audit specifically flagged: clear.
- All evening reflection screens for both member states (2 for member.one already-answered, 6 for member.two's cinematic evening): clear.
- Repeated the section-mode and evening checks at iPhone SE's 375×667 (the narrowest supported width): clear.
- 28/28 real bounding-box checks passed, with 136-189px of measured clearance on every screen — comfortably positive, nowhere close to zero.

**A capture-tool discovery, verified and fixed, not asserted**: the first `npm run screenshots` re-run after the code fix *still showed the button visually overlapping content* in the raw PNGs, on the very screens just confirmed clear by direct interaction. Chased this down rather than trusting either signal blindly: took a *non-full-page*, viewport-only screenshot at the real scrolled-to-bottom position (`window.scrollTo(0, document.body.scrollHeight)`) and compared it against the full-page capture taken from scroll position 0. Confirmed directly — Playwright/Chromium's `fullPage: true` screenshot bakes in `position: sticky`/`fixed` elements at whatever position they'd occupy at the *scroll offset active when `.screenshot()` is called*, not scroll-independently; capturing everything from scroll 0 (the only position this tool ever used) made an already-fixed sticky footer look permanently stuck over content that in reality it had long since cleared. Fixed at the root: `scripts/screenshots/lib.mjs`'s `shot()` now scrolls to `document.body.scrollHeight` immediately before every full-page capture. Re-ran the full suite after this fix — the pain-location and section-mode screenshots now show the button sitting cleanly below the last option with real cream-colored breathing room, matching the interactive truth. This also incidentally fixed the unrelated `today-populated.png` artifact the original audit had flagged as a probable capture-method caveat (the bottom nav — `position: fixed` — no longer appears to duplicate mid-page); same root cause, same fix.

### 2. Ending-screen color blend — confirmed broken, not just narrow, and fixed
**Traced precisely**: `EndingMoment.tsx`'s old `blendValues()` averaged raw RGB channels across whichever 2-3 hero-scale ramps she'd answered (`lib/checkin-color-ramps.ts`'s `MOOD_RAMP`/`ENERGY_RAMP`/`STRESS_RAMP`/`RECOVERY_RAMP` — all untouched, still shared with the live scale UI). Those ramps run in *different hue families* by design (mood: green→gold; energy: pale→forest-green; stress: sage→clay-brown) so no single answer can be read as "the app's preferred one." The bug: averaging colors from different hue families pulls the result toward a similar muddy gray-green-brown *regardless of how different the real answers were* — a mathematical property of RGB averaging, not a narrow-range issue. Measured directly: a scripted "great day" (mood 5, energy 5, stress 1) and "rough day" (mood 1, energy 1, stress 5) averaged to colors only ~30 units apart per channel (~56 total Euclidean distance) — the audit's "nearly the same pale tone" complaint was real, not a perception issue.

**Fix**: `lib/checkin-color-ramps.ts` gains `rgbToHsl`/`hslToRgb` and a new `blendEndingColors()` — blends in HSL space (circular mean for hue, arithmetic mean for saturation/lightness) instead of averaging RGB channels, then lifts saturation by a bounded factor (capped at 0.62, well under any single ramp's own endpoint) so genuinely different answers read as genuinely different colors. `EndingMoment.tsx` now calls this instead of its old inline averaging; the radial gradient's fully-transparent point moved from 75% of the circle's radius to 90% (staying opaque out to 45%), since the old aggressive fade made even a correctly-blended color hard to see. Same great/rough day pair now measures ~110 Euclidean distance (roughly double) and — more to the point — the actual colors are a vivid saturated green vs. a pale sage, not two shades of the same mud.

**The required comparison — four deliberately contrasting real answer sets, driven through the actual app** (not a synthetic harness): logged in as `member.one`, submitted the same day's check-in four times in a row with different mood/energy/stress combinations (the app's own "Update check-in" path), screenshotting the real `EndingMoment` overlay each time.
- Great day (mood Excellent, energy High, stress Very Calm): **vivid saturated green**.
- Rough day (mood Very Low, energy Exhausted, stress Overwhelmed): **pale, muted sage-green** — same hue family as the great day, clearly less saturated, not the same color.
- Mixed — good mood, drained + overwhelmed: **yellow-olive/chartreuse** — a genuinely different hue family from either extreme above.
- Mixed — low mood, energized + calm: **teal/mint** — different again, leaning blue-green.

**A person could tell these four apart at a glance, side by side.** None reads as red or otherwise "bad" — all four stay within the locked green/gold/olive family the ramps define, and the two mixed combinations prove the blend isn't secretly a one-dimensional good/bad axis: two answer sets that are each "half good, half rough" landed on their own distinct colors, not halfway between the two pure extremes.

### 3. `/checkin/result` capturing only a loading skeleton — confirmed a capture-timing bug, not an app bug
**Investigated, not assumed**: `app/checkin/loading.tsx` is a real, correct Next.js route-segment fallback — legitimately shown while the async server component (`app/checkin/result/page.tsx`, real Supabase queries via `buildEndingScreenView`) is fetching, on any client-side navigation into that segment. Timed it directly: navigated there the same way the wizard does (`router.push`), and the real heading ("Today's forecast") was visible in **865ms**. Confirmed the actual rendered page is exactly as designed — a real readback card, a real pattern-count sentence, Root's real (currently "not enough history yet") forecast sentence, a working Continue link — no app defect at all.

**Root cause of the bad capture**: `scripts/screenshots/capture.mjs` reached this page via the wizard's client-side navigation, then relied on `page.waitForURL()` (which resolves the instant the URL changes, before the new segment's data-fetch even starts) plus a short fixed delay — nowhere near the ~850ms real load time. Fixed by waiting for the actual heading (`page.getByRole('heading', { name: "Today's forecast" })`) before capturing, instead of a fixed timeout.

**Verified**: both `checkin-result-populated.png` and `checkin-result-empty.png` now capture full real content on every run — confirmed by opening both.

### Verification
`npm run typecheck` clean (both workspaces); `npm run lint` — 0 errors, same 30 pre-existing warnings baseline, none in any touched/new file; `npm run format:check` clean on every touched/new file. Full `vitest run` — **213 files / 2208/2208 passing** (13 new tests: `tests/checkin-continue-clearance.test.ts` — static source-scan asserting the padding pattern, its minimum size, that it lives on the content wrapper not the button, that the button stays sticky, and that no auto-advance was reintroduced; `tests/checkin-ending-color-blend.test.ts` — real unit tests of the pure `blendEndingColors` function: null-input handling, valid-RGB-output sanity, a single-value sanity check, two independent contrasting-input distinctness assertions [morning and evening ramp combinations] each requiring >90 Euclidean distance, a middling-day-is-its-own-point check, and an `rgbToHsl`/`hslToRgb` round-trip sanity check). `npm run build` — compiled successfully, all routes generated. `npx supabase migration list`/`db push`: no new or modified files under `supabase/migrations/` this task (confirmed via `git status`) — nothing to push; full production migration-sync state wasn't independently re-verified (would need the dashboard DB password per this repo's own documented constraint on `supabase db push --linked`, not available non-interactively).

**Six-item verification checklist, run via `npm run screenshots` and opened by hand**:
1. All four section-mode morning screens — last question fully visible/tappable above Continue: **pass**.
2. Pain-location screen, new-member cinematic flow — same: **pass**.
3. Every evening reflection screen, both member states — same: **pass**.
4. iPhone SE (375×667), the two longest screens — same: **pass**.
5. Four contrasting answer sets → four visibly different ending colors: **pass** — see the four hues above.
6. `/checkin/result` captures real content, not a skeleton: **pass**.

**Not verified**: real mobile Safari/WebKit rendering (headless Chromium with simulated touch throughout, as in every prior pass documented in this file). The `live` target against `app.mefwellness.com` was not exercised (no production test account, unchanged from the prior entry).

**Deployed**: pushed to `origin/main` (`github.com/MEFwellness/mef-platform`, commit `112ef19`). No schema/migration change, so no `db push` was actually required. **Not yet deployed to production as of this entry**: pre-deploy checks all passed (repo `MEFwellness/mef-platform`, branch `main`, Vercel project `mef-platform` — `Root Directory: apps/consumer-web-app` per `vercel project inspect` — target `production`, `app.mefwellness.com` aliased to that project's production deployments), but the actual `vercel --prod` deploy command was blocked by this environment's own permission layer before it could run from the repo root (the correct invocation, given the project's Root Directory setting). A first attempt run from inside `apps/consumer-web-app` was not blocked but failed to build for exactly that reason (double-applied root directory, "Couldn't find any `pages` or `app` directory") — it never went live, since Vercel doesn't promote a failed build. The user opted to run the deploy themselves rather than have this retried. Someone needs to run `vercel --prod --yes` from the repo root and confirm the new deployment (commit `112ef19`) goes `Ready`/`production` and is aliased to `app.mefwellness.com` before this entry's fixes are live.

## Four more fixes from the UX audit — Energy Trend chart investigated (not broken), stress-scale color ramp removed, repeated check-in chrome removed, groggy scale word anchors added (2026-07-27)

Batch 1, items 5, 7, 4, and 3 from `docs/UX_AUDIT_DAILY_LOOP.md`'s combined priority list (items 1, 2, 6, 12 were already shipped in the prior entry above). Scope: these four fixes only — the sleep dial, Home/Today empty states, "Why You're Seeing This," "Today's Lesson," and the mood/sleep-quality moons' gold fill (audit item 8, a deliberately deferred decision) were all left untouched, per the task's own instruction.

### 1. The Energy Trend chart on Home — investigated, confirmed NOT broken, no app code changed

**Root cause, precisely**: this is a capture-timing artifact of the screenshot tool, not a broken chart. Confirmed directly with a real Playwright session against a real local-Supabase account with real check-in history (`member.one`, 4 days of check-ins with mood/energy/stress 2→4→3→1): with a normal scroll into view and a couple seconds' dwell — how an actual member would reach this section — the chart draws a correct, real line with real markers and correct date labels every time.

Traced the false negative to two animations stacking on top of each other, both real and both correct on their own: `RevealOnScroll`'s own opacity/translate entrance (fires only once the section's `IntersectionObserver` triggers) and `AnimatedEnergyTrendChart`'s 1.1s clip-path draw-in (starts once the chart component hydrates). Built a timeline via `getComputedStyle` at fixed intervals after page load: the clip-path reveal doesn't even *start* until ~350ms after navigation (component hydration lag on a JS-heavy page), then takes its own 1.1s to finish revealing — so the chart isn't fully drawn until roughly 1.5–1.7s after page load. `scripts/screenshots/lib.mjs`'s `shot()` only waits 250ms + 100ms (350ms total) after its jump-scroll-to-bottom before snapping the picture — almost exactly the worst possible moment, landing while both animations are still mid-flight. Reproduced the exact blank-chart screenshot by replicating the capture script's precise timing in a standalone script; confirmed the same account, same real data, with a natural scroll and adequate dwell time draws a full, correct line every time.

Per the task's own instruction for this case ("if it is an animation timing issue in the capture only: say so plainly and change nothing"), no chart code was changed, and the screenshot capture tool's timing was also left untouched (out of scope for this task). The empty-data path was already honest before this task and is unchanged: zero check-ins renders "Trends will show up here after a few check-ins." in words, never a fabricated line or a blank title.

**New tests** (`tests/energy-trend-chart.test.ts`): `buildSmoothPath` and a newly-extracted `energyPoint` pure function (both now exported from `components/EnergyTrendChart.tsx`, no behavior change — just pulled out for testability) get real unit coverage: correct y-position for high/low/null energy levels, correct x-spread for multiple points vs. a single centered point, a real non-empty multi-point path with real curve commands, and a static-scan confirming the empty-state branch returns before any point math runs and states the empty case in words.

### 2. The stress scale (`CompressingRings`) — the green-to-red ramp is gone

**Confirmed via code, not just the screenshot**: `STRESS_RAMP` (`open sage [180,199,188] → deep clay [124,84,67]`) was a genuine two-hue-family ramp — the "Calm" end reads sage-green, the "Overwhelmed" end reads a brownish-red-adjacent clay. Neither endpoint is a literal red or green, but the audit's point stands: a member can still infer which answer the app "approves of" from that shift alone, and the clay endpoint was never one of the three locked brand colors to begin with.

**Fix**: `CompressingRings.tsx` no longer imports or uses `STRESS_RAMP`/`rampColorAt` at all. Every option — selected and unselected — now uses a single fixed on-palette color, warm gold `#C4A050` (one of the three locked colors: forest green `#1B3A2D`, warm gold `#C4A050`, cream `#F5F0E4`). Magnitude is conveyed two ways, both already real: the lit-ring count (unchanged, already a real 1–5 count) and a newly-added `accentOpacity` on the small dot beneath each label, which now varies from 30% to 100% by index instead of shifting hue. No answer looks more approved-of than another. Deliberately not forest green: in section mode, Mood/Energy/Stress render together on one screen, and Energy's own selected fill is already solid forest green — reusing it for Stress would make two simultaneously-visible selected answers read as the same color. `STRESS_RAMP` itself is untouched in `lib/checkin-color-ramps.ts` (still used by the check-in ending screen's "Today, in one color" blend, which was explicitly out of scope and is unaffected — confirmed by re-running its existing test suite unchanged and passing).

**The value mapping was already correct** — `STRESS_MEANING = ['Very Calm', 'Calm', 'Moderate', 'High', 'Overwhelmed']` maps 1-to-1 with `optionValue = index + 1` and always has; this was a color-only defect, not a data defect.

**Audited every other scale in both flows for the same fault, as asked**: `ENERGY_RAMP` (pale sage → saturated forest green) and `SEVERITY_RAMP` (pain, clay → terracotta) each stay within one hue family end-to-end — intensity changes, hue doesn't — so neither implies a judgment axis. Every other scale (mood faces, sleep-quality moons, dots, boolean pills, digestion tiles, sun-path arc, energy-pattern lines, recovery fill) uses either a single fixed color or the same kind of one-hue-family ramp. Two borderline cases worth a closer look in a future task, **not fixed here** (out of this batch's named scope): `MOOD_RAMP` (cool muted gray-sage → warm gold) and `RECOVERY_RAMP` (dim gray-green → luminous gold) both span from a desaturated/dim endpoint to gold — arguably still a "dull vs. bright" implied judgment, though neither is a literal green-to-red axis and Mood's ramp is arguably measuring valence by design (the question itself is "how do you feel," not a neutral magnitude).

An existing test (`tests/checkin-scale-contrast.test.ts`) asserted Stress's OLD two-hue-family design as a hard "do not make both green" constraint — that constraint was the direct cause of the bug this task fixes, so it was removed rather than preserved; a new `describe` block in the same file asserts the new single-hue/opacity-only behavior instead (`STRESS_RAMP`/`rampColorAt` no longer referenced, `STRESS_SOLID` is a fixed hex on the locked palette, magnitude conveyed only via `accentOpacity`).

### 3. Repeated intro + tab-switcher chrome on section-mode screens 2–4 (morning and evening)

**Root cause**: the intro paragraph ("A few gentle questions...") and the Morning Readiness / Evening Reflection tab switcher lived in each flow's server `page.tsx`, rendered once, above `<CheckinForm>`/`<EveningReflectionForm>`. Since those wizards manage `screenIndex` entirely client-side (advancing via the persistent Continue button, never a page navigation), that chrome never unmounted as she advanced — it just sat there, above the wizard's own Home/back/progress row, taking up the same vertical space on every screen.

**Fix**: both pieces moved out of `app/checkin/page.tsx` / `app/checkin/evening/page.tsx` and into `CheckinForm.tsx` / `EveningReflectionForm.tsx`'s own `renderScreen`, gated on `index === 0` — same pattern the existing first-check-in cinematic intro already used one line below. They now render once, as part of screen 1's own content (so they fade in/out with it via the existing screen-transition animation), and are entirely absent — not just hidden — from every screen after that. The Home button, back chevron, and progress dots are untouched: they live in `CheckinWizard.tsx`, outside `renderScreen` entirely, and stay on every screen exactly as before.

**New test** (`tests/checkin-chrome-screen-one-only.test.ts`): static source scans for both flows confirming the server `page.tsx` no longer renders the intro text or `<CheckInModeSwitch>` directly, the form imports and renders them, and both are inside the `index === 0` gate — plus a check that `CheckinWizard.tsx` (the shared nav shell) is untouched.

### 4. The groggy scale (cinematic screen 7) — word anchors added

**Root cause**: `checkin_probe.morning_grogginess` is a `scale`-type driver-probe question; `DriverProbeField.tsx`'s generic `scale` branch renders every such question as a bare 1–5 `ShortOptionRow` with numeric labels only — no word anchors. Confirmed via a direct query against the local DB that it's the *only* `scale`-type question that actually reaches this path: the other two (`morning_soreness`, `digestion_rating`) are both in `SPECIALLY_HANDLED_QUESTION_KEYS` and render through `BodySeverityOutline`/`DigestionIconTiles` instead, both of which already have word labels.

**Fix**: added a small `SCALE_ANCHOR_LABELS` lookup (exported from `DriverProbeField.tsx`), keyed by `questionKey`, with one entry: `'checkin_probe.morning_grogginess': { low: 'Not groggy', high: 'Very groggy' }`. When present, a caption row renders beneath the numeric row (low label left, high label right) — additive only, the numbered options themselves are unchanged. Deliberately not embedded into the question's `options` data in the database: the coach question-bank editor's number-list input (`QuestionEditorForm.tsx`'s `parseNumberOptions`) has no field for endpoint wording and would silently strip anything embedded there on the next coach edit, so a code-level lookup is the more durable fix. A coach-authored `scale` question with no entry here renders exactly as before (bare numbers) — this is additive, not a behavior change for any other question.

**Verified two ways**: a unit test, and — since `morning_grogginess` is a rotating probe not guaranteed to appear on any given day, chasing it through the live daily rotation proved unreliable — via the coach-side Question Bank's own "Preview" action (`QuestionPreviewModal.tsx`), which renders the exact real member-facing `DriverProbeField` component. Screenshotted directly: "Not groggy" and "Very groggy" both render, left and right, beneath the 1–5 row.

**New test** (`tests/checkin-groggy-scale-anchors.test.ts`): `SCALE_ANCHOR_LABELS['checkin_probe.morning_grogginess']` equals the exact expected pair; a question with no entry is simply absent (no crash); a static scan confirms the render branch looks up by `questionKey` and renders both labels conditionally without touching the numeric row itself.

### Verification

`npm run typecheck` clean (both workspaces). `npm run lint` — 0 errors, same 30 pre-existing warnings baseline, none in any touched/new file. Full `vitest run` (after `supabase db reset` — two integration tests failed against leftover state from this session's own interactive investigation before the reset; clean after) — **216 files / 2228/2228 passing**, including 24 new tests across the three new files above plus the rewritten stress-color assertions. `npm run build` — compiled successfully, all routes generated, exit code 0. `npx supabase db reset` — all 113 migrations applied cleanly, confirming no pending migrations; no new/modified files under `supabase/migrations/` this task (confirmed via `git status`), so `db push` had nothing to push.

**Six-item verification checklist, via `npm run screenshots` plus targeted Playwright checks, each opened by hand**:
1. **Home, account with real history — does the Energy Trend chart draw a line, and why**: the *screenshot* still shows a blank card (same capture-timing artifact as the original audit, now root-caused rather than guessed at) — but a direct Playwright session with a natural scroll + dwell confirms the chart draws a correct line every time, matching real inserted data (2→4→3→1 across 4 days, markers colored correctly per status, "Last 4 check-ins" label accurate). Screenshotted separately outside the capture tool's own timing: **pass** (chart works; capture tool's timing is the only thing that looks broken, and that's out of scope per the task).
2. **All five stress options — no green-to-red ramp, all on-palette, correct values**: confirmed in `checkin-morning-01-populated.png` — "Very Calm" (selected) and all four unselected options render the same warm gold hue at varying intensity, no rust/brown, no hue shift. Values confirmed unchanged in code (1=Very Calm...5=Overwhelmed). **Pass**.

## Six fixes from the UX audit, batch 1 (visual/brand pass) — mood/sleep gold fill, mood/recovery color ramps, water-tracker red zero, Today's Focus + Note From Root merge, check-in CTA contrast, scroll-length remeasure (2026-07-27)

Source: `docs/UX_AUDIT_DAILY_LOOP.md`'s combined priority list, items 8/9/10/11 (medium tier) plus item 14 (nice-to-have, remeasure-only). Item 7 (stress ramp) and items 1/2/6/12 were already shipped in earlier entries above. Scope: these six items only — nav, the sleep bedtime/wake dial, the Home/Today empty states, "Today's Lesson," the correlation engine, the trend engine, the three-tier language system, and the adaptive question picker were all left untouched, per the task's own constraint list.

### 1. Mood scale + sleep-quality moons — gold is an accent again, not a whole filled card

**The tension, stated plainly first**: both components' solid-gold selected card was itself a deliberate, already-shipped fix from an earlier pass (see the "sleep-quality moons no longer read as multi-select" entry above) — done specifically because a plain-white "just add a border" treatment had made selection ambiguous against the moons' own cumulative fill. This task's brief ("gold is accent-only, never a whole filled surface") directly conflicts with that earlier fix, so the risk of shipping something less legible was real and named up front rather than discovered after the fact.

**The fix, both components**: the card surface no longer changes color at all, selected or not. Gold (`#C4A050`) now appears only as: a solid 2px ring/border around the selected card, the icon's own fill/stroke (face or moon), and a bolder/darker label (`font-bold text-[#1B3A2D]` vs. `font-medium text-[#6B7A72]`). `FiveFacesScale.tsx` (mood) additionally gained a small per-option accent dot (opacity-graded, matching the stress-scale pattern below) since it had no other magnitude indicator once the per-index fill color was removed. Forest green was deliberately *not* used as a substitute solid fill for either: Mood renders on the same section-mode screen as Energy (already solid forest green), and `FiveMoonsScale` groups with a boolean follow-up on the same section-mode sleep screen that also uses solid forest green via `TapBleedTile` — reusing that color would recreate the exact "two simultaneously-selected cards read as the same answer" ambiguity `CompressingRings.tsx`'s own comment already warns about for Stress vs. Energy.

**The moons' specific legibility risk, checked directly**: unlike Mood, a filled-but-unselected moon (the cumulative-magnitude effect) already renders solid gold, so "gold icon" alone can't distinguish "chosen" from "just filled." The truly selected moon gets three things no merely-filled moon gets — the gold ring, a slightly larger icon (`scale-110`), and the bold dark-green label — while filled-but-unselected moons stay a plain white card with a gray, regular-weight label.

**Verified live** (Playwright, `member.one@example.test`, 2s+ post-load dwell per the task's own screenshot-timing rule): selected "Excellent" and "Very Low" on the mood scale — both read clearly via the gold ring + bold label + gold-accented face icon, with no color difference between the frown and smile ends (only the geometric curve differs, as before). Selected "Terrible" alone on the sleep moons — clear. Selected "Good" with three moons already filled beneath it — the selected moon's gold ring and bold label are visibly distinct from the plain, gray-labeled filled moons beside it, though the contrast is honestly a step down from the old fully-inverted white-on-gold card; call this a real trade-off made in the brand rule's favor, not a bug, and worth a second look on a physical phone if it ever gets flagged again.

### 2. Mood + Recovery accent dots — the last two dim→gold color ramps are gone

The prior color-ramp pass (see "the stress-scale color ramp removed" entry above) explicitly flagged `MOOD_RAMP` and `RECOVERY_RAMP` as the two remaining borderline cases — both ramp from a desaturated/dim endpoint to gold, which still reads as an implied "worse → better" axis even though neither endpoint is a literal red or green. Fixed the same way item 7 was: `RecoveryFill.tsx`'s per-option accent dot no longer calls `rampColorAt(RECOVERY_RAMP...)`; it uses one fixed hue (`RECOVERY_ACCENT`, the ramp's own gold "to" endpoint) with a computed `accentOpacity` that varies by index instead. `RecoveryFill`'s selected-card fill (already a fixed solid gold, not a ramp) is untouched — it wasn't a ramp to begin with and wasn't named in this task's item 1. `MOOD_RAMP`/`RECOVERY_RAMP` themselves are untouched in `lib/checkin-color-ramps.ts` — both are still used by the check-in ending screen's "Today, in one color" blend, explicitly out of scope here, same discipline the stress fix already established.

### 3. Water tracker's "0 cups" — no longer red

**Root cause**: `waterStatus()` (`lib/wellness/status.ts`) classifies `cups === 0` as `'poor'` (its thresholds: `>=6` good, `>=3` attention, else poor) — the same red this app otherwise reserves for real alarm states (stress, pain). 0 cups logged so far today is a neutral, expected state, not a failure.

**Fix, deliberately local, not a change to `waterStatus()` itself**: that function's own header comment states it's the single source of truth shared by the coach dashboard (`app/coach/clients/[id]/page.tsx`) and the Wellness Index score (`lib/wellness/wellness-index.ts`) — a genuinely *completed* day with 0 cups is a different, real signal there than "hasn't gotten to it yet" on a still-accumulating, same-day tracker. So the override (`total === 0 ? 'no-data' : waterStatus(total)`) lives only at the two Today-page call sites that read the *live* total: `HydrationTracker.tsx`'s own big number, and the "Today's Recommendations" water line in `app/today/page.tsx` (which reads the identical `hydrationTotal` and was found, while auditing, to have the exact same bug — the whole sentence "Log water as you drink it, any time today." rendered in red for a zero total, not just a lone digit).

**Audited for other zero-as-red instances, as asked**: `painStatus(0)` is already `'good'` (0 pain = good, correct, not a bug). `movementStatus('none')` is `'poor'` (red) on the Home dashboard grid and the coach client page — found, but **not fixed**: unlike water's ambiguous "haven't logged yet" zero, `'none'` is an explicit categorical answer ("I did no movement today"), not a different component from the water tracker, and out of this item's stated scope ("only fix beyond the water tracker if it is literally the same component"). No other hardcoded red styling was found on Home/Today outside genuine error/destructive states (a failed save, sign-out, a real alert banner).

**Verified live**: `member.one`'s water total is 0 this session — screenshotted the Today page and confirmed the tracker's "0" and the "Log water as you drink it, any time today." line both render in the app's neutral gray-green text, not red.

### 4. "Today's Focus" + "A Note from Root" — merged into one card

**Checked for independent data sources first, as the task required**: `focus_text` (`buildFocusText`, `lib/feed/copy.ts`) and `coachNote` (`buildCoachNote`, same file, called with a fallback to `feedItem.coach_note` which is always `null` at creation time per `lib/feed/service.ts`) are both templated from the exact same `reason`/`category` for the same feed item — `buildCoachNote`'s own doc comment already states "never a second copy of focus_text/why_text — this is a warmer, complementary lead-in, not a restatement." They cannot disagree; merging was safe.

**The merge**: one `<section>` in `app/today/page.tsx` — the focus text leads (`today.feedItem.focus_text`, unchanged), a divider, then "A Note from Root" with `coachNote` beneath it in italic as the reasoning, signed "— Root." The decorative gold corner blob from the old Note-from-Root card carries over onto the merged card. "Why You're Seeing This" / "Ask your coach why," further down the page, is completely untouched — still its own section, same copy, same link.

**Verified live**: screenshotted the merged card — "Movement is today's focus..." leads, "A Note from Root" sits beneath a divider with Root's fuller, warmer sentence, exactly as specified.

### 5. "Update today's check-in" — now unmistakably primary

**Root cause**: this control is a single `<Link>` that is simultaneously the card and the only tap target — `bg-[#1B3A2D]` (forest green) with plain white text inside it, no distinct button shape. Structurally there's no separate "darker-green button nested in a dark-green card" as the audit's screenshot read; the whole flat block just doesn't visually announce "this is the one thing to tap" the way a real button does.

**Fix**: the card keeps its forest-green identity; a new cream (`#F5F0E4`, locked palette) pill — "Update check-in" / "Start check-in" + an arrow icon, dark green text — sits inside it as the actual button surface. No color outside the three locked hex values was introduced.

**Verified live**: screenshotted the card — the cream pill reads immediately as a tappable button against the dark green card, clearly the strongest visual element on the page's upper half.

### 6. Today page scroll length — remeasured after item 4

Measured via a real Playwright session (`member.one`, logged in, 2.5s post-load dwell, real DOM `getBoundingClientRect`/`scrollHeight` reads, not a screenshot ruler) at both viewports the task named.

| Viewport | Before (screens to "Was this helpful?") | After | Change |
|---|---|---|---|
| iPhone SE (375×667) | 6.39 | 6.21 | −0.18 screens (~120px) |
| iPhone 14 (390×844) | 4.97 | 4.80 | −0.17 screens (~143px) |

The merge saved roughly one card's worth of header/padding/divider overhead (the two cards' combined chrome minus the one merged card's chrome), as expected — real but modest. The page is still **roughly five phone-screens long on the standard iPhone, and closer to six on the SE** — the merge alone doesn't resolve the audit's "long page" observation, which the task anticipated (it asked only to remeasure and name the next target, not to keep cutting).

**Next single best target to tighten, identified but not implemented, per the task's own instruction**: the Water/Movement "Quick Log" grid (`HydrationTracker` + `MovementLevelTracker`, both `min-h-[172px]`) currently renders `grid-cols-1 md:grid-cols-2` — one column on mobile, so the two trackers stack vertically and cost roughly 172px + gap + 172px ≈ 360px of scroll on every phone. Both cards are already compact, roughly-square widgets (an icon/label row, a number with two small +/− buttons, a one-line status caption, a progress bar) that don't need full mobile width to stay tappable — switching to a 2-up grid on mobile too (`grid-cols-2` unconditionally, matching the pattern already used at `md:`) would remove close to a full extra screen's worth of height on the SE with no content cut and no card touched beyond its layout class. Not implemented this pass, since the task asked only to identify it.

`npm run typecheck` clean (both workspaces). `npm run lint` — 0 errors, 30 pre-existing warnings, none in any touched/new file. Full `vitest run` — **219 files / 2251/2251 passing** (41 new/changed tests: `tests/checkin-mood-gold-accent.test.ts`, `tests/checkin-water-zero-neutral.test.ts`, `tests/today-focus-merge-and-checkin-cta.test.ts` all new; `tests/checkin-scale-contrast.test.ts` and `tests/checkin-sleep-quality-single-select.test.ts` updated in place for the new accent-only/opacity-based assertions). No new/modified files under `supabase/migrations/` (confirmed via `git status`) — no schema change, nothing to push.

**Verified live** (Playwright, `member.one@example.test`, all screenshots taken 2+ seconds after page load per the task's own timing rule): mood scale high/low selections, sleep-quality moon selection against a filled backdrop, Today page water tracker + recommendations text color, the merged Today's Focus/Note-from-Root card, and the check-in CTA pill — six items, six confirmed live, screenshots opened and read by hand, not just captured.
