## Three animation changes — Energy Trend chart replays on scroll + gets vertical bars, mood face animates on selection (2026-07-27)

Scope: exactly these three items. Nav, the sleep bedtime/wake dial, the Home/Today empty states, "Today's Lesson," the correlation engine, the trend engine, the three-tier language system, and the adaptive question picker were all left untouched — confirmed via `git diff --stat` showing only `app/globals.css`, `components/EnergyTrendChart.tsx`, `components/dashboard/AnimatedEnergyTrendChart.tsx`, `components/checkin/scales/FiveFacesScale.tsx`, and their tests.

### 1. Energy Trend chart — replays every time it scrolls into view

**The shared-mechanism check the task asked for, done first**: grepped every `RevealOnScroll` usage in `app/dashboard/page.tsx` — it wraps six different Home sections (the hero/greeting row, Today's Numbers, "What Root Is Noticing," the Root's Daily Brief/Wellness Index pair, and the Energy Trend section itself), and its own doc comment is explicit that "reveals once and stays revealed... is a calm entrance, not a scroll-driven toggle" is deliberate for all of them. Changing it to replay would replay all six sections' fade/rise-in on every scroll past-and-back — not what was asked, and a much bigger behavior change than "just the chart." **Decision: left `RevealOnScroll.tsx` completely untouched**, still wrapping the chart's card for its own one-time fade/rise entrance exactly as before. The replay logic instead lives entirely inside `AnimatedEnergyTrendChart.tsx`, which already had its own separate, dedicated clip-path wipe animation distinct from `RevealOnScroll`'s fade — that component's job just changed from "mount-triggered, once" to "visibility-triggered, every time."

**The fix**: `AnimatedEnergyTrendChart.tsx`'s old mount-time `requestAnimationFrame` trigger — meaning the 1.1s clip-path wipe used to start the instant the component mounted, finishing unseen if the chart was below the fold at page load — is replaced with its own `IntersectionObserver` (threshold `0.3`, no threshold array, no scroll-linked continuous updates): `drawn` flips true the moment the card crosses 30% visible, and back to false the moment it drops back below that line, via a `setDrawn` updater that only actually changes state on a genuine crossing (`if (entry.isIntersecting && !current) return true`; `if (!entry.isIntersecting && current) return false`; otherwise a no-op) — which is what keeps it from replaying while already fully on screen or flickering on a slow/reversed scroll: the callback only fires on an actual threshold crossing, not continuously. The reset-to-closed direction is instant (`transition: 'none'`) so leaving the viewport never shows a visible "wipe closed"; only the reveal direction gets the real 1.1s `ease-out` transition. `prefers-reduced-motion` skips the observer entirely and renders the finished chart, same as before.

**A real bug found and fixed while verifying this live, not a testing artifact**: the first live-scroll attempt showed the chart never opening at all, no matter how long the wait — root-caused by attaching a raw, React-free `IntersectionObserver` directly to the same DOM node in the browser console to rule out a React bug, which reproduced the exact same failure. The cause: the clip-path style (`inset(0 100% 0 0)` when closed) was applied to the *same* element being observed. Chromium's `IntersectionObserver` computes intersection against the element's effective clipped render area, and a `inset(0 100% 0 0)` clip reduces that area to zero width — so a closed chart reports `isIntersecting: false, ratio: 0` regardless of its actual scroll position, which means the observer that's supposed to open the clip can never fire while the clip is closed. A genuine deadlock, not a screenshot-timing illusion. Fixed by splitting the element in two: an outer `<div ref={containerRef}>` with no styling at all (always reports its true, unclipped geometry to the observer) wrapping an inner `<div style={{ clipPath, transition }}>` (still carries the visual wipe). Confirmed the fix directly: clip-path samples taken every ~120ms while scrolling back into view show a real progressive open (`inset(0px 90% 0 0)` → `76%` → ... → `0%`) landing fully open within ~1.1s of crossing into view, exactly matching the intended animation — this would not have been caught without actually driving the app and reading computed styles over time, not just checking it "looks done" in one screenshot.

**Verified live** (Playwright, `member.one@example.test`, real seeded check-in history, natural incremental scrolling — a single large `window.scrollTo()` jump was tried first and does *not* reliably trigger Chromium's IntersectionObserver recompute in this headless environment, confirmed via a minimal static-HTML repro that worked fine, so all scroll verification here uses real `mouse.wheel` steps instead):
1. Scroll down to the chart, dwell 1.8s — clip-path fully open (`inset(0px 0% 0px 0px)`). **Pass.**
2. Scroll back up, away from the chart — clip-path resets closed (`inset(0px 100% 0px 0px)`), confirmed via the chart's real DOM position (2333px from top, nowhere near viewport). **Pass.**
3. Scroll back down — sampled every ~100ms: stays closed until the card crosses into view, then a real progressive open over ~1.1s, landing at `0%`. **Pass — this is the replay.**
4. A small reverse-then-forward wobble (−60px, +60px) while the card is already fully open — clip-path stays at `0%` throughout, no restart, no flicker. **Pass.**

### 2. Energy Trend chart — vertical bars added

**Scoped to Home only, via an opt-in prop, not a fork**: grepped every importer of `EnergyTrendChart` first (per this repo's own "check every caller before restyling a shared component" discipline) — it's also used unchanged by `app/progress/page.tsx` and the coach client view (`app/coach/clients/[id]/page.tsx`), and the task's own request is framed entirely in terms of "the chart on the Home screen." Added a `showBars?: boolean` prop (default `false`) to `EnergyTrendChart.tsx` rather than forking the component; only `AnimatedEnergyTrendChart.tsx` (Home) passes `showBars`. Progress and the coach view render exactly as before — not verified to be pixel-identical by fresh screenshot (out of scope to re-audit), but confirmed via source that neither file changed and the prop defaults off.

**The fix**: a `<rect>` per point, `x` centered on the point, `y` from the point down to the same `baseline` the existing area-fill already uses, so bar height is real magnitude (`Math.max(baseline - p.y, 0)`), not decorative. Rendered before the area-fill/line/dot layers (furthest back in z-order) at a single fixed low opacity (`#1B3A2D` at `0.14`) — locked palette, no per-status color, so bars can never be mistaken for the dot-color encoding (which is completely untouched: `DOT_FILL`'s four hex values are unchanged, confirmed by a direct test). **Animates with the line draw for free, no separate animation code**: since Home's clip-path wipe (item 1) reveals the chart's entire DOM subtree left-to-right together, and bars are just more elements inside that same subtree, they wipe in with the line and dots automatically — this is why "no separate delayed step" fell out of the existing architecture rather than needing new code.

**Bar width scaling, both named extremes checked**: a new pure function, `energyBarWidth(count)` — `spacing = (100 - 2*PAD_X) / max(count-1, 1)`, `width = clamp(spacing * 0.55, 1.2, 10)` (viewBox units, 0-100 scale). At 3 points, spacing is huge (~45 units) — clamped to the 10-unit max so bars read as clearly separated blocks, not a dominating slab that competes with the line. At 30 points, spacing shrinks to ~3.1 units — clamped to a 1.2-unit floor (renders as ~1.7 units at 30 real points, confirmed) so bars stay real, visible slivers rather than thinning to nothing. Verified visually via a temporary, throwaway dev-only preview route (`app/dev-energy-chart-preview/`, real component + synthetic data, deleted before finishing — never committed) rendering 1-point, 3-point, and 30-point charts side by side: the 3-point chart shows three clearly separate bars under the line; the 30-point chart shows a continuous, dense bar-chart texture that's still individually legible and stays visually subordinate to the line/dots. 20 new unit tests cover `energyBarWidth` at both named extremes plus monotonicity and a full 1-90 day sweep for non-zero/finite output.

**Verified live**: on the real Home dashboard with 6 seeded real check-in days (synthetic rows inserted directly for this verification, deleted afterward), bars render correctly under the real line/dots, subdued and clearly secondary to the colored status dots. Bar `<rect>` count matched point count exactly (3/30/1) and measured bar widths matched the pure function's predictions exactly, confirmed via direct DOM query, not just visual judgment.

### 3. Mood face — animates on selection

**The fix**: `FiveFacesScale.tsx`'s `FaceIcon` gains an `animate` prop. When true, the face SVG gets `mef-mood-face-pop` (a new keyframe in `app/globals.css`: scale `0.55 → 1.18 (60%) → 1.0`, `cubic-bezier(0.34, 1.56, 0.64, 1)`, `0.42s` — the same overshoot-easing "spring settle" technique `mef-scale-settle` already uses elsewhere in this file, just without the opacity fade since the face is already visible) and the mouth path gets `mef-mood-face-draw` (a `stroke-dashoffset` reveal using the `pathLength={1}` trick, so it works identically for all five frown-to-smile curves without per-curve length math). Both are short — well under half a second — and purely visual: nothing about them blocks `onChange` or delays advancing to the next question, since the existing tap handler still fires `onChange` synchronously and the animation is layered on top, not gating anything.

**`animate` fires only on a genuine new selection**, not on mount with an already-answered value: `FiveFacesScale` tracks a `previousValueRef` and only sets `justSelectedValue` (the trigger) when `value` actually changes after mount — so revisiting a completed screen never replays the animation, only a real tap does. Only the option matching both `isSelected` and `justSelectedValue` animates; the previously selected option's `animate` becomes `false` the instant a different one is chosen, so it just renders at rest with no leftover transform — no explicit "return to rest" animation needed.

**Haptic**: already covered — `triggerHaptic()` (`lib/haptics.ts`, a no-op wherever `navigator.vibrate` doesn't exist, e.g. iOS Safari/desktop) was already called on every tap before this task; untouched, no new code needed.

**Motion only, confirmed**: the gold ring border class string, the icon stroke-color logic (gold when selected, dark green otherwise), and the bold-label logic are byte-identical to before this task — verified both by direct source diff and by dedicated tests asserting the exact class strings.

**`prefers-reduced-motion`**: `FiveFacesScale` reads the media query once via `useEffect` (matching the pattern already used by `RootScoreCountUp.tsx` and others in this codebase, not a new shared hook) and forces `animate = false` unconditionally when it's on — the selected state (ring/icon-color/bold label) is entirely unaffected, only the motion is skipped.

**Verified live**, precisely, not just visually (a 420ms animation is hard to judge from a still screenshot, so this was measured): sampled `getComputedStyle(icon).transform` every ~20ms after tapping "Excellent" — real values: `scale(0.55)` at 8ms → overshoots to `scale(1.24)` at ~170ms → settles to exactly `scale(1)` by ~460ms, matching the intended spring shape precisely. Switched to "Very Low" immediately after: the new face reproduced the identical curve while the previous ("Excellent") face's `transform` read `none` throughout — clean, instant return to rest, no lingering animation. Under a Playwright context with `reducedMotion: 'reduce'`: the icon's `transform` stayed `none` for the full sample window (no `mef-mood-face-pop` class applied at all), while `aria-pressed="true"` and the gold border class were both still present — confirming "skip the motion, keep the selected state." `navigator.vibrate` was spied and confirmed called with `[8]` on tap (the existing haptic, untouched).

### Verification

`npm run typecheck` clean (both workspaces). `npm run lint` — 0 errors, same 30 pre-existing warnings baseline, none in any touched/new file. Full `vitest run` — **221 files / 2286/2286 passing** (37 new/changed tests: `tests/checkin-mood-face-animation.test.ts` and `tests/energy-trend-chart-scroll-replay.test.ts` both new; `tests/energy-trend-chart.test.ts` extended in place with `energyBarWidth` coverage and `showBars` gating assertions). One `npm run test` run mid-session hit the same session-contamination pattern documented in every prior entry in this file (this session's own interactive Playwright clicking wrote real rows for `member.one` today) — confirmed via `npx supabase db reset` followed by a clean full rerun, zero failures. `npm run build` (`--workspace=apps/consumer-web-app`) — compiled successfully, all routes generated including `/dashboard`, `/today`, `/checkin`, `/progress`, and the coach client route; the temporary dev-preview route used for bar-width verification is confirmed absent from the build output. No new/modified files under `supabase/migrations/` — no schema change, nothing to push.

**Test-DB hygiene**: 6 synthetic `daily_checkins` rows were inserted directly for `member.one` (via `docker exec ... psql`, not the app) to get a real chart on Home for the scroll-replay and bar-rendering verification above; all 6 were deleted before finishing, followed by a full `supabase db reset` and a clean test rerun to confirm zero contamination.

**Not verified**: real mobile Safari/WebKit rendering (headless Chromium throughout, matching every prior entry in this file). The `live` target against `app.mefwellness.com` was not exercised pre-deploy (no production test account, unchanged from every prior entry). The "wobble" and "reversed scroll" checks were driven via synthetic `mouse.wheel` steps in headless Chromium, not a physical trackpad/touchscreen — the underlying mechanism (a single-threshold `IntersectionObserver`) is the same regardless of input device, but real-device scroll-momentum/inertia behavior specifically wasn't tested.

**Deployed and confirmed live**: pushed to `origin/main` (`github.com/MEFwellness/mef-platform`, commit `396bcfb`); the push triggered Vercel's GitHub-integration auto-deploy. Pre/post-deploy checks via `npx vercel`: repo `MEFwellness/mef-platform` ✓, branch `main` ✓, Vercel project `mef-wellness/mef-platform` ✓, target `production` ✓ (not Preview). `npx vercel inspect` on the new deployment (`dpl_96NeTGmMFpyanNPK5AZHshr1de8j`) confirms its build log shows `Cloning github.com/MEFwellness/mef-platform (Branch: main, Commit: 396bcfb)`, status `Ready`, Aliases list includes `https://app.mefwellness.com` directly. `curl -I https://app.mefwellness.com` confirms the domain responds `HTTP/2 307`, served by Vercel. Not verified beyond that: no logged-in browser session against the production URL itself — all verification above ran against the local dev server + local Supabase.

## Four UX fixes — batch 3: "Predict tomorrow" overlap, wearable-modal timing, subhead contrast, quick-log cards side by side (2026-07-27)

Scope: exactly these four items. Nav, the sleep bedtime/wake dial, the Home/Today empty states, "Today's Lesson," the "Why You're Seeing This"/"Ask your coach why" pattern, the Energy Trend chart, the correlation engine, the trend engine, the three-tier language system, and the adaptive question picker were all left untouched — confirmed via `git diff --stat` showing only the files named below and their tests.

### 1. "Predict tomorrow" overlapping labels — root-caused and fixed at the shared component

**Confirmed live before touching anything**, per this file's own repeated lesson about not trusting a screenshot alone: logged in as `member.two`, drove to the evening reflection's forecast screen at 375px width, and measured real button bounding boxes. "Exhausted" (98.9px wide) visibly overlapped "Low" (starting at x=88.6) by 30px; "Moderate" overlapped "Good" by 25px — a real, reproducible bug, not a capture artifact.

**Root cause**: `TapBleedTile` (`components/checkin/scales/shared.tsx`), the one shared selected-state control `ShortOptionRow` and `StackedOptionRows` both render through, only applied `w-full` to its `fullWidth` variant. `ShortOptionRow` always wraps each tile in a `min-w-0 flex-1` column, but without `w-full` the `<button>` itself sized to its own text content rather than filling that column — invisible for short one-word labels, but a 9-character word like "Exhausted" rendered at its full natural width and visually spilled into the next tile once 5 options were packed into one row (the forecast question's own case: 5 options, not the 2-4 most `ShortOptionRow` questions use).

**Fix**: added `w-full` unconditionally to `TapBleedTile`, confirmed safe for its only other caller (`StackedOptionRows` already passes `fullWidth` on every tile, so it already had `w-full`). The existing `whitespace-normal break-words` on the label span now does its job — text wraps inside its own column instead of overflowing. Re-measured live after the fix: a consistent 8px gap (the row's own `gap-2`) between every pair of tiles, not overlap; "Exhausted" and "Moderate" now wrap onto 2-3 lines within their own tile.

**Audit, as asked**: queried `driver_probe_questions` directly (not guesswork) for every `single_select` question and cross-referenced against `resolveSingleSelectLayout`'s length-based row/stacked decision. The only evening-screen (`screen = 'evening'`) single_select question is `checkin_probe.movement_today` — its longest label ("Full Session", 12 chars) already exceeds `MAX_INLINE_LABEL_LENGTH`, so it renders via `StackedOptionRows`, never `ShortOptionRow`, and was never at risk. Three morning-screen questions (`room_temperature_comfort`, `session_intensity`, `emotional_load_today`) do render via the row layout with 3-4 options up to 10 chars — out of this item's "evening flow" scope to audit further, but now covered by the same fix regardless, since both paths render through the one shared `TapBleedTile`.

**New tests** (`tests/checkin-option-tile-full-width.test.ts`): static source scan confirming `w-full` is unconditional (not inside the `fullWidth` ternary), `StackedOptionRows` is unaffected, and the wrap-not-truncate label span is intact.

### 2. Wearable-connect modal — retimed to surface after the first glance, not on top of it

**Which component, resolved first**: the task named it "the 'Unlock smarter coaching' modal," but that exact title belongs to `ConnectWearableCard` (`variant="dashboard"`) — a full-bleed inline *panel* in the page's normal scroll flow, not a modal, with no dismiss button at all. The component that actually behaves like an interrupting, dismissible overlay (`role="dialog"`, `fixed inset-0 z-50`, localStorage-backed one-time dismissal) is `WearableWelcomeModal.tsx`, titled "Get the Most From Root." Every behavioral detail in the task (interrupts the first thing she came to look at, must stay dismissible, dismissal must stick) matches only `WearableWelcomeModal`; retimed that one and left `ConnectWearableCard`/`ProviderLogos` completely untouched, trivially satisfying "don't change the copy or the three provider options" since that file was never opened.

**Mechanism chosen: scroll position, not a flat delay** — "surfaces after that first glance" is a behavioral condition (has she actually looked past the hero and started reading), not a fixed amount of time an arbitrary reader needs, so a scroll trigger ties the reveal to real engagement rather than a guess. Reveals once `window.scrollY` passes 55% of one viewport height (past the hero greeting, not so far she has to hunt for it), reusing a plain scroll listener rather than a new abstraction. A 4-second fallback timer covers the member who reads without scrolling at all, so the pitch still surfaces in the same session rather than silently never showing.

**Verified live**, 8 real checks via Playwright against `member.one` with real seeded check-in history: no modal at 1.5s with no scroll; modal appears immediately once scrolled past the threshold (copy confirmed unchanged: "Get the Most From Root"); dismiss via "Maybe Later" removes it immediately; reload + scroll after dismissal shows no modal (**sticks**, confirmed); a fresh session with zero scrolling shows no modal at 1s but does at ~4.8s (**fallback timer fires**); `member.two` (no check-ins yet, the explicit "first-time member" case the task asked to check) sees no modal at 2.2s *or* after scrolling *or* past the fallback window — the pre-existing `hasCheckins` suppression gate in `app/dashboard/page.tsx` (untouched) still fully suppresses it, so a first-timer's experience is unchanged, not worse.

**New tests** (`tests/wearable-welcome-modal-timing.test.ts`): static source scan confirming the scroll-threshold gate, the bounded fallback timer with cleanup, the once-only reveal guard, byte-identical dismissal/copy/button logic, and that the dashboard page's suppression gate is untouched.

### 3. Subhead contrast — measured live, failed AA, darkened within the palette

**Measured on real rendered screens, not palette values on paper**, per the task's own instruction: since most screens using this style sit on a `bg-gradient-to-b` (not a flat cream fill), the real on-screen color behind the text varies by scroll position — so a Playwright script sampled the actual background pixel directly behind the text (via a real screenshot decoded with `sharp`, not DOM `background-color`) on 5 representative real screens, plus `getComputedStyle` for the real text color.

| Screen | Real sampled background | Ratio (before, `#6B7A72`) |
|---|---|---|
| `/checkin` | rgb(240,232,214) | 3.70:1 — FAIL |
| `/questionnaires` | rgb(240,246,242) | 4.12:1 — FAIL |
| `/membership` | rgb(242,247,243) | 4.16:1 — FAIL |
| `/connections` | rgb(240,246,242) | 4.12:1 — FAIL |
| `/checkin/evening` | rgb(225,228,222) | 3.52:1 — FAIL |

All five fail WCAG AA (4.5:1 for this 15px/400-weight text — well under the "large text" 24px/18.66px-bold threshold that would only need 3:1).

**Fix**: darkened by blending 35% toward the locked palette's own forest green (`#1B3A2D`, not an unrelated hue) — `#6B7A72` → `#4F645A`. Re-measured live on the same 5 screens after the change: 5.21:1 / 5.80:1 / 5.87:1 / 5.80:1 / 4.95:1 — all pass, worst case (`/checkin/evening`) still clears with a real margin, not just barely.

**Applied everywhere, not screen by screen**: grepped for the exact style string (`text-[15px] leading-relaxed text-[#6B7A72]`) across the whole `app/` and `components/` tree — found in 19 files (onboarding, welcome, food-lens, membership, connections, conversation, body-assessment, both check-in flows, and a few report list items sharing the identical style) — and replaced the color in every one, verified by a second grep confirming zero remaining matches of the old pattern.

**New tests** (`tests/subhead-contrast-ratio.test.ts`): real WCAG relative-luminance math (not just string matching) proving the old color fails and the new color passes against all 5 real measured backgrounds, that the new color is a genuine blend toward forest green rather than an arbitrary pick, and that all 19 known files carry the new value.

### 4. Water/Movement quick-log cards — side by side on mobile

**Fix**: `app/today/page.tsx`'s quick-log grid changed from `grid-cols-1 md:grid-cols-2` to an unconditional `grid-cols-2` (with a slightly tighter `gap-3 sm:gap-5` in place of a flat `gap-5`, to give each column a little more room at the narrowest widths) — exactly the change this file's own prior entry identified as "the single best remaining way to shorten the page."

**No content lost, still comfortably tappable — verified live at 375px (iPhone SE)**: both cards render side by side with zero horizontal overflow (checked every element's right edge against the 375px viewport directly, not eyeballed). Tap targets are byte-identical to before the change — the water ±1 buttons are still their fixed `h-8 w-8` (32×32px), the movement pills still their original `px-3.5 py-1.5` padding — neither shrinks to fit the narrower column; the movement pills' existing `flex-wrap` instead reflows from 2 rows to 4 (one level stacked per option) since the narrower ~121px inner column can no longer fit two pills side by side. This is a real, honest trade-off worth stating plainly: the Movement card grows taller as a result, which ate into the expected savings (see below).

**Today's scroll length to "Was this helpful?", measured before and after via `git stash`** (isolating just this one file's change, not a guess from memory):

| Viewport | Before | After | Change |
|---|---|---|---|
| iPhone SE (375×667) | 6.26 screens | 6.18 screens | −0.08 screens (~53px) |
| iPhone 14 (390×844) | 4.83 screens | 4.77 screens | −0.06 screens (~51px) |

**Reported plainly, not oversold**: this is a real but modest saving, smaller than the naive "~172px card height" estimate from the prior entry. The reason, confirmed by direct inspection rather than assumed: putting the cards side by side halves each column's width, which is exactly what makes the Movement card's 4 pills wrap to more rows than before (2-per-row at full width → mostly 1-per-row at half width) — so the combined section's height is now bounded by the *taller* of the two cards rather than by two stacked ~172px cards, largely offsetting the horizontal-layout savings. No further padding/sizing changes were made to chase a bigger number, since the task's actual requirements (side by side, no content lost, comfortably tappable, honestly measured) were already met.

**New tests** (`tests/today-quicklog-side-by-side.test.ts`): static source scan confirming the grid is unconditionally 2-column (and specifically the quick-log grid, not the unrelated Check-In Progress grid earlier on the same page), both trackers still render in order, and neither component's tap-target dimensions or option set changed.

### Verification

`npm run typecheck` clean (both workspaces). `npm run lint` — 0 errors, same 30 pre-existing warnings baseline, none in any touched/new file. Full `vitest run` — **225 files / 2315/2315 passing** (28 new tests across the four new files above). One `npm run test` run mid-session hit the same session-contamination pattern documented in every prior entry in this file (this session's own interactive Playwright driving wrote real rows for `member.one` today) — confirmed via `npx supabase db reset` followed by a clean full rerun, zero failures. `npm run build` (`--workspace=apps/consumer-web-app`) — compiled successfully, all routes generated including `/today`, `/dashboard`, `/checkin`, and `/checkin/evening`, exit code 0. No new/modified files under `supabase/migrations/` — no schema change, nothing to push.

**Test-DB hygiene**: synthetic `daily_checkins` rows were inserted directly for `member.one` (via `docker exec ... psql`, not the app) twice during this session — once to verify the wearable modal's scroll-trigger against a real populated Home page, once to verify the quick-log grid against a real populated Today page. Both were deleted immediately after their respective verification, followed by a full `supabase db reset` before the final test run to guarantee zero contamination.

**A real environment bug found and fixed along the way, unrelated to any of the four items**: the dev server's `.next` directory had been corrupted by an `npm run build` from the prior session (documented pattern in this file — a running dev server and a production build sharing one `.next` directory corrupts the dev server's asset serving, 404s on every JS/CSS chunk). Diagnosed via direct request-failure logging (not guessed), fixed by stopping the dev server, clearing `.next`, and restarting — required before any of this session's own live verification could run at all.

**Not verified**: real mobile Safari/WebKit rendering (headless Chromium throughout, matching every prior entry in this file). The `live` target against `app.mefwellness.com` was not exercised pre-deploy (no production test account, unchanged from every prior entry). Real touch/trackpad scroll momentum for the wearable modal's scroll trigger wasn't tested (synthetic `mouse.wheel` steps in headless Chromium, same caveat as the prior entry's Energy Trend chart verification).

**Deployed and confirmed live**: pushed to `origin/main` (`github.com/MEFwellness/mef-platform`, commit `54afd46`); the push triggered Vercel's GitHub-integration auto-deploy. Pre/post-deploy checks via `npx vercel`: repo `MEFwellness/mef-platform` ✓, branch `main` ✓, Vercel project `mef-wellness/mef-platform` ✓, target `production` ✓ (not Preview). `npx vercel inspect` on the new deployment confirms its build log shows `Cloning github.com/MEFwellness/mef-platform (Branch: main, Commit: 54afd46)`, status `Ready`, Aliases list includes `https://app.mefwellness.com` directly. `curl -I https://app.mefwellness.com` confirms the domain responds `HTTP/2 307`, served by Vercel. Not verified beyond that: no logged-in browser session against the production URL itself — all verification above ran against the local dev server + local Supabase.

## Today page — full layout redesign: one list, two zones (2026-07-27)

**Scope**: a complete restructure of Today's layout, not a tweak. Nav, the sleep bedtime/wake dial, Home, the check-in flows, the correlation engine, the trend engine, the three-tier language system, and the adaptive question picker were all left untouched.

### What was on Today before this task

Read in full before changing anything. Top to bottom: header (eyebrow, "Today," day/mode/risk pills, encouragement line) → `FirstCheckInWelcome` empty state (zero check-ins ever) → **Check-In Progress** card + green CTA card ("Complete/Update today's check-in") → **Quick Log** (`HydrationTracker` + `MovementLevelTracker`, side by side) → **Evening Reflection** link → **Guided Posture & Movement Assessment** card (`MovementAssessmentCard`) → **Comprehensive Health Assessment / Personalized Insights** card (`ComprehensiveAssessmentCard`) → **Today's Habits** (`TodayHabits`, read-only checklist + link to check-in) → **Today's Recommendations** (wearable brief + hydration/digestion status) → **Coach Messages** (`CoachMessages`, unread notifications) → the daily feed block: merged **Today's Focus / A Note from Root** card → **Today's Lesson** → **Today's Challenge** (+ Bonus, + Adjusted For You) → **Talk to Root** → **FeedInteractions** (complete/save/dismiss/reflection/helpful) → **Coach Insight** (conditional) → **Why You're Seeing This / Ask your coach why** → **Past Lessons** list.

### Structure: one list, two zones

**Forward Zone** (`app/today/TodayZones.tsx`, new client component) — the merged Focus card stays exactly where it was, unchanged copy, leading the zone (page.tsx still owns it, untouched). Beneath it: open quick actions only — check-in (if not done today), water, movement, each of the member's active habits not yet logged today, and unread coach messages (`CoachMessages`, reused as-is). Each disappears from this list the moment it's done. Once nothing is left open, the whole quick-actions area collapses to one line ("You're all set for today.") instead of leaving an empty container or a stranded heading — confirmed live at every combination.

**Accomplished Zone**, in the required order:
1. **Done Today** — check-in complete (with a small "Update" link, since that ability shouldn't disappear just because the big CTA left the Forward Zone), water logged, movement logged, and any habits logged today. Only renders once `doneTodayCount > 0` — no stranded "Done Today" heading at 7am before anything is logged.
2. **Your Totals** — two real, all-time, monotonic counts: **check-ins logged** and **days movement logged**. Both new count-only queries (`getTotalCheckinCount`, `getTotalMovementLoggedDaysCount` in `app/actions/checkin.ts`), reusing the existing `daily_checkins_current` view — no new table, no new column. Total check-ins and "total days logged" are the same number in this schema (`daily_checkins_current` is already one row per `user_id, local_date`), so only one stat is shown instead of a fabricated duplicate — the brief's third example ("total movement sessions") is instead **days movement was logged** (count of days with a non-null `movement_today`), since that's the exact same column the Today page's own `MovementLevelTracker` already writes to; the separate `member_wellness_events` `movement_logged` event stream is a different, unrelated logging feature not connected to this tracker, and using it here would show a number that doesn't match what tapping the Today control actually does. Always renders once a member has any check-in history at all — never gated behind a "something done today" check.
3. **Earned Capability** (`lib/today/capability.ts`) — real thresholds, not invented ones: `MIN_PAIRED_OBSERVATIONS` (imported directly from `lib/correlation-engine/evidence.ts`, not copied — currently `21`) is the actual minimum count of genuinely-paired days the correlation engine requires before it will even test a relationship, for any of its 18 seeded driver/outcome pairs. That gate is per-pair, not global — showing 18 separate progress bars would read as a scoreboard, not encouragement — so this uses the member's total logged days as the single clearest honest proxy: a real, necessary (though not alone sufficient — effect size and split-window agreement still have to hold) condition for every one of those 18 pairs. Copy is a remaining-count ("Log 12 more days…"), never a fraction or a percentage. Unlocks exactly at the threshold, stays unlocked, and gets the page's one real celebration the first time a member's browser ever sees it cross (localStorage-gated, same one-time-reveal pattern `WearableWelcomeModal` already uses — never replays on a later visit).

**Forbidden language audited, not just avoided by habit**: no streaks, no "in a row," no adherence percentages, no red for a zero/unlogged item, no denominator the member didn't choose. `tests/today-zones-redesign.test.ts` asserts all of this by source scan (absence of "streak"/"consecutive"/"%", no fraction expressions, no decrement path on either cumulative counter).

### Removed from Today, with a confirmed alternate path

`MovementAssessmentCard` (Guided Posture & Movement Assessment) and `ComprehensiveAssessmentCard` (Comprehensive Health Assessment / Personalized Insights) are gone from Today entirely. Confirmed **before** removing, not assumed: both are already rendered on Home (`app/dashboard/page.tsx:461,466`) — the movement card even gets Home's richer `imageBacked` variant — so every member state (not yet started, submitted/analyzing, analyzed) still has a real path to the assessment and to reviewing a completed one (`/assessment`, `/assessment/[id]`, `/profile/baseline`), just not duplicated on Today too.

**Called out per the brief's instruction, not removed** (not named in the brief's removal list, so left alone): **Today's Recommendations** (wearable brief + hydration/digestion status) and **Coach Insight** read as Home's job (scores/status/patterns) more than Today's (attention/accomplishment) — worth a look next time Home and Today are both in scope. **Past Lessons** is a historical archive that doesn't cleanly fit either "needs attention" or the new Accomplished Zone's three defined types, but wasn't asked to move.

**A judgment call, stated plainly**: **Today's Habits** (`TodayHabits.tsx`) wasn't named in either the removal list or the Forward/Accomplished Zone's example lists. It's genuinely open-or-accomplished content (a habit not yet logged today / logged today), so rather than leaving it as a third, disconnected card, its per-habit rows were folded directly into the two zones — open habits in the Forward Zone's quick-actions list, logged habits into Done Today — using the exact same `getActiveHabits`/`getHabitLogsForDate` data and the same "log it in your check-in" link, just relocated. `TodayHabits.tsx` itself was deleted as dead code (nothing else imported it).

### The three animations, and nothing else

1. **Item completing → travels from Forward to Accomplished.** A small purpose-built FLIP implementation (`useZoneTravelFlip` in `TodayZones.tsx`) — measures each tracked row's position every render, and animates the delta whenever it changes, which only ever happens when an item moves zones. Verified live: tapping a cup on `HydrationTracker` while it's still in the Forward Zone moved it into Done Today in the same render, still fully interactive there (same component, same tap targets — `HydrationTracker`/`MovementLevelTracker` only gained an optional `onTotalChange`/`onLevelChange` notification prop each, never touched their own markup or behavior). Moving zones remounts the tracker (different parent subtree), so `TodayZones` — not the tracker — now owns the live total/level, specifically so the remount reinitializes to the current value instead of resetting to the page-load snapshot; confirmed live (logged to 1 cup, watched it read "1" correctly after the zone move, not reset to 0).
2. **A cumulative number ticking up when it increments.** `TickingNumber` (not a reuse of `RootScoreCountUp`, which always counts up from 0 on mount — wrong for a returning member reopening the app) only animates on a post-mount value change. Verified live: tapping "Light" on the Movement tracker for the first time today ticked "Days movement logged" from 2 → 3 in place.
3. **A capability unlocking — the one real celebration.** Reuses the existing, already reduced-motion-safe `mef-scale-settle` keyframe (no new decorative motion added), gated by a `mef_today_capability_unlocked_seen` localStorage flag so it only ever plays once. Verified live via direct DOM/localStorage inspection (a sub-second CSS animation is hard to judge from a screenshot): the flag was absent, `mef-scale-settle` was present on the capability `<section>` the first time a seeded account crossed 21 logged days; after a reload, the flag was set and the class was absent — confirmed it doesn't replay.

All three respect `prefers-reduced-motion`: the FLIP hook skips the transform entirely (still tracks positions so a later change isn't judged against a stale reference), `TickingNumber` jumps straight to the new value, and `mef-scale-settle` already had its own reduced-motion override from the welcome flow work that introduced it.

### Thin data, tested explicitly at all three named points

- **Zero check-ins ever** (`recentCheckins.length === 0`): the existing `FirstCheckInWelcome` empty state, untouched — confirmed there's no "accomplished zone" to show at literal zero, since the whole zones UI (like everything else on Today) only renders once a member has at least one check-in ever. Screenshotted live.
- **3 check-ins across 10 days** (the brief's own example): seeded directly for `member.one` — Your Totals read "3 Check-ins logged" / "2 Days movement logged" (never "you missed 7 days"), capability read "Log 18 more days…" — real, non-zero, encouraging content, not rows of zeros. Screenshotted live, Forward Zone still fully open (nothing done today yet).
- **30+ check-ins** (seeded to 33, 17 with movement logged): capability card reads "Unlocked" with the real celebration confirmed above; Your Totals read "33 Check-ins logged" / "17 Days movement logged."
- Also screenshotted: the fully-done-for-today state (check-in + water + movement + both habits logged) — Forward Zone correctly collapses to "You're all set for today." with the Accomplished Zone carrying everything.

**Worth knowing, not a bug introduced here**: logging a movement level via the Today quick-tap resubmits today's `daily_checkins` row (pre-existing behavior of `logMovementLevel` → `submitEveningBodyCheckin`), which makes `getTodaysCheckin` — and therefore this redesign's "check-in complete" Done Today row — read as done even without visiting the full check-in form. This is the same signal the original page's "Check-In Progress" card already used the same way; not something this task changed.

### Scroll length, measured before and after on the same real data (not estimated)

Isolated via `git stash`, same seeded `member.one` account (33 check-ins, everything logged for today) on both versions, both measured via `document.documentElement.scrollHeight` after a 2-second post-load wait:

| Viewport | Before | After | Change |
|---|---|---|---|
| iPhone SE (375×667) | 4552px = 6.82 screens | 4048px = 6.07 screens | −504px (−0.75 screens) |
| iPhone 14/standard (390×844) | 4505px = 5.34 screens | 3959px = 4.69 screens | −546px (−0.65 screens) |

This is the fully-populated, everything-logged state (the longest realistic page); a typical mid-day state (some quick actions still open) measured 6.53 screens on the SE, in between the two removed-assessment-card savings and the new Accomplished Zone's added content.

### Verification

`npm run typecheck` clean. `npm run lint` — 0 errors, same 30 pre-existing warnings baseline, none in any touched/new file. Full `vitest run` — **227 files / 2344/2344 passing**, including 4 new/rewritten Today-specific files (`today-zones-redesign.test.ts`, `today-capability-progress.test.ts` new; `today-focus-merge-and-checkin-cta.test.ts`, `today-quicklog-side-by-side.test.ts` updated to point at the CTA's and the quick-log grid's new home in `TodayZones.tsx`). `npm run build` — compiled successfully, `/today` route generated, exit code 0. No new/modified files under `supabase/migrations/` — no schema change.

**Test-DB hygiene**: synthetic `daily_checkins` rows (3, then 33) and `habit_logs` rows were inserted directly for `member.one` (via `docker exec ... psql`) to produce the thin-data and unlocked-capability screenshots and the before/after scroll measurement; all deleted immediately after, followed by a full `npx supabase db reset` and a clean full `vitest run` (2344/2344) to confirm zero contamination.

**A real environment note, same documented pattern as prior entries**: running `npm run build` while the dev server was up corrupted the dev server's `.next` directory again; fixed the same way as every prior entry (stop dev server, clear `.next`, restart) — flagging again since this is now a recurring, known interaction in this environment, not a one-off.

**Not verified**: real mobile Safari/WebKit rendering (headless Chromium throughout). ## "Your Wellness Story" — both charts get the Home draw-in and bars (2026-07-27)

**Found the page first, as asked**: "Your Wellness Story" is the member-facing `<h1>`/title on `app/progress/page.tsx` (confirmed by grepping for the literal string, not assumed from the route name) — the exact same page commit `fa5a451` already touched. That commit flipped on `showBars` for this page's Energy Trend chart (`app/progress/page.tsx:193` at the time), so the Energy Trend chart here already had bars going into this task — confirmed, not redone. It had no scroll-replay draw-in yet, and neither did this page's second chart, the Root Score trend (`components/RootScoreTrendChart.tsx`, rendered via `app/progress/ProgressRootScorePanel.tsx`) — that component had no `showBars`, no `energyBarWidth` equivalent, and no animation wrapper at all, exactly as `fa5a451`'s own entry predicted ("RootScoreTrendChart.tsx... deliberately left alone... likely has neither the animation nor the bars").

### What was already done vs. what had to be built

| | Energy Trend (Progress) | Root Score (Progress) |
|---|---|---|
| Bars | ✅ already on (`fa5a451`) | ❌ built here |
| Scroll draw-in | ❌ built here | ❌ built here |

### The scroll-watcher: lifted, not duplicated

Commit `396bcfb`'s replay-on-every-scroll mechanism (IntersectionObserver, 0.3 threshold, reducer-style state so a small in-view nudge is a no-op, instant reset on exit, 1.1s wipe on entry, reduced-motion skip) lived entirely inside `components/dashboard/AnimatedEnergyTrendChart.tsx`. Per the task's own instruction, this was lifted out unchanged into a new shared `components/ScrollDrawIn.tsx`, and `AnimatedEnergyTrendChart.tsx` was reduced to a thin wrapper (`<ScrollDrawIn><EnergyTrendChart ... showBars /></ScrollDrawIn>`) — same file, same export name, same import path for `app/dashboard/page.tsx`, so Home's own code didn't need to change at all. A new `components/AnimatedRootScoreTrendChart.tsx` wraps `RootScoreTrendChart` in the same `ScrollDrawIn`, so both charts now share one observer implementation, not two. `ScrollDrawIn` was deliberately kept separate from `components/dashboard/RevealOnScroll.tsx` (the shared one-shot fade/rise mechanism five other Home sections use) — same reasoning `AnimatedEnergyTrendChart`'s original doc comment already recorded, now also stated on `ScrollDrawIn.tsx` itself.

**The known trap, avoided by construction**: `ScrollDrawIn` keeps the exact same outer-unclipped/inner-clipped split the Home fix already required — the `IntersectionObserver` watches an outer wrapper div that is never itself clipped, while the clip-path style lives only on a nested inner div, so the observed element's true on-screen position is never zeroed out by its own closed state. This was applied from the start (the split is literally lifted from the already-fixed Home code, not re-derived), and the live verification below confirms it actually works for both charts — the observer visibly re-fires on every scroll back into view, which it could not do if it were watching its own clipped-to-zero element.

### Root Score chart: bars built, reusing the existing width formula

`RootScoreTrendChart.tsx` gained the same opt-in `showBars` prop as `EnergyTrendChart` (default `false` — `app/root-score/page.tsx`'s own call site is untouched, still plain and unanimated). The bar block is a direct copy of `EnergyTrendChart`'s (same fixed `#1B3A2D` / `0.14` opacity, same "before the area/line so it sits furthest back," same `height={Math.max(baseline - p.y, 0)}` real-magnitude rule) — but the width itself calls the existing `energyBarWidth` function imported directly from `EnergyTrendChart.tsx` rather than re-deriving the same spacing formula a second time; `PAD_X` is identical (`5`) in both charts, so the math is genuinely the same, not coincidentally similar.

### Scope respected

Home (`app/dashboard/page.tsx`) required zero changes — it still imports `AnimatedEnergyTrendChart` from the same path, which still behaves identically (confirmed live, see below). The coach client view (`app/coach/clients/[id]/page.tsx`) was not touched at all — still the plain `EnergyTrendChart` with `showBars`, no animation, no Root Score chart of its own. `app/root-score/page.tsx` (the Root Score detail page, which also renders `RootScoreTrendChart`) was left exactly as it was — plain chart, no bars, no animation — since the task only named Progress ("Your Wellness Story") and didn't ask for the detail page to change; flagging this here as a deliberate scope boundary rather than an oversight, since the shared component could trivially support it later.

### Verified live, driving the app, not trusting a screenshot

Seeded 35 real `daily_checkins` days and 90 real `root_score_snapshots` days for `member.one` (hitting each chart's real cap: Energy Trend's `getRecentCheckins(30)`, Root Score's `getMyRootScoreHistory(90)`) and drove `/progress` end to end via Playwright:

- **Before any scroll** (300ms after load, both charts below the fold at an 800px viewport): both charts' inline `clip-path` read `inset(0px 100% 0px 0px)` — closed, confirming the draw-in is genuinely scroll-gated, not a mount-time animation a slow capture could catch mid-flight.
- **Scrolled into view + 2s wait** (past the 1.1s wipe, the exact timing this task warned screenshots have gotten wrong before): Root Score read `clip-path: inset(0px 0% 0px 0px)` (fully open) with **90 bars and 90 dots** in the DOM (`<rect fill="#1B3A2D" fill-opacity="0.14">` count, not eyeballed), bar width `1.2` (the floor clamp — correctly thinner than Energy Trend's 30-point width of `1.71`, confirming the auto-adjust rule holds at the longest real density this page produces). Energy Trend read fully open with **30 bars and 30 dots**, width `1.71`, matching the prior task's own Progress measurement exactly.
- **Scrolled fully out of view**: both charts' `clip-path` reset to closed (`inset(0px 100% 0px 0px)`) — the exit reset fires for real.
- **Scrolled back into view**: both re-opened (`inset(0px 0% 0px 0px)`) — the replay genuinely fires a second time, not just once per page load.
- **A tiny 5px-then-back wheel nudge while Energy Trend was already fully on screen**: `clip-path` stayed exactly `inset(0px 0% 0px 0px)` — confirmed no restart on a small scroll.
- **Reduced motion** (`reducedMotion: 'reduce'` context): neither chart ever gained an inline `clip-path` style at all (the component skips the style object entirely rather than setting it to the open value) — both render fully drawn immediately, no observer, no gating, per the requirement.
- **Home, unaffected**: same 800px-viewport test against `/dashboard` — closed before scroll, open with 12 bars after scrolling and waiting — byte-identical behavior to before this task.
- **Coach client view, unaffected**: no `clip-path` anywhere on the page at any point (no animation, as required), 14 bars present (unchanged from the prior task).

Screenshotted both Progress charts fully drawn: bars visibly behind the line/dots on both, colored dots unchanged, date labels and the Momentum/Resilience text beneath the charts fully legible with no bar or clip artifact underneath them — same contrast treatment already shipped and accepted on Home, extended to two more chart instances rather than a new one.

### Verification

`npm run typecheck` clean. `npm run lint` — 0 errors, same 30 pre-existing warnings baseline, none in any touched/new file. Full `vitest run` — **228 files / 2372/2372 passing**: `tests/energy-trend-chart-scroll-replay.test.ts` retargeted at `ScrollDrawIn.tsx` for the mechanism itself (the assertions that used to check `AnimatedEnergyTrendChart.tsx` directly now confirm it delegates instead of duplicating), `tests/energy-trend-chart.test.ts`'s stale "Progress and coach both stay line-only" assertions corrected to the real, current split (Progress animated, coach not), and a new `tests/wellness-story-charts.test.ts` covering the Root Score chart's `showBars`/bar block/reused width function, `AnimatedRootScoreTrendChart`'s delegation to the shared `ScrollDrawIn`, the Progress page's wiring, and that `app/root-score/` and the coach view are both untouched. `npm run build` — compiled successfully, exit code 0. No new/modified files under `supabase/migrations/` — no schema change.

**Test-DB hygiene**: 35 synthetic `daily_checkins` rows and 90 synthetic `root_score_snapshots` rows were inserted directly for `member.one` (via `docker exec ... psql`) for the live verification above; all deleted immediately after, followed by a full `npx supabase db reset` and a clean full `vitest run` (2372/2372) to confirm zero contamination.

**Not verified**: real mobile Safari/WebKit rendering (headless Chromium throughout). The live target (`app.mefwellness.com`) was not exercised pre-deploy (no production test account) — all screenshots and DOM/replay verification above ran against the local dev server + local Supabase.

## Four-part daily-loop follow-up batch: production test accounts, question-bank sweep, app-wide clearance fix, self-grading forecast (2026-07-27)

**Scope**: four independent tasks in one batch. Nav, the sleep dial, the correlation/trend engines, and the three-tier language system were touched only where a task explicitly named them (Part 4's driver-nudge; nothing else).

### Part 1 — Production test accounts

**Found first, not assumed**: `docs/DEPLOYMENT.md` reads as if no production deployment exists yet, but it's stale — `npx vercel env ls production` confirms a real, populated Production environment (`NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL`, `_ANON_KEY`, `SITE_URL`, `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY`, `ANTHROPIC_*`, `CRON_SECRET`, `EXERCISE_API_KEY` all set), and this file's own history (`c93f3df`, and every "Deployed and confirmed live" entry above) confirms production has been live and auto-deploying on every push to `main` for a while. `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` being a real Vercel Production var (which `docs/DEPLOYMENT.md` says shouldn't exist there) is explained by the four `app/api/cron/*` routes, which legitimately need it server-side — that doc is simply out of date on this point, not wrong about a real risk.

**Schema** (`supabase/migrations/00000000000114_test_account_flag.sql`): one column, `profiles.is_test boolean not null default false`. Excluded, defensively, from: `app/actions/admin.ts`'s `listUsers()` (admin's full member list) and `listAssignmentHistory()` (drops any assignment touching an `is_test` id on either side); `app/actions/coach.ts`'s `listAssignedClients()` (a real coach's caseload never shows an `is_test` client — the one deliberate exception is a coach who is themself `is_test`, who still sees their own assigned test member, since that pairing is the fixture's whole point). `packages/shared-types-contracts/src/database.types.ts`'s hand-maintained `Profile` interface updated to match. New integration test `tests/is-test-account-exclusion.test.ts` (5 tests) proves all three exclusion paths plus the one exception, against real local Supabase/RLS.

**Seeding script** (`apps/consumer-web-app/scripts/seed-production-test-accounts.mjs`): one coach + **three** member states (not just one) — `populated` (40 days check-ins, 35 scored days each for her/Root's energy forecast), `belowThreshold` (4 days check-ins, 3 scored forecasts — deliberately under `MIN_SCORED_FORECASTS_FOR_CALIBRATION = 5`), `empty` (zero history). Three states instead of one because the screenshot tool already expects exactly `memberPopulated`/`memberEmpty`/`coach` (see below) and Part 4's own live-verification step needs a third, "a few days in" state the screenshot tool doesn't ask for. All data uses a seeded pseudo-random day-profile generator (weekly cycle + drift + noise, never a flat or monotonic line) and a deliberately imperfect forecast-miss distribution (~75% real accuracy for her, ~65% for Root — worse than her, on purpose, per the task's "if Root is worse than she is, say so"; confirmed live before the fix, first draft came out 97%/97%, suspiciously perfect and identical, recalibrated). Uses `auth.admin.createUser` (not raw SQL against `auth.users`) — safer for a real production project. Idempotent: reuses an existing account by email, re-flags `is_test`, replaces its own date-range data rather than duplicating. **Dry-run tested twice against local Supabase** under throwaway emails (`qa.member.*@example.test`) — created accounts, seeded data, confirmed a real Root Score (72/100) and a real graded forecast + calibration chart rendered live via Playwright, re-ran to confirm idempotency (second run correctly said "already exists" / didn't duplicate), then fully deleted (`auth.admin.deleteUser`, cascades) — zero trace left in local Supabase.

**Documentation** (`docs/PRODUCTION_TEST_ACCOUNTS.md`): what exists, where credentials live (`apps/consumer-web-app/scripts/screenshots/.env.local` — gitignored, the exact convention the screenshot tool already uses for its own `live` target), how to run the seeding script (needs its own separate env vars, including the production service-role key, supplied fresh each run, never committed), and how to point the screenshot tool at production (`SCREENSHOT_TARGET=live npm run screenshots` — this flag and its credential-fallback discipline already existed, built in anticipation of this exact task, never exercised until now).

**Not yet done — genuinely blocked, not skipped**: actually running the seeding script against production requires the production Supabase service-role key and database password, which only the user has and which this agent has no way to obtain (a permission classifier in this environment blocked even pulling production secrets to a local file, and there is no local record of a production DB password). Asked the user directly for these. Once supplied: apply migration 114 to production, run the seeding script, then verify live.

### Part 2 — Full question-bank sweep (delegated to a background agent, full report below)

Simulated 87 days of real check-in submissions (through the actual `getTodaysCheckinPlan`/`submit_daily_checkin`/probe-answer code paths, backdated, on a throwaway account) until every one of the 87 reachable questions (the 88th, `checkin_probe.movement_today`, is permanently deactivated per migration 113) had rotated into the plan at least once — confirmed via `member_daily_probe_selections`. Then, since the real `/checkin` page only ever shows *today's* real plan (no backdating the browser itself), forced today's real plan directly through the same tables the picker reads/writes, across 10 cumulative batches, and drove the actual logged-in check-in UI with Playwright at both 375×667 and 390×844 — all 87 questions confirmed rendered in context (real neighbours, real triggered follow-ups), 32 screenshots captured. Both throwaway accounts and all scaffold files fully deleted afterward; confirmed zero orphaned rows.

**Problems found (report only — nothing fixed, per the task's own "I want to read the list first"):**

1. `lib/daily-checkin-adaptive/screenGrouping.ts`'s `PARENT_KEY_DOMAIN_HINTS` has no entry for `checkin_probe.desk_hours_today` — its follow-up (`got_up_hourly`) lands on the "Anything else" screen instead of next to its real parent on the body screen.
2. `app/checkin/CheckinForm.tsx` renders every local follow-up in one batch *after* all of a section's rotating probes, not immediately after its own specific parent — so a follow-up routinely appears several unrelated questions away from what triggered it (affects all 9 generic-`requires` follow-ups; the two hand-coded pain follow-ups are unaffected).
3. `checkin_probe.morning_soreness` has no visible control anywhere — its value is silently derived from the shared body-severity tap, so when the rotation picks it, the member sees no question at all for that slot.
4. `checkin_probe.digestion_rating` renders as a bare, unlabeled 1–5 scale (via `DriverProbeField`'s `SCALE_ANCHOR_LABELS`, which only covers `morning_grogginess`) — ambiguous which end is good vs. bad, unlike every other scale in the app.
5. `checkin_probe.night_waking_count`'s own authored prompt is never shown — only a generic sleep-arc hint appears; the control itself works.
6. Minor/observational: `stress_source_today` and the emotional-load follow-up can read as near-duplicates on the rare day both appear together — low priority, needs an uncommon rotation coincidence.

Re-audited specifically for the same shape of bug as the already-fixed `TapBleedTile` "sizes to its own text" issue — did not reproduce anywhere across all 87 questions at either screen size.

### Part 3 — App-wide bottom-nav / status-bar clearance fix

**Measured before touching anything**: a Playwright script logged in as both a populated and a brand-new local member, at iPhone SE (375×667) and iPhone-standard (390×844), across 18 representative static-route pages, and found the *majority* (76 of ~90) of `<BottomNav>`-rendering pages used only `pb-16` (64px) of bottom padding against a real, measured ~88px "danger zone" (the gold Check-In button's own top, which pokes above the nav bar itself) — a real, systemic shortfall, worse still on any real device with a nonzero home-indicator inset (which this environment's headless Chromium always reports as 0, a documented limitation repeated in this file many times before).

**Fix, at the shared level, not page-by-page**: two new CSS utilities in `app/globals.css` — `.pt-safe-header { padding-top: calc(2rem + env(safe-area-inset-top)) }` and `.pb-safe-nav { padding-bottom: calc(7rem + env(safe-area-inset-bottom)) }` — additive over the app's own existing dominant convention (2rem/7rem), so a zero-inset device (everything tested here) renders pixel-identical to before, and a real notch/home-indicator device gets genuine extra clearance on top. Reuses the exact `calc(<rem>+env(...))` technique `CheckinWizard.tsx`'s own Continue-button fix already used — not its specific 7rem constant, which was sized for that button, not this nav. A mechanical sweep script (run once, then deleted) replaced bare `pt-8`/`pt-5` with `pt-safe-header` on every page with a `<main>` wrapper (81 files), and bare `pb-12/16/24/28/32/40` with `pb-safe-nav` specifically on pages that render `<BottomNav>`. Two pages (`dashboard`, `coach/programs/[id]`) had deliberately larger baselines (8rem, 10rem) before this fix — kept their own larger reservation (`pb-[calc(8rem+env(safe-area-inset-bottom))]`, `pb-[calc(10rem+...)]`) rather than being shrunk to the shared 7rem, so "don't change spacing rhythm" holds exactly, not approximately. A handful of pre-authentication flows (welcome, onboarding, guest preview, `/name`, a print-only coach report) use a different `py-8`-combined CONTAINER pattern and a print page respectively — out of this sweep's mechanical scope, flagged rather than silently skipped.

**Verified**: `tests/safe-area-clearance.test.ts` (155 tests, static source scan, same "no rendering harness in this repo" pattern as `tests/checkin-continue-clearance.test.ts`) — confirmed red-then-green by temporarily reverting one file and watching it fail with a clear message, then restoring. Live via Playwright, post-fix, same 18-page/2-viewport/2-account sweep: **zero pages overlap** (previously up to 76 pages had insufficient padding), real measured margin range 24px–464px, `getComputedStyle` confirms the resolved padding is byte-identical to before the fix (32px/112px) at zero inset, proving no visual change at the only inset value this environment can produce.

### Part 4 — Self-grading forecast

**Found first, reused rather than duplicated**: the entire "predict tomorrow → grade it the next morning" loop already existed, for one metric (`energy_level`), built in a prior session (`9306f19`, "Add forecast and calibration loop to the daily check-in") — `energy_forecasts`/`root_energy_forecasts` tables with immutability triggers, a rolling-mean Root forecast, gap/tolerance/accuracy-percentage math gated behind `MIN_SCORED_FORECASTS_FOR_CALIBRATION = 5` on both sides independently, a "Predict tomorrow" question at the end of Evening Reflection, and a dedicated `/checkin/result` ending screen with three states (day-one/thin-data, scored, calibration-over-time). This satisfied nearly every part of the brief already. Built only the real gaps:

1. **Erratic-history abstention** (`lib/energy-forecast/rootForecast.ts`): `computeRootForecast` already refused to guess below 3 days of history; it did not refuse when history was *volatile* (e.g. alternating 1/5/1/5). Added `ROOT_FORECAST_MAX_VOLATILITY = 1.4` (population stddev on the 1-5 scale) and a new `too_volatile` result kind/`RootStatusView` kind, with its own honest sentence ("Root sat this one out — your last N energy answers have been swinging too much for a single call to mean anything"). An abstention writes no row at all, same discipline as the existing insufficient-history case — never counted as a hit or miss.
2. **Driver-informed forecast**: Root's forecast was a plain rolling mean, using none of "any driver relationship the correlation engine has genuinely earned for her account" (the brief's own words). New `getEnergyDriverBasis` (`lib/energy-forecast/data.ts`) finds her single most-confident genuinely-earned (`state` in the same `EARNED_STATES` Case View itself uses) `checkin.*`-sourced driver for `checkin.energy` (deliberately never a `wearable.*` driver, so the feature works identically with no wearable connected — the task's own constraint, not a shortcut), and `computeDriverNudge` (pure, `rootForecast.ts`) shifts the rolling-mean baseline by exactly ±1 point when her most recent real value of that driver sits more than 1 standard deviation from her own norm for it, in the earned direction. Method is recorded (`recent_average` vs. `recent_average_with_driver_nudge`) in the already-existing `root_energy_forecasts.method` column (free text, no migration needed).
3. **Backfill + grading safety net**: grading was purely view-triggered (only fired when a member happened to load `/checkin/result` for that date) — a member who predicted but never revisited that exact page stayed ungraded forever, and any forecasts stored before this task would start the calibration feature from zero. New `backfillOutstandingForecastsForMember` (`lib/energy-forecast/service.ts`) grades every outstanding forecast whose date has passed once a real `daily_checkins_current.energy_level` exists for it; wired into a new fifth cron, `app/api/cron/forecast-grading` (15:00 UTC, after the existing four), same service-role/`Promise.allSettled`-per-member pattern as every other cron in that directory. `vercel.json` updated.
4. **A real UI gap, not just a missing feature**: `buildEndingScreenView` hid the entire calibration section (both accuracy percentages + chart) whenever the member had *any* earned Case View finding, replacing it with a link to `/case` — a prior task's own judgment call, now in direct tension with this task's explicit "neither rate appears until enough graded forecasts... [otherwise] show both." Calibration and the Case View handoff link now render independently — both can show at once. Also added the brief's own required "define accuracy transparently, in her words" line directly on the card ("Within a point counts as a hit — the scale only has five steps, so exact and one-off are both a real read; anything further off is a miss").
5. **Placement**: unchanged — still `/checkin/result`, reached automatically right after the morning check-in submits. No new nav tab, as required; this is already "the return moment" the brief describes, not a new location.

**Verified live** (local Supabase + dev server, via the Part 1 dry-run seeding above, reused rather than a separate fixture): `/checkin/result` for the `populated` throwaway account rendered a real graded prediction, Root's own graded prediction, and — after recalibrating the seeded miss distribution off an initial suspiciously-perfect 97%/97% — a real, imperfect, non-identical calibration chart with both lines visibly distinct.

### Verification (all four parts together)

## Quick Actions: scrolling carousel replaced with a fixed icon grid (2026-07-27)

**Problem**: the Home dashboard's Quick Actions row (`components/dashboard/QuickActionsCarousel.tsx`) was a horizontally scrolling strip of pill chips. Any action past the right edge required a member to already know to scroll — actions were being buried, flagged directly by this task's own brief.

**What changed**: the file is replaced outright by `components/dashboard/QuickActionsGrid.tsx` — a static CSS grid (`grid-cols-4`, no `overflow-x-auto`/`snap-x`/`mef-scrollbar-hidden`), 4 items per row, max 2 rows (8 slots). Each tile is a single tappable `Link`/`button` containing a circular icon (pale sage `bg-[#E8F0EA]` background, `lucide-react` icon stroked forest green `#1B3A2D`) with a one-word `DM Sans` label centered directly beneath. `app/dashboard/page.tsx` swaps its import/render from `QuickActionsCarousel` to `QuickActionsGrid`; the "Quick Actions" zone header and its label styling (`ZONE_LABEL`) are untouched.

**Contents — sourced from the existing `LINKS` array, nothing invented**: same 4 links + the "Flag a Concern" toggle button the carousel already had, same hrefs/icons/behavior, only the labels were shortened to fit one word (kept in the same `LINKS` data structure, not hardcoded per-item markup): "Your Case" → "Case", "Food Lens" → "Lens", "Flag a Concern" → "Concern". "Movement" and "Progress" were already one word. 5 actions total, so the grid renders 4 + 1 across two rows — no placeholder tiles padded into the empty 3 slots on row 2, per the brief's explicit instruction not to. No gold `#C4A050` introduced anywhere in the new file (confirmed by a test assertion). The "Flag a Concern" toggle still opens the exact same `ConcernFlag` panel via the same `open`/`onOpenChange` controlled props — the only thing that moved is the trigger's visual container, not `app/actions/events.ts`'s `flagConcern()` or the safety pipeline behind it.

**What this task deliberately didn't touch**: routing/destinations for any action (unchanged), the coach platform (out of scope, not touched), any database/schema (none needed, presentational only).

**Tests**: new `tests/quick-actions-grid.test.ts` (11 tests, same static-source-scan pattern as `today-zones-redesign.test.ts` — no component-render harness exists in this repo). Covers: old carousel file is gone, Home imports/renders the grid not the carousel, the zone header is unchanged, no horizontal-scroll classes remain, the grid is 4 columns, all 5 original actions survive with their original hrefs/icons and a genuinely one-word label each, the action list stays a single `LINKS.map()` data structure rather than hardcoded markup, and gold is absent.

**Verified**: `npm run typecheck` — clean. `npm run lint` (scoped to the touched files, matching this repo's per-app ESLint config) — 0 errors/warnings. Full `vitest run` — **231 files / 2559/2559 passing**. `npm run build` (`next build`, dev server stopped and `.next` cleared first per this repo's known dev-server/build `.next`-corruption interaction) — compiled successfully, `/dashboard` included. Live-verified via Playwright against the local dev server + local Supabase: seeded one throwaway `daily_checkins` row for the local `member.one@example.test` fixture to get past the pre-first-checkin empty state, confirmed the grid renders correctly at 375px width and holds with no overflow/wrapping at 768px (tablet), confirmed tapping "Case" still routes to `/case` and tapping "Concern" still opens the same reveal panel inline — then deleted the seeded row (verified zero rows remaining for that account afterward, same test-DB hygiene discipline as every prior entry in this file).

**Deployed and confirmed live**: verified the deploy target both before and after. Before: production was already serving the prior task's commit `4c7a8f8` (`npx vercel inspect https://app.mefwellness.com` showed status `Ready`, target `production`). Pushed to `origin/main` (`github.com/MEFwellness/mef-platform`, commit `f03e10e`); the push triggered Vercel's GitHub-integration auto-deploy. After: repo `MEFwellness/mef-platform` ✓, branch `main` ✓ (main is what deploys to production), Vercel project `mef-wellness/mef-platform` ✓, target `production` ✓ (not Preview). `npx vercel inspect` on the new deployment (`dpl_9igB92MJEUYKQ8Y67emhMRCPpKH8`) confirms its build log shows `Cloning github.com/MEFwellness/mef-platform (Branch: main, Commit: f03e10e)`, status `Ready`, Aliases list includes `https://app.mefwellness.com` directly. `curl -I https://app.mefwellness.com` responds `HTTP/2 307` to `/login`, `curl -I https://app.mefwellness.com/login` responds `200`, both served by Vercel. ## Food Lens and Progress moved into the member bottom nav; Quick Actions rebuilt as two status pills (2026-07-28)

**Problem**: the three-item member nav (Home, Check-In, Today) read unbalanced — Home's highlight stretched to the left edge, Today sat stranded on the right. Separately, the icon-grid Quick Actions shipped the previous task (4 icons + 1 stranded on row 2, `components/dashboard/QuickActionsGrid.tsx`) took too much vertical space and truncated "Food Lens" to "Lens".

**Nav** (`components/BottomNav.tsx`): Food Lens and Progress added as real tabs, reusing their existing Quick Actions hrefs/icons (`/food-lens` `UtensilsCrossed`, `/progress` `BarChart2`) — no new routes. Final member order: Home, Food Lens | Check-In (center) | Progress, Today — two tabs on each side, symmetric. The coach nav is untouched: `MEMBER_LEFT_ITEMS`/`MEMBER_RIGHT_ITEMS` only apply when `isCoach` is false; a new `COACH_LEFT_ITEMS`/`COACH_RIGHT_ITEMS` pair preserves the exact prior coach layout (Home, Coach | Check-In | Today) so the shared `BottomNav.tsx` — used by both member and coach pages via the `isCoach` prop, confirmed there is no separate coach nav file — renders two different tab sets from one component. Mobile label font dropped from `text-[10px]` to `text-[9px]` across every tab (including the Check-In label, for consistency) so "Food Lens" fits without wrapping or truncating at the narrower four-tab width; verified via Playwright at both 390×844 and 375×667 — no tab wraps or truncates, left/right groups measure symmetric widths (75px/75px at 390px, 71px/71px at 375px), and the Check-In button's own size/shadow/gold styling is untouched (56×56 circle, unchanged classes). The existing gold active-state pill (`bg-[#F5B700]/[0.16]`) is reused unmodified for the two new tabs — no new gold introduced, just the existing pattern applied to two more tabs.

**Quick Actions** (`components/dashboard/QuickActionsGrid.tsx`, rewritten): now exactly two capsule pills, Case and Movement, in a `grid-cols-2` row. Food Lens and Progress removed from the `ACTIONS` data structure (they live in the nav now); Flag a Concern removed from Quick Actions entirely — grepped the whole member app first: `ConcernFlag.tsx`/`flagConcern()` (`app/actions/events.ts`) had exactly one caller, this file, so **Quick Actions was the only entry point to Flag a Concern in the member app**. The component and server action themselves are untouched and still exist; they just have no trigger anywhere in the UI right now — worth relocating deliberately rather than left orphaned. Pills: `rounded-full` capsule, icon in a small solid forest-green circle (`bg-[#1B3A2D]`, cream icon) for contrast against a soft sage pill background (`bg-[#EFF6F1]`) with a hairline border and light shadow, `mef-press` for the tap state, and the existing `RevealOnScroll` wrapper (unchanged) for the entrance animation — no gold anywhere in the file (test-asserted). Labels ("Case", "Movement") stay in the `ACTIONS` array, not hardcoded markup.

**Status lines — real queries only, no new ones**: `app/dashboard/page.tsx` already fetches `questionnaireCatalog` (`getMyQuestionnaireCatalog()`) and `bodyAssessments` (`getMyAssessmentsAction()`) in its existing `Promise.all` batch for other cards on the same page.
- **Case pill**: `caseStatus` = `"${completedCount} of ${totalCount} complete"` from `questionnaireCatalog`, the same completion count already shown verbatim on `/questionnaires` and passed to `QuestionnairesHomeCard`. Note for the record: this is whole-library questionnaire completion, not something scoped specifically to the `/case` route's own goal/driver/correlation data (no such count exists anywhere in the app) — it's the only real "N of M" completion query in the codebase, and it matches the task's own requested wording ("questionnaire completion"), so it's what the Case pill shows. Renders only when `totalCount > 0`.
- **Movement pill**: `movementStatus` = `"Completed N days ago"` (or "today"/"yesterday"), computed from the most recent `bodyAssessments` entry with a non-null `completed_at` (list is ordered newest-first by `started_at`, same lookup `MovementAssessmentCard` already does). This is the Guided Posture & Movement Assessment's completion data (a distinct feature from the `/movement` Movement Dashboard the pill still links to) — reused because it's the only existing query matching the task's literal ask ("whether an assessment has been completed, or how long since the last one"). Renders only when an analyzed assessment exists; a member with none sees the Movement pill with its label only, no invented value.
Both pills carry a `min-h-[64px]` with vertically centered content, so Case (two lines) and Movement (one or two lines depending on data) render at an identical height regardless of which has a status line — confirmed live both with and without seeded Movement data.

**Height**: Quick Actions section measured at 390px width, real member account, via Playwright — **200px before** (4-icon row + 1 stranded row) → **92px after** (two 64px pills + header).

**Verified**: `npm run typecheck` — clean. `npm run lint` — 0 errors, same 30 pre-existing warnings baseline, none in a touched file. Full `vitest run` — **231 files / 2563/2563 passing** (`tests/quick-actions-grid.test.ts` rewritten for the new pill design and the nav's member/coach split). `npm run build` — compiled successfully. Live-verified via Playwright against the local dev server + local Supabase at both 390×844 and 375×667: seeded one throwaway `daily_checkins` row plus one throwaway `body_assessments` row (status `analyzed`, `completed_at` 3 days back) for `member.one@example.test` to exercise both pills' real status lines and get past the pre-first-checkin empty state; confirmed "3 of 6 complete" and "Completed 3 days ago" both rendered in full with no truncation (an initial pass truncated the status text at 390px — fixed by tightening the pill's icon size/padding/gap, not by shrinking the pill or hiding data); confirmed the Movement pill correctly shows label-only with no line when the seeded body assessment was removed; confirmed coach login (`coach.one@example.test`) still shows exactly Home, Coach, Check-In, Today with no Food Lens/Progress tabs. All seeded rows deleted immediately after (verified zero remaining for both tables), followed by a full `npx supabase db reset` and a clean full `vitest run` (2563/2563) to confirm zero contamination.

**Not done**: Flag a Concern was not given a new home — the task asked only to report whether Quick Actions was its sole entry point (confirmed yes) so the user can decide where it goes next; adding one would have been inventing a decision that wasn't asked for.

**Not verified**: real mobile Safari/WebKit rendering (headless Chromium throughout, matching every prior entry in this file).
