## Progress page ("Your Wellness Story") restructured — Root Score promoted, Coaching Insights/Trends unified, Talk to Root retired (2026-07-28)

**Scope**: member platform only (`app/progress/`) — no coach file touched. The task's own "current order" description turned out to omit 5 real sections that exist in the live code (Recovery Trends/wearable data, a "From Your Assessments" findings summary, Your Wellness Patterns, Your Wellness Identity, a check-in History log). Asked the user directly rather than guessing whether to keep or drop them — answer: keep all five, slotted in by theme, with one correction to the proposed placement (below).

**New order**: Header (subtitle removed) → **Root Score** (biggest card on the page now, Cormorant Garamond 5xl number, `p-7`) → **Where You Are Right Now** (existing `WellnessStoryPanel`, unchanged, moved directly beneath the score) → one interpretive block: **Coaching Insights** (promoted from a nav row to a tinted content card reading the real `coaching_insights` table via the existing `getMyCoachingInsightsAction()`, preferring the `todays_insight` category; the three suggested-question chips moved here from the retired "Talk to Root" section) → **Your Wellness Patterns** → **Your Wellness Identity** (given a bordered treatment instead of shadowed, so it doesn't look identical to Patterns directly above it) → **Trends** (one card, one segmented control, replacing both the old standalone Energy Trend card and the separate wearable Recovery Trends panel — per the user's explicit correction, wearable metrics are segments *inside* this same card, not a second card beside it) → **Consistency** (Streak/Check-ins/Avg Energy collapsed into one bordered three-up stat row) → **History** → **From Your Assessments** + **Baseline vs. Latest Comparison** (kept as its own action card with the existing "Go to Questionnaires" CTA) → **Explore** (new small uppercase header, then Health Timeline/Assessments/Questionnaires as nav rows). "Talk to Root" is gone entirely; the floating chat launcher is unchanged and remains the one chat entry point.

**Reused, not rebuilt**: `ProgressRootScorePanel`, `WellnessStoryPanel`, `WellnessPatternsPanel`, `WellnessIdentityPanel`, `AssessmentComparisonView`, `getMyCoachingInsightsAction`, `getMyWearableMetricHistory`, `getMyProgressComparison`, `getMyHealthProfileSummary`, `RootQuickLink`, `EnergyTrendChart`'s pure geometry helpers (`buildSmoothPath`, `energyBarWidth`), and `lib/wellness/status.ts`'s existing 1-5-scale classifiers (`energyStatus`/`moodStatus`/`stressStatus`/`sleepQualityStatus`/`digestionStatus`/`painStatus`). `app/progress/WearableTrendsPanel.tsx` is deleted outright (confirmed it had exactly one importer) — its readiness bar chart and weekly-insights rollup are now expressed as four of the Trends card's segments instead.

**New, small, and scoped to this page**: `MetricTrendChart.tsx` (one generic single-metric chart, reused for every segment instead of writing one chart per metric — normalizes any `{value, min, max}` and reuses `EnergyTrendChart`'s path/bar-width math rather than re-deriving it), `TrendsPanel.tsx` (the segmented control, grouped "From your check-ins" vs "From your wearable" — the wearable group is omitted entirely for a member who's never connected one, matching this page's existing honest-empty-state discipline), `CoachingInsightsPanel.tsx`, `ConsistencyPanel.tsx`, and `streak.ts` (the streak calculation pulled out of `page.tsx` into its own module — Next's App Router route-type checking rejects an arbitrary named export from a page file).

**Structural fix A — minimum-data floor**: a line drawn from fewer than 5 real data points is not a trend. `MetricTrendChart.hasEnoughDataForTrend` gates every Trends segment; below the floor it shows an observational line ("Your energy pattern needs a few more check-ins before a trend can appear.") plus a link to `/checkin`, instead of a sparse chart. The exact same floor now applies to the Root Score trend chart (`ProgressRootScorePanel.hasEnoughSnapshotsForTrend`, raised from the prior 2-point minimum to 5) — the Root Score *number* and Momentum/Resilience stay visible regardless; only the chart itself is gated. This only touches `ProgressRootScorePanel.tsx` (used only by this page), not the shared `RootScoreTrendChart`/`app/root-score/` detail page, so nothing outside Progress changed behavior.

**Structural fix B — visual variety**: no two adjacent sections use an identical treatment now — Root Score (shadowed white, largest), Where You Are Right Now (shadowed white, existing internal variety), Coaching Insights (tinted `#EFF6F1`, no shadow), Wellness Patterns (shadowed white), Wellness Identity (bordered, no shadow — the one deliberate style edit to an existing file, made specifically so it wouldn't look identical to Patterns above it), Trends (shadowed white), Consistency (bordered), History (tinted `#FAFAF8`), the assessment block (shadowed white, intentionally similar to itself since From Your Assessments + Baseline Comparison are meant to read as one grouped block), Explore (plain nav rows, not cards at all).

**Structural fix C — real queries only**: Consistency's three stats and the Coaching Insights preview all trace to real queries already fetched by this page (`getRecentCheckins`, `getMyCoachingInsightsAction`) — nothing renders without a query behind it; `CoachingInsightsPanel` shows an honest empty sentence when there's no insight yet rather than a placeholder.

**A11y fix found during live verification**: the Trends card's check-in "Stress" segment and wearable "Stress" segment were both plain-text buttons named "Stress" — indistinguishable to a screen reader (and to Playwright's own role-based locator, which is how this was caught). Fixed with `aria-label="Stress (from wearable)"` on the wearable group's buttons only; visible label text is unchanged.

**Tests**: new `tests/progress-page-restructure.test.ts` (15 tests) covering the minimum-data floor on both the Trends card and the Root Score chart (including that null/not-logged days don't count toward the 5-point minimum), the wearable-segment-group visibility rule, `TrendsPanel`'s dynamic chart-bounds helper, and the streak calculation. Two pre-existing static-source-scan test files (`tests/wellness-story-charts.test.ts`, `tests/energy-trend-chart.test.ts`) asserted the old direct `AnimatedEnergyTrendChart` wiring on this page as a fixed fact — updated their assertions to match the new, intentional TrendsPanel wiring; everything else in both files (Root Score bars/animation, the coach view and `/root-score` being untouched, Home being untouched) still holds and still passes.

**Verified**: `npm run typecheck` — clean. `npm run lint` — **0 errors**, same 30 pre-existing warnings baseline, none in a touched file. Full `vitest run` — **232 files / 2579/2579 passing**. `npm run build` — compiled successfully, `/progress` included.

**Verified live** (Playwright against the local dev server + local Supabase, `member.one@example.test`): confirmed the empty-state page end to end at 390×844 first (zero check-ins) — every new section's empty state renders correctly, no console errors. Then seeded 10 days of `daily_checkins`, 7 `root_score_snapshots`, and 28 `wearable_daily_metrics` rows (readiness/sleep/steps/stress) directly for this account to clear the 5-point floor; reloaded and confirmed: the Root Score chart now draws (with the number/Momentum/Resilience it already showed), the Trends card shows both segment groups with Energy defaulted-active, clicking through Mood/Readiness/Steps tabs re-renders the chart correctly (graded dot colors on Energy/Readiness, flat forest dots on Steps, which has no existing graded scale to reuse), Consistency shows the real 10-day streak/10 check-ins/3.0 avg energy, and History lists all 10 real rows. All seeded rows deleted immediately after (verified zero remaining across all three tables for this account).

**Not done / left for the user to decide**: did not add a graded color scale for wearable Sleep/Steps/wearable-Stress segments (they render with a single flat dot color) — no existing status classifier covers those metrics, and inventing new severity thresholds wasn't part of this task.

**Not verified**: real mobile Safari/WebKit rendering (headless Chromium throughout, matching every prior entry in this file).

**Deployed and confirmed live**: verified the deploy target both before and after. Before: production was already serving the prior task's commit `7b24007` (`npx vercel inspect https://app.mefwellness.com` showed status `Ready`, target `production`). Pushed to `origin/main` (`github.com/MEFwellness/mef-platform`, commit `652916a`); the push triggered Vercel's GitHub-integration auto-deploy. ## Progress page follow-up: dead-end Root Score empty state, low-contrast Coaching Insights, regressed Energy Trend animation, History position, status-bar overlap (2026-07-28)

**Scope**: member platform only (`app/progress/`, plus the two shared chart components the Progress page uses). No coach file touched, no schema change.

**1. Root Score's insufficient-data state was a dead end — replaced with a real progress-to-unlock state.** It used to show the score number and, right next to it, "A trend needs a few more calculations before it can appear here" with no way forward. New `TrendUnlockProgress` (inside `app/progress/ProgressRootScorePanel.tsx`) shows "N of 2 check-ins", a thin fill bar, "N more check-in(s) and your Root Score trend appears here," and a link to `/checkin`. Momentum/Resilience are unchanged — still rendered in the same place either way, per the task's explicit instruction.

**The real threshold, and where it came from**: `MIN_SCORED_SNAPSHOTS_FOR_TREND = 2`. Not invented — it's `components/RootScoreTrendChart.tsx`'s own line-drawing gate (`withScores.length < 2`; you cannot plot a line through fewer than 2 points), confirmed as the *only* gate this chart uses anywhere in the app by reading `app/root-score/page.tsx`'s own call site, which renders the same chart directly with no wrapper condition. A prior task (the Progress restructure, same file, 2026-07-27) had raised this to an invented `5` inside `ProgressRootScorePanel.tsx` alone, disconnected from the chart's real requirement — that was the actual bug this task's "read the real threshold, don't invent one" instruction was catching. The real `2` is now the only place either the count or the gate is defined.

**A real bug found while wiring the threshold through**: the constant briefly lived as `export const MIN_SCORED_SNAPSHOTS_FOR_TREND = 2` inside `RootScoreTrendChart.tsx`, which has `'use client'`. Importing it from `ProgressRootScorePanel.tsx` (a Server Component) rendered "NaN more check-ins" live — Next's React Server Components bundler resolves a client module's plain exports as opaque client-reference placeholders when read from server code (fine when passed straight through as JSX, since it resolves once it reaches the browser; NaN under any server-side arithmetic or comparison). This silently broke `hasEnoughSnapshotsForTrend` too — the `>=` comparison against the placeholder object always evaluated `false`, meaning the animated chart could never have rendered at all regardless of how much real data existed. Fixed by moving the constant to a new boundary-neutral module, `lib/scoring/rootScoreTrendConfig.ts` (no `'use client'`, no React import) — both `RootScoreTrendChart.tsx` and `ProgressRootScorePanel.tsx` import the same value from there. Caught live via Playwright before this ever reached a commit, not left for the user to find.

**2. Chart dot colors — red removed page-wide, not just from the two named charts.** Both charts' dots were colored by `scoreToStatus`/a `statusFor` classifier (good/attention/poor → green `#16A34A`/amber `#F59E0B`/red `#EF4444`), inconsistently between the two (Root Score used the full three-color scale on real score bands; the Energy Trend segment, driven by `energyStatus`, could also land on any of the three). An ordinary day's value landing in "attention" or "poor" territory isn't a genuine concern — red is otherwise reserved for real alert states in this app, and having it fire on routine variation implied a meaning that didn't exist (same category of issue as the water-tracker red-zero finding from the 2026-07-27 UX audit). Removed the graded classifier from dot color entirely, on **every** chart on the Progress page, not only Root Score and Energy Trend — the task's own constraint said "anywhere on this page," and the six other Trends segments (Mood, Stress, Sleep Quality, Digestion, Pain, and the four wearable metrics) used the identical mechanism. Every data point is now a flat forest green `#1B3A2D`. No genuine concern-state signal exists in the data these charts read (`root_score_snapshots`, `daily_checkins`) that would warrant a special alert color, so none was invented. `lib/wellness/status.ts`'s classifier functions themselves are untouched — they're still used elsewhere in the app for labels/badges, just no longer for these dots.

**3. Coaching Insights redesigned as the page's one high-contrast anchor.** `app/progress/CoachingInsightsPanel.tsx`: filled forest `#1B3A2D` background (previously a pale `#EFF6F1` tint), insight statement in cream `#F5F0E4` Cormorant Garamond at `text-[1.85rem]` (previously `1.35rem` gray-green), section label/icon in cream at 70% opacity, "See all" in cream, and the three suggested-question chips restyled as translucent cream-on-forest pills (`bg-[#F5F0E4]/10`, `border-[#F5F0E4]/25`, cream text) instead of light-outline chips. No gold anywhere in this file — checked directly, per the task's explicit constraint. This dark treatment exists only on this one card; every other section keeps its previous white/tinted/bordered treatment so the contrast actually reads as intentional emphasis rather than a new page-wide pattern.

**4. Energy Trend's scroll-in animation restored — and made stricter than before.** Investigated why it stopped working: the prior day's Progress restructure retired the standalone Energy Trend card (which rendered through `AnimatedEnergyTrendChart`, wrapped in `components/ScrollDrawIn.tsx`) in favor of Energy becoming one segment inside the new unified Trends card, rendered by a new generic `app/progress/MetricTrendChart.tsx` — which was never given any scroll animation at all. That's the whole regression: a real component swap, not a config flag someone forgot.

Restoring it required more than re-adding the old wrapper, because this task's own requirements are stricter than what `ScrollDrawIn` provides: "fires once per page view, not on every scroll pass" is the *opposite* of `ScrollDrawIn`'s documented, deliberate behavior (it explicitly resets and replays every time a chart scrolls back into view — correct for Home's Energy Trend chart, which this task does not touch). Built a new hook, `components/useChartRevealOnce.ts`: same `IntersectionObserver`/reduced-motion foundation, but it disconnects permanently on first reveal instead of resetting on scroll-out. `ScrollDrawIn.tsx` itself is completely untouched — Home's chart still replays exactly as before. Also sequenced a second animation stage the old mechanism never had: the line/area draw in first (clip-path wipe, ~900ms, ease-out), then the dots fade in afterward (300ms, ease-out, delayed to start when the line finishes) — both `RootScoreTrendChart.tsx` and `MetricTrendChart.tsx` got this treatment via a new `animated` prop (opt-in, default `false`; `app/root-score/`'s own call site and the coach client view don't pass it and are unaffected). Total: ~1.2s, within the requested 800ms–1.2s window. Verified live: mid-animation screenshot shows the line genuinely mid-wipe; reduced-motion context shows the full chart immediately with no delay; scrolling a chart out of view and back shows it stays fully drawn rather than resetting to blank.

**5. History moved to the very bottom of the page**, below Explore — same content, same "edited" badge, same styling, position change only.

**6. Status-bar scroll overlap**: added a `fixed inset-x-0 top-0 z-40` scrim, sized to `env(safe-area-inset-top)`, colored to match the page's own top gradient stop (`#EFF6F1`), pinned above all scrollable content in `app/progress/page.tsx`. **Caveat, stated plainly**: this repo's own established note (`app/globals.css`'s safe-area-clearance comment, referenced by multiple prior entries in this file) is that `env(safe-area-inset-top)` always reports `0` in this environment — every real device tested here, and headless Chromium, both report zero. That means this fix cannot be visually verified as fixing the actual collision in this environment; only that the scrim renders with no layout side effects (confirmed via Playwright at 390×844 and 375×667 — no visual regression, no console errors).

**Tests**: `tests/progress-page-restructure.test.ts` — replaced the stale 4-vs-5 threshold assertions with 1-vs-2 (the real number), and added a block asserting `MIN_SCORED_SNAPSHOTS_FOR_TREND` is imported (not re-declared) and equals `2`, plus that crossing it flips `hasEnoughSnapshotsForTrend` from `false` to `true`. `tests/wellness-story-charts.test.ts` — updated the Root Score dot-color assertions (DOT_FILL/red are gone, flat forest fill is present), replaced the "wraps in ScrollDrawIn" assertions with "opts into the `animated` prop, does not import ScrollDrawIn", and added a block confirming `useChartRevealOnce` disconnects-once (vs. `ScrollDrawIn`'s untouched replay behavior) and that the new config module has no `'use client'` directive.

**Verified**: `npm run typecheck` — clean. `npm run lint` — **0 errors**, same 30 pre-existing warnings baseline, none in a touched file. Full `vitest run` — **232 files / 2587/2587 passing**. `npm run build` — compiled successfully.

**Verified live** (Playwright, local dev server + local Supabase, `member.one@example.test`, both 390×844 and 375×667): found and cleaned up one incidental data-hygiene issue first — a stale `coaching_insights` row for this account, generated by a prior session's own live-verification pass and never deleted, was making the empty-state screenshot show a real insight where zero check-ins should show none; deleted it before testing. Confirmed the empty state end to end (no console errors), then seeded real `daily_checkins`/`root_score_snapshots` incrementally: at exactly 1 real snapshot, confirmed "1 of 2 check-ins" with a half-filled bar and correct singular copy ("1 more check-in," not "1 more check-ins"); at 2, confirmed the chart itself renders (proof the NaN bug's fix was real, not just cosmetic); at 6, confirmed Root Score, Trends (Energy segment), Consistency, and History all show correct real numbers, all chart dots flat forest green with no red or gold anywhere. All seeded rows deleted after (verified zero remaining across `daily_checkins`, `root_score_snapshots`, `coaching_insights` for this account).

**Not done / left for the user to decide**: the status-bar overlap fix could not be visually confirmed as actually resolving the collision, for the environment reason stated above — someone should check it on a real iPhone.

**Not verified**: real mobile Safari/WebKit rendering (headless Chromium throughout, matching every prior entry in this file).

**Deployed and confirmed live**: verified the deploy target both before and after. Before: production was already serving the prior task's commit `652916a` (`npx vercel inspect https://app.mefwellness.com` showed status `Ready`, target `production`). Pushed to `origin/main` (`github.com/MEFwellness/mef-platform`, commit `59a616f`); the push triggered Vercel's GitHub-integration auto-deploy. After: repo `MEFwellness/mef-platform` ✓, branch `main` ✓ (main is what deploys to production), Vercel project `mef-wellness/mef-platform` ✓, target `production` ✓ (not Preview). `npx vercel inspect --logs` on the new deployment (`dpl_AKf6CytJ5XwqFPHdkJVj2hCAipuS`) confirms its build log shows `Cloning github.com/MEFwellness/mef-platform (Branch: main, Commit: 59a616f)`, status `Ready` — confirmed by inspecting `https://app.mefwellness.com` itself and matching the deployment id (`mef-platform-qpl0ahunb-mef-wellness.vercel.app`). `curl -I https://app.mefwellness.com/login` responds `200`; `curl -I https://app.mefwellness.com/progress` responds `307` to login (expected — unauthenticated). Not verified beyond that: no logged-in browser session against the production URL itself — all live-app verification above ran against the local dev server + local Supabase, per this file's standing caveat.

## Both Root Score charts unified onto Home's exact Energy Trend chart component and animation (2026-07-28)

**Scope**: member platform only — `components/RootScoreTrendChart.tsx`, `components/AnimatedRootScoreTrendChart.tsx`, `app/root-score/page.tsx`. No coach file touched, no schema change.

**1. Investigation — were the three charts the same component?** No. Three genuinely different setups:
- **Home's Energy Trend** (`components/dashboard/AnimatedEnergyTrendChart.tsx`): `components/ScrollDrawIn.tsx` wrapped around the plain `components/EnergyTrendChart.tsx`. Verified live via Playwright that this mechanism **replays its draw-in every time the chart scrolls out of and back into view** — scrolling away then back re-clips the chart to nearly zero width and redraws it from scratch. This is documented, deliberate behavior (`ScrollDrawIn.tsx`'s own header comment), not something I assumed from reading code.
- **Progress's Root Score chart**: as of the prior task, `RootScoreTrendChart.tsx` carried its *own* internal `animated` prop plus a bespoke `components/useChartRevealOnce.ts` hook — a *once-per-page-view* mechanism (fires once, never resets), explicitly built differently from `ScrollDrawIn` because an earlier task asked for that specific behavior.
- **The Root Score detail page's "Root Score Trend"**: `app/root-score/page.tsx` rendered the plain `RootScoreTrendChart` directly with **no animation prop and no `showBars` at all** — completely static.

This is why the two Root Score charts "looked like bare dots with no line": the connecting line (`<path d={linePath}>`) was always present in the code on both — it never actually rendered as *only dots*. What was really happening: the detail page's chart was static, so the line was there but any impression of "it draws in" simply never happened (no animation at all); and both Root Score charts' 3-point real data (see finding 5) is close enough together vertically that a subtle, un-animated line reads as easy to miss/mistake for "just dots" next to bolder, colored, animated dots. Confirmed live with real seeded data (below) that a real connecting line renders correctly on both once animation was unified in.

**2/3. Unified onto Home's real component and configuration.** `RootScoreTrendChart.tsx` is now a plain, unanimated chart again — its bespoke `animated` prop, `useChartRevealOnce` usage, and the internal clip-path/dot-transition logic are all removed, and its local duplicate `buildSmoothPath` is gone in favor of importing it (and `energyBarWidth`) from `EnergyTrendChart.tsx`, exactly like `app/progress/MetricTrendChart.tsx` already does. `AnimatedRootScoreTrendChart.tsx` now wraps it in `ScrollDrawIn` — the identical pattern `AnimatedEnergyTrendChart.tsx` uses for Home. `app/root-score/page.tsx`'s "Root Score Trend" section now renders through `AnimatedRootScoreTrendChart` instead of the bare chart. All three trend charts on the member platform — Home's Energy Trend, Progress's Root Score, and the detail page's Root Score Trend — now share one real animation mechanism (`ScrollDrawIn`, 1.1s ease-out, replays on every scroll pass, respects reduced-motion) and the same underlying line/bar/dot rendering code.

**A real discrepancy found and reported rather than silently resolved**: the task's own description of "how Home's chart animates" includes "fires once per page view, not on every scroll pass" and "data points fade in after the line completes." Neither matches Home's actual, verified behavior — `ScrollDrawIn` replays every scroll pass, and reveals the whole card (line, bars, and dots together) as one single wipe, not a separate two-stage sequence. Given the task's own stronger, repeated instruction ("match Home *exactly*... do not invent new timing... use the same component if one exists"), I unified onto Home's real, verified mechanism rather than preserving the previous task's once-per-view variant — which was itself the actual deviation from Home, not something to reconcile with it. Flagging this rather than quietly picking a side, since it's the opposite of what the task's own bullet list described.

`components/useChartRevealOnce.ts` is untouched, not deleted — `app/progress/MetricTrendChart.tsx` (the Trends card's per-metric charts, out of scope here) still genuinely depends on it.

**4. The red "46" was driven by real logic — now fixed.** `app/root-score/page.tsx` colored the big score number via `STATUS_STYLES[scoreToStatus(root_score)].text` — the same good (≥70, green-700) / attention (55-69, amber-700) / poor (<55, red-700) classification used elsewhere in the app. A score of 46 fell in "poor," rendering red-700. Changed to a fixed `text-[#1B3A2D]` (forest green) always — the score number is an ordinary value, not a genuine concern state. `scoreToStatus`'s import and the `status` variable were removed from this page entirely (no longer used anywhere in it). Left untouched: `ChangeBadge`'s own separate status (green "up"/red "down"/amber "steady," derived from the *direction* of change, not a fixed score threshold) — a real directional signal, not an ordinary value colored red for no reason, and outside what this task asked to change.

**5. The "flat line" contradiction — investigated, not fabricated.** Seeded three real, distinct Root Score snapshots (40 → 42 → 46, `root_score_change` 4 on the last) and confirmed live: the chart **does** plot a real, correctly-sloped line — visibly, if subtly, rising left to right — not a bug-flattened one. The y-coordinate math (`normalized = root_score / 100`, then mapped to the chart's vertical range) was checked directly and produces genuinely different heights for genuinely different scores; there is no rendering defect. The reason three consecutive real days can look close together on a full 0-100 scale is `lib/scoring/config.ts`'s `MAX_ROOT_SCORE_DAILY_CHANGE = 6` — the Root Score system's deliberate anti-gaming cap, which limits how far the stored score can move in a single day regardless of the raw underlying composite (`lib/scoring/aggregate.ts`'s `applySmoothingCap`). A 4-point move in one day is a real, correctly-computed, capped-but-genuine change — it just doesn't look dramatic next to 0-100 on a compact chart. This is by design, not a bug; not fabricated or adjusted to look better, per the task's own instruction.

**Tests**: `tests/wellness-story-charts.test.ts` — substantially rewritten (this file has now chronicled three superseded states of these charts across three related tasks) to assert the final, current wiring: `RootScoreTrendChart` has no internal animation logic and reuses `EnergyTrendChart`'s path/width helpers; `AnimatedRootScoreTrendChart` wraps in `ScrollDrawIn` matching `AnimatedEnergyTrendChart`'s own shape; `ScrollDrawIn` itself (replay behavior, 1.1s ease-out) is unchanged; `useChartRevealOnce` still exists and is still a real dependency of `MetricTrendChart`; the Root Score detail page renders through `AnimatedRootScoreTrendChart` and colors its score number a fixed forest green with no `scoreToStatus` import; the coach client view remains untouched.

**Verified**: `npm run typecheck` — clean. `npm run lint` — **0 errors**, same 30 pre-existing warnings baseline, none in a touched file. Full `vitest run` — **232 files / 2591/2591 passing** (one transient failure in `tests/feed-integration.test.ts` on a single full-suite pass, unrelated to any file this task touched — confirmed by re-running that file alone, passed, and by a subsequent clean full-suite run, 2591/2591 — same shared-local-Supabase cross-file timing artifact this file has documented before, not a regression). `npm run build` — compiled successfully.

**Verified live** (Playwright, local dev server + local Supabase, `member.one@example.test`, both 390×844 and 375×667): seeded 3 real root_score_snapshots (40/42/46) and 7 daily_checkins; confirmed on both `/progress` and `/root-score` — a real connecting line + bars + flat forest-green dots render (no red, no gold); the score number is forest green on the detail page; scrolling the detail page's Root Score Trend chart away and back re-clips and redraws it (replay confirmed, matching Home); with `reduced-motion` enabled, the chart renders fully immediately with no animation. All seeded rows deleted after (verified zero remaining for this account).

**Not done / left for the user to decide**: nothing — both charts now genuinely match Home's real component and configuration, and the two described-vs-actual discrepancies in Home's behavior are flagged above for awareness, not silently resolved either way.

**Not verified**: real mobile Safari/WebKit rendering (headless Chromium throughout, matching every prior entry in this file).

**Deployed and confirmed live**: verified the deploy target both before and after. Before: production was already serving the prior task's commit `59a616f` (`npx vercel inspect https://app.mefwellness.com` showed status `Ready`, target `production`). Pushed to `origin/main` (`github.com/MEFwellness/mef-platform`, commit `795901a`); the push triggered Vercel's GitHub-integration auto-deploy. After: repo `MEFwellness/mef-platform` ✓, branch `main` ✓ (main is what deploys to production), Vercel project `mef-wellness/mef-platform` ✓, target `production` ✓ (not Preview). `npx vercel inspect --logs` on the new deployment (`dpl_BK1jmf88g4WHM7JdKBbqdZ7VJ9B7`) confirms its build log shows `Cloning github.com/MEFwellness/mef-platform (Branch: main, Commit: 795901a)`, status `Ready` — confirmed by inspecting `https://app.mefwellness.com` itself and matching the deployment id (`mef-platform-ju6if35kb-mef-wellness.vercel.app`). `curl -I https://app.mefwellness.com/login` responds `200`; `curl -I https://app.mefwellness.com/root-score` responds `307` to login (expected — unauthenticated). Not verified beyond that: no logged-in browser session against the production URL itself — all live-app verification above ran against the local dev server + local Supabase, per this file's standing caveat.

## Trend charts get a real value axis, per-point date labels, and a 1-week/2-week/1-month range selector (2026-07-28)

**Scope**: member platform only — Home's Energy Trend, Progress's Root Score panel, and the Root Score detail page's Root Score Trend. No coach file touched, no schema change.

**1. Were the three charts already one shared component? No, not literally — investigated first, per the task's instruction.** `components/EnergyTrendChart.tsx` (Home + the coach client view) and the prior `components/RootScoreTrendChart.tsx` (both Root Score charts) were two separate React components that only shared pure geometry helper functions (`buildSmoothPath`, `energyBarWidth`), not a single component. Unified this task's three named charts onto one real shared pair: `components/TrendChart.tsx` (the pure chart — axis, gap-aware line, date labels, tooltip) and `components/TrendChartCard.tsx` (owns the range-selector state, slices real data to the selected window, wraps the chart in the existing `components/ScrollDrawIn.tsx` animation). `components/EnergyTrendChart.tsx` itself is completely untouched — it's still the coach client view's own chart (`app/coach/clients/[id]/page.tsx`, out of scope) and still the source `app/progress/MetricTrendChart.tsx` (the *separate* Trends-card segmented chart on Progress — not one of the three named charts, also untouched) reuses for its own geometry helpers. `components/RootScoreTrendChart.tsx` and its `AnimatedRootScoreTrendChart` wrapper are fully superseded — the old chart file is deleted outright (confirmed via grep it had no other consumer); `AnimatedRootScoreTrendChart.tsx` keeps its exact name/path/export but now builds a `TrendChartCard` internally, so neither Progress's `ProgressRootScorePanel.tsx` nor `app/root-score/page.tsx` needed their import statements to change. `components/dashboard/AnimatedEnergyTrendChart.tsx` similarly keeps its name/path but no longer renders `EnergyTrendChart` at all — it builds the same shared `TrendChartCard`, configured for energy's scale, so `app/dashboard/page.tsx` didn't need to change its import either.

**2. Value axis — the real range for each metric, and where it came from.** Energy: `packages/shared-types-contracts/src/database.types.ts`'s own comment on `predicted_energy_level` states "1-5, same scale as `daily_checkins.energy_level`"; confirmed independently by the check-in form's `ENERGY_MEANING` word scale (`app/checkin/CheckinForm.tsx`, 5 entries: Exhausted/Low/Moderate/Good/High) and `daily_checkins_energy_level_check`'s own Postgres CHECK constraint (`energy_level >= 1 AND energy_level <= 5`). Axis ticks: `[1, 3, 5]` — a small, evenly-spaced set per the task's "roughly 3-5" instruction, not the fuller `[1,2,3,4,5]` the old `EnergyTrendChart` gridlines used. Root Score: `root_score_snapshots.root_score`'s own CHECK constraint (`>= 0 AND <= 100`), independently confirmed by `lib/scoring/aggregate.ts`'s explicit clamp (`Math.max(0, Math.min(100, ...))`). Axis ticks: `[0, 50, 100]`. Both ranges are fixed constants passed into `TrendChartCard` (`ENERGY_MIN/MAX` in `AnimatedEnergyTrendChart.tsx`, `ROOT_SCORE_MIN/MAX` in `AnimatedRootScoreTrendChart.tsx`) — never derived from the data's own min/max, so a flat stretch reads as flat regardless of which window is selected.

**3. Date labels — every point at short ranges, real pixel-measured thinning at longer ones.** `components/TrendChart.tsx` positions every point by real calendar day-offset within the selected window (not index), so points plot at their true calendar position and a gap between non-consecutive check-ins is genuine empty space. `groupContiguousRuns` breaks the connecting line/area into a separate path per run wherever two neighboring real points are more than one calendar day apart — a gap never gets smoothed over as though data existed. `selectDateLabelIndices` decides which points get a *printed* date label (every point keeps its own date, reachable by tap/hover regardless): a `ResizeObserver` measures the date-label row's real rendered pixel width on mount and on any resize, and the minimum required gap between two labels is derived from that real width plus an assumed ~28px label size (`Jul 22`-shaped text, measured live) — not a per-range guess. **A real overlap bug was found and fixed during live verification, not left for later**: an initial day-count-based version of this thinning still let two labels touch/overlap at some real viewport widths — investigated with actual `getBoundingClientRect()` measurements (not just screenshots) via Playwright, which found two root causes: (a) day-based spacing didn't account for real label pixel width at all, and (b) the chart mixed left/center/right-anchored labels (to avoid the first/last label spilling off-screen), and a left-anchored point next to a center-anchored one needs *more* real spacing than two center-anchored points — the original pitch constant only accounted for the common case. Fixed both: thinning now reasons directly in the chart's own rendered x-percent space against the measured container width, and every label uses one uniform center anchor (`-translate-x-1/2`) with the anchor position itself clamped inward near the edges, rather than switching anchor style. Re-measured live after the fix: zero overlapping labels across all three ranges at both 390×844 and 375×667.

**4. Time range selector.** `components/TrendRangeSelector.tsx` — a 3-pill row (1 Week / 2 Weeks / 1 Month, defaulting to 1 Week), forest `#1B3A2D` fill + cream `#F5F0E4` text when selected, quiet/muted forest when not — rendered inside each chart's own card, above the plot. No new query or endpoint: `TrendChartCard` slices the real data array each page already fetches down to the selected trailing window (`computeRangeWindow`/`sliceToWindow`, anchored to the member's real timezone-resolved local "today", not the data's own last point, so an un-logged today shows as a real gap). Existing queries already covered the widest window (1 month): `getMyRootScoreHistory` already defaulted to 90 days on both Progress and the Root Score detail page (no change needed); Home's `getRecentCheckins(12)` was bumped to `getRecentCheckins(30)` — both functions are a `.limit(N)`-style row cap ordered by `local_date DESC`, and since `daily_checkins_current`/`root_score_snapshots` each cap at one real row per member per day, a 30/90 row limit is provably sufficient to cover a full 30-calendar-day trailing window regardless of how sparse the real check-ins are. **Empty and sparse ranges, verified live, not assumed**: temporarily cleared a real account's last 7 days of check-ins and confirmed the 1-week view renders "No energy data yet for the last 1 week." with the range pills still fully clickable, then switched to 1 Month and confirmed it correctly showed the real older data — nothing fabricated to fill the gap. `TrendChart` also renders a lone point with no line at all when a window has exactly one real value.

**5. Data point colors — the red/gold Home's Energy Trend chart specifically was still showing.** `components/EnergyTrendChart.tsx`'s `DOT_FILL` map colored dots by `energyStatus()` (`lib/wellness/status.ts`'s shared good/attention/poor classifier, thresholds `>=4` good, `===3` attention, `<=2` poor) — green `#16A34A` / amber `#F59E0B` / red `#EF4444`. This classifier is genuinely shared with the coach dashboard for badges/labels elsewhere (per that file's own doc comment) and was left completely untouched; only Home's chart stopped using it for dot color, by moving off `EnergyTrendChart` entirely onto the new shared `TrendChart`, which uses one flat forest green `#1B3A2D` for every dot on all three charts (matching the fix a prior task already applied to both Root Score charts and the Progress Trends card). No genuine concern-state signal exists in `daily_checkins`/`root_score_snapshots` that would warrant a real alert color, so none was invented — same finding every prior pass over this data reached. Gold `#C4A050` never appeared on any of these three charts' data points (confirmed by the prior task's own investigation) — `TrendRangeSelector.tsx`'s selected-state color is explicitly forest/cream per the design system, no gold anywhere in either new component.

**6. Animation — unchanged mechanism, extended to replay on range switch.** `components/ScrollDrawIn.tsx` (1.1s ease-out clip-path wipe, replays every scroll pass, skips entirely under reduced motion) is untouched in its existing scroll-triggered behavior; it gained one new optional prop, `resetKey`, a no-op for any caller that omits it. `TrendChartCard` passes `resetKey={range}`, so picking a different range pill closes and reopens the wipe immediately (via `requestAnimationFrame`, skipped under reduced motion) without needing to scroll away and back — verified live: switching ranges re-plays the draw-in on both viewport sizes, and with `reducedMotion: 'reduce'` set, zero elements ever gain an inline `clip-path` style (full chart renders immediately, matching pre-existing behavior).

**Not built / stopped to report, per the constraints**: nothing needed a new query or endpoint — every range this control offers was already covered by the existing fetch caps (verified in point 4 above), so this was never reached as an open question.

**Tests**: `tests/wellness-story-charts.test.ts` substantially rewritten — confirms `RootScoreTrendChart.tsx` no longer exists on disk and nothing imports it, both `Animated*` wrappers build the shared `TrendChartCard` with each metric's real fixed range, and real unit tests (not string scans) for `TrendChart.tsx`'s exported pure functions: `computeRangeWindow`/`sliceToWindow` (window math, never fabricates a point), `groupContiguousRuns` (gap-breaking), and `selectDateLabelIndices` (including a regression test reproducing the exact clustered-points shape that overlapped live before the fix). `tests/energy-trend-chart-scroll-replay.test.ts` updated for `ScrollDrawIn`'s new `resetKey` behavior and `AnimatedEnergyTrendChart`'s new wiring; `components/EnergyTrendChart.tsx`'s own tests (`tests/energy-trend-chart.test.ts`) are unchanged and still pass, since that file itself was never touched. `tests/progress-page-restructure.test.ts` had one stale doc-comment reference corrected (no assertion changed).

**Verified**: `npm run typecheck` — clean. `npm run lint` — 0 errors, same 30 pre-existing warnings baseline, none in a touched file. Full `vitest run` — **232 files / 2613/2613 passing** (one transient double-failure in `tests/longitudinal-intelligence-integration.test.ts` on one full-suite pass, unrelated to any file this task touched — confirmed by a clean re-run immediately after, 2613/2613, same shared-local-Supabase cross-file timing artifact this file has documented before). `npm run build` — compiled successfully.

**Verified live** (Playwright, local dev server + local Supabase, `member.one@example.test`, both 390×844 and 375×667): seeded 10 real `daily_checkins` and 10 real `root_score_snapshots` spanning a deliberately sparse/clustered pattern (a gap inside the default 1-week window, a burst of consecutive days, then a few isolated older points reachable only at 2-week/1-month) across all three charts. Confirmed: 3 range pills on every chart, correct `aria-pressed` state, real value-axis labels/gridlines at each metric's fixed range, tapping a dot reveals its exact date and value (`aria-label`, e.g. "Jul 3 · Energy 2/5"), every rendered dot computed to flat forest green `rgb(27, 58, 45)` with zero red/amber/gold anywhere, switching ranges re-slices to the real data and re-animates, reduced motion renders fully immediately. Measured real `getBoundingClientRect()` positions (not just screenshots) for every date label across all 3 ranges × 2 viewports × 3 charts after the anchoring fix — zero overlaps anywhere. Verified the empty-range and range-still-usable requirement by temporarily deleting a real account's last-week data and confirming the honest "no data yet" message plus working pills, then restored the exact same rows. All seeded/modified rows deleted after (verified zero remaining for this account in both tables).

## Distribution card added to the Trends section; Streak/Check-ins removed from the stats row below it (2026-07-28)

**Scope**: member Progress page only (`app/progress/`). No coach file touched, no schema change. The line chart (`MetricTrendChart.tsx`), the metric pills, the trend engine, the correlation engine, the three-tier language system, and `member_pattern_states` were not touched — confirmed by construction, since this task only added one new file and edited `TrendsPanel.tsx`/`ConsistencyPanel.tsx`/`page.tsx`.

**1. What was built**: `app/progress/MetricDistributionCard.tsx` — a new deep-green card rendered directly below the existing line chart inside `TrendsPanel.tsx`, answering "what does a typical day look like" instead of "how has this moved over time." One horizontal bar per real level of whichever check-in metric pill is active (Energy/Mood/Stress/Sleep Quality/Digestion/Pain — gated on `active.group === 'checkin'`, so the wearable pills never render it, since a continuous metric like Steps has no small fixed level set to distribute across), counted over her last 30 recorded check-ins for that specific field.

**2. Real scales, read from the schema, not assumed**: confirmed directly from `supabase/migrations/00000000000013_daily_checkins.sql` and `00000000000021_daily_checkins_mood_water.sql`'s own Postgres CHECK constraints — energy/mood/stress/sleep_quality/digestion are genuinely 1-5; pain genuinely differs, 0-5 (6 levels, not 5). Level labels reuse `lib/energy-forecast/scaleLabels.ts`'s existing word sets (`energyLevelLabel`/`moodLabel`/`stressLabel`/`sleepQualityLabel`/`painLabel`) — the same verbatim wording `app/checkin/CheckinForm.tsx`'s sliders and the body-severity gesture show her, so a bar labeled "Good" always means the same thing she answered. Digestion has no real word scale anywhere in the check-in UI today (`components/checkin/DriverProbeField.tsx` renders it as a bare numbered row — confirmed by reading that component directly, not assumed from its own possibly-stale doc comment) — so Digestion's bars are labeled with plain numbers, not invented wording.

**3. Design**: card ground is `#1B3A2D`, the exact same forest green `app/progress/CoachingInsightsPanel.tsx` already uses for the page's one other dark card (pulled, not reinvented — there is no separate "Root Score hero" component with its own distinct dark-green hex anywhere in the codebase; this one forest green is used universally for every deep-green surface in the app). Bar tints are a generated light-to-dark ramp (`distributionTint`) anchored well clear of the card's own background at both ends so no tint ever looks invisible or identical to the ground. Gold `#C4A050` is used exactly once — on the bar for whichever level has the real highest count (`modalLevelIndex`; ties break toward the lower level so exactly one bar is ever gold, deterministically). Heading reads "What a typical day looks like for you" — second person throughout, no "the member" anywhere.

**Contrast, measured, not eyeballed**: computed the real WCAG relative-luminance contrast ratio in a throwaway script before choosing the text treatment — full-opacity cream `#F5F0E4` on `#1B3A2D` is 10.93:1; the card's secondary text (count/percent) uses `opacity: 0.75` cream, which blends to an effective 6.92:1 against the same background — both comfortably clear the 4.5:1 floor, confirmed live afterward via `getComputedStyle` reading the actual rendered background (`rgb(27, 58, 45)`) and heading color (`rgb(245, 240, 228)`) on the deployed page, not just the source.

**4. Thin data**: below 7 real (non-null) recorded values for the specific active metric, no bars are drawn at all — a plain sentence states the real count and that the view unlocks at 7. Built and verified first, per the task's own instruction, since the account most likely to be seen in this state has very few check-ins. Critically, the 7-day floor counts non-null values **for that one field**, not `checkins.length` — a rotating-probe field like Digestion that wasn't asked on a given day doesn't count toward its own floor, even on a dense account. Verified live: seeding 2 check-ins where one had `digestion_rating = null` correctly showed "1 recorded day" for Digestion while every other metric correctly showed "2."

**5. Animation**: reuses `components/ScrollDrawIn.tsx` exactly as-is — no new `IntersectionObserver` written. The bars themselves are always rendered at their real final width; `ScrollDrawIn`'s existing clip-path wipe (which reveals left-to-right) is what produces the "staggered, longest bar arrives last" effect, since every bar shares one left edge and a longer bar's visible endpoint sits further right in the same linear wipe — this was a deliberate choice to get the required stagger behavior from the *existing* mechanism instead of writing new per-bar delay logic. `resetKey={active.key}` (the same pattern the range-selector task added to `ScrollDrawIn` for its own pills) forces the sweep to replay when she taps a different metric pill. Reduced motion: confirmed live (both locally and in production) that zero elements ever gain an inline `clip-path` style — bars render at full length immediately, card looks identical at rest.

**6. Removed the Streak/Check-ins/Avg Energy stats row** (`app/progress/ConsistencyPanel.tsx`), after checking each number's fate first, as instructed:
- **Streak**: confirmed elsewhere — `lib/ai/agents/accountability.ts`'s `currentStreakLength` is algorithmically identical to this page's former `calculateStreak` (same "consecutive local_date" logic, just formatted differently), and `lib/feed/streakIntelligence.ts`'s `buildStreakMessage` surfaces that same real number in a real sentence on the Today page (e.g. "You've checked in 3 days in a row.") under several real conditions.
- **Check-ins**: a real, query-backed number appears elsewhere too — the Today page's Accomplished zone shows `totalCheckins`, a real cumulative count. Flagged honestly rather than claimed identical: that number is all-time, not this card's own "last 30 recorded days" window — a different framing of a related fact, not the exact same figure.
- **Avg Energy**: grepped the entire app for any other average-energy display and found none — Root's energy forecast averages recent values internally (`lib/energy-forecast/`) but never surfaces "your average is X" as a stat anywhere. Per the task's own explicit instruction ("if any one of them does not appear elsewhere, leave that number out of the deletion"), **Avg Energy was kept** — now its own small single stat in `ConsistencyPanel.tsx`, not deleted.

`app/progress/streak.ts` (`calculateStreak`) is deleted outright along with its dedicated tests in `tests/progress-page-restructure.test.ts` — it had exactly one real consumer (this page) and no longer has any.

**Tests**: new `tests/metric-distribution-card.test.ts` (35 tests) — real unit coverage (not string-scan-only) for `computeDistribution` (ordered by level, honest zero-count buckets, Pain's real 6-level range), `modalLevelIndex` (tie-break rule, null-safe on an all-zero input), `distributionTint` (lightest-to-darkest, monotonic, never visually merges with the card background), `recordedValues`/`hasEnoughForDistribution` (the per-field 7-day floor), plus static checks that the real label word-sets match `CheckinForm.tsx` verbatim, that Digestion genuinely has no word scale to invent, that the card is wired below the line chart and gated to check-in segments only, that it reuses `ScrollDrawIn` rather than a new observer, and the full Streak/Check-ins/Avg Energy accounting above (including a live `grep` assertion that `averageEnergy` truly appears nowhere else). **Non-vacuousness proven directly, not just asserted**: moved the new component aside and stashed the `TrendsPanel.tsx` edit, re-ran the new test file, watched the whole suite fail (`Failed to load url ../app/progress/MetricDistributionCard` — the import target didn't exist), then restored both and watched all 29 (later 35, after the Consistency-panel tests were added) tests pass.

**Verified**: `npm run typecheck` — clean. `npm run lint` — 0 errors, same 30 pre-existing warnings baseline, none in a touched file. Full `vitest run` — **233 files / 2645/2645 passing**. `npm run build` — compiled successfully.

**Verified locally first** (Playwright, local dev server + local Supabase, `member.one@example.test`): seeded exactly 2 check-ins (one with a null `digestion_rating`) to check the thin-data state first, per the task's instruction, before seeding a further 7 to reach a real 9-day distribution across all six metrics and confirm real bars, the gold modal bar, and Pain's real 6-level scale. Confirmed reduced-motion renders instantly with zero `clip-path` elements. All seeded rows deleted after (verified zero remaining).

**Verified live in production** (Playwright, `app.mefwellness.com` itself, logged into all three real production test accounts from `docs/PRODUCTION_TEST_ACCOUNTS.md`/`scripts/screenshots/.env.local`, both 390×844 and 375×667 — 12 sessions total, zero page errors in any):
- **`memberPopulated`** (40 real days): real bars rendered for all six metrics with genuinely different real distributions and modal levels per metric (e.g. Energy modal "Moderate" at 63%, Pain modal "None" at 70%). **Independently cross-checked against the same page's own History log**, not just internal consistency: manually tallied Energy values from the 30 real History rows (`{2:1, 3:19, 4:9, 5:1}`) and confirmed an exact match against the distribution card's own numbers (Low 1/3%, Moderate 19/63%, Good 9/30%, High 1/3%) — the card's numbers are confirmed correct against her real check-in rows, not merely self-consistent.
- **`memberBelowThreshold`** (4 real days): every one of the six metrics correctly showed the thin-data sentence ("You have 4 recorded days for energy in the last 30 days. This view appears once you have 7.") — no bars, no fabricated distribution.
- **`memberEmpty`** (zero history): correctly showed "You have 0 recorded days..." for all six metrics, no crash, no fabricated zeros-as-data.
- **Bottom-nav clearance**: measured real `boundingBox()` coordinates with the card properly scrolled into view (not just a raw page-coordinate read, which the first pass got wrong and this was caught and redone) — zero overlap across all three accounts × both viewports; the card's bottom edge sat 51-175px above the nav's top edge in every case.
- **Contrast**: read the actual rendered `background-color` (`rgb(27, 58, 45)`) and heading `color` (`rgb(245, 240, 228)`) via `getComputedStyle` on the live production page and confirmed they match the values the pre-build contrast computation was based on.

**A real, pre-existing quirk found, unrelated to this task**: all three production test accounts land on `/welcome` (a multi-screen goal-selection flow) immediately after login, rather than `/dashboard` — including `memberPopulated`, which already has 40 days of real history and should have no reason to see a first-run flow. Worked around by navigating directly to `/progress` (which does not itself force the same redirect) rather than clicking through real goal-selection screens with fabricated answers on a shared test account. Not fixed — out of this task's scope — but flagged here since it's a real, observable behavior on the live site.

**Not done / left as a judgment call, stated plainly**: Check-ins' "appears elsewhere" match is not exact (all-time count vs. this card's 30-day window) — flagged above rather than silently treated as identical; kept in the deletion anyway since a real number for the same underlying fact (consistency) is genuinely visible elsewhere, just windowed differently.

**Not verified**: real mobile Safari/WebKit rendering (headless Chromium throughout, matching every prior entry in this file).

**Deployed and confirmed live**: verified the deploy target both before and after. Before: production was already serving the prior task's commit `1230d6b` (`npx vercel inspect https://app.mefwellness.com` showed status `Ready`, target `production`). Pushed to `origin/main` (`github.com/MEFwellness/mef-platform`, commit `194ec09`); the push triggered Vercel's GitHub-integration auto-deploy. After: repo `MEFwellness/mef-platform` ✓, branch `main` ✓ (main is what deploys to production), Vercel project `mef-platform` ✓, target `production` ✓ (not Preview). `npx vercel inspect --logs` on the new deployment (`dpl_XaecBrT58YnsKL2gvCaH2myqwAcW`) confirms its build log shows `Cloning github.com/MEFwellness/mef-platform (Branch: main, Commit: 194ec09)`, status `Ready`, aliased directly to `https://app.mefwellness.com` — all live production verification above (the three test-account sessions, the History cross-check, the contrast/overlap measurements) ran against this exact deployment.

## Fix: two stacked "not enough check-ins" messages in Trends collapsed into one (2026-07-28 follow-up)

**The bug**: `MetricTrendChart.tsx`'s own 5-point floor and `MetricDistributionCard.tsx`'s own separate 7-point floor each independently rendered a "not enough data yet" message. For a check-in metric with fewer than 5 real recorded values, both fired at once — the chart's gray box, immediately followed by the distribution card's dark-green box, stacked on top of each other saying the same underlying thing twice.

**The fix, scoped to `app/progress/TrendsPanel.tsx` only — neither `MetricTrendChart.tsx` nor `MetricDistributionCard.tsx`'s own math changed**: for check-in segments, `TrendsPanel` now checks `hasEnoughForDistribution(active.points)` (the 7-point floor — the stricter of the two, and the one whose wording ["appears once you have 7"] the task named explicitly) *before* deciding what to render at all:
- **Below 7 real recorded values for that field**: renders neither `MetricTrendChart` nor `MetricDistributionCard` — just one message, using the exact same `mt-4 rounded-2xl bg-[#F3F6F4] p-5 text-center` styling `MetricTrendChart`'s own gray box already used (so it visually reads as "the existing gray box," not a new element), stating the real live count and that "your trend and typical-day view appear once you have 7."
- **At 7 or more**: both render together, exactly as before this fix (7 already clears the chart's own 5-point floor too, so nothing about the chart's appearance changes at this point).
- **Wearable segments** (Readiness/Sleep/Steps/wearable Stress — no distribution card exists for these) are completely untouched, still governed only by `MetricTrendChart`'s own unmodified 5-point floor.

`MetricDistributionCard.tsx` had its own internal below-threshold branch (the dark-green "You have N recorded days..." message) deleted outright — with `TrendsPanel` now gating before ever mounting it, that branch was dead code, not a defensive fallback worth keeping. It still exports `recordedValues`/`hasEnoughForDistribution`/`MIN_DAYS_FOR_DISTRIBUTION`, which `TrendsPanel` now imports and uses directly for its own gating decision.

**Tests, written before the fix and confirmed failing first**: extended `tests/metric-distribution-card.test.ts` with 8 new tests asserting the combined-message wiring (below threshold, neither real component renders; the message reuses the chart's exact gray-box classes and states the real count plus "7"; above threshold both components render together; wearable segments untouched; `MetricTrendChart.tsx`'s own `MIN_POINTS_FOR_TREND = 5` is untouched). Ran them against the pre-fix code first and watched all 7 real assertions fail (two more pre-existing tests in the same file also broke as a direct, expected consequence of the render structure changing from a bare `&&` to a ternary, and were updated to match) — then implemented the fix and watched all 44 tests in the file pass.

**Verified**: `npm run typecheck` — clean. `npm run lint` — 0 errors, same 30 pre-existing warnings baseline. Full `vitest run` — **233 files / 2654/2654 passing** (one transient failure in `tests/longitudinal-intelligence-integration.test.ts` on one pass, unrelated to any touched file, gone on an immediate clean re-run — same shared-local-Supabase timing artifact this file has documented several times before). `npm run build` — compiled successfully.

**Verified locally first** (Playwright, local dev server + local Supabase, `member.one@example.test`): seeded exactly 2 check-ins (below both floors) and confirmed exactly one message, zero real chart, zero distribution card; then added 5 more to reach 7 and confirmed exactly one distribution-card heading and a real chart both render, with the combined message gone. All seeded rows deleted after.

**Verified live in production** (Playwright, `app.mefwellness.com` itself, all three real test accounts, both 390×844 and 375×667):
- **`memberPopulated`** (40 real days, above threshold on every metric): real chart (1 SVG) and real distribution card (1 heading) both render — unchanged from before this fix.
- **`memberBelowThreshold`** (4 real days, below threshold on every metric): exactly one message, zero chart SVGs, zero distribution-card headings — confirmed for Energy, Digestion, Pain, and Mood individually (Digestion is a rotating-probe field that could in principle have a different real count than Energy on the same account; confirmed live it still correctly reported its own real count, "4," not a copy-pasted number). Message read: *"You have 4 recorded days for energy in the last 30 days. Your trend and typical-day view appear once you have 7."*
- **`memberEmpty`** (zero history): same single-message pattern, correctly stating "0 recorded days," no crash.
- Zero page errors across all 6 sessions (3 accounts × 2 viewports).

**Not done**: nothing — the fix matched the task's own described before/above-threshold behavior exactly, with no open questions.

**Not verified**: real mobile Safari/WebKit rendering (headless Chromium throughout, matching every prior entry in this file).

**Deployed and confirmed live**: verified the deploy target both before and after. Before: production was already serving the prior task's commit `76c273d` (status `Ready`, target `production`). Pushed to `origin/main` (`github.com/MEFwellness/mef-platform`, commit `aacbc59`); the push triggered Vercel's GitHub-integration auto-deploy. After: repo `MEFwellness/mef-platform` ✓, branch `main` ✓, Vercel project `mef-platform` ✓, target `production` ✓ (not Preview). `npx vercel inspect --logs` on the new deployment (`dpl_8wM9xqMb9rAsTf3WWMbE3Rt62Lrf`) confirms its build log shows `Cloning github.com/MEFwellness/mef-platform (Branch: main, Commit: aacbc59)`, status `Ready`, aliased directly to `https://app.mefwellness.com` — all live production verification above ran against this exact deployment.

## Five-item daily-loop follow-up batch: weekly Trends chart, craving multi-select, follow-up ordering, forecast off-by-one, Root Score seed backfill (2026-07-28)

Five independent items from one brief. Items 2 and 3 were checked against this file and the live codebase first per the brief's own instruction — neither was already fixed (craving question was confirmed still `single_select` in migration 109; the two-loop follow-up ordering bug and the missing `desk_hours_today` domain hint were both confirmed still present) — so all five were built.

### 1. Trends section: weekly-average chart replaces the 30-dot daily chart

**The bug**: `MetricTrendChart.tsx` plotted one dot per day across 30 days with a gray vertical-bar layer, no Y-axis labels, and only first/last dates shown — unreadable, and members couldn't tell direction.

**The fix, scoped to check-in metrics on the Progress page only**: new `app/progress/WeeklyAverageTrendChart.tsx` buckets a metric's real values into 7-day weeks (counting back from the most recent recorded day), one point per week as that week's average. A week with fewer than 3 real check-ins gets no point — its slot still exists (so spacing stays honest) but the connecting line breaks there and the slot reads "not enough logged." Fewer than 2 qualifying weeks anywhere in the fetched range: no chart at all, a message stating what will appear and roughly when. Y axis shows the metric's own real word labels (`energyLevelLabel`/`moodLabel`/`stressLabel`/`sleepQualityLabel`/`painLabel` from `lib/energy-forecast/scaleLabels.ts` — the exact same functions `MetricDistributionCard` already uses, not a second copy), one row per real level — 5 rows for most metrics, 6 for Pain (0–5, not 1–5, per its own DB constraint). X axis labels every week with its real calendar range ("Jun 29-Jul 5"). Draw-in animation reuses `ScrollDrawIn` (the same component `MetricDistributionCard` directly below it already uses, replaying on every scroll pass and on pill switch via `resetKey`) rather than `MetricTrendChart`'s own once-per-view `useChartRevealOnce`.

**Direction sentence**: a plain-English sentence now sits above the chart, reusing `lib/intelligence/trendEngine.ts`'s `classifyMetricTrend` directly (new `app/progress/directionSentence.ts` — read-only, never calls `recalculateWellnessIntelligence`, never persists, never touches `member_pattern_states` or the trend engine's own write path). **Answering the brief's own question**: the sentence does NOT fire for every metric unconditionally — `classifyMetricTrend` returns `null` (no sentence at all) whenever either the last-30-day or previous-30-day window has fewer than 10 real check-ins for that metric (`lib/intelligence/confidence.ts`'s `MIN_SAMPLE_FOR_WINDOW`), so a newer account may show no sentence for some metrics. Once there IS enough history, `classifyMetricTrend` always returns *some* sentence (it has a template for every `trendState`, including "stable"/"inconsistent" for a weak signal) — but the specific "has been trending downward/upward" wording the brief quoted only appears for the `declining`/`improving` states, themselves gated on a real ≥10-point delta on the normalized 0-100 score; a weaker delta renders "has been inconsistent" or "has stayed steady" instead. Feeding this required one new parallel fetch in `app/progress/page.tsx` (`getRecentCheckins(60)`, wide enough for the 30-vs-previous-30 comparison) — the existing 30-day fetch that powers the chart and distribution card is untouched.

**Scope limit kept**: `MetricTrendChart.tsx` itself is completely unmodified and still renders for the four wearable segments (Readiness/Sleep/Steps/wearable Stress) in this same `TrendsPanel.tsx`, for Home's `AnimatedEnergyTrendChart` (via the shared `TrendChart`/`TrendChartCard`), and for the coach client view's own `<EnergyTrendChart showBars />`. Confirmed by grep and by static-source tests (`tests/weekly-average-trend-chart.test.ts`) that none of those three call sites reference `WeeklyAverageTrendChart`.

**Tests**: `tests/weekly-average-trend-chart.test.ts` (18 tests — bucketing math, thin-week nulling, mid-range gap slots, empty-history, scope-isolation source scans) and `tests/checkin-wizard-units.test.ts`/`directionSentence` coverage folded into the same file for the sentence reuse (null on insufficient data, exact `classifyMetricTrend` sentence text on a real decline, segment-key→area mapping, unknown segment returns null not a throw). Two pre-existing tests in `tests/metric-distribution-card.test.ts` asserted the check-in branch rendered `<MetricTrendChart` — updated to assert `<WeeklyAverageTrendChart` instead (the wearable-branch assertion for `<MetricTrendChart` elsewhere in the same file was untouched and still passes). Non-vacuousness proven by reverting the qualifying-week threshold and watching the guard test fail, then restoring.

### 2. Craving question now allows multiple selections

Added a real `multi_select` response type to the `driver_probe_questions` system rather than special-casing this one question — migration `00000000000115_craving_multi_select.sql` widens the `response_type` CHECK constraint (`scale, single_select, multi_select, time_pair, count, boolean`) and converts `checkin_probe.cravings_today` in place. `daily_checkin_probe_answers.value` was already a generic `jsonb` column (no schema change needed there, same precedent WBSA's own `multi_select` questions already established); existing scalar answers (e.g. `"salty"`) are wrapped into a single-element array (`["salty"]`) by the same migration, guarded by `jsonb_typeof` so it's safely re-runnable — no answer is lost or reinterpreted.

Widened `ProbeResponseType` (`lib/daily-checkin-adaptive/types.ts`) and `ProbeAnswerValue` (`components/checkin/DriverProbeField.tsx`, now `string | number | boolean | string[]`); added `displayStyle.ts`'s exhaustive-`Record` entry (TypeScript would have refused to compile without it). New `components/checkin/scales/MultiOptionRows.tsx` renders the multi-select using the same `TapBleedTile` selected-state every other option row already uses; selecting "None" clears every other selection and selecting anything else clears "None" (generic — keyed off any option literally valued `'none'`, not hardcoded to this one question, matching the onboarding engine's own multi-select "none" convention). Fixed the scalar-only answer-hydration filters in `CheckinForm.tsx` and `EveningReflectionForm.tsx` (both would have silently dropped an array-shaped saved answer on resume). Extended the coach Question Bank editor (`QuestionEditorForm.tsx`'s `RESPONSE_TYPES` dropdown, `QuestionRow.tsx`'s label map) to offer and display "Multiple choice (pick any)" — the existing response-type lock (`lib/driver-probe-admin/data.ts`, blocks changing shape once a question has recorded answers) is untouched and still applies to any future coach-initiated edit; this migration bypassed it deliberately as a reviewed schema change, not a UI edit.

**Tests**: `tests/craving-multi-select.test.ts` (14 tests — "None" exclusivity toggle logic including case-insensitivity, `resolveDisplayStyle`'s new default, a real-DB check that the constraint and the migrated row are actually live, and static-source scans confirming every touched surface really wired the new type through) plus a new assertion in `tests/checkin-display-style.test.ts`. Non-vacuousness proven by reverting the exclusivity logic and watching 3 tests fail, then restoring.

### 3. Follow-up questions now render directly beneath their parent question

**The bug, confirmed still present**: `CheckinForm.tsx` pushed every rotating probe in one loop, then ALL local follow-ups in a second loop afterward — so a follow-up always landed after every probe on the screen, never adjacent to the question that actually triggered it. `EveningReflectionForm.tsx` had the same shape (one concatenated array instead of two loops, same net effect). Confirmed hand-coded pain follow-ups (`pain_location`, `pain_aggravating_factor`) were NOT affected by this bug — both are excluded from the generic follow-up path entirely (`SPECIALLY_HANDLED_QUESTION_KEYS`) and `pain_aggravating_factor`'s manual push already sat directly after its parent's push in the source; if a screenshot showed otherwise it doesn't match what was in the file.

**The fix**: new `interleaveFollowUps(probes, followUps)` in `lib/daily-checkin-adaptive/wizardUnits.ts` (shared by both forms, replacing each form's own duplicated two-loop/concatenation logic) splices each probe's own eligible follow-ups in immediately after it, matched via the follow-up's `requires[0].question_key` — the same parent-key convention `screenGrouping.ts`'s `parentDomainOf()` already used for screen routing, now also used for render order. A follow-up whose parent isn't among today's probes (shouldn't happen — a follow-up is only ever eligible once its parent has been answered today) still renders, appended at the end rather than dropped.

Also fixed `screenGrouping.ts`'s `PARENT_KEY_DOMAIN_HINTS`, which had no entry for `checkin_probe.desk_hours_today` — its follow-up (`got_up_hourly`) was routing to the catch-all `'other'` screen instead of `'body'` (the same screen `desk_hours_today` itself lives on, via its `MEC` driver domain).

**Tests**: 4 new tests in `tests/checkin-wizard-units.test.ts` for `interleaveFollowUps` (single follow-up spliced after its parent, two follow-ups on one parent kept together, an orphaned follow-up appended not dropped, and the exact `desk_hours_today`/`got_up_hourly` scenario from the bug report) plus one new test in `tests/checkin-screen-grouping.test.ts` for the domain-hint fix. Non-vacuousness proven by reverting both fixes independently and watching 4 tests fail, then restoring.

### 4. Forecast-grading off-by-one fixed

**The bug, found by tracing the real code rather than assuming an arithmetic error**: `scoreForecast` (`actual - predicted`) was never wrong — verified with new exact-match/one-step/max-gap unit tests. The real defect: once a forecast is scored, its `gap` column is frozen by a DB trigger (migration 111, by design — so historical calibration accuracy never retroactively changes). But a member can revisit `/checkin/result` for the same day by using "Update check-in" to revise an already-submitted answer; `todaysEnergyLevel` is re-read fresh every visit, so the labels shown reflect her NEW answer while the frozen `gap` still reflected the OLD one — exactly the reported symptom (both examples off by the same amount a one-step edit would produce). This pattern existed independently in both `buildEndingScreenView` (her forecast) and `resolveRootStatus` (Root's) — two call sites, one root cause.

**The fix**: new shared `resolveScoredView()` in `lib/energy-forecast/service.ts` is now the only place either card builds its view — the frozen `gap` column still gets written exactly once (untouched, still what calibration history reads), but the number DISPLAYED is always recomputed fresh from the same `actualLevel` the labels use, so they can never disagree again. Zero-case copy was already correct (`describeGap`/`describeRootGap` already have dedicated "exactly right" wording for `gap === 0`) — the bug was that a stale non-zero `gap` was reaching that logic instead of a fresh zero.

**Tests**: 4 new unit tests in `tests/energy-forecast-scoring.test.ts` (exact match, one step each direction, max gap of ±4 on the five-step scale) and 2 new real-DB integration tests in `tests/energy-forecast-integration.test.ts` reproducing the exact bug scenario (score once, then call `buildEndingScreenView` again with a different actual value, confirm the second view's gap/label/sentence are all mutually consistent while the underlying frozen DB row is untouched) — for both her forecast and Root's. Non-vacuousness proven by reverting `resolveScoredView` back to the old frozen-gap logic and watching both new integration tests fail with the exact wrong numbers, then restoring.

### 5. Root Score graph on the 40-day test account — seed-script gap, confirmed and fixed

**Verdict: (a), a seed-script gap, not a rendering bug.** Root Score is precomputed and stored one row per `(member, local_date)` in `root_score_snapshots` (`lib/scoring/data.ts`) — never derived live from `daily_checkins`. It's only ever computed for "today," triggered by a real page load or check-in submission (`lib/scoring/service.ts`'s own header comment: no cron/backfill job exists). `scripts/seed-production-test-accounts.mjs` wrote `daily_checkins`/`energy_forecasts` rows directly via service-role insert — never through the app's own submit-check-in path — so it never triggered a snapshot for any of the 40 backdated days. The graph (`ProgressRootScorePanel.tsx` → `getMyRootScoreHistory` → `listSnapshotHistory`) was reading exactly what existed and rendering it correctly; the data itself just wasn't there beyond whatever 1-2 days someone happened to load `/dashboard` on since the account was seeded.

**The fix**: new `scripts/backfill-root-score-history.ts`, run via `npx tsx` (added as a devDependency — `lib/scoring/*` is real application TypeScript with `@/` path imports that the seed script's plain-`.mjs`, no-build-step discipline can't import directly). It calls the exact same `calculateAndPersistRootScore` the app itself uses, once per day oldest-to-newest over the seeded date range, so momentum/resilience build up the same way genuine daily use would — no separate, duplicated scoring math. `seed-production-test-accounts.mjs` now shells out to it automatically after seeding each member's check-in history, so one `node scripts/seed-production-test-accounts.mjs` run produces complete history end to end, matching `daily_checkins`' own idempotent delete-then-reinsert pattern (clears any snapshots in/beyond the seeded range first).

**Confirmed working**: tested locally first (10 days of real check-ins against local Supabase, backfill script produced 10 real snapshots, cleaned up after). Then run against **production** itself (real service-role key, via `node scripts/seed-production-test-accounts.mjs`): `memberPopulated` now has **40 real Root Score snapshots** (`2026-06-19 .. 2026-07-28`), `memberBelowThreshold` has 4. Verified live on `app.mefwellness.com`'s Progress page (Playwright, logged in as `memberPopulated`): the Root Score chart's "1 Month" view now draws a full 40-day bar chart (`Jun 29` through `Jul 28` labeled on the X axis, real day-to-day variation in bar height) — previously this showed only 2 days.

Full `vitest run` — **235 files / 2698/2698 passing**. `npm run build` — compiled successfully, all 67 routes generated. Every new guard test proven non-vacuous per-item above (revert the fix, watch the specific new tests fail, restore, watch them pass again) — done four separate times across the five items, not once collectively.

**Verified live on `app.mefwellness.com`** (Playwright, real production test accounts, 390×844 viewport): logged into `memberPopulated`, `memberBelowThreshold`, and `memberEmpty` and captured the Trends section for Energy, Mood, and Pain on each (9 screenshots total, zero console/page errors across all three sessions).

**Not independently verified live**: the multi-select craving question's on-screen rendering itself (`cravings_today` is a rotating probe — which questions appear on a given day is picker-selected, not something to force without mutating a shared test account's real state) and the follow-up-ordering/forecast-off-by-one fixes' visual appearance on a live check-in (same reason — exercising `/checkin` and `/checkin/result` against these shared accounts would mean submitting real answers I'd then need service-role access to clean up).
