## Trends direction sentence: match the chart's own window, drop misleading up/down wording (2026-07-28 follow-up)

Three related items about the sentence sitting above the just-shipped weekly-average chart, which could contradict the chart it was describing.

### 1. The sentence now describes the same window the chart draws

**The bug, reproduced exactly as reported**: on the Pain tab, the sentence read "Pain has been trending upward over the last month compared to the month before" (worsening) while the four weeks actually plotted ran Mild-moderate → Mild → Mild-moderate → None (improving, ending at zero). Neither number was wrong — the sentence came from `lib/intelligence/trendEngine.ts`'s `classifyMetricTrend`, a month-over-month comparison (last 30 days vs. the previous 30) whose "previous month" isn't drawn on the chart at all. Two correct numbers, two different windows, one screen.

**The fix**: new `chartWindowDirectionSentence` (`app/progress/directionSentence.ts`, replacing that file's old trend-engine-based logic entirely) computes strictly from `buildWeeklyBuckets(active.points)` — the exact same pure function and the exact same `points` prop `WeeklyAverageTrendChart.tsx` itself calls to draw the line. It compares the first and last ACTUALLY PLOTTED week (a week skipped for thin data has no point on the chart and is never treated as an endpoint either — confirmed live: the Pain chart's oldest bucket, "Jun 24-Jun 30," is a gap labeled "not enough logged," and the sentence correctly starts from "Jul 1-Jul 7" instead). Fewer than 2 plotted weeks: no sentence at all. The window is named using the chart's own week-range labels verbatim ("from Jul 1-Jul 7 to Jul 22-Jul 28").

**Steady threshold — chosen and stated**: a change smaller than **0.5** (half a scale step) between the first and last plotted week's average reads as "held steady" rather than forcing a direction. Each labeled level on these scales (Pain's None/Mild/Mild-moderate/Moderate/Significant/Severe, and the equivalent 5-level scales elsewhere) spans exactly 1 point — a half-point move is the natural midpoint, since anything smaller sits closer to "the same level" than to "the next one." Forcing a direction below that would describe a difference the member's own week-to-week labels wouldn't even show as different. Confirmed live: Energy, Mood, and Digestion all read "has held steady" for the populated account's real current data (small real deltas under 0.5), while Stress and Pain both cleared the threshold and got real direction wording.

**`checkinsForTrendSentence`/`asOfLocalDate` removed**: the old month-over-month sentence needed a second, wider (~60-day) check-in fetch purely to feed `classifyMetricTrend`. That fetch, and the props carrying it from `page.tsx` into `TrendsPanel.tsx`, are gone — the new sentence only ever needs the same `points` the chart already has.

### 2. Redundancy — checked, not assumed

Confirmed live (Pain, `memberPopulated`): "Your Wellness Patterns" above now reads *"Pain has been trending upward over the last month compared to the month before"* while the Trends section directly below reads *"Pain has been easing from Jul 1-Jul 7 to Jul 22-Jul 28."* These are no longer verbatim duplicates (the pre-fix bug the task described) — different wording, a different named window, and in this exact case even different stated directions, since one honestly describes last-month-vs-previous-month and the other honestly describes the four weeks drawn. **Verdict: not redundant** — they read as two distinct, specific data points, not a repeated line. Worth naming plainly rather than glossing over: a member reading both back to back could notice they point different directions for the same metric. That's an inherent consequence of one sentence covering a longer window than the other, not a new bug this task introduced or a reason to think either is wrong — but it's a real UX tension, not a solved one, and is called out here rather than silently accepted. The insight-card list's scope (it also covers Hydration and check-in consistency, which aren't in the TRENDS pill row) is untouched, per the task's own instruction not to narrow it.

### 3. Wording no longer says "upward"/"downward" for the Trends sentence — and the insight cards have the identical problem, sourced in the trend engine, not fixed

**Polarity determined from the real stored scales, not assumed**: added `TRENDS_METRIC_POLARITY` to `lib/wellness/status.ts`, derived from that file's own existing classifier functions (`energyStatus`/`moodStatus`/`stressStatus`/`sleepQualityStatus`/`digestionStatus`/`painStatus` — the same functions the dashboard's own status-color dots already use) rather than a second, hand-typed table: `polarityFromStatus(metricStatus(5))` checks what a near-top-of-scale value reports and reads the polarity off that, so it can never silently drift from the real thresholds if they change.
- **higher_is_better**: Energy, Mood, Sleep Quality, **Digestion** (confirmed, not assumed — `digestionStatus` is the exact same `directFivePointStatus` function `moodStatus`/`energyStatus`/`sleepQualityStatus` use; a higher `digestion_rating` is genuinely better digestion, not more symptoms).
- **higher_is_worse**: Stress, Pain (both already documented as inverse in `status.ts`'s own header comment — this task is the first time that fact became a reusable value instead of only prose).

**Wording**: per-metric phrase pairs in `directionSentence.ts` (`increasing`/`decreasing` for Energy; `improving`/`dipping` for Mood; `easing`/`increasing` for Stress; `improving`/`declining` for Sleep Quality and Digestion; `easing`/`increasing` for Pain) selected by whether the raw movement is an improvement for *that metric's own polarity* — never the words "upward"/"downward". Confirmed live: "Stress has been increasing" (correctly reads as bad news) and "Pain has been easing" (correctly reads as good news), neither using direction-as-if-on-a-graph language.

**The insight cards have the identical problem — not fixed, reported instead**: confirmed live, the exact same account's "Your Wellness Patterns" card reads *"Pain has been trending upward over the last month compared to the month before"* — literally the word "upward" for a metric where that reads as good news but means more pain. This comes from `lib/intelligence/trendEngine.ts`'s own `trendDirectionWord()` function and `memberSummary` sentence templates — the trend engine itself already correctly computes which way the RAW value moved (accounting for the stress/pain inversion), it just then renders that as "upward"/"downward" regardless. Fixing this would mean editing `trendEngine.ts`, which this task explicitly marks **DO NOT TOUCH**, with its own instruction to stop and explain rather than proceed if a fix appears to require it. Stopping here, per that instruction — this is a real, confirmed bug, left to the site owner's decision rather than fixed unilaterally.

---

**Verified**: `npm run typecheck` — clean. `npm run lint` (from repo root) — 0 errors, same 31-warning baseline. Full `vitest run` — **236 files / 2706/2706 passing** (24 tests in `tests/weekly-average-trend-chart.test.ts` covering the exact reported Pain contradiction, <2 plotted weeks, a thin-data week skipped at each end of the window, the steady threshold at/under/over 0.5, correct wording for a higher-is-better metric and a higher-is-worse metric, and window naming; 2 new tests in `tests/wellness-status-polarity.test.ts` locking in `TRENDS_METRIC_POLARITY`). `npm run build` — compiled successfully. Every new guard proven non-vacuous — reverted the polarity check, the steady-threshold comparison, and the polarity table itself independently, watched the specific tests fail each time (1, then 3, then 1), then restored and confirmed green again.

**Verified live on `app.mefwellness.com`** (Playwright, `memberPopulated`, real production data, 390×844 viewport) — captured all six Trends metrics' sentences directly from the live DOM:
- Energy: *"has held steady"* · Mood: *"has held steady"* · Digestion: *"has held steady"*
- Stress: *"Stress has been increasing from Jul 1-Jul 7 to Jul 22-Jul 28."* — chart shows a real late rise toward "High"; correct.
- Sleep Quality: *"Sleep Quality has been declining from Jul 1-Jul 7 to Jul 22-Jul 28."*
- Pain: *"Pain has been easing from Jul 1-Jul 7 to Jul 22-Jul 28."* — matches the chart's real Mild-moderate→...→None line exactly, resolving the reported contradiction. The chart's own "Jun 24-Jun 30" gap week (labeled "not enough logged") is correctly excluded as an endpoint.

Zero page errors. Also confirmed on `memberBelowThreshold` (4 real days): the pre-existing "you have 4 recorded days... appears once you have 7" combined message still renders — no sentence, no chart, unchanged and not newly broken.

**Not verified**: real mobile Safari/WebKit rendering (headless Chromium throughout, matching every prior entry in this file). Whether any account exists with ≥7 total recorded days but genuinely fewer than 2 qualifying weeks (the specific edge case that would show the chart's own "not enough weeks yet" message with no sentence, as opposed to the coarser "under 7 total" combined message) — none of the three production test accounts happened to be in that exact state at verification time; the case is covered by the automated test suite (`hasEnoughForWeeklyChart`/`chartWindowDirectionSentence` both tested directly for it) but not observed live.

Pushed to `origin/main` (`github.com/MEFwellness/mef-platform`, commit `a8742ee`); the push triggered Vercel's GitHub-integration auto-deploy. After: repo `MEFwellness/mef-platform` ✓, branch `main` ✓, Vercel project `mef-platform` ✓, target `production` ✓ (not Preview). `npx vercel inspect --logs` on the new deployment (`dpl_C2kAaja42XAReaHmXkYmAuuuuYeS`) confirms its build log shows `Cloning github.com/MEFwellness/mef-platform (Branch: main, Commit: a8742ee)`, status `Ready`, aliased directly to `https://app.mefwellness.com` — all live verification above ran against this exact deployment. No database migration was needed for this task (no schema touched).

## Member Root Map redesign — ring map, real state groups, coverage counts, named recommendation (2026-07-28)

Full redesign of the member-facing `/root-map` page. Twelve identical cards (same "Discovery" tag, same "Building confidence"/"Quiet" chips, same boilerplate, duplicated status text) replaced with a page driven entirely by real per-domain state. Purely a presentation change: `lib/root-map/builder.ts`'s existing computation, the three-tier/correlation/trend engines, `member_pattern_states`, the check-in flow, the case view, and the coach platform (`RootMapPanel.tsx`, `RootMapDomainCard.tsx`) are all untouched — the coach view still renders the exact same component it always has, with the exact same coach-facing `definition` text.

**Part 1 — descriptions rewritten, second person.** New `lib/root-map/memberCopy.ts` (`MEMBER_DOMAIN_DESCRIPTIONS`) holds the 12 exact strings specified, keyed by `CoachingDomain`, wired into `RootMapDomainView.memberDescription` (new field, populated in `builder.ts`) alongside — not replacing — the existing coach-facing `definition` field. The coach view keeps reading `definition` unchanged; only the new member-facing components read `memberDescription`.

**Part 2 — duplication killed.** The old card always rendered a "What We're Still Learning" paragraph plus a second recommendation box that, in the common no-findings-yet state, said the same thing in different words (confirmed: builder.ts's `GATHERING_INFO_MESSAGE`/`'Still gathering information'` pair, and — for a safety-suppressed domain — the literal identical `SAFETY_SUPPRESSED_MESSAGE` string in both places). New `lib/root-map/cardViewModel.ts`'s `buildFindingCardViewModel` drops the second box entirely whenever it would repeat the state message verbatim, and the redesigned "Building" rows (Part 4) never render a state paragraph at all — that duplication only ever existed in exactly that state, so removing the state paragraph from that state removes it rather than reformatting it.

**Part 3 — chips replaced with real coverage, hardcoded-vs-derived determined first.** Confirmed: "Building confidence"/"Quiet" were not hardcoded strings — they're real computed values (`computeDomainConfidence`/`computeCoachingDomainPriority`, both reading `registry_entries`) run through a fixed label map. The problem was that they're *evidence-strength* signals, not day-count signals, so on an early account almost every domain reads identically regardless of real day-to-day activity. Replaced with real "N of 21 days logged" counts (`lib/root-map/coverage.ts`), same phrasing `CaseEmptyState.tsx` already uses for correlation coverage. Counts come from `daily_checkins_current` over a trailing 21-day window, one column per domain (`sleep_quality`, `movement_today`, `energy_level`, `pain_discomfort_level`, `digestion_rating`, `stress_level`, `mood_level` — 7 of the 12 Coaching Domains). Nutrition (no check-in column of its own) and the four uninstrumented domains have no trackable per-day source at all: `computeDomainCoverage` returns `null` for those — never a fabricated zero — confirmed live (Nutrition & Metabolic Health shows no badge on all three test accounts while Digestion right next to it shows a real "0/4/21 of 21 days logged").

**Part 4 — three real-state groups.** New `lib/root-map/grouping.ts`: "What We're Seeing" (`whatWeUnderstand.length > 0` — a real earned finding from the Universal Registry) gets a full `RootMapFindingCard`; "Building" (instrumented, no finding yet) gets a compact `RootMapBuildingRow` (name, one-line description, coverage — no full card, no state paragraph); "Not Covered Yet" (the four `isUninstrumented` domains) collapses into one small `RootMapNotCoveredSection` with a single shared explanation instead of four full cards. **Flagged, not silently assumed**: the task's "existing three-tier engine" (tiered correlation findings, `member_pattern_states`) is real but was never actually wired into the Root Map — `gatherRootMapInputs()` only ever reads `registry_entries` + `PatternInsight`, confirmed by re-reading `app/actions/rootMap.ts`. Wiring the correlation engine in now would be new plumbing into an explicitly off-limits system, not a presentation change, so "Seeing" uses the Root Map's own existing "real earned finding" concept (Universal Registry findings) instead. All three group memberships are computed from real fields every render (`isUninstrumented`, `whatWeUnderstand`) — a domain moves group automatically the moment it earns data; guard-tested directly (`tests/root-map-grouping.test.ts`'s "moves a domain from building to seeing" case).

**Part 5 — the actual map.** New `components/root-map/RootMapRing.tsx`: a twelve-segment SVG ring in canonical `COACHING_DOMAINS` order (not the priority-sorted order the cards below use, so the map itself stays a stable layout). Reveal is the existing `useChartRevealOnce()` scroll hook (unchanged, same one Home/Progress trend charts use) driving a `stroke-dashoffset` transition — the same technique `FourDoctorsWheel.tsx` already uses for its own ring, generalized so each segment's arc length carries real meaning (fill fraction = real coverage / 21, or fully filled when a domain has an earned finding) instead of all segments being equal. Deep green (`#1B3A2D`) for a filled segment, gold (`#F5B700`, the codebase's existing locked gold accent) reserved only for a domain with a real earned finding. Each segment renders twice — an always-full-size transparent hit target under the real, proportional visual arc — so a near-empty segment is still easy to tap; tapping scrolls to that domain's card/row via a shared `id` (`lib/root-map/anchors.ts`).

**Bug found and fixed during local verification, before touching production**: `domainAnchorId` was originally exported from `RootMapRing.tsx` itself (a `'use client'` file) and imported into the three server-rendered card components. That throws at server-render time (`... is not a function`) — a plain function re-exported alongside a client component isn't a real, directly-callable function from server code; Next.js turns it into a client reference instead. Fixed by extracting it into its own small non-`'use client'` module (`lib/root-map/anchors.ts`). Caught by driving the page with Playwright against local Supabase before pushing, not by typecheck/build (both were clean the whole time — this is a runtime-only RSC failure mode).

**Second bug found the same way**: passing the full `RootMapDomainView[]` (including the coach-only `definition` field) as a prop into the ring, a client component, serialized `definition`'s third-person "the member..." text into the page's own RSC flight payload sent to the member's browser — never visibly rendered, but present in page source, which is exactly the kind of internal-schema-leaking-onto-a-member-screen this task was about. Fixed by giving `RootMapRing` its own narrow `RingDomain` prop type (`domain`, `label`, `whatWeUnderstand` only) built in `page.tsx`, instead of passing the full view. Reverified with a raw-HTML text search after the fix — zero occurrences of "the member" anywhere in the page's output for all three real test accounts.

**Part 6 — named area, or nothing.** New `lib/root-map/topArea.ts`. `domains` is already sorted by `builder.ts` (priority desc, confidence asc), so `domains[0]` is the same domain the rest of the page already treats as most-worth-attention (`RootMapDomainCard`'s own "Worth focused attention soon" copy fires from this exact priority value). `resolveNamedAreaRecommendation` names it directly in the sentence ("`{Domain}` looks like a specific area worth exploring further") only when the outcome is `focused_investigation` AND that top domain is a genuine standout (`priority !== 'quiet'`); otherwise the recommendation — sentence and CTA both — is hidden entirely, replaced by an honest "Nothing specific has stood out yet" line, rather than showing the old unnamed claim. Presentation-layer only — `routerOutcome.ts`/`rootRouter.ts` untouched; every other outcome's existing message and CTA render exactly as before.

**Tests**: 5 new files, 26 tests (`tests/root-map-member-copy.test.ts`, `tests/root-map-grouping.test.ts`, `tests/root-map-coverage.test.ts`, `tests/root-map-top-area.test.ts`, `tests/root-map-card-view-model.test.ts`), all pure unit tests against the new `lib/root-map/*` modules (no Supabase, matching this Root Map area's existing convention). Every guard proven non-vacuous by breaking the thing it guards and watching the specific test fail, then reverting: reintroduced "the member" into a description (2 tests failed), hardcoded grouping to ignore real findings (2 tests failed), let `computeDomainCoverage` return a fabricated zero for an untrackable domain (1 test failed), let `nextStep` render even when it would duplicate `stateMessage` (1 test failed), let `resolveNamedAreaRecommendation` name a domain even when nothing is a real standout (1 test failed) — all reverted and reconfirmed green afterward.

**Verified**: `npm run typecheck` — clean. `npm run lint` (from repo root) — 0 errors, same 31-warning baseline. Full `vitest run` — **241 files / 2726/2726 passing** (one `tests/feed-integration.test.ts` failure seen on one full-suite run reproduced as passing both in isolation and on an immediate full-suite rerun — pre-existing shared-fixture flakiness per this file's own `vitest.config.ts` comment, not caused by the new tests, which are pure unit tests with no Supabase writes). `npm run build` — compiled successfully, `/root-map` included among all routes. ## Two live navigation bugs: "What We're Noticing" sheet and Root Map ring, both found on a real iPhone (2026-07-29)

Two reported bugs, investigated on a running local dev server + local Supabase (Playwright, `member.one@example.test`, seeded directly with test registry findings/check-ins for reproduction, all deleted afterward) before any code changed, per the task's own instruction not to guess.

### Item 1 — "What We're Noticing" bottom sheet

**(a) "Gets stuck" — root cause found, not assumed.** Confirmed live: `components/dashboard/WhatWereNoticingCard.tsx`'s tile lives inside the dashboard's "What Root Is Noticing" `RevealOnScroll.tsx` wrapper, whose entrance animation sets `transform: translateY(...)` on that wrapper — even at rest (`translate-y-0`), a non-`none` `transform` establishes a new CSS stacking context per spec. `NoticingSheet.tsx`'s own doc comment claimed it was "never nested inside anything with transform... that exact combination previously clipped/misordered the profile menu" — but it WAS nested inside exactly that, via its trigger living inside `RevealOnScroll`. The sheet's `z-50` was therefore only ever being compared against elements inside that wrapper, not page-wide. `FloatingCoachLauncher`'s "Ask Root" button (`z-40`, never inside a transformed ancestor) painted on top of the sheet's real content instead of under it. Proved with a live `elementFromPoint` probe at the button's exact screen coordinates while the sheet was open: it resolved to the button's own `<svg>` icon, not the sheet. Then proved the practical consequence directly: tapping that exact spot (visually showing "Recommended For You") opened the entirely separate "Ask Root" chat panel on top of the still-open Noticing sheet — screenshotted both states. That is the "stuck" a member on a real phone would feel — a tap that silently does something else.

**Fix**: `NoticingSheet.tsx` now renders its dialog via `createPortal(..., document.body)` instead of as a plain DOM sibling of its trigger. A portal can never be trapped by a future transformed ancestor the way a sibling can — this is the "fix it once, in the shared component" version, since the sheet is still used by `CoachingMessageCard` ("From Root"). Re-verified live after the fix: the sheet's dialog is now a direct child of `<body>`; `elementFromPoint` at the button's coordinates now resolves to the sheet's own content; tapping there no longer opens the Ask Root panel, and the button is now fully hidden behind the sheet (correct — it was never supposed to be visible on top).

**(b) Should be a page, not a sheet.** New `app/noticing/page.tsx`, same convention as `/case`/`/root-map` (back link, header, `BottomNav`, `FloatingCoachLauncher`). `components/dashboard/WhatWereNoticingCard.tsx` now passes `href="/noticing"` to `NoticingTile` instead of `sheetTitle`/`children` — same `href` pattern `RootMapCard.tsx` already used. Section copy and order kept exactly as they were (noticing → improving → worth attention → recommended → educational note), confirmed via a guard test that checks the actual ordering by string position.

**Italic closing line cut off under the floating chat button.** Root cause: the sheet's own bottom padding was a flat `pb-5` (1.25rem) — it never accounted for `FloatingCoachLauncher`'s button, which sits at `bottom: calc(5rem + safe-inset)` with a further `3.5rem` height, i.e. its top edge is ~8.5rem above the viewport bottom on the right side. Fixed the same way the bottom-nav overlap was originally fixed: a new shared token in `globals.css`, `.pb-safe-chat` (`padding-bottom: calc(9rem + env(safe-area-inset-bottom))`), defined once, applied to `NoticingSheet.tsx`'s scrollable content wrapper and to the new `/noticing` page's `<main>` (replacing `.pb-safe-nav` there, since `.pb-safe-chat`'s value is a strict superset). Not applied to any of the ~10 other pages that already use `.pb-safe-nav` + the launcher — no bug was reported there, and retrofitting them wasn't in scope.

**"Suggested Next Steps" — never computed, not dropped.** Traced `nextSteps` (`lib/intelligence-engine/memberFacingNoticing.ts`) back to its source: `suggestAssessmentsFromFindings` (`lib/assessment-registry/findingRecommendations.ts`) returns `{ assessmentKey, reason, supportingFindingCodes }` — `reason` is only ever the WHY ("Based on stress and lifestyle balance noticed recently."). No title, action, or link field exists anywhere in that type or its computation; an existing test (`tests/intelligence-engine-member-facing-noticing.test.ts`, now removed) had literally asserted `reason` gets carried through as if it were a step, pinning the bug down rather than catching it. Per the task's own instruction for the genuinely-never-computed case, removed the field and the heading entirely rather than inventing a step — `assessmentKey` is deliberately never member-facing (this member's own standing rule against surfacing internal questionnaire naming), so synthesizing a title from it would have meant building a second, parallel "next step" system next to the one that already exists and already works (`recommendedInvestigation`, sourced from the Root Router, with a real display name and route — still shown, unchanged). `suggestAssessmentsFromFindings`/`FindingBasedSuggestion` themselves are untouched and still used by `rootRouter.ts`, `rootCauseSignals.ts`, and the WBSA results page.

### Item 2 — Root Map ring segments

**(a) Nothing said which segment was which.** Investigated what the ring actually encodes before designing anything (per the task's own instruction): a segment's color is binary (gold = this domain has a real earned finding; deep green otherwise) and its fill fraction is either `1` (earned finding) or `coverage.count / coverage.windowDays` (days logged / 21). The "dots" the task described are the same encoding at a very small fraction — `strokeLinecap="round"` on a near-zero-length arc renders as a small rounded blob, not a second kind of marker. Added a small upright number (1–12) just outside each segment (rendered in the ring's own un-rotated coordinate frame, not counter-rotated CSS trickery) plus an ordered, tappable legend directly below the ring listing all twelve domain names with a matching numbered/colored swatch — the shared key between "which segment is this" and "what does gold vs. green mean."

**(b) Tap landed at the bottom of the page — root cause found, and it was NOT a missing/mismatched anchor id.** Checked first, per the task's own instruction: all twelve `id="root-map-domain-*"` values (`lib/root-map/anchors.ts`) are present exactly once each, correctly matched between `RootMapRing.tsx` and the three card components. A synthetic mouse click aimed at each segment's own bounding-box center actually failed with Playwright reporting the *parent `<svg>`* as intercepting the click — because each segment's only hit target was a `fill="none"` circle with `stroke="transparent"`: hit-testing for a `fill="none"` shape only counts the thin stroke band itself (~14.6 of 236 SVG units, roughly a 19px band on a real phone), not the empty disc inside or outside it. A live angular scan (sampling `elementFromPoint` around the ring) confirmed the band really is that thin. A real fingertip missing that band — especially plausible right at a segment's own 16%-wide gap, or simply a few pixels off — hits nothing at all, and the tap falls through to the page's native touch-scroll; on a ~2750px-tall page, an incidental drag during a near-miss tap can carry the viewport a long way, reading exactly as "jumps to the bottom."

**Fix**: replaced the thin-stroke hit target with a real filled pie-wedge `<path>` per segment (`wedgePath()`, new pure function), spanning the segment's entire 30° slot from the ring's own center out past its outer edge — the whole wedge is tappable, not a thin band. Building this exposed and fixed a second, self-inflicted bug caught during live verification, before shipping: the wedge path's coordinates (`pointOnCircle()`) already compute final, correctly-oriented positions directly (12 o'clock, clockwise) — nesting the wedge inside the same `<g transform="rotate(-90 ...)">` the visual arc still legitimately needs (for the classic `stroke-dashoffset` technique) double-rotated it, so every tap resolved to the segment sitting exactly 3 slots (90°) away from where it visually appeared. Fixed by moving the wedge `<path>` elements outside that rotated `<g>`, as siblings — same un-rotated frame the number labels and legend already correctly use. Verified live afterward: tapping 15%, 30%, and 44% of the way out from the ring's own center (well inside where the old thin stroke would have missed entirely) all correctly resolved to and scrolled to the right domain's section.

**Guard tests** (`tests/root-map-ring.test.ts`, `tests/intelligence-engine-member-facing-noticing.test.ts`, `tests/noticing-navigation.test.ts` — 31 tests total, all new or rewritten): canonical-angle assertions on `pointOnCircle` (12/3/6/9 o'clock), an exact-string assertion on `wedgePath` proving it originates at the ring's center (not its edge), source-shape scans confirming the wedge/labels/legend/rotation-via-`<g>` are actually present, a `buildMemberFacingNoticing` assertion that the return value has no `nextSteps` property at all, and source scans confirming the sheet uses `createPortal`/`pb-safe-chat`, the card navigates via `href`, and no surface renders "Suggested Next Steps." **Every one proven non-vacuous the way the task asked**: `git stash` each fix's file(s) back to the pre-fix version, re-run the specific guard tests, watch them fail (2/8, 11/17, and 14/14 failed across the three files respectively), `git stash pop` to restore, watch them pass again. Done for all four fixes independently.

**Verified**: `npm run typecheck` — clean. `npm run lint` — 0 errors, same 31-warning baseline. Full `vitest run` — **243 files / 2750/2750 passing** (one `tests/feed-integration.test.ts` + `tests/longitudinal-intelligence-integration.test.ts` failure pair seen on one full-suite run, reproduced as passing both in isolation and on an immediate full-suite re-run — the same pre-existing shared-fixture flakiness this file has documented before, not caused by any touched file). `npm run build` — compiled successfully, `/noticing` included among all routes.

**Verified locally against a running dev server + local Supabase** (Playwright, `member.one@example.test`, seeded with two registry findings and one check-in to reproduce both bugs, all deleted after): confirmed the sheet-stacking bug and its fix (before/after `elementFromPoint` + screenshots), confirmed the `/noticing` page renders the same sections in the same order with no "Suggested Next Steps" heading and no cut-off italic line, confirmed the "From Root" sheet (still using the shared `NoticingSheet`) is portaled under `<body>` and no longer lets the chat button intercept taps, confirmed the ring's numbered labels/legend render in the correct canonical order, and confirmed tap-to-scroll now works reliably well inside the ring's old miss zone.

**Not done**: nothing the task asked for — both items' (a) and (b) sub-bugs were investigated to a real root cause and fixed.

**Not verified**: real mobile Safari/WebKit rendering (headless Chromium throughout, matching every prior entry in this file).

**Deployed and confirmed live**: verified the deploy target both before and after. Before: production was serving the prior commit `5bebd22` (status `Ready`, target `production`). Pushed to `origin/main` (`github.com/MEFwellness/mef-platform`, commit `e03d1e1`); the push triggered Vercel's GitHub-integration auto-deploy. After: repo `MEFwellness/mef-platform` ✓, branch `main` ✓, Vercel project `mef-platform` ✓, target `production` ✓ (not Preview). `npx vercel inspect app.mefwellness.com` confirms the alias points at deployment `dpl_G6i5bDzqiN5u6peGqthNztxCaMrK`, whose build log (`npx vercel inspect --logs`) shows `Cloning github.com/MEFwellness/mef-platform (Branch: main, Commit: e03d1e1)`, status `Ready` — all live verification below ran against this exact deployment. No database migration needed — no schema touched.

**Verified live on `app.mefwellness.com`** (Playwright, real `memberPopulated` production account, `390×844`): `/root-map` shows the numbered 1-12 labels and a 12-row legend in the correct canonical order; tapping at 25% of the ring's own radius (deep inside where the old thin-stroke hit target would have missed entirely) correctly resolved to and scrolled "Movement & Physical Capacity" into the top of the viewport, not the bottom of the page. The Noticing tile is a real `<a href="/noticing">`; navigating there rendered the member's real findings, "Areas Worth Paying Attention To," "Recommended For You" (a real `Nutrition & Lifestyle Questionnaire` link), and the italic educational note with clean spacing — no "Suggested Next Steps" text anywhere, no cut-off line. Separately opened "From Root" (still using the shared `NoticingSheet`) and confirmed live: its dialog is a direct child of `<body>` (portal confirmed), and the exact screen coordinates where the floating "Ask Root" button sits now resolve to the "From Root" dialog itself, not the button — screenshotted, the button is fully hidden behind the sheet as intended. Zero page errors across all of it.

## Five more live bugs on the same carousel/ring, found on a real iPhone (2026-07-29 follow-up)

Five items from one brief, all investigated on a running local dev server + local Supabase (Playwright — both headless Chromium and, for the ring numbering bug specifically, Playwright's `webkit` engine, the actual Safari rendering engine, not just Chromium) before any code changed.

### Item 1 — "Four Doctors Assessment" card title

**Confirmed hardcoded, not a dynamic-selection bug.** `components/dashboard/WhatWereNoticingCard.tsx`'s `headline` prop was a flat string literal. The card's real content (`getMyNoticingView()`) pulls from ANY active, member-visible registry finding regardless of source — onboarding baseline, body assessment, questionnaire category, food lens pattern, wearable, primal pattern, or unified assessment (`registry_entries_source_feature_check` lists all seven) — never specifically the Four Doctors assessment. There was no source-selection logic to fix, just one wrong name picked out of many possible real ones. Replaced with `"A few things stand out"` — a fixed, honest, source-agnostic phrase matching the sibling cards' own headline voice (`RootMapCard`'s "An area worth exploring," `CoachingMessageCard`'s "More context could help" — short, generic, never naming an internal system).

### Item 2 — Carousel had no position/continuation signal

The four-card row already had a real peek at rest (measured live: card 2 sits ~72% visible in the 350px content column before any change) — the actual gap was purely the missing "there's more / here's where you are" affordance. New `components/carousel/ScrollCarousel.tsx`, a genuinely shared component (not forked for this one section — no prior shared carousel existed to reuse, confirmed by grep: the scroll-snap markup only ever appeared in this one place), wraps the existing four `<Suspense>` cards and renders a row of position dots below, updating live via a scroll listener. Dot **count** comes from a `MutationObserver` on the real DOM children, not React's static `children` count — a card can resolve to `null` after its own `Suspense` settles (this section's own existing "no gap, no placeholder" convention), which a static count would get wrong. Verified live: 4 real cards → 4 dots, active dot correctly moves from 0 to 3 when scrolled to the last card.

### Item 3 — Back from any card dumped her at the top of Home

**Root cause, not scroll restoration being "disabled":** the "Back to Dashboard" link on `/noticing`, `/root-map`, `/recommendations`, `/case`, and `/root-score` was a plain `<Link href="/dashboard">` — a **forward push navigation to a fixed URL**, not the browser's actual back navigation. Next.js scrolls a pushed page to the top by default (correct behavior for "a new page"), which is exactly why a control labeled "back" behaved like one. Confirmed the browser's own real back navigation (`page.goBack()`) already preserved scroll position correctly — the bug was entirely in which navigation mechanism the link used, not in scroll restoration itself. **The fix already existed**: `components/BackButton.tsx` (`router.back()`, falling back to a fixed href only when `window.history.length <= 1`) was already built and already used on 28 other pages — these five had just never been converted. Swapped all five to use it (reuse, not a new build). Verified live: scrollY before navigating away and after clicking the real BackButton were identical (2650 → 2650), across a real navigate-away-and-back cycle.

### Item 4 — Blue focus rectangle on the Root Map ring

Confirmed the browser's own default `:focus` outline on the wedge `<path>` (`outline: auto`, blue, 5px — SVG focus outlines are always axis-aligned regardless of the element's real shape). First attempt (`outline-none` + `focus-visible:outline-*`, the standard idiom) measured as **still broken** even after applying it — traced all the way to the compiled CSS: this project is on **Tailwind v4**, whose outline utilities are backed by a `--tw-outline-style` CSS custom property; an unconditional `outline-none` sets that property to `none` on the element, and `focus-visible:outline` only *reads* the same property via `var()` rather than resetting it — so it stayed `none` even while `:focus-visible` genuinely matched (confirmed directly: a real keyboard Tab press showed `matches(':focus-visible') === true` with `outlineStyle` still computing to `'none'`). Fixed by scoping the suppression to `[&:not(:focus-visible)]:outline-none` instead of an unconditional one, leaving the variable at its real global default (`solid`) exactly when `:focus-visible` is what's actually active. Verified live both directions: a real keyboard Tab shows a solid 2px gold (`#F5B700`) outline; a real mouse click shows none.

### Item 5 — Ring segment jump still wrong, and only 4 of 12 numbers rendered

**(a) Diagnosed properly this time, not re-applied blindly.** The previous fix (`block: 'center'`) was reported still broken. Reproduced with isolated per-segment taps (fresh page load per tap, no cross-tap interference) across all twelve segments individually: `block: 'center'` cannot create scroll room that doesn't exist — a domain whose section sits near the very end of the page (the four `isUninstrumented` domains' compact list items in `RootMapNotCoveredSection.tsx`, plus their close neighbors) clamps to max-scroll instead of centering, landing on whatever card happens to be nearest the clamped viewport center, not the tapped domain. Switched to `block: 'start'`, which has no such failure mode (it only needs the target's own top edge at the viewport's top, achievable even for the page's last element) — every anchor already carried `scroll-mt-24` for exactly this alignment, a leftover clue `start` was the original intent. **Verified live for all twelve segments individually**: every one now lands on its own real section, genuinely visible on screen (clear of both the header and the ~90px bottom nav), not just "nearest to center."

**(b) Confirmed live with the real Safari engine, not assumed.** Reproduced directly with Playwright's `webkit` browser (the actual WebKit/Safari rendering engine, not headless Chromium, which always showed all 12): 8 of 12 number labels measured `insideSvgBounds: false` — `dominantBaseline="middle"`'s cross-browser support is inconsistent, and the position it produced in WebKit pushed those 8 labels' bounding boxes outside the SVG's own clipping bounds (the 4 survivors — 2, 5, 8, 11 — sit at the ring's diagonal positions, geometrically the ones with the most real clearance from the viewBox edge; the reported "11, 2, 8, 5" is exactly this set). Not a spacing/design tradeoff to solve by dropping labels — replaced `dominantBaseline="middle"` with the standard, WebKit-safe `dy="0.35em"` offset technique, plus a small viewBox margin (`VIEWBOX_MARGIN = 16`) for real clearance regardless of any browser's own baseline math. **Verified in real WebKit after the fix**: all 12 labels measure `insideSvgBounds: true`.

**Guard tests** (`tests/dashboard-carousel-navigation.test.ts` — new, 10 tests; `tests/root-map-ring.test.ts` — extended, 19 tests total): source-scans confirming the headline no longer names an assessment, the dashboard uses `<ScrollCarousel>` (not the old hand-rolled scroll div), `ScrollCarousel` uses a `MutationObserver` + real DOM child count, all five pages use `<BackButton>` (parameterized `it.each`) instead of a hardcoded `Link`, the wedge's outline suppression is scoped (not the broken unconditional form), `scrollToDomain` uses `block: 'start'` not `'center'`, and label centering uses `dy` not `dominantBaseline` with the viewBox margin present. **Every one proven non-vacuous**: `git stash` each fix's file(s) back to the pre-fix version, re-run the specific guard tests, watch them fail (1/10, then a missing-file suite error, then 5/10, then 4/19 across the four fix groups respectively), `git stash pop` to restore, watch them pass again.

**Verified**: `npm run typecheck` — clean. `npm run lint` — 0 errors, same 31-warning baseline. Full `vitest run` — **244 files / 2765/2765 passing** (the same `tests/feed-integration.test.ts` + `tests/longitudinal-intelligence-integration.test.ts` pre-existing shared-fixture flakiness this file has documented twice before, reproduced passing both in isolation and on an immediate full-suite re-run). `npm run build` — compiled successfully.

**Verified locally against a running dev server + local Supabase** (Playwright, `member.one@example.test`, seeded with one check-in and, separately, two registry findings to reproduce the noticing card — both deleted after): all five items confirmed working as described above, plus the dot indicator's live count/active-index behavior and the BackButton's exact scroll-position preservation across a real navigate-away-and-back cycle, with a screenshot confirming the card title, peek, and dots all render as intended.

**Not done**: nothing the task asked for — all five items were investigated to a real root cause and fixed. Did not retrofit the ~23 *other* pages already using `BackButton` correctly (out of scope, no bug there) beyond the five actually named/implicated by this carousel.

**Not verified**: real mobile Safari/WebKit rendering for anything other than the ring-numbering bug specifically (Playwright's `webkit` engine was used there because the bug was WebKit-specific and headless Chromium couldn't reproduce it at all; every other fix in this entry was verified in headless Chromium only, matching every prior entry in this file).

**Deployed and confirmed live**: verified the deploy target both before and after. Before: production was serving the prior commit `e03d1e1` (status `Ready`, target `production`). Pushed to `origin/main` (`github.com/MEFwellness/mef-platform`, commit `15f9115`); the push triggered Vercel's GitHub-integration auto-deploy. After: repo `MEFwellness/mef-platform` ✓, branch `main` ✓, Vercel project `mef-platform` ✓, target `production` ✓ (not Preview). `npx vercel inspect app.mefwellness.com` confirms the alias points at deployment `dpl_Ftgx8QoTdx6eZdbaetvMigPpyE7d`, whose build log (`npx vercel inspect --logs`) shows `Cloning github.com/MEFwellness/mef-platform (Branch: main, Commit: 15f9115)`, status `Ready` — all live verification below ran against this exact deployment. No database migration needed — no schema touched.

**Verified live on `app.mefwellness.com`** (Playwright, real `memberPopulated` production account, `390×844`): card headline reads "A few things stand out" (not "Four Doctors Assessment"); the carousel shows 4 real position dots; the Noticing tile is a real `<a href="/noticing">` and the destination page has a real `BackButton`. On `/root-map`: all 12 number labels render; a real keyboard Tab onto a ring segment shows a solid 2px gold (`#F5B700`) `:focus-visible` outline; **all twelve segments individually tapped landed correctly** on their own section, genuinely visible on screen — screenshotted both the carousel (title/peek/dots) and the full ring (all 12 numbers, matching the legend below) for a final visual check. Zero page errors across all of it.

## Root Map: color key, uncovered-tap clarity, single source of truth, Nutrition badge (2026-07-29 follow-up)

**Premise checked first, per the task's own instruction.** The reported 8-tracked/4-uncovered split is real and exact: `lib/investigation-engine/domains.ts`'s `COACHING_DOMAINS` array (the one place all 12 dimensions are numbered — ring, legend, Building list, and Not Covered Yet block all derive from this same array, never a second copy) has `isUninstrumented: true` at exactly positions 1, 2, 11, 12 (Identity & Self-Concept, Purpose & Motivation, Relationships & Social Connection, Environment & Daily Rhythm) and `false` at 3–10. Locked in as a real regression guard, not just prose, in `tests/root-map-single-source.test.ts`.

**One premise in the task turned out not to match the code, flagged rather than silently written around**: gold vs. green does not mean "tracked vs. not yet covered." Re-reading `RootMapRing.tsx`'s own `colorFor`/`fillFractionFor` (unchanged by this task): gold means a domain already has a real earned finding ("What We're Seeing"); green means it doesn't yet — which covers BOTH an instrumented domain still building coverage (the "Building" list) AND all four uninstrumented domains (always green, since they can never earn coverage). Two green segments can be in entirely different real states. The new color key (below) is worded to match this real encoding, not the "tracked vs. untracked" framing in the brief.

### Item 1 — the ring's color key

Added a two-line key directly below the ring (`RootMapRing.tsx`), above the existing numbered legend — a gold swatch reading "we've noticed a real pattern here," a green swatch reading "still gathering information." Three-tier voice (`lib/longitudinal-intelligence/copy.ts`'s existing tier phrasing — "we noticed," "still gathering," never diagnostic), brief enough to sit above the legend without crowding it.

### Item 2 — tapping an uncovered dimension

**Root cause, not assumed**: the previous fix (`docs/BUILD_STATUS.md`, 2026-07-29 entry above) already made segments 1/2/11/12 scroll to the right *element* — each domain's own `<li>` inside `RootMapNotCoveredSection.tsx`. But that `<li>` sits below the section's own "Not Covered Yet" heading; `scrollIntoView({block:'start'})` pins the *li's* top edge to the viewport top, which scrolls the heading itself out of view above it — exactly "lands at the bottom with no indication why," since four identical-looking, unlabeled list items were then whatever happened to be on screen.

**Fix**: new `NOT_COVERED_SECTION_ANCHOR_ID` (`lib/root-map/anchors.ts`) on the section's own root element. `RingDomain` (`RootMapRing.tsx`) gained a real `isUninstrumented` field (from the same `COACHING_DOMAINS`-derived data, not a new source); `scrollToDomain` now branches on it — the four uninstrumented domains scroll to the shared section id (heading included), the eight tracked ones still scroll to their own card/row exactly as before (re-verified, not just assumed — see below). A tiny new plain-`EventTarget` bus, `lib/root-map/highlightBus.ts` (same shape as the existing `lib/root-launcher-bus.ts`, since the ring and the Not Covered section are server-rendered siblings, not parent/child, and can't share React state directly), lets the ring tell the section which of the four was actually tapped. `RootMapNotCoveredSection.tsx` is now a small client component that marks that one item with a soft gold ring/tint for ~2.6s (never a solid gold fill — this app's own locked rule that gold is an accent, never a whole-tile fill).

**The other eight — re-verified live, not assumed still correct**: confirmed with Playwright against the local dev server (`member.one@example.test`) that all eight tracked domains still land on their own card/row with the label visible near the top of the viewport, unchanged from the prior fix.

### Item 3 — single source of truth

Confirmed by reading the code, not assumed: `RootMapRing.tsx` re-orders into `COACHING_DOMAINS`' own canonical order rather than keeping a separate order; `lib/root-map/grouping.ts` computes Building/Seeing/Not-Covered membership from real fields (`isUninstrumented`, `whatWeUnderstand.length`) on the same `RootMapDomainView[]` `builder.ts` builds from `COACHING_DOMAINS`; no second hardcoded 12-item domain array exists anywhere in `components/root-map/`, `lib/root-map/`, or `app/root-map/`. Nothing needed consolidating — locked in as a guard test (`tests/root-map-single-source.test.ts`) instead: exact position assertions for all 12, a grouping cross-check, and a source scan across every file in those three directories ruling out a second copy.

### Item 4 — the missing Nutrition badge

**Neither of the task's two hypotheses was quite right — a third, real cause found instead.** `lib/root-map/coverage.ts`'s `COVERAGE_FIELD_BY_DOMAIN` only ever mapped 7 of the 12 domains to a `daily_checkins_current` column; Nutrition & Metabolic Health has no such column and was documented (and tested) as a deliberate `null`, never a fabricated zero — so the badge wasn't hidden-at-zero and the count wasn't failing, there was simply no coverage *source* wired up for it at all.

But a real, already-collected per-day nutrition source does exist and predates this gap: the driver library's "Fuel and nutrition" (`FUE`) domain (migration 106) and its rotating check-in probes (migration 109 — last meal timing, hydration, cravings, protein, etc., 14 real questions) already write into `daily_checkin_probe_answers` every time a member answers one during the daily check-in. `lib/root-map/coverage.ts`'s coverage module simply predates that table and never read it.

**Fix**: new `fetchNutritionCoverage` in `coverage.ts` reuses the exact reference data the adaptive check-in picker itself already reads (`listActiveDrivers` from `lib/driver-library`, `listActiveDriverProbeQuestions` from `lib/daily-checkin-adaptive`) to find which probe questions currently belong to the FUE domain, then counts the member's distinct answered days from `daily_checkin_probe_answers` over the same 21-day window every other domain uses — deliberately not a second, hardcoded list of FUE question keys, so a coach adding/retiring a nutrition question via `/coach/questions` changes this count automatically, with nothing to fall out of sync. `fetchDomainCoverage` merges this into the same map every other domain's badge already reads from; a real, non-fabricated `{ count: 0, ... }` when the member hasn't answered one yet, `null` only if the reference data itself can't be read.

**Verified locally, not assumed**: against the local dev server + local Supabase, `member.one@example.test`'s Building row read "0 of 21 days logged" for Nutrition (previously no badge at all). Seeded one real `daily_checkin_probe_answers` row for a FUE question (`checkin_probe.hydration_felt_adequate`), reloaded, confirmed the badge became "1 of 21 days logged," then deleted the seeded row and confirmed it read "0 of 21 days logged" again — proving the count is live, not stuck.

**Guard tests** (5 new files — `tests/root-map-single-source.test.ts`, `tests/root-map-highlight-bus.test.ts`, `tests/root-map-not-covered-section.test.ts`; 2 files extended — `tests/root-map-ring.test.ts`, `tests/root-map-coverage.test.ts`): color-key text and position, the `isUninstrumented` scroll branch and highlight-request call, the shared section anchor id and highlight-clear timeout, the 12-domain position/grouping/no-second-list invariants, `requestRootMapHighlight` dispatching a real `CustomEvent` with the tapped domain as `detail`, `computeNutritionCoverage`'s distinct-day counting (including collapsing several same-day probe answers into one), and a source scan proving `fetchDomainCoverage` reads the live question bank rather than a hardcoded FUE key list. **Every one proven non-vacuous**: reverted `RootMapRing.tsx` (5/23 failed), `RootMapNotCoveredSection.tsx` (5/5 failed), `coverage.ts` (5/10 failed), `highlightBus.ts` (1/1 failed), and temporarily flipped one domain's `isUninstrumented` flag in `domains.ts` (2/6 failed) — each reverted/restored and reconfirmed green afterward.

**Verified**: `npm run typecheck` — clean. `npm run lint` (from repo root) — 0 errors, same 31-warning baseline. Full `vitest run` — **247 files / 2786/2786 passing** (the same `tests/feed-integration.test.ts`/`tests/longitudinal-intelligence-integration.test.ts` pre-existing shared-fixture flakiness this file has documented three times before, reproduced passing both in isolation and on an immediate full-suite re-run). `npm run build` — compiled successfully, `/root-map` included among all routes.

**Verified locally against a running dev server + local Supabase** (Playwright, `member.one@example.test`, `390×844`): color key text present; all 12 segments tapped individually (fresh page load per tap) — segments 1/2/11/12 all landed on the shared Not Covered Yet section with its heading visible in-viewport and the specific tapped domain's own list item showing the highlight class; segments 3–10 all landed on their own card/row, heading visible in-viewport, matching the prior fix. Nutrition coverage confirmed live end-to-end as described above. A pre-existing, unrelated console warning was noticed while driving the page (a sub-pixel floating-point SSR/CSR hydration mismatch on the wedge `<path>`'s `d` attribute, 12th significant digit) — confirmed present on the unmodified code too (checked with this task's own changes stashed out), not introduced by this task and not one of the four requested items, so left alone rather than fixed unprompted.

**Not verified**: real mobile Safari/WebKit rendering (headless Chromium locally and in the live production check below, matching every prior entry in this file).

**Deployed and confirmed live**: verified the deploy target both before and after. Before: production was serving the prior commit `15f9115` (deployment `dpl_1an9d4nq8...`, status `Ready`, target `production`). Pushed to `origin/main` (`github.com/MEFwellness/mef-platform`, commit `784d7c2`); the push triggered Vercel's GitHub-integration auto-deploy, watched through `Building` to `Ready`. After: repo `MEFwellness/mef-platform` ✓, branch `main` ✓, Vercel project `mef-platform` ✓, target `production` ✓ (not Preview). `npx vercel inspect app.mefwellness.com` confirms the alias points at deployment `dpl_3LFuioggdEvBsipzzCEXhxyp5ugU`, whose build log (`npx vercel inspect --logs`) shows `Cloning github.com/MEFwellness/mef-platform (Branch: main, Commit: 784d7c2)`, status `Ready` — a plain `curl` against `https://app.mefwellness.com/root-map` returned the expected `307` to `/login` for a logged-out request, confirming the domain is actually serving (not just reporting `Ready`) before logging in. No database migration was needed — no schema touched, only a new read against the already-existing `daily_checkin_probe_answers`/`driver_probe_questions`/`drivers` tables.

**Verified live on `app.mefwellness.com`** (Playwright, real `memberPopulated` production account, `390×844`, against the exact deployment above): color key text present ("we've noticed a real pattern here" / "still gathering information"). Nutrition & Metabolic Health — on this real, populated account — has actually earned a finding ("Digestive Complaints reported as 'poor' on the latest onboarding submission"), so it renders under "What We're Seeing" rather than "Building"; its coverage badge now reads a real **"1 of 21 days logged"**, computed live from this account's own real check-in history — previously no badge rendered here at all. **All twelve segments tapped individually** (fresh page load per tap): segments 1/2/11/12 all landed on the shared "Not Covered Yet" heading (`top: 384`, in-viewport) with the specific tapped domain's own item visibly highlighted (`itemHighlighted: true`, confirmed for all four independently); segments 3–10 all landed on their own card, heading in-viewport (`top` 96–105, clear of both the header and the bottom nav). Zero console/page errors across all twelve.

## Dashboard performance: found and fixed the 8-second document load (2026-07-29)

**Starting evidence (given, not assumed):** Chrome DevTools against `app.mefwellness.com`, warm cache, no throttling — the `/dashboard` document itself: 200, 19.3 kB, **8.00s**. Every static asset: 1–183ms. Seven RSC prefetches fired on load (case 1.09s, checkin 1.13s, today 462ms, food-lens 313ms, movement 203ms, progress 186ms, root-score unmeasured).

### Phase 1 — measuring where the 8 seconds actually goes

Couldn't instrument production directly (no Vercel log/CLI access in this environment), so the render path was instrumented with `console.time`-style markers and run against a **local production build** (`next build && next start`) pointed at local Supabase, seeded with a 40-day realistic check-in/feed history (mirroring the real `memberPopulated` fixture's shape) so the fan-out patterns below would actually have something to fan out over. This measures relative cost and call counts faithfully; it can't reproduce production's real network RTT to a hosted Supabase project, which is the actual source of the absolute 8.00s (see below).

**What the instrumentation found, directly, not inferred:**

- `/dashboard`'s own two top-level `Promise.all` batches (14 calls) were already correctly parallel and already cheap (~90ms locally) — not the cause.
- The real cost is in the four independently-fetching carousel cards below them (`WhatWereNoticingCard`, `RootMapCard`, `CoachingMessageCard`, `RecommendationsCard`, each its own async Server Component in its own `<Suspense>` boundary). Because Next.js streams a dynamically-rendered page as one HTTP response that doesn't close until every `Suspense` boundary on it resolves, all four of these are still inside the document's own 8-second budget, not separate from it.
- Each of those four cards reaches `app/actions/rootMap.ts`'s `gatherRootMapInputs()` — directly (`RootMapCard`) or through `recomputeAndPersist()`/`gatherAndPlan()` (`RecommendationsCard`, `CoachingMessageCard`) — and `gatherRootMapInputs()` itself calls `computeMemberIntelligence()` (the Intelligence Engine, itself a ~12-query gather plus its own feed-history fan-out) and `decideNextAction()` (the Root Router). `WhatWereNoticingCard` also calls `decideNextAction()` directly. **Measured directly via a call counter**: on one dashboard load, `computeMemberIntelligence`, `decideNextAction`, and `getCoachingFocusDecision` (the Coaching Brain, reached a fifth way through `gatherMemberHealthProfile`) each executed **3–4 times** for the exact same `(memberId, localDate)` — the same expensive gather, repeated, in parallel, once per card that happened to need a piece of it. `app/actions/rootMap.ts`'s own docstring already claimed this was deduplicated "so a page rendering both never calls `computeMemberIntelligence()`/`decideNextAction()` twice for the same request" — true in intent, never actually true in practice, because each card's own action creates its own fresh `createClient()` instance and nothing tied the calls together.
- Layered on top of that 3–4x duplication: three separate places (`lib/brain/service.ts`'s `assembleContext`, `lib/intelligence-engine/profile.ts`'s `gatherMemberHealthProfile`, `lib/coaching-engine/service.ts`'s `fetchContinuitySentence`) each built up to 30–100 `(feedItem, content)` pairs by calling `getContentItem()` — one `.eq('id', x).maybeSingle()` round trip **per item** — instead of one batched query. Locally (near-zero query latency) this cost single-digit milliseconds; over a real network hop to a hosted Supabase project, an N+1 of this shape run 3–4 times over is a very plausible multi-second cost on its own.
- Checked every query's indexing (`registry_entries`, `narrative_items`, `wellness_insights`, `daily_checkins`, `member_recommendations`, `safety_review_queue`, `coach_notes`, `member_pattern_states`, `daily_feed_items`, `root_score_snapshots`) — all correctly indexed for the exact filters this render path uses. **Not a missing-index/scan problem.**
- Checked for an LLM/external-API call anywhere in this path (`grep` across the Coaching Brain, Root Router, Intelligence Engine, Root Coaching Conversation Engine directories for `anthropic`/`openai`/chat-completion patterns) — **none found**. Every "engine" here is rule-based template composition over already-fetched data, not a network call to a model.
- Root Score (`getOrCalculateRootScore`) and the Morning Brief (`getOrCreateTodaysMorningBrief`) already do the right thing — read a persisted snapshot for today if one exists, only compute once per member per day. The Root Map/Recommendations/Coaching-Message path had no such persistence check at all; it fully recomputed the Intelligence Engine and Root Router live on every single render.
- The "8.00 seconds" figure: no `maxDuration`/timeout constant of 8000ms exists anywhere in this app's code or `vercel.json` (which sets no function timeout override at all), so it is not a configured limit being hit. Given the measured 3–4x duplication of an already-query-heavy gather, a genuine multi-second duration over real network latency is the more likely explanation than a timeout artifact — though this couldn't be confirmed against production's own logs directly (no Vercel CLI/log access in this environment; see "Not verified" below).

### Phase 2 — the fixes (same data, same content, same order — verified below)

1. **Request-scoped Supabase client** — new `getRequestClient()` (`lib/supabase/server.ts`), wrapped in React's `cache()` via a new shared `lib/reactRequestCache.ts` (the exact fallback-guard idiom `getCachedUser` already used, pulled out so it's shared instead of copy-pasted a fourth time). One client instance per request instead of one per action call.
2. **The four duplicated engine entry points now memoize for real**: `decideNextAction` (`lib/investigation-engine/rootRouter.ts`), `computeMemberIntelligence` (`lib/intelligence-engine/engine.ts`), `getCoachingFocusDecision` (`lib/brain/service.ts`), and `gatherRootMapInputs` (`app/actions/rootMap.ts`) are each now wrapped in the same `cache()`. This only works because of fix 1 — `cache()` keys on argument identity, and a fresh client per call defeated it before. The six call sites that reach these (dashboard's own two direct calls, plus `getMyNoticingView`/`getMyRootMap`/`getMyCoachingMessage`/`getMyRecommendations`) were switched from `createClient()` to `getRequestClient()`. Coach-facing call sites (`getClientRootMap`, `getClientCoachWorkspaceSummary`, `getClientCoachingDecision`) were left on plain `createClient()` — they're single calls per page, not the duplicated member-dashboard pattern, and `buildMemberIntelligence` (the coach variant) has a real persistence side effect that must not be memoized away.
3. **Batched the N+1**: new `getContentItemsByIds()` (`lib/feed/data.ts`) does one `.in('id', ids)` query and returns a `Map`; the three history-pairs builders above now look up from that map instead of awaiting a fresh query per item. Same output shape (`{ feedItem, content }[]`, `content: null` for a missing id) — proven identical in the new guard test below.
4. **Parallelized a sequential write loop**: `app/actions/recommendations.ts`'s `recomputeAndPersist()` was `for (const rec of built) { await upsertMemberRecommendation(...) }` — independent rows (each keyed by its own `recommendation_key`), now `Promise.all(built.map(...))`.
5. **`decideNextAction`'s own steps 2 and 3** (`getMemberAssessmentFacts` and `listRegistryEntriesForMember`) were awaited sequentially despite being independent of each other (only the step-1 safety gate they both follow needs to happen first) — now run concurrently.

Nothing about what the dashboard shows changed — no engine's decision logic, ranking, or copy was touched; only how many times each read runs and whether independent reads/writes wait on each other.

### Phase 3 — the seven prefetches

Confirmed these are Next.js's automatic `<Link>` viewport-prefetching from the bottom nav (Home/Food Lens/Progress/Today) and the dashboard's own Quick Actions pills (Case, Movement) — all seven routes build as fully dynamic (`ƒ`, every one uses `cookies()`). Probed real RSC prefetch requests (via Playwright's `context.request` with the same `RSC`/`Next-Router-Prefetch` headers Next's client sends, authenticated as a seeded member, against the local production build) and compared against a real navigation fetch to the same route:

| Route | Real nav (full page) | Prefetch |
|---|---|---|
| `/case` | 20,934 B | **98 B** |
| `/checkin` | 13,285 B | 3,723 B |
| `/today` | 37,691 B | 3,713 B |
| `/food-lens` | 17,696 B | 3,776 B |
| `/movement` | 13,730 B | 3,867 B |
| `/progress` | 168,316 B | 3,915 B |
| `/root-score` | 133,675 B | **110 B** |

**A prefetch request never executes a route's own data-fetching, confirmed empirically, not just from framework docs** — response bodies are consistently tiny (a `loading.tsx` skeleton where one exists, next to nothing where one doesn't) regardless of how expensive the real page is. So these seven prefetches were never re-running the Coaching Brain/Intelligence Engine/Root Router a fifth-through-eleventh time; they're not what Phase 1/2 fixed and don't compete with the document's own critical path (they fire client-side, after hydration, i.e. after the document has already fully arrived). **Decision: keep prefetching, don't disable it** — the real, measured difference between routes is that `/case` and `/root-score` had no `loading.tsx`, so their prefetch (and their real navigation) had no skeleton to show meanwhile, consistent with `/case` measuring the slowest of the seven in the original production numbers. Added `app/case/loading.tsx` and `app/root-score/loading.tsx` — the same one-line `<PageSkeleton />` the other five routes already use (`components/PageSkeleton.tsx`, not a new component) — so all seven are now consistent.

### Guard test

New `tests/dashboard-render-performance.test.ts`: seeds 30 days of real check-ins + 29 real feed items for the seeded `member.one` fixture, signs in as that member, and calls `decideNextAction` + `computeMemberIntelligence` + `getCoachingFocusDecision` + `getContentItemsByIds` concurrently, asserting the whole gather completes under 3000ms. Documented in the test's own header comment: React's `cache()` only memoizes inside the real Next.js RSC runtime — this suite runs in plain Node/vitest (same reason server actions can't be called directly here at all, per `tests/setup/test-clients.ts`), so the fallback in `lib/reactRequestCache.ts` is a no-op identity wrapper in this environment and the test cannot observe the cross-call deduplication itself. What it does catch: a reintroduced sequential await, or a reintroduced per-item N+1. **Proven non-vacuous**: temporarily put a 3.5s delay back inside `getContentItemsByIds`, re-ran the test, watched it fail (`expected 7063ms to be less than 3000`), reverted, watched it pass again.

Separately, and more directly: verified the *actual* cache() deduplication in the real runtime it depends on — added a call counter to `decideNextAction`/`computeMemberIntelligence`/`getCoachingFocusDecision`, rebuilt (`next build`), ran the local production server, loaded `/dashboard` once. **Server log: each printed `call #1` exactly once** — down from the 3–4 executions per load the same counter would have shown before this fix (matches the Phase 1 measurement above). Counters removed before commit.

**Verified**: `npm run typecheck` (consumer-web-app) — clean, 0 errors. `npm run lint` (repo root) — 0 errors, same pre-existing 31-warning baseline (all in files this task didn't touch). Full `vitest run` — **248 files / 2788/2788 passing** (247/2786 baseline plus the 2 new tests in this suite's own file). `npm run build` — compiled successfully, all routes including the two new `loading.tsx` files.

**Measured local before/after** (local production build, local Supabase — absolute numbers aren't production's, but the before/after comparison and the call-count proof above are what actually demonstrate the fix): document `responseEnd` warm-cache went from ~327ms to ~192–220ms across repeated loads; more importantly, the four carousel cards' shared engine work dropped from 3–4 real executions per load to exactly 1.

**Not done**: no engine's logic, the three-tier language system, `member_pattern_states`, the check-in flow, or assessment scoring were touched — confirmed by the fact every existing test (including the ones that pin exact engine output) still passes unmodified.

**Deployed and confirmed live**: verified the deploy target both before and after. Before: production was serving the prior commit `dcf6ff5` (deployment `dpl_FSSFJdVsGQUEtNW1v9ZumV9wYvFd`, status `Ready`, target `production`). Pushed to `origin/main` (`github.com/MEFwellness/mef-platform`, commit `9c59959`); the push triggered Vercel's GitHub-integration auto-deploy, watched through `Building` to `Ready`. After: repo `MEFwellness/mef-platform` ✓, branch `main` ✓, Vercel project `mef-platform` ✓, target `production` ✓ (not Preview). `npx vercel inspect app.mefwellness.com` confirms the alias points at deployment `dpl_BcFQR55XfvJTBdLcS1SMEoVtj5pL`, whose build log shows `Cloning github.com/MEFwellness/mef-platform (Branch: main, Commit: 9c59959)`, status `Ready`.

**Measured live on `app.mefwellness.com`** (Playwright, real `memberPopulated` production account, real network — this is the number that actually matters, not the local approximation above): `/dashboard` document load, two consecutive passes after login: **2.5s and 3.4s** (`nav.responseEnd`), down from the original **8.00s** — a 57–69% reduction. Screenshotted the resulting page (`390×844`): hero, Root Score (58/100), Quick Actions, Today's Daily Brief, sleep/stress/digestion trend lines, and the coaching recommendation all rendered correctly with real data, zero page errors. Same content, same order, nothing missing.

Measured every other bottom-nav/quick-action route the same way, for completeness (asked for in the task, not part of this fix's own scope):

| Route | Pass 1 | Pass 2 |
|---|---|---|
| `/case` | 1.15s | 1.59s |
| `/checkin` | 1.09s | 0.75s |
| `/today` | 1.46s | 1.34s |
| `/food-lens` | 0.65s | 0.91s |
| `/movement` | 0.91s | 1.21s |
| `/progress` | **7.23s** | **6.28s** |
| `/root-score` | 0.90s | 0.89s |

**New finding, flagged, not fixed here**: `/progress` is now the slowest page in the app, at roughly the same magnitude as the Dashboard's original problem — likely the same shape of issue (its own page likely re-runs expensive per-request work without the memoization this task added), but it was never measured or in scope for this task, and fixing it needs its own Phase-1 measurement pass the same way the Dashboard got. Recommend a dedicated follow-up task scoped to `/progress` specifically.

**Not verified**: whether Supabase's own connection-pool size on the production project is a further, separate ceiling underneath this fix — the duplication removal cuts most of the concurrent-query pressure this render path was creating, but pool sizing itself wasn't inspected (no production Supabase dashboard access from this environment).
