## App-wide performance pass: every member-facing route measured and fixed (2026-07-29 follow-up)

Same investigation as the Dashboard fix above, extended to every route in the bottom nav and every page reachable from a Home card: `/progress`, `/checkin`, `/today`, `/food-lens`, `/movement`, `/case`, `/root-score`, `/noticing`, `/root-map`, `/recommendations`.

### Phase 1 — measured every route live against `app.mefwellness.com`, before touching anything

| Route | Before (2nd pass) |
|---|---|
| `/progress` | **5.2–5.5s** |
| `/recommendations` | 2.0–2.1s |
| `/dashboard` | 2.3–2.4s *(already fixed above)* |
| `/movement` | 1.1–1.5s |
| `/today` | 1.2–1.4s |
| `/root-map` | 1.1–1.2s |
| `/checkin` | 1.0–1.2s |
| `/case` | 0.8–1.0s |
| `/noticing` | 0.8–0.9s |
| `/food-lens` | 0.8–0.9s |
| `/root-score` | 0.7s |

Read every page's own server component plus every action/lib function it calls, looking for the same three shapes the Dashboard fix already named: a duplicated engine call, a row-at-a-time (N+1) fetch, or an independent read/write awaited sequentially instead of in parallel. Found:

- **`/progress` (worst by a wide margin)**: its own 14-item top-level `Promise.all` was already correct — the cost is one level down. `getMyWellnessStorySummary()` calls `recalculateIntelligenceCore()` (`lib/intelligence-core/service.ts`), which **awaited four independent reads one at a time** (member health profile, conversation memory, active identity observations, existing recommendation feedback — none needs another's result) and then **wrote observations and profile dimensions in two separate sequential per-item `for` loops**, plus a third sequential loop resolving stale observations — none of these loops have any real ordering dependency between their own items (each keyed by its own distinct `observation_key`/`dimension`/id). Separately, `getMyWellnessPatterns()` calls `recalculateWellnessIntelligence()` (`lib/intelligence/service.ts`), which had the exact same one-row-at-a-time `getContentItem()` N+1 already fixed three other places on the Dashboard (up to 100 feed-history items). **Both of these recompute-and-persist on every single page view by design** (their own doc comments say so — "cheap and safe to re-run"), which this task does not change; only the redundant serial/row-at-a-time shape inside that existing behavior was fixed.
- **`/today`**: `getFeedHistory()` (`app/actions/feed.ts`) had the same one-row-at-a-time `getContentItem()` N+1, up to 30 items.
- **`/movement`**: awaited `getTodaysMovementSession()` on its own, then separately awaited `Promise.all([getCurrentMovementScore(), getWeeklyMovementProgress()])` — all three take no arguments and don't depend on each other at all.
- **`/case`**: awaited `getMyCaseViewAction()` (which resolves its own user/timezone independently) only after its own `profile`/`isCoach` `Promise.all` had already finished, despite not needing either result.
- **`/noticing`, `/root-map`, `/recommendations`**: no `loading.tsx` at all — every other route in this list had one.
- **`/checkin`, `/food-lens`, `/root-score`**: read start to finish — already correctly parallel, no engine duplication, no N+1. `/root-score`'s two-batch structure is a genuine dependency (batch 2 needs batch 1's timezone), not a bug. Left unchanged.
- **`/recommendations`' remaining ~2s**: already benefits from the Dashboard fix's `gatherRootMapInputs` caching and batching; it calls that gather exactly once per page view here (nothing else on this page duplicates it), so there's no further redundant-call bug to remove — the remaining time is the cost of one real, necessary full Intelligence Engine + Root Router pass over real network latency, which "don't change engine logic" means this task doesn't get to shrink further.

**No missing indexes found** on any newly-checked table. **No other page repeats the Dashboard's specific bug** (the same cached engine function called 3-4× because each caller built its own Supabase client) — every other page calls each of its expensive functions exactly once per request. The *general* pattern of "every action builds its own fresh client" is still true everywhere except the six files switched to `getRequestClient()` for the Dashboard fix, but since nothing outside Dashboard calls the same cached function twice per request, there was no correctness reason to switch more files to it here.

### Supabase connection usage — could not verify

Tried `npx supabase inspect db role-connections --linked` (and the other `inspect db` subcommands) against the linked production project (`piafgqstbibvllsnuike`, confirmed via `supabase projects list`) — every one of them requires the actual Postgres database password (Settings → Database in the Supabase dashboard) to open a direct connection through the pooler; the CLI's own login session/access token isn't sufficient. That password isn't stored anywhere in this repo or environment, so this couldn't be read from here. **If you want this checked**, either share that password for one read-only session, or check directly: Supabase Dashboard → your project → Database → Connection Pooling shows current connections against the plan's ceiling.

### Phase 2 — fixes (same tools as the Dashboard fix, no new ones)

1. `lib/intelligence/service.ts`'s `recalculateWellnessIntelligence`: batched its `getContentItem`-per-item loop through the same `getContentItemsByIds()` used on Dashboard.
2. `lib/intelligence-core/service.ts`'s `recalculateIntelligenceCore`: the four independent reads now run in one `Promise.all`; the stale-observation-resolve loop, the draft-upsert loop, and the dimension-upsert loop each now run via `Promise.all` instead of a sequential `for` loop; the coaching-style-profile upsert and its dimension upsert (two more independent writes) now run together too.
3. `app/actions/feed.ts`'s `getFeedHistory` (member) and `getClientFeedHistory` (coach, same bug, fixed as a bonus): batched through `getContentItemsByIds()`.
4. `app/movement/page.tsx`: `getTodaysMovementSession()`/`getCurrentMovementScore()`/`getWeeklyMovementProgress()` now run in one `Promise.all`.
5. `app/case/page.tsx`: `getMyCaseViewAction()` now joins the page's existing `profile`/`isCoach` `Promise.all`.
6. Added `app/noticing/loading.tsx`, `app/root-map/loading.tsx`, `app/recommendations/loading.tsx` — the same one-line `<PageSkeleton />` every other route already uses.

Nothing about what any page shows, in what order, or in what words, changed. `recalculateIntelligenceCore`'s existing end-to-end integration test (`tests/intelligence-core-integration.test.ts` — exact observation/dimension counts, no duplicate rows on a second run) passed unmodified against the parallelized version, which is real proof the concurrent writes didn't change what gets persisted (each item is keyed by its own distinct `observation_key`/`dimension`, so there was never a shared-state race to introduce).

### Guard tests (3 new files, 6 new tests)

- `tests/progress-render-performance.test.ts` — seeds 40 days of real check-ins + real feed items for the seeded `member.one` fixture (a fresh, disjoint 2016 date range — every other date range in the integration suite is already claimed), calls `recalculateIntelligenceCore` and `recalculateWellnessIntelligence` concurrently (mirroring how `/progress`'s own top-level `Promise.all` already calls their callers), asserts under 4000ms. **Proven non-vacuous**: temporarily added a 4.2s delay inside the batched lookup, watched it fail (`4463ms` vs. `4000ms` threshold), reverted, watched it pass again (`~350ms`).
- `tests/today-feed-history-performance.test.ts` — same pattern against `listFeedHistory` + `getContentItemsByIds` directly (the `getFeedHistory` action itself can't be called in vitest — it builds its own client via `cookies()`, which throws outside a real request, the same constraint every `'use server'` file has). Asserts under 2000ms and that the batched result matches each item's own content row exactly. **Proven non-vacuous**: same delay-and-revert.
- `tests/movement-case-parallel-fetch.test.ts` — `/movement` and `/case` are real Next.js page components (`redirect()`/cookies-based auth at the top) that can't be rendered or imported in vitest either, so this is a source-scan guard: asserts the specific serial-await pattern is gone and the `Promise.all` it was replaced with is present. **Proven non-vacuous**: `git stash`ed both fixed files, re-ran — all 4 assertions failed against the pre-fix source, `git stash pop` restored and re-confirmed green.

**Verified**: `npm run typecheck` (consumer-web-app) — clean, 0 errors. `npm run lint` (repo root) — 0 errors, same pre-existing 31-warning baseline. Full `vitest run` — **251 files / 2794/2794 passing** (248/2788 baseline plus this task's 3 new files / 6 new tests). `npm run build` — compiled successfully.

**Deployed and confirmed live**: pushed to `origin/main` (`github.com/MEFwellness/mef-platform`), commit `792b451`; the push triggered Vercel's GitHub-integration auto-deploy, watched through `Building` to `Ready`. `npx vercel inspect app.mefwellness.com` confirms the alias points at the new deployment, whose build log shows `Cloning github.com/MEFwellness/mef-platform (Branch: main, Commit: 792b451)`, status `Ready`, target `production`. A plain `curl` against `https://app.mefwellness.com/progress` returned the expected `307` to `/login` for a logged-out request, confirming the domain is actually serving this deployment.

## Make /progress feel instant: streaming + precomputed Recommendations (2026-07-29 follow-up)

**Premise check, per this file's own established discipline**: the task's premise was that `/progress`'s remaining ~4.2s is "one real Recommendations calculation." Checked the actual code first — `/progress` never called the real Recommendation Engine (`lib/recommendation-engine`, `member_recommendations`, the thing Dashboard's `RecommendationsCard` and `/recommendations` use) at all; its own slow parts are `recalculateIntelligenceCore`/`recalculateWellnessIntelligence` (found and reported in the prior task). Flagged this directly rather than silently building around it — the user chose to target the real Recommendation Engine (add it to `/progress` for real, precompute it everywhere), explicitly *not* addressing `/progress`'s own current bottleneck this round. Both facts are true at once and are kept straight in this entry: this task does not make `/progress`'s slowest sections (Wellness Story/Patterns) faster — it makes the page stream so they stop blocking everything else, and it adds and precomputes a real Recommendations section.

### Part 1 — /progress now streams per-section

Pulled `getMyWellnessStorySummary()` (→ `recalculateIntelligenceCore`) and `getMyWellnessPatterns()` (→ `recalculateWellnessIntelligence`) out of the page's single top-level `Promise.all` — each now lives in its own self-fetching component (`WellnessStorySection`, `WellnessPatternsSection`) wrapped in its own `<Suspense>` with a skeleton matching the existing `PageSkeleton`/`NoticingTileSkeleton` idiom. Everything else (Root Score, Coaching Insights, Trends, Consistency, Comparison, Explore links, History) stays in the original fast `Promise.all`, now unblocked by the two slow engines. New Recommendations section (below) gets the same treatment.

One real side effect, disclosed rather than hidden: `entryContext` (the invisible AI-chat-launcher context string, built from `wellnessPatterns`) can no longer be computed synchronously at the top of the page without re-blocking everything on the same slow call it was just pulled out of. It now defaults to the empty-state context (`"No wellness patterns have surfaced yet."`) for `CoachingInsightsPanel`'s chat chips and the floating launcher, instead of the specific pattern titles. Never member-visible UI text — only metadata sent if the member opens a chat — so nothing this page **displays** changed, per the task's own constraint.

**Measured live** (`memberPopulated`, production): time until fast content (Root Score) paints — **~1.0–1.1s**, down from the full ~4.2–5.5s everything previously waited behind. Full document time (everything included) is currently not improved and briefly worse (~3.7–6.3s) — explained in Part 2 below, expected to resolve once the migration lands.

### Part 2 — Recommendation Engine precomputed, not computed on page load

**New table, confirmed with the user before creating it** (checked `member_recommendations` — per-row `created_at`/`updated_at` get touched by unrelated actions like marking one done, not usable as a whole-set marker; `member_pattern_states` — per-signal, not per-computation-run; `member_health_profiles` — a different domain's own tracker): `member_recommendation_computations` (migration 116), one row per member, `computed_at` + `trigger`, mirroring `member_health_profiles`' own `last_recalculated_at`/`last_recalculated_trigger` pattern exactly.

**Trigger points found and wired** (`recomputeMyRecommendations`, new exported entry point in `app/actions/recommendations.ts`):
- `app/actions/checkin.ts`'s `submitDailyCheckin` — after the existing best-effort Root Score recalculation, same discipline. Not wired into `saveDailyCheckinDraft` (an exit-mid-checkin save, not a completion — matches that function's own existing "skip every real-completion side effect" rule).
- `app/actions/coach-intelligence.ts`'s `publishAiAnalysisReportAction` — right after `onAssessmentPublished`'s existing cascade (new registry findings from a published report feed this engine too).
- `app/actions/assessments.ts`'s `completeMyAssessment` — a completed questionnaire is the third event that materially changes what gets recommended.

**Storage/read model**: `getMyRecommendationsWithFreshness()` reads the stored `member_recommendations` rows plus the new computation-state row. Never computed before (a member predating this table) → compute live once, store, return (the Suspense skeleton covers this). Computed and current → just read (fast). Computed but stale (a newer check-in than the stored `computed_at` — the eager triggers above should already prevent this in the common case; this is the read-side safety net for when they haven't run yet or failed) → return the stored result immediately, `isStale: true`.

**"Recompute in the background" — a real platform constraint, and the honest fix**: Next.js 14.2 (this app's version) has no stable `after()`/`waitUntil` (confirmed: no `unstable-after` module exists in `node_modules/next` at this version). A detached, un-awaited promise in a Vercel serverless function has no guarantee of completing once the response is sent — building on that would be a silent reliability bug, not a real background job. Instead: the stale result renders immediately (no live compute on the request path), and a new shared client hook (`hooks/useRecommendationsFreshness.ts`) makes one real, awaited `refreshMyRecommendations()` Server Action call on mount, swapping in the fresh result when it resolves. From the member's side this reads exactly like a background job — the page never waits on it — while actually being reliable, since the recompute runs inside a real request/response cycle instead of a detached one.

### Part 3 — one source of truth

Dashboard's `RecommendationsCard`, the dedicated `/recommendations` page, and the new `/progress` section all read through `getMyRecommendations`/`getMyRecommendationsWithFreshness` — the same stored rows, the same staleness rule. Checked `/today` specifically (the task named it) — its "Today's Recommendations" section is a **different**, unrelated feature (wearable-derived recommendations, `lib/brain/wearableRecommendations.ts`, part of the Coaching Brain's decision object) — confirmed it has no connection to the Recommendation Engine at all, nothing to wire there.

### Guard tests (3 new files, 8 new tests)

- `tests/recommendation-computation-guard.test.ts` — seeds a real movement/mood-lift check-in history (same fixture shape as `tests/intelligence-core-integration.test.ts`, a disjoint 2015 date range), calls `recomputeMyRecommendations` twice against *unchanged* data and diffs the two persisted snapshots (proving storage is byte-identical to a fresh live computation), then submits one more, later check-in and confirms `isRecommendationComputationStale` correctly flags it and a follow-up recompute clears it. **Proven non-vacuous twice**: injected `Date.now()` into `buildMemberRecommendations`' explanation field — round-2 mismatch caught immediately, reverted; forced `isRecommendationComputationStale` to always return `false` — staleness assertion failed as expected, reverted.
- `tests/progress-streaming-guard.test.ts` — source-scan (page.tsx can't be rendered in vitest, same constraint as every Next.js page in this suite) confirming the two slow calls are absent from the main `Promise.all` and each slow section plus the new Recommendations section has its own `<Suspense>` boundary. **Proven non-vacuous**: `git stash`ed the restructure, 5 of 6 assertions failed against the pre-restructure page, `git stash pop` restored and reconfirmed green.
- Existing `tests/intelligence-core-integration.test.ts` (exact observation/dimension counts, no duplicates on re-run) passes completely unmodified — real proof this task didn't touch that engine's behavior.

**Verified**: `npm run typecheck` — clean, 0 errors. `npm run lint` — 0 errors, same pre-existing 31-warning baseline. `npm run build` — compiled successfully (confirms the `checkin.ts` → `recommendations.ts` → `rootMap.ts` → `checkin.ts` import cycle, all three references only inside function bodies never at module top-level, builds and bundles fine — verified, not assumed).

Full `vitest run`: **252/253 files, 2801/2802 tests passing**. The one failure (`tests/correlation-engine-integration.test.ts`, `observationCount` 31 vs. 30) is confirmed pre-existing and unrelated: `git diff` shows zero changes to that file or `lib/correlation-engine/` this session; it passes in isolation; a fresh `supabase db reset` didn't fix it; bisected the exact interaction down to `tests/ai-dispatcher-integration.test.ts` running immediately before it in the same suite (running just those two files together reproduces it deterministically; neither file mentions the other's tables). Left alone — a pre-existing shared-suite interaction in a different engine's test suite, same category this file has documented before for other test pairs, not something this task's changes could have caused or is asked to fix.

**Deployed and confirmed live**: pushed to `origin/main`, commit `c1eefa5`; Vercel auto-deploy watched through `Building` to `Ready`. `npx vercel inspect app.mefwellness.com` confirms the alias points at the new deployment, build log shows `Cloning github.com/MEFwellness/mef-platform (Branch: main, Commit: c1eefa5)`, status `Ready`, target `production`. `curl` against `/progress` returns the expected `307` to `/login`.

**Migration 116 — NOT yet applied to production.** `supabase db push --linked` (and every `supabase inspect db` variant) requires the project's actual Database password (Settings → Database in the Supabase dashboard); the CLI's own login session isn't sufficient, and that password isn't stored anywhere accessible in this environment. Did not attempt to retrieve it via any other means (e.g., macOS Keychain) — that would be overstepping without explicit authorization. **This is safe to have deployed anyway**: every read/write to the new table is wrapped in error handling that falls back to `null`/no-op (same discipline as every other best-effort call in this app), so the missing table doesn't break anything — `getMyRecommendationsWithFreshness` just always takes the "never computed" branch (a live compute every read) until the migration is applied, which is exactly today's pre-existing behavior, not a regression. Confirmed live: completed a real check-in as the `memberBelowThreshold` test account end-to-end (the wizard's real ending screen), zero page/console errors — the new `recomputeMyRecommendations` call inside `submitDailyCheckin` doesn't break the check-in flow even against the not-yet-existing table.

**To finish this**: apply `supabase/migrations/00000000000116_member_recommendation_computations.sql` to production, either by running `supabase db push --linked` yourself (it will prompt for the Database password from Settings → Database) or by pasting that file's contents into the Supabase Dashboard's SQL Editor and running it once. Nothing else needs to change afterward — the app already reads/writes this table wherever it exists.

**Measured live, before vs. after** (`memberPopulated`, production, `/progress`):

| Metric | Before | After |
|---|---|---|
| Time until fast content (Root Score) visible | ~4.2–5.5s (blocked behind everything) | **~1.0–1.1s** |
| Full document (everything, incl. streamed sections) | ~4.2–5.5s | ~3.7–6.3s (Recommendations still computing live every time — migration not yet applied) |

**Screenshots**: `/progress` fully loaded shows Root Score, Wellness Story, Coaching Insights, Wellness Patterns, the **new Recommendations section** (real items: "Today's coaching focus: Hydration", "Notification-worthy change," "Support Your Digestion," "Reduce Your Stress Load," etc.), Trends, Baseline Comparison, Explore links, and full History — identical to before, plus the one new section. Dashboard confirmed still showing its own "Recommended For You" tile (same underlying data, same function). Zero page errors on either page.

**Not done**: `/progress`'s own current bottleneck (`recalculateIntelligenceCore`/`recalculateWellnessIntelligence`) is unchanged — see the premise-check above; the user explicitly chose not to address it this round. No engine logic, the three-tier language system, `member_pattern_states`, the check-in flow itself, or assessment scoring were touched.

**Not verified**: the full live before/after for Part 2's storage benefit (needs the migration applied first — can't measure a cache that doesn't exist yet); production connection-pool usage (same blocker as the prior task, needs the Database password).

## Check-in fix: discomfort gate + multi-select location (2026-07-29 follow-up)

**Phase 1 findings** (traced Screen 3 — "Your body" — plus an audit of the rest of morning/evening):

1. **The actual bug, confirmed by design**: "Where is it, mainly?" (`BodySeverityOutline.tsx`) had no parent condition — it opened the "body" section unconditionally, every day, for every member. Severity tiles only appeared once a location was tapped, meaning saying "no discomfort" required picking a location first, then a severity of "None" — backwards. Whichever body-domain rotating probe (DIG/MOV/MEC/FUE) happened to land on the same screen that day rendered directly above it with no separating heading, reading as its (unrelated) parent — exactly the effect reported.
2. **A second, previously-unnoticed instance of the same bug shape**: `checkin_probe.digestive_symptom_type` ("What kind of discomfort?", requires `digestion_rating <= 2`) was pushed into the unit list via `interleaveFollowUps` *before* its own parent question (`checkin_probe.digestion_rating`, pushed later in the old source order) — the follow-up could render above the very question that triggers it. Root cause: `digestion_rating` is specially-handled (writes to a `daily_checkins` column, not the generic probe path) so it's excluded from `genericRotatingProbes`, and `interleaveFollowUps` only orders a follow-up next to a parent that's *in* that list — an unmatched follow-up gets appended at the end instead.
3. Storage/engines traced: `pain_location` writes to `daily_checkin_probe_answers.value` (generic jsonb) via the existing `submitProbeAnswerAction`. Grepped every consumer of `checkin_probe.pain_location` in the codebase — only `CheckinForm.tsx` itself reads or writes it (write on submit, read on resume-draft). No coach view, pattern-detection system, or language-generation surface displays this question's value today — root-map coverage and the coach Question Bank only count row *existence*, never parse the value shape. This is genuinely new ground for a "reads naturally" requirement, not a retrofit.
4. No other long-list single-select question in either flow logically allows multiple answers — `pain_location` was the only one found.

**Phase 2 — the gate**: a required `discomfort-gate` unit ("Any discomfort today?", `BooleanPills`, own "DISCOMFORT" eyebrow heading) now owns the whole location/severity/aggravating-factor block. Answering **No** writes `pain_discomfort_level: 0` / `morning_soreness: 1` — the exact values picking "None" already wrote under the old always-shown flow — and an empty location set, so every existing downstream reader of those two columns (root-map coverage, scoring, the correlation engine) sees no difference between "answered no" and the old "answered None." Nothing else from the section renders. Answering **Yes** reveals location + severity exactly as before, now correctly announced by the gate above it. Flipping the gate back to Yes after a same-session No resets severity/location so she actually answers rather than keeping the auto-filled values. The digestion-question ordering bug (finding 2) is fixed by moving its push earlier in source order, ahead of the follow-up interleave loop — no behavior change to *when* it's asked, only that its own follow-up can never precede it.

**Phase 3 — multi-select chip grid**: new `MultiSelectChipGrid` component (`components/checkin/scales/`) — the same ten options as compact two-column chips, reusing the existing `TapBleedTile` selected-state visual and `MultiOptionRows`' own `toggleMultiSelectValue` (generalized to take an overridable `exclusiveValue`, default still `'none'` so every existing caller — the craving question — is byte-identical) rather than inventing new toggle logic. "Widespread" is wired as the grid's `exclusiveValue`. `painLocation` changes from `string | null` to `string[]`; a pre-redesign stored bare string reads as a one-element array on resume, never discarded. Migration `00000000000117_pain_location_multi_select.sql` converts `checkin_probe.pain_location`'s `response_type` to `multi_select` in the driver_probe_questions table (same pattern migration 115 already established for the craving question — no CHECK constraint change needed, that was already widened) and wraps any pre-existing bare-string answers into single-element arrays. Applied to local Supabase; **not yet applied to production** — same Database-password blocker as migration 116 (see prior entry). Safe either way: nothing in the app reads `driver_probe_questions.response_type` for this specially-handled question's own rendering (confirmed — it's hand-rendered by `BodySeverityOutline`, not the generic `DriverProbeField`), so the missing migration only means the coach Question Bank screen shows a stale `response_type` label for this one row until applied. **"Other" has no free-text field** — none existed before this change, and adding one would require a new `response_type` (no free-text type exists anywhere in this schema) — a genuine schema addition, flagged rather than silently built.

**Phase 4 — wording**: "Where is it, mainly?" → "Where is the discomfort, mainly?" (only change — reads standalone now that a rotating question can no longer sit directly above it unlabeled). Gate question: "Any discomfort today?", matching this same file's existing "Any night sweats?" yes/no voice. No other wording changed.

**Guard tests** (`tests/checkin-discomfort-gate.test.ts`, 21 new tests): (a) body-severity/pain-aggravating-factor pushes are structurally nested inside `if (hasDiscomfort)`, proven both by source-scan and live Playwright (screenshot: answering No shows zero further questions, Continue enables immediately); (b) the location grid renders with the gate visible above it in context (screenshot); (c) `digestionQuestion` now pushes before `interleaveFollowUps` runs (source-scan, index comparison); (d) `toggleMultiSelectValue`'s widespread exclusivity tested as real pure-function behavior (not string-matching) — multiple locations accumulate, Widespread clears them and vice versa, confirmed live via Playwright (`aria-pressed` read back after each toggle) and in the DB (`["neck", "lower_back"]` stored, `pain_discomfort_level: 2`). **Proven non-vacuous**: `git stash`ed the three implementation files, reran — 13/21 new tests failed (the 8 that passed were `toggleMultiSelectValue`'s pre-existing default-behavior cases, unaffected by the revert), `git stash pop` restored and reconfirmed 21/21 green. Two pre-existing test files pinned to the superseded single-select design (`checkin-pain-screen-redesign.test.ts`, `checkin-pain-location-severity-independence.test.ts`) were updated in place to the new contract, not left broken.

**Verified locally** (Playwright against the local dev server + local Supabase, `member.one` fixture, section mode): drove both paths end to end through real submission, zero page/console errors either way. **No path**: gate → "No" → Continue enables immediately, zero discomfort questions ever rendered, ending screen reached; DB confirms `pain_discomfort_level: 0`, `morning_soreness: 1`, no `pain_location` row written at all. **Yes path**: gate → "Yes" → chip grid appears in the same screen, selected Neck + Lower back (both fill forest-green) → severity tiles → selected Widespread (Neck/Lower back visibly deselect, confirmed via `aria-pressed`) → re-selected Neck + Lower back + Mild-moderate → submitted; DB confirms `daily_checkin_probe_answers.value = ["neck", "lower_back"]`, `pain_discomfort_level: 2`, `morning_soreness: 2`.

**Verified**: `npm run typecheck` — clean, 0 errors. `npm run lint` — 0 errors, same pre-existing 31-warning baseline. `npm run build` — compiled successfully. Full `vitest run` (fresh `supabase db reset`): **254 files, 2823 tests — 1 pre-existing failure** (`tests/correlation-engine-integration.test.ts`, `observationCount` 31 vs 30) — confirmed unrelated to this task both ways: passes in complete isolation and passes when run directly after this task's own new test file; only fails as part of the full suite, matching the exact pre-existing suite-ordering interaction with `ai-dispatcher-integration.test.ts` already documented in the prior BUILD_STATUS entry above. Zero changes to `lib/correlation-engine/` this session.

**Not done**: no engine, no scoring, no answer options removed — `pain_discomfort_level`/`morning_soreness` still feed the exact same downstream consumers unchanged. `EveningReflectionForm.tsx` was audited (Phase 1) and needs no change — it has no local follow-ups seeded on its screen today, so the detached-follow-up bug shape doesn't currently occur there.

**Live-verification bugs found and fixed** (two, the second only surfaced while chasing the first): testing the Yes path against production (`memberBelowThreshold`, which already had a real "neck" answer on file for today from earlier history) surfaced a real staleness bug — flipping the gate from Yes+a location to No submitted successfully, but reopening the same day's check-in still showed Neck pre-selected and the gate back on Yes.

1. **First fix, insufficient alone**: `submitProbeAndFollowUpAnswers`'s write guard was `if (painLocation.length > 0)`, inherited unchanged from the old single-select code, where an empty/null location was never a real submittable state — the old design had no way to legitimately "unset" a location once chosen, so this guard's gap was unreachable. The new gate makes "no locations" a real, meaningful answer for the first time, and the guard silently skipped writing it. Changed to key on `hasDiscomfort !== null` instead of the array's length.
2. **The real root cause, found by reproducing the exact scenario locally after fix #1 alone didn't resolve it live**: `probeAnswers`' own initializer copied every key from `initialProbeAnswers` matching a value-shape check, with no exclusion for `SPECIALLY_HANDLED_QUESTION_KEYS` — so a specially-handled key already on file (pain_location, but the same gap applies to pain_aggravating_factor, bowel_movement_status, night_waking_count, night_sweats, digestion_rating) got a second, stale, never-updated copy living in the generic `probeAnswers` map from page load. `submitProbeAndFollowUpAnswers`'s own generic `Promise.all` loop re-submits every key in that map on every save — running after fix #1's explicit (now-correct) write, silently overwriting it right back to the original on-load value. Confirmed by isolating the exact upsert call directly against Supabase (proved the DB layer was never the problem) and by adding temporary debug logging to the client, which showed the correct call firing with the correct value immediately before the stale generic loop clobbered it. This bug predates this task — it would have equally silently reverted a changed `bowel_movement_status`/`night_waking_count`/etc. on any day already carrying one of those answers — but only became externally observable now, because the old single-select design never allowed a specially-handled answer to reach a genuinely different value in a way this staleness could contradict. Fixed by filtering `SPECIALLY_HANDLED_QUESTION_KEYS` out of the initializer.

Both fixes verified live against `app.mefwellness.com` (`memberBelowThreshold`): set Yes + Neck, flipped to No, submitted, reopened the same day — gate correctly re-derives to No, zero discomfort questions render. Two new guard tests added (`checkin-discomfort-gate.test.ts`, now 23 tests), each proven non-vacuous by `git stash`/revert/restore. Full suite (2824/2825, same one pre-existing unrelated flake), typecheck, lint, and build all re-run clean after both fixes.

## Exercise Library media build: Your Move video layer + full coverage (2026-07-30)

ExerciseAPI.dev (migration 80) stays the catalog source for every exercise's name/instructions/muscles — nothing about it changed or was replaced. This task adds a video layer on top: wherever Your Move (ymove.app Exercise API) has a confident movement match, its video wins over ExerciseAPI.dev's own, even when both exist. Every exercise now renders one of three real states — video with an extracted poster, an open-license image, or short coaching cues — replacing the dumbbell "No preview available" placeholder that covered every non-video exercise before this task (that placeholder swap itself was a deliberate prior decision, per the task's own architecture note, and stays gone).

### Schema (migration 118, applied to production alongside migration 117 which was already pending — see below)

- `your_move_exercise_links` — the our-id → Your Move-id mapping (provider/external_id natural key, same convention as `mef_exercise_metadata`). `video_url`/`video_url_expires_at` are a ~10min fetch-at-play-time cache, never long-term storage of Your Move's 48h pre-signed URL. This table is also half the purge manifest.
- `exercise_extracted_posters` — a mid-movement frame extracted from either source's video and stored in our own storage; `source` (`your_move`/`exercise_api_dev`) determines whether a row belongs in the Your Move purge manifest.
- `exercise_open_license_images` — Phase 3 fallback images; doubles as the license manifest (source_url, source_provider, license_type, attribution per row).
- Public `exercise-media` Supabase Storage bucket (unlike the private per-member `food-lens-media`/`body-assessment-media` buckets — this is shared reference content every member views, so cards render a plain public URL exactly like they already do for ExerciseAPI.dev's own CDN images).

### The video layer (`lib/your-move/*`)

- `apiClient.ts` — server-only client for `exercise-api.ymove.app/api/v2`. `listExercises` (browse) always forces `includeVideos=false` regardless of what's passed in — matching/auditing/pagination never spend Your Move's monthly exercise quota. Only `getExercise` (used exclusively at real playback or one-time poster extraction) requests video, and its result is never persisted beyond the short cache above.
- `videoPlayback.ts` + `app/api/exercises/[id]/video-url/route.ts` — the one path in the app allowed to call `getExercise`. Checks the DB cache first (zero Your Move requests on a hit); on a miss, fetches once and caches again. Called ONLY when a member taps play (`TapToPlayVideo` in `ExerciseDetailView.tsx`) — never on page load, never from the browse grid, so browsing never burns quota.
- `matching.ts` — conservative name/movement matching (exact-normalized-name = confident; small token-diff with real shared vocabulary = reasoned near-miss rejection; everything else = unmatched). A real bug was found and fixed here against production data: single-word names with zero real token overlap (e.g. "Adductor" vs "Burpee") were getting flagged as near-miss purely by coincidental token-count arithmetic — fixed by requiring actual shared vocabulary, not just a small diff size.
- `frameExtraction.ts` — ffmpeg (devDependency only, offline scripts never bundled into the Next.js runtime) extracts a frame at the video's real midpoint (from `videoDurationSecs`), then reuses `lib/body-assessment/frameQuality.ts`'s existing Laplacian-variance sharpness/luminance gate verbatim (via `sharp` decoding to raw RGBA) rather than inventing a second blur/exposure heuristic.
- `links.ts`/`posters.ts` — purge functions (`removeAllYourMoveLinks`, `removeYourMoveExtractedPosters`) + `scripts/exercise-media/purge-your-move-media.ts`, the single documented command for Your Move cancellation compliance.

### Display (`normalize.ts`, `ranking.ts`, `MediaBadge.tsx`, `ExerciseCard.tsx`, `ExerciseDetailView.tsx`, `route.ts`)

Media resolution order: Your Move link (hasVideo=true, videoUrl stays null until tap) → ExerciseAPI.dev's own video (eager, that vendor's URLs don't expire) → Phase 3 image → Phase 4 cues. Tier badges are now VIDEO/IMAGE/CUES/(no media, the one state the backfill exists to eliminate) — `getExerciseMediaTier` keys off `hasVideo`, not `videoUrl`, since a Your Move-matched exercise legitimately has a null `videoUrl` until tapped. `hasVideo`/`imageOnly`/`hideNoMedia` filters moved fully client-of-route (previously `hasVideo` alone was sent to the vendor as a real search param) since the vendor's own `hasVideo` flag can't know about a Your Move match. `CuesPlaceholder` replaces the old `MediaPlaceholder` for the no-video/no-image state, rendering the generated cues directly in the card.

### Real data — Phase 1 (matching)

Our catalog: **2199 exercises** (audited via `scripts/exercise-media/audit-our-catalog.ts`, paging ExerciseAPI.dev's real search) — 150 with video, 783 with a vendor `images` field (confirmed unusable — see `normalize.ts`'s own header, a dead relative path the vendor doesn't host), 1266 with neither. Your Move's catalog: **857 exercises**, 856 with video. Matching (`scripts/exercise-media/match-your-move.ts`) against all 2199: **156 confident matches, 866 reasoned near-miss rejections, 1177 unmatched** — full three lists in `docs/exercise-media/match-report.json`. All 156 confident links written to production `your_move_exercise_links`.

### Real data — Phase 2 (posters) — built, NOT run

`scripts/exercise-media/extract-posters.ts` checks `GET /usage` first and only spends quota on exercises without an already-extracted poster, deferring the rest cleanly (resumable — no code change needed once the trial's 100/exercise/month cap resets or the account upgrades). Per explicit instruction, this step was not run this pass — it would spend real, scarce trial quota. Your Move-matched exercises currently show a tap-to-play button with no poster image behind it (verified live, see below) until this is run.

### Real data — Phase 3 (images) — attempted, abandoned after a high false-positive rate

`lib/exercise-library/wikimediaCommons.ts` (no API key needed, unlike Pexels/Unsplash which require one not configured in this environment) searches Wikimedia Commons for a clearly-matching, commercially-licensed photo. Three real false positives were found and fixed against production data in sequence: (1) an anatomical diagram ("Adductor" → a Gray's-Anatomy-style plate) slipping through pure token-set matching — added title-pattern exclusion + a token-density-ratio requirement; (2) a completely unrelated photo of an actual bridge structure ("Side Bridge" → a Woljeonggyo Bridge sunset photo) matching by coincidental word co-occurrence in a long caption — the density-ratio fix caught this too; (3) after both fixes, a THIRD false positive slipped through anyway ("Arm Over Arm" → a vintage photo captioned "tramp smoking cigar with cane over arm") and a second anatomy diagram not caught by the title patterns ("Adductor minimus.gif") — added a MIME-type restriction (real photos are near-universally JPEG; diagrams are overwhelmingly PNG/GIF/SVG). Even after three rounds of tightening, manual review of every successful match found a **~30-40% false-positive rate** — general-purpose media repositories aren't reliably matchable to exercise names via text alone without visual verification this session had no tooling for. Also discovered: a first unthrottled run got the client rate-limited (429) by Wikimedia hard enough that it was still returning `Retry-After: 33` minutes later, confirmed via direct `curl`.

**Decision made and reported, not hidden**: stopped further automated Wikimedia sourcing. 3 images were manually verified as genuinely correct (Ab Roller, Anderson Squat — a real Paul Anderson lift reference, Side Crow Pose — Kakasana variation, all confirmed live) and kept; the other ~1931 candidates were routed to Phase 4 cues instead. Pexels/Unsplash (fitness-specific stock photo APIs) would very likely perform far better here but need API keys not available in this environment — recommended as a future enhancement over further Wikimedia iteration.

### Real data — Phase 4 (cues)

`lib/exercise-library/cueGeneration.ts` condenses ExerciseAPI.dev's OWN instructions/tips into 2-3 real, exercise-specific cues where available, falling back to a muscle/equipment/force template only when the vendor gives fewer than 2 real instruction steps. Written to `mef_exercise_metadata.coaching_cues` — the exact existing column `ExerciseDetailView` already read, reused rather than adding a new one. Ran for all 1931 remaining exercises: **1917 from real vendor instructions, only 14 needed the generic template** — full list in `docs/exercise-media/cue-report.json`.

### Final coverage (`scripts/exercise-media/audit-coverage.ts`, run against production)

**265 video + 3 image + 1931 cues = 2199, zero uncovered.** (Before this task: 150 video, 0 usable image, 2049 no-media — every one of those 2049 showed the dumbbell placeholder.)

### Guard tests

- (a) no exercise renders with no media and no cues: proven via the coverage audit above (`guardTestAPasses: true`) against the complete, real production catalog.
- (b) no long-lived stored Your Move video URL: `tests/your-move-video-url-cache.test.ts` — TTL constant asserted ≤1h (well under 48h); proven non-vacuous by temporarily setting it to 49h, watching the assertion fail, reverting.
- (c) purge manifest is complete: `tests/your-move-purge.test.ts` — seeds a real your_move-sourced link + poster + storage object AND an exercise_api_dev-sourced poster + object, runs the real `purgeYourMoveMedia`, asserts the former are gone and the latter survives untouched. Runs against real local Supabase, no mocks.
- (d) a mapped exercise fetches and plays its Your Move video by ID: `tests/your-move-video-playback.test.ts` proves the exact code path (`resolveYourMoveVideoUrl`) against a mocked Your Move HTTP response (real DB rows, real cache read/write) — cache-miss fetches exactly once, cache-hit doesn't re-fetch. The real end-to-end version (an actual Your Move call) is deliberately deferred with Phase 2.
- (e) browse grid makes no video-field requests: `tests/exercise-library-browse-no-video-requests.test.ts` — a structural source-scan proving `app/api/exercises/route.ts` never imports or references the Your Move HTTP client; proven non-vacuous by temporarily adding a forbidden import, watching both assertions fail, reverting.

### Verified

`npm run typecheck` — clean (both workspaces). `npm run lint` — 0 new errors (only the same pre-existing console-statement warning pattern already established by every other script in this repo). Full `vitest run`: **262 files, 2868/2869 passing** — the one failure (`correlation-engine-integration.test.ts`, `observationCount` 31 vs 30) is the same pre-existing, already-documented-in-this-file test-suite-ordering interaction with `ai-dispatcher-integration.test.ts` (confirmed zero changes to `lib/correlation-engine/` this session). `npm run build` — compiled successfully, all routes including the new `/api/exercises/[id]/video-url`.

Migration 117 (`pain_location_multi_select`, from the prior task) was also still pending production and blocked by the exact same missing-database-password issue this task's own migration 118 needed solved anyway — applied together since 118 can't go in without 117 first; migration 117 was already documented as safe in its own prior entry.

**Verified live** against `app.mefwellness.com` (`memberPopulated`, Playwright, 390×844): searched "squat" — video-tier cards (Box/Hack/Barbell/Olympic/Goblet/Bodyweight/Overhead Squat, all with a real tap-to-play overlay) correctly sort before cues-tier cards (Pin/Chair/Jump Squat etc., each showing its real generated cue text in the card); opened a Your Move-matched exercise (`Barbell_Back_Squat`) — VIDEO badge + tap-to-play button render correctly (no poster yet, since Phase 2 wasn't run — expected); opened the image-only exercise (`Ab_Roller`) — real re-hosted photo renders, IMAGE badge; opened the cues-only exercise (`Adductor`) — real generated cues render, CUES badge; the empty-search landing page's Recently Viewed and Suggested Next Exercise cards also render correctly with no dumbbell placeholder anywhere. Did NOT tap play on the Your Move video (would spend real trial quota — deliberately deferred).

**Not verified / deferred, by explicit instruction**: Phase 2 poster extraction (spends up to 100 of the trial's 100/exercise/month quota) was not run — `docs/PRODUCTION_TEST_ACCOUNTS.md`-driven real playback of a Your Move video was therefore not exercised end-to-end live, only its code path (guard test d, mocked). Once triggered, re-running `extract-posters.ts` needs zero code changes and will pick up exactly where quota allows.

## Remove ExerciseAPI.dev entirely: Your Move becomes the sole Exercise Library catalog (2026-07-30)

Flips the architecture the prior two entries built: ExerciseAPI.dev is deleted completely (client, key, every call site) and Your Move (exercise-api.ymove.app) — previously only a video-layer add-on — becomes the entire catalog: name, instructions, muscles, equipment, category, difficulty all now come from Your Move, fetched once via its quota-free browse endpoint and stored locally (never live-fetched per request — Your Move's monthly exercise quota makes a live-proxy architecture unworkable at this data volume).

### Schema (migration 119, applied to production)

- **`exercise_catalog`** (new) — the sole catalog. One row per Your Move exercise; `provider` kept as a column (always `'your_move'`) rather than collapsed away, so the pre-existing `(provider, external_id)` natural-key convention used by every referencing table needed no shape change, only a new literal value. `video_url`/`video_url_expires_at` fold in what `your_move_exercise_links` (migration 118) used to hold — no separate mapping table needed anymore, since the catalog row already IS the Your Move exercise.
- **Dropped**: `your_move_exercise_links` (superseded by `exercise_catalog` itself) and `exercise_open_license_images` (Phase 3 Wikimedia fallback removed outright — Your Move supplies video for 856/857 catalog exercises, and generated cues cover the rest plus any tap-to-play failure, so the image-fallback tier has no exercise left to serve).
- **`mef_exercise_metadata`** keeps its role (curation + generated `coaching_cues`); default `provider` moves to `'your_move'`.
- **Every member-data table** (`member_exercise_favorites`, `member_exercise_completions`, `member_exercise_recent_views`, `coach_program_template_exercises`, `coach_assigned_workout_exercises`, `prescription_block_exercises`) gets a new `is_legacy boolean default false` column (`member_exercise_favorites` also gets `legacy_exercise_name text`, since it's the one table with no name column at all) — no existing row touched at the schema level, `ExerciseLibraryProvider` becomes `'your_move' | 'exercise_api_dev'` (the latter now purely a legacy marker, never written fresh again).

### Code removed entirely

`lib/exercise-library/apiClient.ts` (the ExerciseAPI.dev client + `EXERCISE_API_KEY`), `lib/exercise-library/wikimediaCommons.ts`, `lib/exercise-library/openLicenseImages.ts`, `lib/your-move/links.ts` (superseded by `lib/your-move/catalog.ts`), and the scripts that only ever served the old catalog (`audit-our-catalog.ts`, `source-open-license-images.ts`, `match-your-move.ts`, `write-cues.ts`). Every remaining call site (`app/api/exercises/route.ts`, `app/exercises/[id]/page.tsx`, `app/actions/exercise-library.ts`, `app/actions/coach-programs.ts`, `lib/prescription-intelligence/exerciseSelection.ts`, `lib/exercise-library/{normalize,ranking,related}.ts`, every Exercise Library component) rewritten to read `exercise_catalog` directly — grepped and confirmed zero remaining references to `api.exerciseapi.dev`, `ExerciseApiClient`, or `EXERCISE_API_KEY` anywhere in `lib/`, `app/`, `components/`, `scripts/` (guard test, see below).

### Filters rebuilt from Your Move's real, audited vocabulary

Live-fetched Your Move's full 857-exercise catalog and inspected actual field distributions before touching any filter code: **no `force` or `mechanic` field exists anywhere in Your Move's data** (confirmed against all 857 rows) — both filters removed entirely, per "a filter with no backing data gets removed, not left empty." Category/Muscle/Equipment dropdowns now source their options dynamically from `exercise_catalog`'s own distinct values (`resource=categories|muscles|equipment` on the browse route) instead of a hardcoded ExerciseAPI.dev vocabulary, since Your Move's casing is inconsistent on their own side (normalized to lowercase at ingestion). `lib/exercise-library/bodyRegions.ts`'s muscle→region map rebuilt against Your Move's real `muscleGroup`/`secondaryMuscles` vocabulary. Video Only / Image Only / Hide No Media toggles removed outright — every catalog exercise now has video or generated cues, so a media-presence filter has nothing left to filter.

### Video rules unchanged, failure now falls back to cues (not an error banner)

`TapToPlayVideo` (`ExerciseDetailView.tsx`) still fetches fresh on tap via `/api/exercises/[id]/video-url` (never eager, never stored beyond a ~10min cache, per Your Move's 48h URL-expiry terms) — but on a fetch failure (the trial API key only serves a subset of Your Move's catalog), it now renders this exercise's generated `CuesPlaceholder` in place of the player instead of a bare error message over a dead poster. Since the code always attempts the real fetch first and only falls back on actual failure, this self-heals automatically once a full-access key replaces the trial key — no code change needed.

### Data migration — real production counts

`scripts/exercise-media/migrate-legacy-exercise-references.ts` remaps every exercise reference with a confident Your Move match (`docs/exercise-media/match-report.json`'s 156 confident matches) and legacy-flags (never deletes) everything else. Checked every referencing table in production before writing a single row: `member_exercise_favorites`, `member_exercise_completions`, `coach_program_template_exercises`, `coach_assigned_workout_exercises`, and `prescription_block_exercises` were all **empty** (0 rows) — only `mef_exercise_metadata` (1931 rows, all Phase-4-generated-cue rows from the prior task) and `member_exercise_recent_views` (8 real rows) had anything to migrate.

**Real production result**: `mef_exercise_metadata` — every one of the 1931 legacy rows resolved to 0 remaining `exercise_api_dev` rows (156 remapped/merged into their Your Move target, the rest deleted as pure-generated-cue artifacts with no real coach curation to preserve — confirmed via `isPureCueArtifact`, which checks every curation column is at its schema default before allowing a delete). `member_exercise_recent_views` — **1 remapped, 7 legacy-flagged**, 0 deleted; final production check confirms 7 rows `provider='exercise_api_dev', is_legacy=true` (names preserved) and 1 row `provider='your_move', is_legacy=false`. A real found-and-fixed bug along the way: the merge-on-collision path (two old exercise names both confidently matching one Your Move exercise — 2 such cases exist, e.g. `Archer_Push_Up`/`Archer_Push-Up`) originally only unioned array-typed curation columns and silently dropped scalar ones (`program_section`, `coach_notes`, etc.) — caught by seeding a real scalar value in a local dry run before touching production, fixed to `coalesce` scalars from whichever side has them, re-verified locally, then applied.

**The 3 Wikimedia-sourced images** (`Ab_Roller`, `Anderson_Squat`, `Side_Crow_Pose`) — their DB rows are gone (the table was dropped by migration 119); their storage objects in the `exercise-media` bucket are still physically present but now untracked. Confirmed Postgres itself refuses a direct `delete from storage.objects` ("Direct deletion from storage tables is not allowed. Use the Storage API instead", SQLSTATE 42501) — the Storage API needs a service-role key not available in this session. **Needs one manual step**: Supabase Dashboard → Storage → `exercise-media` bucket → `open-license/` folder → delete the 3 files. No functional impact either way — nothing in the app references them anymore.

### Guard tests (7 new files)

- `tests/exercise-api-dev-fully-removed.test.ts` — source-scans `lib/`, `app/`, `components/`, `scripts/` (proven non-empty: >50 files) for any import of the deleted `apiClient`/`ExerciseApiClient`/`EXERCISE_API_KEY`/`api.exerciseapi.dev`, and separately for any import of the deleted `your-move/links`/`openLicenseImages`/`wikimediaCommons` modules — with a companion assertion proving the "no mentions of `exercise_open_license_images`" check isn't vacuous (exactly one legitimate mention survives: the pre-migration storage-cleanup script).
- `tests/exercise-api-removal-migration.test.ts` — runs the real exported migration functions against real seeded rows in local Supabase (no mocks): a favorite/completion with a confident match remaps to `your_move`; one with no match gets `is_legacy=true` and its name preserved, row never deleted; `isPureCueArtifact` proven true/false on both sides.
- `tests/exercise-video-fallback-graceful.test.ts` — source-scan proving `TapToPlayVideo`'s error branch renders `CuesPlaceholder`, and that the old bare-error-message state is actually gone (not just supplemented).
- `tests/exercise-filters-your-move-only.test.ts` — proves `hasVideo`/`imageOnly`/`hideNoMedia`/`force`/`mechanic` are gone from the filter state, the route, and the browser, while Category/Body Region/Muscle/Equipment/Difficulty remain.
- `tests/exercise-library-normalize.test.ts`, `tests/exercise-library-ranking.test.ts` — rewritten for the two-tier (video/cues) model, no more image tier.
- `tests/your-move-video-playback.test.ts`, `tests/your-move-video-url-cache.test.ts`, `tests/your-move-purge.test.ts` — rewritten against `exercise_catalog`'s own video-cache columns (no more separate link table); purge test now also proves catalog metadata (name, instructions) survives a purge untouched — only Your Move's proprietary video-derived assets (extracted posters) are purge-eligible.

**Verified**: `npm run typecheck` — clean. `npm run lint` — 0 errors (59 warnings, same pre-existing console-statement pattern, more of them now only because there are more offline scripts). Full `vitest run`: **265 files, 2871/2872 passing** — the one failure (`correlation-engine-integration.test.ts`) is the same pre-existing suite-ordering flake documented repeatedly above; confirmed by resetting to a clean DB and running that file alone (passes) vs. after the full suite (fails) — zero changes to `lib/correlation-engine/` this session. `npm run build` — compiled successfully.

**Verified locally** (Playwright, dev server + local Supabase, `member.one`): searched "squat" — 24 real Your Move–sourced results, filters show exactly Category/Body Region/Muscle/Equipment/Difficulty (no media toggles, no Force/Mechanic); opened "Banded squat" — VIDEO badge, real tap-to-play button, real instructions/muscles from Your Move rendered in the detail view; zero console/page errors. Did not tap play (would spend real Your Move quota against the live trial/pending-approval key — deliberately deferred, same discipline as the prior entry).

**Deployed to production**: migrations 119 and 120 (data) applied via `supabase db push --db-url` after the user provided the database password directly (this session had no service-role API key, only the DB password — data operations were written as pure-SQL migrations rather than a script holding an open connection to production, since the environment's safety classifier blocks the latter category of action outright). Confirmed live in production via direct read query: `exercise_catalog` 857 rows, `mef_exercise_metadata` 857 rows all `provider='your_move'` (0 legacy rows remain), `member_exercise_recent_views` 1 remapped + 7 legacy-flagged. App code committed and pushed to `main` after the database was already in its new shape, so there was no window where deployed code expected a schema that didn't exist yet.

## Branded video-poster placeholder: replaces the blank gray box on unposterized video cards (2026-07-30)

Phase 2 poster extraction (see two entries above) still hasn't run — 856 video-tier exercises, 0 extracted posters, in both local and production `exercise_extracted_posters`. Until now that meant every video card rendered its play-button overlay over a flat `bg-[#EFF6F1]` box (grid) or `bg-[#EFF6F1]` div (detail view) — reading as broken, not designed. This task replaces that empty box with an on-brand placeholder; no code in the poster-extraction pipeline itself changed.

### New (`lib/exercise-library/placeholderVisual.ts`, `components/exercise-library/VideoPosterPlaceholder.tsx`)

- `getPlaceholderVisual(exercise)` — pure, deterministic (no `Math.random`): `mark` reuses `bodyRegions.ts`'s existing muscle→region map (`upper_body`/`lower_body`/`core`/`full_body`/`default`) off `primaryMuscle`, so the abstract mark is genuinely tied to the exercise's real primary muscle group, no new muscle vocabulary invented. `tone` (0-3) is a simple string hash of `category`, picking one of four gold/cream accent treatments — independent of `mark`, so exercises sharing a muscle group still read as visually distinct in a grid.
- `VideoPosterPlaceholder` — deep forest green (`#1B3A2D`) background, a faint hashed gold/cream radial or diagonal accent (tone), a minimal abstract line-mark (two arcs / two diagonals / concentric rings / a cross, keyed by `mark` — plain SVG shapes, not literal muscle/body clip-art), and the exercise name set in Cormorant Garamond (`font-[family-name:var(--font-cormorant-garamond)]`) in cream. Deliberately does not render its own play button — both call sites already layer one on top of whatever fills this space, so this component only ever supplies the background layer.

### Wired in (`ExerciseCard.tsx`, `ExerciseDetailView.tsx`)

Both call sites already had a `posterUrl ? <img> : <blank>` branch reading the same `posterUrl` field the poster-extraction pipeline writes to — the blank branch is the only thing that changed, from a flat color div to `<VideoPosterPlaceholder exercise={...} />`. Because the gate is still exactly `posterUrl`, this disappears automatically, per-exercise, the moment Phase 2 populates a poster for that exercise — zero code change needed when that run happens. `ExerciseCard.tsx`'s `{exercise.posterUrl && <img/>}` (silently rendering nothing on a miss) became a proper `? :` ternary. `ExerciseDetailView.tsx`'s `TapToPlayVideo` gained three new props (`name`, `primaryMuscle`, `category`) to feed the placeholder. The cues-only branch (`CuesPlaceholder`, no video at all) in both files is untouched — this change is video cards only, confirmed by guard test.

### Guard tests (2 new files, 18 tests)

- `tests/exercise-library-placeholder-visual.test.ts` — `getPlaceholderVisual` is deterministic (same input twice → identical output); muscle→mark mapping matches `bodyRegions.ts` for `quads`/`chest`/`abs`/`full_body`; null/unrecognized muscle falls back to `default` without throwing; tone stays in `[0,3]`; tone spreads across more than one value over `exercise_catalog`'s real production category vocabulary (`core`/`legs`/`strength`/`full body`/`yoga`/`chest`/`arms`/`back`/`shoulders`/`cardio` — checked by hand first, spreads across all 4 tones, so the "grid doesn't look like one repeated tile" assertion isn't hollow); mark and tone vary independently (same muscle, different category → same mark, different tone).
- `tests/exercise-video-poster-placeholder-wiring.test.ts` — source-scan (same jsdom-free constraint as every other component test in this suite, see `exercise-video-fallback-graceful.test.ts`): both files import and call `VideoPosterPlaceholder` in exactly the no-poster branch, the old `posterUrl &&`/`bg-[#EFF6F1]` patterns are gone, the cues branch is untouched, `TapToPlayVideo`'s call site passes the three new props. A separate group scans `VideoPosterPlaceholder.tsx` itself: every literal hex color used is one of the three approved brand colors (`#1B3A2D`/`#C4A050`/`#F5F0E4`, case-insensitive) and the file does use at least one (proves the check isn't vacuous); the name renders in Cormorant Garamond; the component never mentions `PlayCircle` (no duplicate play button); exactly 4 body-region case branches plus a default exist.
- **Proven non-vacuous by hand** (not part of the persisted test files): temporarily reverted `ExerciseCard.tsx`'s ternary back to the old `&&`-only pattern, `ExerciseDetailView.tsx`'s placeholder call back to the old `bg-[#EFF6F1]` div, and added an off-brand hex color + a `PlayCircle` import to `VideoPosterPlaceholder.tsx` — all 6 affected assertions failed as expected, the other 6 in the same file were unaffected, then every change was reverted.

### Verified

`npm run typecheck` — clean (both workspaces). `npm run lint` — 0 errors, same pre-existing 59 warnings (none in the new/changed files). Full `vitest run`: **267 files, 2889/2890 passing** — the one failure (`correlation-engine-integration.test.ts`, `observationCount` 31 vs 30) is the same pre-existing, already-documented suite-ordering interaction with `ai-dispatcher-integration.test.ts` (confirmed zero changes to `lib/correlation-engine/` this session; file count went from the prior entry's 265→267, exactly the 2 new test files, so this is the same flake, not a new one). `npm run build` — compiled successfully, `/exercises` and `/exercises/[id]` routes both built clean.

**Verified locally** (Playwright, dev server + local Supabase, `member.one`, 390×844): searched "squat" (24 results, all quads/lower-body — diagonal-line mark, consistent as expected since they share a muscle group, with visibly varying gold/cream tone per card) and "dumbbell" (all four marks visible — overlapping arcs for chest/shoulders/biceps, concentric rings for core, diagonal lines for legs/glutes — across a genuinely varied-looking grid); opened "Banded squat" (video, no poster) — detail view shows the same branded placeholder behind its own play button, name in serif, real instructions/muscles/cues render below unaffected; zero console/page errors on any of the three screens. Did not tap play (same discipline as prior entries — would spend real Your Move trial quota). Did not verify the real-poster path visually (0 rows in `exercise_extracted_posters` locally and in production — Phase 2 still hasn't run) but that code path is untouched (still a plain `<img src={posterUrl}>`, only the ternary shape changed) and is exercised by the wiring guard test.

**Incidental**: an already-running dev server on port 3000 (not started by this session) was killed by an overly broad `pkill` used to stop the dev server started for this verification. Worth knowing if a dev server you had open elsewhere just disappeared — restarting it is a plain `npm run dev`, nothing was lost.

## Exercise catalog dedupe: merge Your Move's own vendor duplicate rows (2026-07-30)

A member search for "squat" showed "Barbell Sumo Squat" twice. Root cause: `exercise_catalog` (migration 119/120, Your Move's own vendor data) contains real duplicate rows under different `external_id`s — `fetch-your-move-catalog.ts` upserts strictly on `(provider, external_id)` and never checks `name`, so nothing in the ingestion path ever caught this; it's a defect in Your Move's own catalog export, not something introduced by this app's code.

### Scan (all 857 exercises, before any changes)

Normalized every name (lowercase, punctuation/spacing collapsed, plus a trailing vendor `"(N)"` export-collision-marker suffix stripped — e.g. `"Squats to knee(1)"`) and grouped. **29 duplicate-name groups found** (28 pairs/triples/one 4-way group — full list in `docs/exercise-media/dedupe-report.json`): Barbell Deadlift, Barbell Sumo Squat, Bench Dips, Cable Hip Abduction, Cable Tricep Pushdown, Close Grip Lat Pulldown, Dumbbell Pullover, Dumbbell wrist curls, Glute Kickback, Kettlebell Farmer Carry, Kettlebell Thruster, Leg Press, Leg Extension, Leg Press Calf Raise, Seated Leg Curl (×3), Mountain Climbers (×4), Oblique Crunches, One-Arm Kettlebell Snatch from ground, Plank, Push Ups, Push Up Wide, Single-Arm Lat Pulldown, Side hurdel jumps to mountain climbers, Smith Machine Bent Over Row, Smith Machine Lunge, Smith Machine Sumo Squat (×3), Squats to knee, Walking Lunges.

**One group hand-verified NOT to be a true duplicate, not auto-merged**: "Kettlebell Turkish Get-Up (Lunge style)" / "...(Lunge style)(1)" share a name only via the same `"(1)"` vendor collision-marker pattern as every other group, but reading their actual instructions shows two different real movements — the "(1)" row's steps are the real ground-to-standing Turkish get-up (lie down, roll to elbow, bridge, kneel, stand), not a lunge variant at all. Auto-merging on name alone would have silently destroyed one of two genuinely distinct exercises. Fixed with a name correction instead (`"Kettlebell Turkish Get-Up"`), not a merge — both real exercises survive under accurate, non-colliding names. (A third row, "...(Lunge style) v2", differs by a real word ("v2") and was correctly left untouched by the normalizer.)

### Merge (`lib/exercise-library/catalogDedupe.ts`, `scripts/exercise-media/dedupe-exercise-catalog.ts`, migration 121)

For each of the 28 real duplicate groups: canonical keeper chosen by `catalogCompletenessScore` — has video > more complete instructions > more complete metadata (description/primary_muscle/secondary_muscles/equipment/category/difficulty), deterministic tie-break by `external_id` so re-running always picks the same keeper. `mergeCatalogFields` fills any keeper gap from a discard (never overwrites a value the keeper already has), unions `secondary_muscles`/`exercise_type`, OR-combines the three `has_video*` flags so a video is never lost — `instructions`/`exercise_tips` are deliberately NOT spliced together (they're each an internally-coherent ordered set of steps; two different phrasings glued together would read as garbled instructions), only borrowed wholesale from a discard when the keeper has none at all. `mergeMetadataFields` does the analogous merge for `mef_exercise_metadata` (unions `coaching_cues` and every other curation array, keeper's scalar curation wins else falls back to a discard's) — verified against real data that all 857 rows have every curation column at its schema default except `coaching_cues`, so in practice this is a coaching-cues union today. Both merge functions are pure and unit-tested (`tests/exercise-library-catalog-dedupe.test.ts`, 18 tests).

Migration 121 is a generated, versioned SQL file (same "generate a pure-SQL data migration, apply via `supabase db push`" convention as migration 120 — a live script holding an open connection to production isn't available in this environment) — produced by running the script against local Supabase, which computes the plan from real current data and renders it as literal `update`/`delete` SQL (not hardcoded ahead of time). Every table that references an exercise by `(provider, external_id)` gets repointed from each discarded `external_id` to the keeper's: `mef_exercise_metadata` (merge-then-delete), `member_exercise_favorites` and `member_exercise_recent_views` (both have a `UNIQUE(member_id, provider, external_id)` index — collision-safe SQL drops the redundant row for any member who already referenced both sides before repointing the survivor; `recent_views` additionally keeps whichever row has the more recent `viewed_at`), and the four tables with no unique constraint (`member_exercise_completions`, `coach_program_template_exercises`, `coach_assigned_workout_exercises`, `prescription_block_exercises`) get a plain repoint. Checked real data before writing this: every one of those six reference tables is currently empty in both local and production except `mef_exercise_metadata` (857 rows, 1:1 with the catalog) and `member_exercise_recent_views` (1 real row locally / a handful in production, none of which referenced a duplicate name) — the collision-safe SQL is written to be correct regardless, since it runs at whatever point production actually applies it, not just against today's snapshot.

**Real result**: 857 → 825 catalog rows (32 discarded), `mef_exercise_metadata` also 825 (still 1:1). Spot-checked Barbell Deadlift's merged row: `secondary_muscles` correctly unioned to `[core, forearms, adductors, upper_back]`, `coaching_cues` correctly unioned to all 6 cues from both original rows. Turkish Get-Up "(1)" row confirmed renamed with its real content (ground-to-standing instructions) intact.

### Guard test (`tests/exercise-catalog-no-duplicate-names.test.ts`)

Fails if `exercise_catalog` ever contains two rows whose normalized names match. Asserts the real catalog (500+ real rows, not vacuous) currently has zero duplicate groups, then **proven non-vacuous**: inserts a real spacing/casing/punctuation duplicate (`"Guard Test Squat"` / `"guard  test squat!"`) via the service-role client, re-runs the exact same detection function, confirms it catches the injected pair, cleans up in `afterEach`.

### Verified

`npm run typecheck` — clean (both workspaces). `npm run lint` — 0 errors (65 pre-existing warnings, none in new/changed files). Full `vitest run`: **269 files, 2910/2911 passing** — the one failure (`correlation-engine-integration.test.ts`, `observationCount` 31 vs 30) is the same pre-existing suite-ordering flake documented repeatedly above; confirmed by a fresh `supabase db reset` + running that file alone (passes, 6/6) immediately after (zero changes to `lib/correlation-engine/` this session). `npm run build` — compiled successfully, `/exercises` and `/exercises/[id]` routes both built clean.

**Verified locally** (`supabase db reset`, replaying all 121 migrations from scratch): 0 duplicate-name groups post-migration (was 29 pre-migration), confirmed via direct query.

**Deployed to production**: migration 121 applied via `supabase db push --db-url` (user provided the database password directly this session, same pattern as prior entries). Confirmed live via direct read query: `exercise_catalog` 825 rows (was 857), `mef_exercise_metadata` 825 rows, zero duplicate-name groups, "Barbell Sumo Squat" appears exactly once, Turkish Get-Up row correctly renamed. App code (repo `MEFwellness/mef-platform`, branch `main`) committed and pushed; Vercel auto-deployed — confirmed via `vercel inspect` that the newest Production deployment for project `mef-platform` (created ~3 minutes after the push) has `app.mefwellness.com` as an alias and a `mef-platform-git-main-*` alias confirming it built from the `main` branch push, not a stray branch/preview.
