## Coach-side workout/program generation via Your Move's generate endpoints (2026-07-30)

Coach-only tool, alongside the existing Program Library — members never see a generate button anywhere; they only ever receive programs their coach assigned. Two entry points: `/coach/generate/workout` (single session) and `/coach/generate/program` (multi-week split), both reached from a new "Generate" card on `/coach` (same "reached from the coach dashboard, not a new nav tab" convention as Program Library/Question Bank).

### Live API research (before writing any code)

Neither `GET /workouts/generate` nor `GET /programs/generate` had ever been called from this codebase — `lib/your-move/apiClient.ts` only had `listExercises`/`getExercise`/`getUsage`. Probed both live against the real trial key (`YMOVE_API_KEY`, currently `pending_approval` status) to learn the real shapes, since no vendor docs exist in this repo:

- **Both endpoints work today, under the account's `pending_approval` review status** — confirmed via direct calls, both returned 200.
- **Both are quota-free**: `monthlyExercisesUsed` in `/usage` was unchanged (4→4) after several generate calls — every embedded exercise carries `videoExcludedReason: "browse_mode"`, i.e. the generate response is browse-mode-shaped data, same free-of-charge tier as `listExercises`.
- **`/workouts/generate?muscleGroup=chest,back&equipment=...&difficulty=...`** — comma-joins multiple muscle groups; returns `{name, muscleGroup, muscleGroups, difficulty, estimatedMinutes, exerciseCount, exercises[], warmup[], cooldown[]}`, each entry `{exercise: {id, title, slug, description, instructions, muscleGroup, equipment, difficulty}, sets, reps, restSeconds, order}`.
- **`/programs/generate?goal=strength&weeks=N&difficulty=...`** — returns `{name, goal, difficulty, daysPerWeek, weeks, split, weeklySchedule: [{day, name, muscleGroup, muscleGroups, exercises[], warmup[], cooldown[]}], notes}`.
- **Vendor quirk found and designed around**: `weeks` is accepted but never honored — every probe (`weeks=2`, `weeks=6`, `numWeeks=2`, absent) returned the identical fixed 4-day/4-week template; the response's own `notes` field says "Repeat this weekly schedule for N weeks," i.e. the vendor's model is "one repeating week, the caller replicates it." `generatedProgramToDraft` therefore takes the coach's requested week count as an explicit parameter and never trusts the vendor's echoed `weeks` — locked in by a test that deliberately returns a mismatched vendor value and asserts the draft uses the caller's number instead.
- `goal` and `difficulty` aren't validated by the vendor (a nonsense value like `not_a_real_goal` is echoed back verbatim, no error) — the coach-facing UI only ever offers the 4 requested goals (`muscle_building`/`weight_loss`/`strength`/`endurance`) and the catalog's own real difficulty vocabulary, so nothing nonsensical reaches the API from this app.

### New API client methods (`lib/your-move/apiClient.ts`)

`generateWorkout({muscleGroups, equipment?, difficulty?})` and `generateProgram({goal, weeks?, difficulty?})` — same `request()` helper (retry/timeout/error-typing) as every existing method, GET only, no new auth pattern.

### Catalog mapping — no extra Your Move call needed (`lib/your-move/generation.ts`)

Per the task's own instruction ("fetch and store via browse mode... no video fields"), `ensureCatalogRowForGeneratedExercise` maps a generate-embedded exercise onto `exercise_catalog` **using the browse-mode-shaped data already embedded in the generate response itself** — zero extra Your Move calls, zero extra quota. Lookup order: (1) exact `external_id` match; (2) normalized-name match (reusing `catalogDedupe.ts`'s own normalizer) against the full catalog, so a Your Move id that migration 121's dedupe already merged away under a different `external_id` is never reintroduced as a fresh duplicate; (3) insert a new row with `has_video`/`has_video_white`/`has_video_gym` forced `false` (never guessed) — the next full catalog refresh (`fetch-your-move-catalog.ts`) backfills the real flag; until then it falls back to generated cues, the same graceful path every other no-video catalog exercise already uses. **Bug caught by its own test**: the first implementation pre-filtered candidates with `.ilike('name', exercise.title)`, which is an exact case-insensitive match with no wildcards — it silently could never catch a real spacing/punctuation variant, defeating the whole point of the fallback. Fixed to compare the normalized name against the full `(external_id, name)` column pull (bounded, ~825 rows, same "load the whole catalog for a name comparison" precedent as `dedupe-exercise-catalog.ts`), caught by a test seeding a genuine spacing/casing/punctuation-variant duplicate and asserting no new row is created.

`generatedWorkoutToDraft`/`generatedProgramToDraft` turn a raw generate response into an in-memory draft (`Warm Up` / main / `Cooldown` sections, empty ones omitted) — never persisted on their own; nothing reaches `coach_program_templates` until the coach explicitly saves.

### Server actions (`app/actions/your-move-generation.ts`) — coach-gated beyond RLS

Unlike every other action in this codebase (RLS is normally the sole authorization boundary), these add an explicit `hasActiveRole(supabase, userId, 'coach')` check before ever making the live Your Move call — because that call, unlike a database write, has no RLS of its own to fall back on. `generateWorkoutDraftAction`/`generateProgramDraftAction` call the vendor, map the result, and log every attempt (success or failure) to the new `your_move_generation_log` table — on failure, the catch block only logs and returns a plain-language error, **never** falls through to the mapper (proven by a source-scan guard test, plus by hand: temporarily added a mapper call inside the catch block, watched the guard fail, removed it). `saveGeneratedWorkoutDraftAction`/`saveGeneratedProgramDraftAction` reuse the existing `createTemplate`/`replaceTemplateContent` functions verbatim (no parallel write path), then explicitly set `status: 'active'` so the saved program shows up immediately in the existing `AssignProgramPanel` (which only lists `active` templates) with no extra manual step. A multi-day program becomes one `coach_program_templates` row per day (named `"<Program Name> — <Day Label>"`, grouped only by a shared `program_tags` entry — no new schema for "a program is several templates," since the existing Program Builder has no native multi-day-split concept, only single-workout templates repeated on a schedule); if any day fails to save, every day already created in that same save attempt is deleted before returning an error (no partial saves), also guard-tested.

### Schema (migration 122)

`your_move_generation_log` only — one row per generate call, `request_params`/`result_summary` jsonb, `saved_template_ids uuid[]` appended to only if/when the coach actually saves the draft. **No changes to `coach_program_templates` or `coach_assigned_workout*`** — an assigned generated program is byte-for-byte the same shape a hand-built one is; nothing about the member experience changes except receiving it, guard-tested by scanning every member-facing rendering file (`MemberProgramsList.tsx`, `MemberAssignedWorkoutDetail.tsx`, `app/programs/{page,[id]/page}.tsx`) for any mention of "generat"/"your move"/"ymove" (proven non-vacuous by hand: temporarily added "Generated by Your Move" to `MemberProgramsList.tsx`, watched the guard fail, removed it).

### UI (`components/your-move-generation/`, `app/coach/generate/*`)

`DraftSectionEditor` — the generated-draft counterpart to `ProgramBuilder.tsx`'s section/exercise tree, reusing the existing `ExercisePickerModal` verbatim for both "add an exercise" and "swap this exercise" (a new `Repeat`-icon button per exercise row). Deliberately lighter than the full 20-field prescription form — sets/reps/rest/notes only, since a coach can always open the saved template in the full Program Builder afterward for deeper editing (tempo/RPE/load/etc.); every generated draft is meant to get adjusted before use, and those adjustments are almost always sets/reps/duration/exercise choice, which this covers directly. `GenerateWorkoutFlow`/`GenerateProgramFlow` hold the whole draft in local React state (form → generate → edit → save) — nothing is persisted before the coach explicitly clicks "Save to Program Library."

### Tests (5 new files, 24 tests)

`tests/your-move-generate-endpoints.test.ts` (mocked fetch — query-building, error propagation), `tests/your-move-generation-mapping.test.ts` (real local Supabase, no mocks — catalog reuse/name-dedup/insert/RLS-blocks-a-member, plus the "trusts the caller's week count, not the vendor's" behavior), `tests/your-move-generation-coach-gate.test.ts`, `tests/your-move-generation-no-partial-draft.test.ts`, `tests/your-move-generation-member-side-unaffected.test.ts` — the latter three are source-scan guard tests (the actions themselves use `cookies()` and can't be invoked directly outside a Next.js request, same constraint every other action-level test in this suite works around), each proven non-vacuous by hand during this session as described above.

### Verified

`npm run typecheck` — clean. `npm run lint` — 0 errors, same pre-existing 65 warnings, none in new/changed files. Full `vitest run` (fresh `supabase db reset`): **274 files, 2940/2941 passing** — the one failure (`correlation-engine-integration.test.ts`, `observationCount` 31 vs 30) is the same pre-existing suite-ordering flake documented repeatedly above; confirmed by running that file alone immediately after the same fresh reset (passes, 6/6) — zero changes to `lib/correlation-engine/` this session. `npm run build` — compiled successfully, all three new routes (`/coach/generate`, `/coach/generate/workout`, `/coach/generate/program`) built clean.

**Not verified live**: this entry's own research already confirmed generation works live against the real trial key during the account's `pending_approval` review status (see "Live API research" above) — that was a real, already-spent probe, not a claim. Full coach-flow live verification (generate → edit → save → assign, on `app.mefwellness.com`) is recorded in this file's next entry once run.

**Verified live** against `app.mefwellness.com` (Playwright, `test.member.populated`, 390×844): searched "squat" — 26 result-area text nodes, "Barbell Sumo Squat" appears exactly once, zero exact-text duplicates among any rendered card, zero console/page errors.

## Lead Capture Agent: public prospect-intake chat for external Leadpages pages (2026-07-30)

A new, entirely separate public-facing chat agent — not Root, not gated by login, not linked from anywhere in the member app. Built to be embedded on external Leadpages landing pages via a single `<script>` tag pointing at this app's own domain.

### What already existed vs. what's new

Reused rather than duplicated: the AI provider layer (`lib/ai/providers/{types,anthropic,registry}.ts` — specifically the `AnthropicProvider` class itself, called directly rather than through `lib/ai/providers/registry.ts`, since that registry's `PROVIDERS` map has exactly one `'anthropic'` slot and registering a second instance there under a possibly-different model would silently collide with Root's own registration), the "pure data functions taking a Supabase client" convention (`lib/conversation-coach/data.ts`'s shape), the generic `notifications` table + `insertNotification()` helper (`lib/notifications/data.ts`) for coach alerts, and the service-role-client-in-a-route-handler pattern from `app/api/cron/wearable-daily/route.ts`. Confirmed via research before building: this repo had **no existing CORS helper, no rate-limiting utility, and no precedent for an anonymous/anon-role RLS-writable table anywhere** — every prior "public" surface (guest onboarding, the wellness-check quiz) only ever writes to `localStorage`, never Postgres, while anonymous. All three of those had to be built new for this feature specifically because none existed to reuse.

### Schema (migration 123, NOT yet applied to production — see below)

`lead_conversations` (session_token, topic, `stage` state machine, retry_count, lead_temperature, routed_to, source_url, status), `lead_messages` (role `lead`/`agent`, content, cascades from conversation), `captured_leads` (first_name, email, topic, lead_temperature, routed_to, notified_at). RLS enabled on all three with **no anonymous policies at all** — every write happens through the service-role client inside `app/api/lead-capture/route.ts`; only a `coach`/`platform_administrator` read policy exists, for a future coach-facing "Leads" view to query directly. Extends `notifications.type`'s check constraint with `'new_lead_captured'` (same additive-constraint convention migrations 45/53 already used), and the shared `NotificationType` TS union to match.

### The conversation flow (`lib/lead-capture/flow.ts`, deterministic — never left to the LLM)

Opening (static, no LLM call) → topic quick-replies (Pain/Energy/Sleep/Stress, or free-typed text classified via keyword match, falling back to `general` rather than guessing wrong) → 3 LLM-generated follow-up questions (where/how long, what they've tried, their goal) → one LLM-generated root-cause-style insight combined with the email ask → email/name extraction (regex-based, up to 2 retries on an invalid reply, then routes anyway rather than trapping a prospect in a loop) → routing. Which stage comes next, when an email actually counts as "captured," and where a lead is routed are all decided by plain functions in `flow.ts`, independent of any model output — the LLM only ever generates the reply text for the current turn. Lead temperature: `hot` only for `topic === 'pain'` AND at least one message containing a readiness keyword (`ready`, `book`, `schedule`, etc.) — routes to `LEAD_DISCOVERY_CALL_URL`; everything else is `warm`, routes to `LEAD_QUIZ_GUIDE_URL`. Both are env-configurable placeholders, not yet filled in with real URLs.

### Fallback path (`lib/lead-capture/fallback.ts`) — same discipline as Root's own fallback

If `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`/`ANTHROPIC_MODEL` (or the optional override `LEAD_AGENT_ANTHROPIC_MODEL`) are unset, or a real call fails, every turn falls back to deterministic, topic-specific templated copy rather than a broken widget — proven by clearing those env vars in the integration test, mirroring `tests/conversation-coach-integration.test.ts`'s own convention.

### Public API route (`app/api/lead-capture/route.ts`) — the first genuinely cross-origin, anonymous, write-capable route in this app

CORS locked to an explicit allowlist (`LEAD_WIDGET_ALLOWED_ORIGINS`, comma-separated) rather than `*`, enforced twice (server returns 403 for a present-but-disallowed Origin, not just omitting the CORS header and hoping the browser blocks it). Per-IP rate limiting (`lib/lead-capture/rateLimit.ts`) is a plain in-memory sliding window — 20 requests/5 minutes — since no Redis/Upstash dependency exists anywhere in this repo; documented honestly as per-warm-instance on Vercel's serverless platform, not a global limit, with the internals swappable later without changing the exported function signature. `middleware.ts` gained a `/api/lead-capture` + `/lead-widget-test` exemption (same pattern as the existing `/api/cron/` and `/onboarding` exemptions) since an anonymous cross-origin request never carries a session cookie.

**A real bug caught only by live browser testing, not the test suite**: the first implementation derived "is this a same-origin request" from `new URL(request.url).origin` — this repo's dev script is `next dev -H 0.0.0.0`, so in dev that's `http://0.0.0.0:3000`, which never matches the `http://localhost:3000` Origin header a real browser sends, causing the widget's own same-origin fetch to get a false 403. Fixed with `getSelfOrigin()` (`lib/lead-capture/cors.ts`), which derives the self-origin from the `Host`/`x-forwarded-host`/`x-forwarded-proto` headers instead (what the browser/proxy actually says it connected to) — correct in both dev and behind Vercel's proxy. Caught by driving the actual widget against the dev server with Playwright before considering this done, exactly per this repo's "verify features you built are actually reachable" habit; a regression test (`tests/lead-capture-cors-ratelimit.test.ts`) now locks in the fix.

### Embeddable widget (`public/lead-widget.js`)

Vanilla JS, no build step, one `<script src="https://app.mefwellness.com/lead-widget.js">` tag. Renders entirely inside a Shadow DOM so it can never collide with — or be broken by — arbitrary Leadpages page CSS in either direction. Brand colors (`#1B3A2D` forest / `#C4A050` gold / `#F5F0E4` cream) and DM Sans hardcoded directly in the widget's own stylesheet rather than imported from the app, since this repo has no centralized color-token file to import from at all (confirmed by research — every brand color everywhere in this app is a copy-pasted Tailwind arbitrary-value hex). The API origin is inferred from the widget's own `<script src>` (`document.currentScript.src`), so a future update to the agent never requires touching Leadpages again. `middleware.ts`'s static-asset matcher also gained a `lead-widget\.js` exclusion (same reasoning as the existing PNG/SVG/favicon exclusions) so an anonymous `<script src>` load isn't redirected to `/login`.

`app/lead-widget-test/page.tsx` — a same-origin manual test harness (plain mock landing page + the widget script), not linked from anywhere in the member app, for trying the widget against a real deployment before ever pasting the snippet into Leadpages.

### Tests (4 new files, 47 tests)

`tests/lead-capture-flow.test.ts` (pure logic — topic classification, stage transitions, email/name extraction, temperature/routing, retry cap), `tests/lead-capture-cors-ratelimit.test.ts` (CORS allowlist + the `getSelfOrigin` regression above + the in-memory rate limiter's window behavior), `tests/lead-capture-data.test.ts` (real local Supabase, no mocks — conversation/message/captured-lead CRUD, cascade delete), `tests/lead-capture-route-integration.test.ts` (real local Supabase, `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` cleared same as Root's own integration suite — the route is a plain Route Handler with no `cookies()` usage, so unlike a server action it can be imported and invoked directly with a constructed `Request`: full hot-lead conversation end-to-end including the real `captured_leads` row and the real `notifications` row for the seeded coach account, a warm-lead conversation, the 2-retry-then-forced-routing path, post-routing idempotency, CORS accept/reject, OPTIONS preflight, and rate-limit blocking after the window limit).

### Verified

`npm run typecheck` — clean (both workspaces). `npm run lint` — 0 errors, same pre-existing 65 warnings, none in new/changed files. Full `vitest run` (fresh `supabase db reset`): **278 files, 2982/2983 passing** — the one failure (`correlation-engine-integration.test.ts`, `observationCount` 31 vs 30) is the same pre-existing suite-ordering flake documented repeatedly above; zero changes to `lib/correlation-engine/` this session. `npm run build` — compiled successfully, `/api/lead-capture` and `/lead-widget-test` both built clean.

**Verified locally** (Playwright, dev server + local Supabase, 390×844, against `/lead-widget-test`): opened the bubble, tapped "Pain," answered all three follow-ups, received the insight+email-ask message, replied with a name+email, received the correct Discovery Assessment routing link — zero console/page errors at any step. Directly queried local Supabase afterward and confirmed the real rows: a `captured_leads` row (`lead_temperature: 'hot'`, `routed_to: 'discovery_call'`, `notified_at` set) and a matching `notifications` row for the seeded coach account (`type: 'new_lead_captured'`). Test data cleaned up afterward.

**NOT applied to production**: migration 123 was deliberately left unapplied per explicit instruction — the user needs to run `supabase db push --db-url "<production-db-url-with-password>"` themselves (see `reference_prod_supabase_migration` precedent: the `--linked` flag's password prompt doesn't work in this environment, `--db-url` with the Settings → Database password does). **`LEAD_WIDGET_ALLOWED_ORIGINS`, `LEAD_DISCOVERY_CALL_URL`, and `LEAD_QUIZ_GUIDE_URL` also still need to be set in Vercel** before the widget can be embedded on a real external domain — until then it only works on the app's own domain (e.g. `/lead-widget-test`).

**Deployed**: pushed to `main` (`MEFwellness/mef-platform`), confirmed via `vercel inspect` on the newest `mef-platform` Production deployment (created ~2 minutes after the push) that its aliases include both `app.mefwellness.com` and `mef-platform-git-main-mef-wellness.vercel.app`, confirming the live domain is serving this exact push. `GET /lead-widget-test` and `GET /lead-widget.js` both return 200 live. `POST /api/lead-capture` on `app.mefwellness.com` currently returns `{"error":"Could not start conversation"}` (500) — **expected, not a bug**: the `lead_conversations` table doesn't exist in production yet since migration 123 hasn't been applied there on purpose. This will start working the moment the user runs the migration; no code change will be needed.

## Lead Capture Agent v2: button-first flow, practitioner voice, two-part insight with named patterns, proactive pop-up (2026-07-30)

A major upgrade to the same Lead Capture Agent above — routing logic, lead storage, notifications, and the endpoint architecture (`app/api/lead-capture/route.ts`'s CORS/rate-limit/service-role-client shape) are unchanged. Everything else about the conversation got rebuilt: it now asks 4 button-first follow-ups instead of 3, sounds like a practitioner instead of support copy, and gives a two-part insight that names the visitor's pattern before asking for their email.

### Schema change (still migration 123, still NOT applied to production)

Since migration 123 had never been applied to production, it was edited in place rather than adding a new migration 124 for what's effectively the same unshipped feature: the `stage` check constraint gained `'follow_up_4'`, and both `lead_conversations` and `captured_leads` gained a nullable `pattern_name` column, constrained to the 8 pattern slugs below. `LeadConversationStage`, `LeadPatternName`, and both interfaces in `packages/shared-types-contracts/src/lead-capture.types.ts` were updated to match.

### Button-first flow (`lib/lead-capture/quickReplies.ts`, new)

Every follow-up stage now returns 3-5 topic-specific quick-reply buttons alongside the reply text — `follow_up_1` (where/when), `follow_up_2` (how long — one shared set across all topics, since duration reads the same regardless of concern), `follow_up_3` (what they've tried), `follow_up_4` (their goal). The free-text input was already always visible in the widget; the route now returns `quickReplies` on every follow-up turn (previously only the opening turn had any). `insight_capture` deliberately has no buttons — an email isn't a multiple-choice answer.

A 4th follow-up stage (`follow_up_4`) was added to the state machine (`flow.ts`'s `nextStage`) specifically so "where" and "how long" could become two separate button-driven questions instead of one combined free-text one, matching the task's own button examples.

### Practitioner voice (`lib/lead-capture/prompt.ts`, `fallback.ts` — both fully rewritten)

New system prompt bans "I understand", "I'm sorry to hear that", "Thanks for sharing", "Great question", "I'd be happy to", "Let's dive in", and exclamation points outright, and instructs the model to make questions carry expertise ("Does it ease off during the day, or build as the day goes on?") rather than reading like a form. All deterministic fallback copy (opening message, all 4 follow-ups × 5 topics, retry/closing messages) was rewritten to the same standard — the old "Hey!" opener and "Thanks for sharing that" fallback lines are gone.

### Pattern names — the small fixed set + assignment rules (`lib/lead-capture/pattern.ts`, new)

8 observational, non-clinical labels (`recovery_deficit`, `compensation_pattern`, `overload_pattern`, `fuel_timing_pattern`, `depletion_pattern`, `wind_down_deficit`, `rhythm_disruption`, `stress_loading_pattern`), assigned by a plain deterministic function — never the LLM — from the visitor's own `follow_up_1` (where/when) and `follow_up_3` (tried) answers. The same rule applies whether those answers came from a tapped button or typed free text (proven by `tests/lead-capture-pattern.test.ts` and the route-integration equivalence test below). Full assignment table and rationale in `docs/LEAD_AGENT_VOICE.md`.

### Two-part insight (the conversion engine)

`generateInsightPart1Reply` (after `follow_up_4`) names the pattern using its exact label, connects two things the visitor said, and deliberately withholds the driver/first-step explanation — then asks for name + email in the same message. `generateInsightPart2Reply` (only after email capture, or after the 2 retries are exhausted) gives the payoff — what usually drives the pattern and one first step — with no link or CTA of its own; the route handler concatenates it with the existing deterministic `buildRoutingMessage` (still the only thing that ever writes the actual routing URL) so the link is never left to LLM variance. `buildRoutingMessage` itself now speaks in the practitioner voice too: hot leads get "this is worth a real conversation" before the Discovery Assessment link, warm leads get a calmer transition before the quiz/guide link.

The assigned pattern name is stored on `lead_conversations.pattern_name` at the `follow_up_4` → `insight_capture` transition, copied onto `captured_leads.pattern_name` at capture time, and included in the coach notification body (`lib/lead-capture/notify.ts`) — e.g. "assigned pattern: a compensation pattern."

### Proactive pop-up (`public/lead-widget.js`, fully rewritten)

On page load, an 8-second timer races a 25%-scroll listener — whichever fires first opens the panel automatically, straight to the opening message and its 4 topic buttons, exactly like a manual bubble tap. Guarded by a `sessionStorage` flag (`mef_lead_autopopped`) so it fires at most once per browser session, and by an in-memory check (`state.open || state.conversationId`) so it never fires at all if the visitor already opened the widget themselves. The panel is a bottom-right card on desktop and a bottom-sheet (slides up from the bottom, rounded top corners, `max-height: 75vh`) on screens ≤480px — both use an opacity+transform transition (no `display:none` toggling) for a smooth open/close, and `pointer-events: none` while closed so the collapsed bubble never blocks page CTAs.

Closing (the ×) always collapses to the small corner bubble, which stays tappable. Since the widget's DOM is never torn down on close, reopening a conversation the visitor already engaged with just resumes naturally (message history is still there). Reopening one they closed *before* answering anything shows a different line instead of repeating the opener — "Still thinking about something? Tell me what's been going on." (`REOPEN_MESSAGE`, mirrored between the widget and `fallback.ts`'s doc reference) — followed by the same 4 buttons, which were never cleared from the DOM in the first place.

### Tests (2 new files, 3 files extended, all against the new 4-follow-up flow)

`tests/lead-capture-pattern.test.ts` (13 tests — every pattern rule, plus a same-button-vs-typed-text equivalence check) and `tests/lead-capture-quickreplies.test.ts` (4 tests — button counts, topic-specificity, the shared duration set, null for non-button stages) are new. `lead-capture-flow.test.ts` (`follow_up_4` in `nextStage`), `lead-capture-data.test.ts` (`pattern_name` round-trips through `updateLeadConversation`/`insertCapturedLead`), and `lead-capture-route-integration.test.ts` were extended/rewritten for the 4-stage flow, plus new coverage for: email captured before the full insight is delivered (asserts `insight_capture`'s reply never contains the part-two/driver language or a routing URL, while the post-email reply does), the pattern name landing correctly on both `lead_conversations` and `captured_leads` and inside the coach notification body, and a dedicated "buttons vs. typed free text" test that runs the identical logical conversation once via quick-reply taps and once via typed messages and asserts both land on the same topic and the same assigned pattern.

The proactive pop-up, dismiss/collapse, no-re-pop-per-session, and reopen-with-alternate-line behaviors live entirely in `public/lead-widget.js` (vanilla DOM script, no module system) — this repo's vitest config runs in a plain Node environment with no jsdom/happy-dom installed, so there's no automated harness for them (same limitation noted for other client-only behavior in this repo, e.g. the SSR component-test gap from the Case View entry above). These were verified instead by driving the actual widget with Playwright against the dev server + local Supabase, per this repo's established "verify what you built is actually reachable" habit — see below.

### Verified

`npm run typecheck` — clean (both workspaces). `npm run lint` — 0 errors, same pre-existing 65 warnings, none in new/changed files. Full `vitest run` (fresh `supabase db reset`): **280 files, 2999/3000 passing** — the one failure is the same pre-existing `correlation-engine-integration.test.ts` suite-ordering flake documented repeatedly above (31 vs 30), unrelated to this change. `npm run build` — compiled successfully, `/api/lead-capture` and `/lead-widget-test` both built clean.

**Verified locally** (Playwright, dev server + local Supabase, 3 separate browser contexts): (1) mobile viewport (390×844) — the panel auto-opened within 10s with no interaction, showed the standard opener + 4 buttons, rendered as a true bottom-sheet (`left`/`right` both `0`), and the page body stayed scrollable; closing collapsed it to the bubble; tapping the bubble again showed the alternate "Still thinking about something?" line with the same 4 buttons still present; reloading the page did not re-trigger the auto pop-up (session flag held). (2) Desktop viewport, buttons-only conversation (Pain → Lower Back → Weeks → Stretching/Foam Rolling → Keep Up With Life) reached `insight_capture` with a correctly-named "compensation pattern" and no leaked driver/first-step language, then typing an email produced a reply containing both the part-two payoff and the correct quiz/guide routing link. (3) A separate desktop context ran the same logical conversation as free-typed text only and landed on the identical "compensation pattern." Directly queried local Supabase afterward and confirmed real `lead_conversations`/`captured_leads` rows with `pattern_name` populated correctly for both the button and typed runs, and a hot-lead run (pain + "ready to book") correctly wrote `lead_temperature: 'hot'`, `routed_to: 'discovery_call'`. All test data cleaned up afterward (`lead_conversations` cascade-deletes messages and captured leads; matching `notifications` rows deleted separately).

### Still NOT applied to production (unchanged from the original build, now with more inside it)

Migration 123 — now including `follow_up_4` and both `pattern_name` columns — is still deliberately unapplied to production. Same command as before: `supabase db push --db-url "<production-db-url-with-password>"` (see `reference_prod_supabase_migration`: `--linked`'s password prompt doesn't work in this environment, `--db-url` with the Settings → Database password does). No other environment variables changed — `LEAD_WIDGET_ALLOWED_ORIGINS`, `LEAD_DISCOVERY_CALL_URL`, `LEAD_QUIZ_GUIDE_URL` still need to be set in Vercel before this can go live on a real external domain, exactly as before.

### New doc

`docs/LEAD_AGENT_VOICE.md` — the full voice standard (banned phrases, tone rules), the pattern-name table with assignment rules, and 6 full example conversations (Pain/Energy/Sleep/Stress, plus a dedicated hot-lead-ending example and a dedicated softer-lead-ending example) showing the buttons offered at each turn and the two-part insight structure end to end. `docs/LEAD_WIDGET_SNIPPET.md` (the coach-facing embed instructions) was updated to describe the proactive pop-up and button-first flow instead of the old 3-question, buttons-only-at-the-start version.

## Lead Capture Agent live 500 fixed: PostgREST schema cache, not a migration/code bug (2026-07-30)

After the user applied migration 123 to production (`supabase db push --db-url` reported "Remote database is up to date"), `app.mefwellness.com/lead-widget-test` still failed immediately with "Sorry, something went wrong on our end" and every `POST /api/lead-capture` returned `{"error":"Could not start conversation"}` (500).

### Diagnosis (against production, not local)

Called the live endpoint directly (`curl -X POST https://app.mefwellness.com/api/lead-capture -d '{}'`) — confirmed the 500 and the generic error body. `npx vercel logs https://app.mefwellness.com` (streamed live while re-triggering the request) surfaced the real underlying error, which the widget's generic catch-all message had been hiding:

```
createLeadConversation failed {
  code: 'PGRST204',
  message: "Could not find the 'pattern_name' column of 'lead_conversations' in the schema cache"
}
```

`PGRST204` is PostgREST's own error for "this column isn't in my in-memory schema cache" — a different thing from "this column doesn't exist in Postgres." Since the user had just confirmed the migration was fully applied (and this was the *first-ever* application of migration 123 to production — brand-new tables, not just a new column on an existing one), the Postgres side was correct; PostgREST's cached reflection of the schema simply hadn't picked up the new tables/columns yet. `supabase db push --db-url` runs migrations over a plain Postgres connection, which doesn't reliably trigger PostgREST's automatic cache reload the way Supabase's own dashboard/API-driven paths do — a known class of issue, not specific to this feature.

Checked the other 3 items on the diagnostic list before concluding, to rule out real alternatives: `npx vercel env ls production` confirmed `LEAD_QUIZ_GUIDE_URL`, `LEAD_DISCOVERY_CALL_URL`, `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `ANTHROPIC_MODEL`, and `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` are all present in the **Production** environment specifically (not just Preview) — none of these were the cause. `LEAD_WIDGET_ALLOWED_ORIGINS` is still not set in either environment, but that's irrelevant to this bug (the test page is same-origin, and `getSelfOrigin()` always allows same-origin regardless of that var) — flagged separately below since it's still a real gap for embedding on an actual external Leadpages domain. The migration file itself (`supabase/migrations/00000000000123_lead_capture_agent.sql`) was re-checked line by line — no typo, no wrong table name, `pattern_name` defined correctly inline on both `lead_conversations` and `captured_leads`.

### The fix

`supabase/migrations/00000000000124_reload_postgrest_schema_cache.sql` — a deliberate no-op at the schema level; its only content is `notify pgrst, 'reload schema';`, which every Supabase project's PostgREST instance listens for and reloads its cache on receipt of. This rides the exact same `supabase db push --db-url` command the user already knows and has credentials for, rather than requiring a separate trip to the SQL Editor or a Dashboard button — one more push fixes both the cache *and* leaves a template in the migration history for if this class of staleness ever recurs after a future `--db-url` push. No application code changed — there was no code bug to fix.

### Verified

Applied locally via `supabase db reset` (124 applies cleanly after 123). `npm run typecheck` clean, `npm run lint` — 0 errors, same 65 pre-existing warnings, `vitest run` — 279/280 files passing (same pre-existing `correlation-engine-integration.test.ts` flake, 31 vs 30, unrelated), `npm run build` — compiled successfully. Pushed to `origin/main`; confirmed via `vercel inspect` that the new Production deployment is aliased to `app.mefwellness.com`.

**Still needs the user to run one command**: `supabase db push --db-url "<production-db-url-with-password>"` to apply migration 124 and send the `NOTIFY`. This is the one action only the user can do (same password-only-they-hold constraint as every prior production migration in this doc) — once it's run, `/lead-widget-test` should work immediately, no redeploy needed since this only touches Supabase, not Vercel.

## Lead Capture Agent live 500, take 2: found and fixed the real root cause (2026-07-30)

The user applied migration 124 ("Finished supabase db push") and the live widget still failed with the exact same error. Re-diagnosed against production directly rather than trusting the prior session's theory.

### What was actually still broken

Called the live endpoint again and pulled fresh logs — same `PGRST204`/`pattern_name`/schema-cache error, meaning the NOTIFY didn't fix it. Rather than guess a second time, made the endpoint resilient to that specific failure first (see below), which unblocked the opening turn — then drove a full live conversation turn by turn against `https://app.mefwellness.com/api/lead-capture` with `curl`, all the way through the 4 follow-ups. **The `follow_up_3` → `follow_up_4` transition failed live** with a *different*, more telling error, pulled from production logs in real time:

```
updateLeadConversation failed {
  code: '23514',
  message: 'new row for relation "lead_conversations" violates check constraint "lead_conversations_stage_check"'
}
```

`23514` is a genuine Postgres check-constraint violation — not a PostgREST cache symptom. This proves production's `lead_conversations.stage` column was never actually given the `follow_up_4` value in its allowed set at all, which means production's table was built from an **older version of migration 123** — one from before this repo's "Lead Capture Agent v2" session added `follow_up_4` and `pattern_name` to that same file. The likely mechanism: `supabase db push` tracks migrations by version number only, never by diffing file content — so once "00000000000123" was recorded as applied in this project's migration history (at some earlier point), every later edit to that same file was silently invisible to subsequent pushes, no error, no warning. Migration 124's `NOTIFY` genuinely ran, but it was reloading PostgREST's cache for a schema that had never received the follow_up_4/pattern_name changes in the first place — a real fix for the wrong problem.

### The two-part real fix

**1. Made the code resilient regardless of cache/migration state** (`lib/lead-capture/data.ts`) — `createLeadConversation`, `updateLeadConversation`, and `insertCapturedLead` now detect PostgREST's specific "column not in schema cache" error (`PGRST204` naming `pattern_name`) and retry once with that field stripped from the payload. The conversation, routing, and coach notification all keep working even if `pattern_name` genuinely can't be written for some reason; only the pattern label itself fails to persist on that row. New test file `tests/lead-capture-schema-cache-resilience.test.ts` (4 tests, hand-built fake `SupabaseClient` since a real local Supabase never has a stale cache to reproduce this against) proves the retry actually drops the field and still succeeds, for all three write paths.

**2. A new, idempotent repair migration** (`supabase/migrations/00000000000125_lead_capture_stage_and_pattern_name_backfill.sql`) — since editing migration 123 further would keep being silently skipped, this is a genuinely new version number. `drop constraint if exists` / `add column if not exists` throughout, so it's safe to run regardless of production's actual current state: rebuilds `lead_conversations_stage_check` to include `follow_up_4`, adds `pattern_name` to both `lead_conversations` and `captured_leads` if either is missing it, rebuilds both `pattern_name` check constraints, and ends with the same `NOTIFY pgrst, 'reload schema'` as migration 124 for good measure.

### Verified — this time against the live production endpoint itself, not just "should work"

After deploying the code-resilience fix (before migration 125 existed), called `https://app.mefwellness.com/api/lead-capture` directly: the opening turn now returns 200 with the real opening question and 4 buttons (previously a 500). Continued the same live conversation turn by turn with `curl` — `follow_up_1`, `follow_up_2`, `follow_up_3` all worked and returned real (non-fallback) LLM-generated questions with the correct quick-reply buttons, confirming `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` is genuinely working in production too — until the `follow_up_4` transition surfaced the `23514` error above, which is what led to migration 125. `npm run typecheck` clean, `npm run lint` — 0 errors, same 65 warnings, full `vitest run` — 280/281 files (3003/3004 tests) passing, same pre-existing unrelated flake. `npm run build` — compiled successfully. Migration 125 applies cleanly locally via `supabase db reset`, including idempotently on top of a database that already has these changes (proving it's safe to run even if some future environment already matches). Code pushed to `origin/main`; confirmed via `vercel inspect` that the new Production deployment is Ready and aliased to `app.mefwellness.com`.

**Still needs the user to run one command** — same as every prior production migration in this doc: `supabase db push --db-url "<production-db-url-with-password>"`. This time it will actually change something (the previous push, for 124, only ran a NOTIFY against a schema that hadn't changed). Once applied, the full conversation through `follow_up_4` → the two-part insight → email capture → routing should work end-to-end, not just the opening turn — the code-resilience fix already makes the opening turn work today, but the `follow_up_4` step needs this migration to genuinely proceed past it.

## Lead Capture Agent: 5th topic — Weight (2026-07-30)

Added "Weight" as a 5th opening topic alongside Pain/Energy/Sleep/Stress, per explicit request ("should we add weight loss to the list"). Everything else about the agent — voice, button-first flow, two-part insight, proactive pop-up, routing/storage/notifications — is unchanged; this only adds one more topic branch through all the same machinery.

### Code changes (every file that already branched on topic)

`LeadTopic` (shared-types-contracts) gained `'weight'`. `flow.ts`'s `classifyTopic` gained a `weight` keyword list (weight, pounds, lbs, scale, losing/gaining weight, body fat) and the exact-match check. `quickReplies.ts` gained a `weight` row in `FOLLOW_UP_1/3/4_BY_TOPIC` (`follow_up_2`'s duration buttons are already shared across every topic, no change needed there): where "what's changed most" asks about cravings/appetite, being stuck despite effort, a big life change, or energy crashes — deliberately never a body part or a number. `fallback.ts`'s `QUICK_REPLY_OPTIONS` (the opening topic buttons) became 5, and gained weight-specific `FOLLOW_UP_1-4` question text. `prompt.ts`'s `TOPIC_LABEL` gained `weight`, and the system prompt gained one new explicit rule: never prescribe a diet/calorie target/exercise plan for the Weight topic, never mention calories/macros/a number, frame weight as a signal from the same connected system as sleep/stress/energy rather than a personal failing.

### Two new pattern names (`lib/lead-capture/pattern.ts`)

`stress_storage_pattern` ("a stress-storage pattern" — weight change that started with a big life change: stress, hormonal shift, menopause, postpartum) and `metabolic_adaptation_pattern` ("a metabolic adaptation pattern" — weight that's slow to shift despite already-tried effort like cutting calories or more cardio). Weight driven by cravings/appetite, energy crashes, or "slow despite effort" with nothing actually tried yet reuses the existing `fuel_timing_pattern` label rather than adding a near-duplicate, since the underlying mechanism (blood sugar swings) is genuinely the same one Energy's afternoon-dip case already names — keeping the pattern set small and consistent, per the original design directive. Fallback PART ONE/PART TWO copy added for both new patterns, same shame-free, non-diagnostic register as every other pattern.

### Migration — a new file, not an edit to 123/125 (lesson from the two live-500 fixes above)

`supabase/migrations/00000000000126_lead_capture_weight_topic.sql` — genuinely new version number, since editing an already-applied migration file is invisible to future `supabase db push` runs (the root cause behind both prior live-500 incidents in this doc). Rebuilds `lead_conversations`/`captured_leads`'s `topic` check constraints to include `'weight'`, and both tables' `pattern_name` check constraints to include the two new pattern slugs — `drop constraint if exists` throughout so it's safe regardless of exactly which prior migration production actually has. Ends with the same `NOTIFY pgrst, 'reload schema'` as 124/125 for good measure. **NOT applied to production** — same command as every prior entry: `supabase db push --db-url "<production-db-url-with-password>"`.

### Tests

Extended `lead-capture-flow.test.ts` (weight keyword classification + exact "Weight" match), `lead-capture-quickreplies.test.ts` (weight added to the topic list, `follow_up_1` weight-specific assertion), `lead-capture-pattern.test.ts` (4 new tests: stress-storage from a life-change answer, metabolic-adaptation from stuck-despite-effort-with-something-tried, the nothing-tried-yet fallback to fuel-timing, and cravings/energy-crash defaults), and `lead-capture-route-integration.test.ts` (opening turn now expects 5 quick replies; new dedicated "weight topic" describe block walking a full conversation through all 4 weight-specific follow-ups to routing, asserting the reply never contains calorie/carb/macro/diet-plan language at either the insight or routing turn, and that the captured lead's `pattern_name` is `stress_storage_pattern` for a life-change answer).

### Verified

`npm run typecheck` clean (both workspaces). `npm run lint` — 0 errors, same 65 pre-existing warnings. Full `vitest run` (fresh `supabase db reset`) — 280/281 files, 3009/3010 tests passing, same pre-existing unrelated `correlation-engine-integration.test.ts` flake. `npm run build` — compiled successfully. Migration 126 applies cleanly locally via `supabase db reset`.

**Verified locally with Playwright** (dev server + local Supabase, 390×844 mobile viewport): the auto pop-up opens showing exactly `Pain, Energy, Sleep, Stress, Weight`; the 5 buttons wrap onto 2 rows with none overflowing the panel and no page-level horizontal scroll introduced; tapping Weight advances to the weight-specific follow-up with its own 4 buttons; a separate run confirmed the reopened-after-dismissal state (before answering anything) also shows all 5 topic buttons alongside the alternate "Still thinking about something?" line. Test data (local only) cleaned up afterward.

## Lead Capture Agent: fixed question/button mismatch bug — buttons and questions now share one source (2026-07-30)

Live bug report: on the Weight path, the agent asked *"Has the shift been gradual over months, or did you notice it change more suddenly around a particular stretch of time?"* while the buttons offered were `Cravings/Appetite, Slow Despite Effort, Since A Big Life Change, Energy Crashes` — buttons for an entirely different question ("what's changed") than the one actually asked ("gradual vs sudden").

### Root cause

Two independent code paths produced the question and the buttons, with nothing forcing them to agree: `fallback.ts` had its own hand-written `FOLLOW_UP_1..4` question-text maps, `quickReplies.ts` had its own separate `FOLLOW_UP_*_BY_TOPIC` button maps, and neither had ever been cross-checked against the other — confirmed by checking: even the *fallback* Weight `follow_up_1` text ("What's changed most — cravings and appetite, energy crashes, or the scale barely moving...") never mentioned "Since A Big Life Change" at all, despite that being one of its own four buttons. On top of that, when the real LLM is configured (as it is in production), `buildFollowUpUserPrompt` only ever told the model a vague topic-agnostic "focus" ("where it shows up most, or when in the day it hits hardest") with zero knowledge of the specific buttons that would be shown next to its answer — free to invent any phrasing on that general theme, which is exactly how "gradual vs sudden" (a real, plausible reading of "when... it hits hardest") ended up paired with buttons written for a different dimension entirely.

### The fix — one shared source, not two independently-maintained lists

New file `lib/lead-capture/followUpScript.ts`: a single `FOLLOW_UP_SCRIPT` record, keyed by stage + topic, pairing each `question` with the exact `buttons` that directly answer it, plus `answerHints` (one keyword/phrase per button, in order, that must appear in the question — the mechanical version of "read the question aloud, then read each button"). `fallback.ts`'s `buildFallbackFollowUp` and `quickReplies.ts`'s `getQuickReplies` were both rewritten to read from this one file instead of their own separate maps — they structurally cannot drift apart again, since there's only one place left to edit. `prompt.ts`'s `buildFollowUpUserPrompt` now also pulls the same fixed `buttons` for that stage+topic and explicitly hands them to the LLM: *"The visitor will see these exact quick-reply buttons next to your question: [...]. Phrase your question so every one of those buttons reads as a direct, natural spoken answer to it — do not ask about a different angle those buttons wouldn't answer."* The model can still phrase things adaptively, but it's now anchored to the same underlying question the fixed buttons answer, not free to wander into an unrelated dimension.

Every fallback question across all 6 topics (Pain/Energy/Sleep/Stress/Weight/General) × 4 stages got a pass against this same "does every button read as a direct answer" standard while rebuilding the shared file — several beyond just the reported Weight bug had a missing 4th option (Energy's "All Day," Sleep's "All Of It," Stress's "All Of It," General's "Sleep"/"Not Sure") never referenced in their question text; those are now included.

### New test (`tests/lead-capture-follow-up-coherence.test.ts`, 75 tests)

For every stage/topic pair: asserts every button has a matching `answerHint`, asserts every hint actually appears in the question text, asserts 3-5 non-empty buttons. Two more tests assert `quickReplies.getQuickReplies` and `fallback.buildFallbackFollowUp` both read the literal same data as the script (proving the wiring, not just the source data, is correct) — and a dedicated regression test asserts Weight's `follow_up_1` includes "Since A Big Life Change" and that the question does *not* contain "gradual"/"suddenly," locking in that the specific reported bug can't silently come back.

### Docs

`docs/LEAD_AGENT_VOICE.md`'s button-first-flow section now documents question+button as one shared pair (not "question focus" + "buttons" as separate columns) and explains the followUpScript.ts mechanism and its coherence test. All 7 example conversations were updated to the corrected question wording (the "all day"/"all of it"/etc. 4th-option fixes plus the Weight rewrite) so the documented examples actually match what ships.

### Verified

`npm run typecheck` clean (both workspaces). `npm run lint` — 0 errors, same 65 pre-existing warnings. Full `vitest run` (fresh `supabase db reset`) — 281/282 files, 3084/3085 tests passing, same pre-existing unrelated `correlation-engine-integration.test.ts` flake. `npm run build` — compiled successfully.

**Verified locally with Playwright** (dev server, no LLM configured so the deterministic fallback path is exercised): drove the Weight path 3 questions deep and confirmed each question and its buttons cohere — `follow_up_1` asks "what's changed" and offers "Since A Big Life Change" (previously mismatched), `follow_up_2` asks "how long" and offers duration buttons, `follow_up_3` asks about what was tried and offers "Cutting Calories." Test data (local only) cleaned up afterward. No production migration needed — this is a pure application-code fix, nothing schema-related.

## Public prospect landing page at /start, natively hosting the Lead Capture Agent (2026-07-30)

A new public, no-login route (`app/start/page.tsx` + `app/start/StartPageClient.tsx`) — the on-domain counterpart to the external Leadpages embed. Reuses the Lead Capture Agent end to end: same widget script (`public/lead-widget.js`), same public endpoint (`app/api/lead-capture/route.ts`), same `lead_conversations`/`lead_messages`/`captured_leads` tables and coach notification — nothing about the agent itself changed. Standalone: no BottomNav, no AvatarLink, no member chrome, not linked from anywhere in the member app; only a two-line legal-basics footer. Six alternating-background sections (forest green → cream → cream → forest green → cream → forest green) using the app's existing font tokens (`--font-cormorant-garamond` headlines, DM Sans body) and locked brand colors (`#1B3A2D` forest, `#F5F0E4` cream, `#C4A050` gold), copy taken verbatim from the task brief. Every one of the 5 CTA buttons across the page calls the same `openChat()` handler — no forms, no pricing, no competing links.

### The one code change to the widget itself

`public/lead-widget.js` gained a single additive line at the end of `createWidget()`: `window.MEFLeadWidget = { open: openPanel }`. This is the only way for a host page's own CTA buttons to open the exact same panel/conversation instead of the page building a second chat UI — nothing in the widget's existing bubble-tap, proactive-popup (8s timer / 25%-scroll), dismissal-to-bubble, or reopen-message logic reads or is affected by this hook; it's purely a new entry point alongside the existing ones. The client component's `useOpenLeadChat()` hook calls `window.MEFLeadWidget.open()` and briefly polls (100ms × up to 30 attempts) if the script hasn't finished mounting yet when a CTA is tapped, rather than silently no-oping on a slow connection.

`middleware.ts`'s `PUBLIC_PATHS` gained `/start`, same pattern as the existing `/lead-widget-test` exemption — anonymous visitors reach it with no redirect, and a logged-in visitor sees the same standalone page rather than being bounced (no branch treats `/start` differently based on session).

### Tests

`tests/start-page.test.ts` (12 tests) — same static-source-scan convention as `tests/subhead-contrast-ratio.test.ts` (this repo's `vitest.config.ts` runs `environment: 'node'`, no jsdom/rendering harness). Covers: `/start` is in `middleware.ts`'s `PUBLIC_PATHS` and the page itself never calls `redirect()`/`auth.getUser()`; no member-chrome components are rendered; the page loads the real `/lead-widget.js` (not a duplicate) and every CTA reuses one shared handler (asserts exactly 5 `onClick={openChat}` uses) that calls the widget's real `window.MEFLeadWidget.open()` hook; the widget's existing proactive-popup/reopen-message/dismissal behavior strings are still present unchanged; `app/api/lead-capture/route.ts` has no `/start`-specific branching; and the copy contains no CHEK cert-level jargon, forms, pricing, or outbound links. No changes were needed to the extensive existing `tests/lead-capture-*.test.ts` suite, since the agent/endpoint are untouched.

### Verified

`npm run typecheck` clean (both workspaces). `npm run lint` — 0 errors, same 65 pre-existing warnings, none in new files. Full `vitest run` (fresh `supabase db reset`) — 282/283 files, 3096/3097 tests passing, same pre-existing unrelated `correlation-engine-integration.test.ts` flake (31 vs 30). `npm run build` — compiled successfully; `/start` built as a static route (2.73 kB, 92.9 kB First Load JS).

**Verified locally with Playwright** (dev server + local Supabase, real `POST /api/lead-capture`, no mocking): (1) 390×844 mobile viewport — `/start` returned 200 with zero `nav` elements on the page (no member chrome); the hero CTA opened the real shadow-DOM widget panel showing the same opening question and 5 topic quick-replies as `/lead-widget-test`; tapping "Pain" produced the real follow-up question ("Where does it show up most...") from the actual agent flow; closing the panel collapsed it back to the bubble. (2) A fresh browser context confirmed the proactive pop-up still fires automatically within ~8s showing the same opener, and reopening after a dismissal-without-answering showed the exact "Still thinking about something?" reopen line. Zero console/page errors in either run. Directly queried local Supabase afterward and confirmed real `lead_conversations` rows with `source_url: 'http://localhost:3000/start'` — proving the page is wired to the genuine endpoint, not a mock. All test data cleaned up afterward.

No database migration was needed for this feature — it only adds a page, an additive widget hook, and a middleware exemption, nothing schema-related.
