## Corrective-role metadata layer + blueprint gap check (2026-07-30)

Data foundation for a future AI corrective program generator — no generator, no UI, no AI/LLM call, no Your Move API call (zero video quota used; this only reads exercise_catalog columns already in the local DB from prior browse-mode fetches).

### Schema — extends mef_exercise_metadata, doesn't duplicate it

Chose to extend `mef_exercise_metadata` (migration 80) rather than create a new table, per "reuse existing systems": it already sits on top of `exercise_catalog` keyed by (provider, external_id), already has an `equipment text[]` column that covers "equipment_needed," and already has a row for all 825 catalog exercises (from the Phase 4 cue-generation script). Migration 127 (schema) adds `corrective_roles text[]`, `muscles_stretched text[]`, `muscles_strengthened text[]`, `strain_level text`, `spinal_flexion_core boolean`, plus 3 check constraints: `corrective_roles` values are restricted to the 7 defined roles; a spinal-flexion exercise can never carry the `stability`/`core_stability` role; a muscle can never appear in both `muscles_stretched` and `muscles_strengthened` on the same row. Migration 128 is a generated pure-SQL data migration (same convention as migration 120) populating all 825 rows.

### The classifier (`lib/exercise-library/correctiveClassification.ts`)

A deterministic, hand-authored rule engine — no AI — reading only each exercise's existing Your Move `exercise_type` tags, `category`, `primary_muscle`/`secondary_muscles`, `difficulty`, and `name`. Assigns 1+ of `release`/`stretch`/`mobility`/`stability`/`strength`/`power`/`core_stability`; canonicalizes Your Move's raw muscle vocabulary (e.g. `iliopsoas`, `rectus_femoris` → "hip flexors") into the plain-English names the 4 corrective blueprints use, filtering out data-quality noise where Your Move put an equipment/category value in a muscle field (`bodyweight`, `smith-machine`, etc.); detects crunch-type spinal-flexion movements by name keyword with an exclusion list for movements that sound like flexion but aren't (`superman crunch` is spinal extension, `plank toe touches` and standing hamstring "toe touch" stretches are not ab crunches at all — both were real false positives caught and fixed during review, see below); assigns strain_level from role + difficulty + compound-lift name matching, not skill difficulty alone. `muscles_stretched`/`muscles_strengthened` are populated mutually exclusively per row (loading role wins if an exercise has both a lengthening and loading role) — guarantees the "no muscle both stretched and strengthened" invariant by construction, not just by the DB constraint.

`scripts/exercise-media/generate-corrective-metadata.ts` runs the classifier over the full catalog (read-only Supabase query) and writes both the reviewable data file (`docs/exercise-media/corrective-metadata-report.json`, one entry per exercise plus a `lowConfidence` list) and migration 128's SQL.

### Bugs caught during review, before committing

Ran the classifier, then spot-checked the output against real exercise descriptions rather than trusting the rules blind: "High plank toe touches," "Plank toe touches," and "Push up(s) to toe touches" were initially flagged `spinal_flexion_core: true` — the "toe touch" keyword matched, but these are plank/push-up stability drills, not ab crunches (confirmed against their actual instructions). "Toe touch- Posterior thigh" is a standing hamstring stretch, not spinal flexion either. Fixed by adding `plank`, `posterior`, and `push[\s-]?ups?` as exclusion keywords. Separately, a bare `category: 'core'` tag was forcing a `strength` role onto exercises that were purely stretches/mobility drills under Your Move's loose "core" category (e.g. that same hamstring toe-touch) — fixed by only applying the core→strength fallback when `stretch`/`mobility` isn't already assigned. A third bug, found only by trying to apply the generated migration: `sqlStringArray` was wrapping array values in both double **and** single quotes (Postgres native-array syntax mixed with SQL `ARRAY[]` constructor syntax), so every role string landed in the DB with literal quote characters baked in and immediately failed the `corrective_roles` check constraint on the very first `db reset` — a real bug caught by actually running the generated migration locally, not just reading the generator code.

### Blueprint gap check (`scripts/exercise-media/corrective-blueprint-gap-check.ts`, `docs/CORRECTIVE_BLUEPRINT_GAP_CHECK.md`)

Role counts across all 825 exercises: strength 651, stretch 125, power 74, core_stability 60, mobility 68, stability 47, release 1 (only one foam-roller exercise exists anywhere in the Your Move catalog — a structural gap, not a classification miss). spinal_flexion_core: 10. 12 exercises flagged low-confidence (thin/bogus source data — see the report's `lowConfidence` list).

6 blueprint slots have zero matching exercises: Lower Cross's TFL (tight), Upper Cross's levator scapulae (tight) and deep neck flexors (long), Forward Head's suboccipitals (tight), deep neck flexors (long), and thoracic extensors (long) — none of these muscles appear anywhere in Your Move's muscle vocabulary for this catalog (not a classification gap — a catalog-content gap). 5 slots are thin (<5 options): Lower Cross's TVA (2 core_stability options), Upper Cross's pecs/lats stretches (2 each) and lower/mid traps strengthening (4), Forward Head's chest/pecs stretches (2).

### Guard tests (`tests/corrective-exercise-metadata.test.ts`, 6 tests, real local Supabase)

Coverage (every catalog exercise has a tagged metadata row), role-vocabulary validity, no-muscle-overlap, and spinal-flexion-excludes-stability — each checked against real classified data, not fixtures. Two more tests plant a bad row directly against the live DB and assert the insert is rejected by the migration-127 check constraints. Verified these aren't vacuous the way the task asked: manually dropped the spinal-flexion/stability check constraint, force-updated a real row (`V-Up`) to violate it, reran the suite and watched both the data-invariant test and the DB-rejection test go red, then restored the row and re-added the constraint and reran to green — full transcript of this cycle is in this session's tool history, not just asserted here.

### Verified

`npm run typecheck` clean (both workspaces) — one pre-existing test fixture (`tests/exercise-library-normalize.test.ts`) needed the 5 new `MefExerciseMetadata` fields added to its literal. `npm run lint` — 0 errors, same 65 pre-existing warnings plus 10 new `no-console` warnings in the two new scripts (same convention as every other `scripts/exercise-media/*.ts` file). Full `npm test` (fresh `supabase db reset`, migrations 127+128 applied cleanly) — 283/284 files, 3102/3103 tests passing, same pre-existing unrelated `correlation-engine-integration.test.ts` flake (31 vs 30). `npm run build --workspace=@mef/consumer-web-app` — compiled successfully.

**Not applied to production** — same constraint as every migration in this doc: the user needs to run `supabase db push --db-url "<production-db-url-with-password>"` themselves. Nothing in this task touches any AI provider, UI, or existing runtime behavior, so there's no live-site regression risk before that migration is applied — it's purely additive columns with no reader yet.

## Custom MEF corrective exercises to fill blueprint gaps (2026-07-30)

Fills every zero/thin-coverage slot found by the Prompt-1 gap check with 28 hand-authored, cue-only exercises — no generator, no new UI, no Your Move API call (zero video quota used).

### Ownership: `provider = 'mef_custom'`, not a new table/column

Reused the exact same `(provider, external_id)` natural key every other table in this schema already keys on, rather than adding an ownership column. Migration 129 widens `exercise_catalog`'s provider check constraint (`your_move` only, migration 119) to also allow `mef_custom`; `mef_exercise_metadata.provider` already accepted any text (no constraint, migration 80). This means every existing provider-keyed lookup (favorites, completions, recent views, coach program/prescription exercises) already distinguishes MEF-owned rows for free, and the Your Move purge script now scopes its `exercise_catalog` video-cache-clear update to `.eq('provider', 'your_move')` explicitly (`scripts/exercise-media/purge-your-move-media.ts`) — a `mef_custom` row is structurally unreachable by that cleanup, not just unaffected because cue-only rows never had a `video_url` to begin with. New guard test `tests/mef-custom-exercise-purge-exclusion.test.ts` proves this by seeding a `mef_custom` row *with* a fake `video_url` (something it would never really have) and confirming the purge leaves it untouched while still clearing a co-existing `your_move` row.

### A real bug found while wiring this in: `provider` was hardcoded 'your_move' in 8+ places

Widening the provider value surfaced that `lib/exercise-library/normalize.ts` hardcoded `provider: 'your_move'` on every normalized exercise regardless of the row's actual provider, and `getExerciseMetadata`/`getExerciseMetadataMap` (`lib/exercise-library/metadata.ts`) took a `provider` argument and filtered reads by it — every call site (search results, exercise detail page, related exercises, favorites list, the cue-generation script, `favoriteMovementTypes.ts`) passed the literal `'your_move'`. Left as-is, a `mef_custom` exercise's `coaching_cues` would never be found by the search grid or detail page — the exact "display as cue-only cards" requirement this task exists for. Fixed by making metadata reads external_id-only (safe: Your Move's UUIDs and MEF's `mef-custom-*` slugs can never collide, so external_id is already effectively unique across providers) and passing through the real `exercise.provider` in `normalize.ts`; updated 8 call sites accordingly. Left `isExerciseFavorited`/`listExerciseCompletionHistory`/`listMyExerciseFavoriteIds` (member-owned data) hardcoded to `'your_move'` deliberately — both their write and read sides already agree on that literal, so favoriting a custom exercise still works correctly end-to-end, just tagged with a provider value that doesn't reflect the exercise's true origin; fixing that fully was out of scope for this task and carries real regression risk across a heavily-tested feature, so it's left as a known, self-consistent gap rather than silently expanded scope.

### The 28 exercises (`docs/exercise-media/mef-custom-corrective-exercises.json`, reviewable)

Hand-authored — name, 3-6 line coach-voice cues (setup, movement, breathing, common mistake, dosage), corrective_roles, muscles_stretched/strengthened using the exact canonical labels the gap-check matches on, strain_level (all `low` per the task), spinal_flexion_core (all `false`), equipment. `scripts/exercise-media/seed-mef-custom-corrective-exercises.ts` reads this file and generates migration 130 (pure SQL, same generated-migration convention as migration 120/128) — one `exercise_catalog` insert + one `mef_exercise_metadata` insert per exercise, both `provider = 'mef_custom'`.

Breakdown: 9 release (foam-roll ×6, ball-release ×3), 10 stretch, 3 mobility (thoracic foam-roll extension + 2 release items also carry a mobility-adjacent role), 3 stability (the 3 chin-tuck/deep-neck-flexor exercises), 3 strength (Prone Y, Prone T, Wall Angels — Wall Angels also carries `stability`), 2 core_stability (the 2 TVA drills). Full per-exercise text is in the JSON file and in this session's final report to the user.

Introduced 5 new canonical muscle labels that didn't exist before this task (no catalog exercise had ever needed them): `TFL`, `levator scapulae`, `deep neck flexors`, `suboccipitals`, `thoracic extensors`.

### A real bug caught before generating the migration: double-quote leak in `sqlStringArray`

Copy-pasted the same array-literal-building bug from Prompt 1's original (already-fixed) helper into this new script on the first draft — caught immediately this time by comparing against the fixed version rather than re-deriving it, and by running the generated migration locally before committing (same "run it, don't just read it" habit as Prompt 1).

### Gap check: re-run against the live DB instead of a frozen JSON snapshot

`scripts/exercise-media/corrective-blueprint-gap-check.ts` was rewritten to query `mef_exercise_metadata` directly (via `SEED_SUPABASE_URL`/`SEED_SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY`) instead of reading `docs/exercise-media/corrective-metadata-report.json` — the JSON snapshot only ever covered the 825 auto-classified Your Move exercises, so it would have silently missed the 28 new custom ones and any future additions. Also changed `Slot.canonicalLabel: string | null` to `canonicalLabels: string[]` so a blueprint's compound muscle names ("lower/mid traps", "SCM/scalenes") can match either label, and changed the pass/fail threshold from the old ad-hoc "<5 is thin" to this task's actual rule: every slot needs ≥3 options, full PASS/FAIL line at the top of the regenerated report.

**Result: PASS — every slot in all 4 blueprints now has ≥3 options, 0 zero-coverage slots, 0 below-minimum slots** (down from 6 zero-coverage + 5 thin slots before this task). Full table in `docs/CORRECTIVE_BLUEPRINT_GAP_CHECK.md`.

### Guard tests (2 new files, 4 tests)

`tests/mef-custom-exercise-purge-exclusion.test.ts` (purge exclusion, described above) and `tests/mef-custom-exercise-cue-cards.test.ts` (drives the real `normalizeExerciseCatalogRow()` against a real seeded `mef_custom` row — Chin Tuck – Supine — and asserts `hasVideo: false`, `posterUrl: null`, ≥4 real coaching cues; a second test asserts all 28 custom exercises have ≥4 cues and no video). The existing `tests/corrective-exercise-metadata.test.ts` (coverage/no-overlap/spinal-flexion guardrails) and `tests/exercise-catalog-no-duplicate-names.test.ts` needed zero changes — both already scan the whole table with no provider filter, so they automatically extended to cover the new rows; both still pass.

### Verified

`npm run typecheck` clean (both workspaces). `npm run lint` — 0 errors, same warnings baseline plus 1 new `no-console` in the new seed script. Full `npm test` (fresh `supabase db reset`, migrations 127-130 applied cleanly) — 285/286 files, 3106/3107 tests passing, same pre-existing unrelated `correlation-engine-integration.test.ts` flake (31 vs 30). `npm run build --workspace=@mef/consumer-web-app` — compiled successfully.

**Not applied to production.** Two migrations are pending: 127+128 (Prompt 1's corrective tagging — confirmed still unapplied, no entry in this doc says otherwise since the last session) and 129+130 (this task). One command applies whichever of the four the production DB doesn't have yet — `supabase db push` tracks applied migrations by version number, so it's safe to run even though 127/128 may already be there: `supabase db push --db-url "<production-db-url-with-password>"`.

## Corrective Program Generator Engine — the 4-week program builder itself (2026-07-31)

Engine + data only, no coach UI, no member-facing surface (the next task builds the review/trigger UI), zero Your Move API calls (reads the already-classified local catalog only). Builds on the corrective metadata layer and MEF gap-fill exercises from the prior two tasks.

### Blueprints as data (`lib/corrective-engine/blueprints.ts`)

The 4 blueprints (Lower Cross, Upper Cross, Forward Head, Flat Back), each with tight-muscle slots (release/stretch/mobility candidates) and long-muscle slots (stability/strength candidates), exactly as recorded in `docs/CORRECTIVE_BLUEPRINT_GAP_CHECK.md`. This is now the single source of truth — `scripts/exercise-media/corrective-blueprint-gap-check.ts` (built in the prior task) was refactored to import from here instead of carrying its own duplicate copy of the same 4 blueprints, so the two can never drift apart. Verified byte-identical: re-ran the gap-check script after the refactor and diffed the regenerated `docs/CORRECTIVE_BLUEPRINT_GAP_CHECK.md` against git — zero changes.

### The MEF Method session template (`lib/corrective-engine/sessionBuilder.ts`)

Every session is full-body, 5 blocks in a fixed order — Release (role `release`, tight muscles only) → Mobility (role `stretch`/`mobility`, tight muscles only) → Stability (role `stability`/`strength`, long muscles, excluding TVA-type core-stability-only slots) → Strength (role `strength`, general full-body compound work, lower-body-led into upper body into a lower-strain accessory tail) → Core (role `core_stability` only, always last, prioritizing any active pattern's core-stability-only muscle like Lower Cross's TVA). Strength is omitted from the session entirely (not just left empty) whenever its budget is 0. A stacked program (e.g. Upper Cross + Forward Head, the task's own example) merges each blueprint's tight/long muscle slots, collapsing genuinely duplicate muscles (e.g. both blueprints' shared "upper traps" tight slot) into one, prioritized worst-severity-blueprint-first, with a defensive backstop that drops any muscle appearing as both tight and long across a stacked combination from both lists rather than risk a violation (not reachable with today's 4 blueprints, checked in case a 5th blueprint is added later without re-auditing this file).

**A real bug found and fixed by this engine's own guard test**: the first implementation matched a Release/Mobility candidate against a muscle slot correctly, but never checked whether that same exercise's *other* stretched muscles included one of the pattern's LONG muscles — e.g. a hamstring stretch that also happens to lengthen "lumbar erectors" (Flat Back's long muscle) was being selected for Flat Back's Mobility block because it correctly matched the "hamstrings" tight slot, silently violating "never stretch a pattern's long muscle" through the muscle it *wasn't* being selected for. Caught by the `never places a stretch/mobility pick against any active blueprint's LONG muscle` guard test failing on Flat Back (`expected [ 'lumbar erectors' ] to deeply equal []`) before this was ever reported as done. Fixed by adding an `excludeLabels` set (every active pattern's full long-muscle list) that every Release/Mobility candidate's entire `musclesStretched` array must clear, not just the one label that matched the slot.

### Severity dial (`lib/corrective-engine/blockBudgets.ts`)

Per-block exercise-count budgets, all inside the task's 8–12/session cap: mild 1/1/2/5/1 (release/mobility/stability/strength/core, 10 total, full strength block), moderate 2/2/3/3/1 (11 total, reduced strength), severe 3/3/4/0/2 (12 total, no Strength section at all — not just an empty one). Overall program severity is the worst individual detected pattern's severity, per the task.

### Posture-finding → blueprint mapping (`lib/corrective-engine/patternMapping.ts`, `findings.ts`) — a documented modeling decision

The Universal Registry's `PostureFindingType` vocabulary predates the 4 named blueprints, so only 2 of the 11 finding types map 1:1 (`forward_head`, `lower_crossed_pattern`). Upper Cross is inferred from `rounded_shoulders`/`elevated_shoulder`/`thoracic_kyphosis` (the real external signals a `static_posture` assessment produces for an upper-quadrant crossed pattern). Flat Back is inferred from `lumbar_posture`, which has no directional signal (it measures "outside neutral" without saying which way) — Lower Cross (hyperlordotic) and Flat Back (flattened) are opposite ends of the same lumbar-curvature spectrum, so `lumbar_posture` only produces a Flat Back pattern when `lower_crossed_pattern` is *not* also present on the same assessment, keeping the two mutually exclusive. A future dedicated finding type (or a directional lumbar signal) would make this a clean 1:1 mapping like the other two — noted here as a real follow-up, not treated as done. "Latest completed posture assessment" = `body_assessments` with `assessment_type: 'static_posture'` and `status` in `('analyzed', 'coach_reviewed')` (the two statuses this codebase's own action code only ever sets after findings actually exist); only `pending_review`/`confirmed`/`coach_overridden` findings count as real signal (dismissed/superseded/draft excluded).

### Exercise pool (`lib/corrective-engine/exercisePool.ts`)

Reads `mef_exercise_metadata` filtered to `spinal_flexion_core = false` and `equipment <@` the caller's allowed set (default `bodyweight`/`foam roller`/`ball`, matching the task's default assumption against the real equipment vocabulary this catalog actually uses), joined to `exercise_catalog` for display names in application code (no FK exists between the two tables — same precedent as `metadata.ts`/`normalize.ts`). **A real bug found before any test ran**: the first join fetched catalog names via `.in('external_id', [...825 ids])`, which blew past PostgREST's URL length limit (`URI too long`) once the exercise pool test actually ran against the real ~850-row catalog. Fixed by reading the whole (skinny, 2-column) catalog page-by-page via `range()` instead — the same "load the whole catalog for a comparison" precedent already used by `dedupe-exercise-catalog.ts` and the Your Move generation mapping.

### Seeded selection (`lib/corrective-engine/rng.ts`)

A small deterministic PRNG (FNV-1a string hash → mulberry32), no `Math.random()` anywhere — "rotate among qualified options via a seed, reproducible for the same seed" from a plain string seed. Candidates are always stably sorted by `externalId` before shuffling (DB query order isn't guaranteed stable across calls).

### Program generator + persistence (`programGenerator.ts`, `save.ts`)

One 4-week phase, `daysPerWeek` (2 or 3) distinct sessions, each independently seeded (`${seed}:session${i}`). The weekly session set repeats identically across all 4 weeks of this phase — a deliberate scope decision, not an oversight: phase-to-phase progression (mobility → stability → strength → power, power only from phase 2 on) is a separate concern this single-phase generator doesn't build, and this "one repeating week, the caller/reader replicates it" shape is the same convention already adopted elsewhere in this codebase for Your Move's own `/programs/generate` response. Output reuses the existing Coach Program Builder tables verbatim (`createTemplate`/`replaceTemplateContent`/`setTemplateStatus` from `lib/coach-program-builder/templates.ts`, zero new write path) — one `coach_program_templates` row per weekly session, all sharing a `program_tags` group id (`corrective-program:<uuid>`), same "a program is several templates, no new schema" precedent the Your Move program-generation feature already established. `corrective_tags` holds the detected blueprint keys; `coach_notes`/`description` record the detected patterns, severities, member, and seed for a coach's review. **Nothing is assigned** — `coach_program_assignments` is never touched, guard-tested directly (asserts a zero count).

### Schema (migration 131) — one additive change

`coach_program_templates.status` only ever had `draft`/`active`/`archived` (migration 82) — widened to add `pending_coach_review`, the same additive-constraint-widening convention already used repeatedly on this table's neighbors (`program_section`, `event_type`, etc.). Also widened `listCoachTemplates`'s default (no-filter) query from excluding just `archived` to excluding both `archived` and `pending_coach_review`, so an engine-generated draft stays invisible in the coach's existing Program Library browse view (`/coach/programs`) until a dedicated review screen exists (still reachable via an explicit `status: 'pending_coach_review'` filter) — a minimal necessary guard against an incomplete/confusing surface leaking into the current UI, not new UI work.

### CLI runner (`scripts/corrective-program/generate-corrective-program.ts`)

The only way to run the engine today (no coach UI yet). Reads a member's real findings (`--member <id>`) or accepts a synthetic `--patterns lower_cross:moderate,...` override for demos/testing without needing a seeded assessment; `--dry-run` prints without saving.

### Guard tests (3 new files, 55 tests)

`tests/corrective-engine-pattern-mapping.test.ts` (pure, 7 tests) — the finding→blueprint mapping rules, including the lumbar_posture/lower_crossed_pattern mutual-exclusivity rule. `tests/corrective-engine-session-builder.test.ts` (real local Supabase pool, no mocks, 39 tests across 6 pattern/severity combinations including the stacked Upper Cross + Forward Head case) — every hard rule from the task: no spinal-flexion exercise ever appears (checked against a real spinal-flexion id set loaded independently of the pool's own exclusion, so the exclusion is proven, not assumed), no stretch/mobility pick ever targets any active blueprint's long muscle, core is always the final block, every session stays within 8–12 exercises, stacked patterns produce zero duplicate exercises, severe has no strength block, same seed reproduces an identical program, a different seed produces different exercises. `tests/corrective-engine-save.test.ts` (real local Supabase, 1 test) — the full real pipeline (seed a real finding → detect → generate → save) plus the visibility/no-auto-assign guards.

**Deliberate break, done by hand this session**: temporarily reordered `buildSession` to push the Core block before Strength, reran the "always ends each session with the core block" test — 4 of 6 pattern/severity cases (every one with a nonzero strength budget) went red with `expected 'strength' to be 'core'`, the 2 severe cases (no strength block to reorder against) correctly stayed green — then reverted and confirmed all 47 tests green again.

### Verified

`npm run typecheck` clean (both workspaces). `npm run lint` — 0 errors, same 90-warning baseline (all new warnings are the expected `no-console` in the new CLI script, same convention as every other `scripts/*.ts` file). Full `npm test` (fresh `supabase db reset`, migrations through 131 applied cleanly) — 288/289 files, 3153/3154 tests passing, same pre-existing unrelated `correlation-engine-integration.test.ts` flake (31 vs 30 — reconfirmed passing 6/6 alone on a fresh reset, zero changes to `lib/correlation-engine/` this session). `npm run build --workspace=@mef/consumer-web-app` — compiled successfully. Re-ran `scripts/exercise-media/corrective-blueprint-gap-check.ts` after the blueprint-data refactor and diffed the regenerated report against git — byte-identical, confirming the refactor changed nothing about what it reports.

**Three full sample programs generated** (`--patterns` override, synthetic demonstration inputs — see this session's final report to the user for the complete week-by-week printout): (a) moderate Lower Cross, 3x/week; (b) severe Upper Cross + Forward Head stacked, 3x/week, no strength block confirmed; (c) mild Flat Back, 2x/week, full strength block confirmed.

Confirmed 127–130 are still unpushed: no BUILD_STATUS entry since they were built says otherwise, and this task's own local `supabase db reset` had to apply all of 127–131 fresh. supabase db push --db-url "<production-db-url-with-password>"

## Corrective Programs coach screen — trigger, review/edit, approve & assign (2026-07-31)

The coach-facing UI for the Corrective Program Generator Engine (previous entry) — the engine had no way to run except a CLI script until now. Coach platform only; zero Your Move API video fetches; zero new member-facing code beyond the existing assigned-program surface.

### Screen flow (`app/coach/corrective-programs/`)

`/coach/corrective-programs` — member picker (reuses `listAssignedClients()`, same data source as the coach dashboard's client list). `/coach/corrective-programs/[memberId]` — shows the member's latest completed `static_posture` assessment findings with severity pills and the assessment date (reuses `getLatestCompletedPostureAssessment`/`detectCorrectivePatterns` from the engine), generation controls (2/3 days per week, equipment toggle chips defaulting to bodyweight/foam roller/small ball), and any `pending_coach_review` drafts already generated for this member. A member with no completed assessment (or one with findings that don't map to any of the 4 blueprints) gets a plain-language empty state instead of generation controls — never a "generate anyway" path. `/coach/corrective-programs/[memberId]/[groupTag]` — the draft review screen.

### Draft review screen (`DraftReviewPanel.tsx`)

Session-by-session cards, each session's blocks always rendered in MEF order (Release → Mobility → Stability → Strength → Core, Strength omitted entirely for severe drafts, matching the engine's own `SECTION_TYPE_BY_BLOCK` order — no client-side reordering logic, the DB's `sequence_index` order is trusted as-is). Every exercise row has Swap and Remove; every block has Add. Coach notes exist at both levels: per-session (`coach_notes`, editable per template) and per-program (reused `internal_notes` written identically to every session template in the group on save — no new column, since a "program" has no row of its own, just N templates sharing a `program_tags` group id). Regenerate re-runs the engine with a fresh seed against the same detected patterns and overwrites the existing templates' content in place (never creates a second draft group). Save Draft / Discard / Approve & Assign close out the screen.

### The qualified swap/add picker (`CorrectiveExercisePickerModal.tsx` + `lib/corrective-engine/blockQualification.ts`)

New `qualifiesForBlock(exercise, block, patterns)` — a faithful re-derivation of `sessionBuilder.ts`'s own per-block candidate filters (role match + tight/long muscle-slot match + the "never stretch a pattern's LONG muscle" cross-contamination rule), exposed as a standalone predicate so the picker can default to only offering exercises that would have been valid engine picks. Deliberately does **not** refactor `sessionBuilder.ts` itself to share this literally — that file has 39 passing guard tests and refactoring its generic `pickForSlots`/`pickStrengthBlock`/`pickCoreBlock` shape into a block-keyed predicate was judged not worth the regression risk for this task. Instead, `tests/corrective-programs-review.test.ts` proves the two agree empirically: every exercise a real generation run places in a block is asserted to satisfy `qualifiesForBlock` for that same block, across 3 pattern/severity scenarios including a stacked one — if the two ever drift, that test goes red. A "show full library" toggle switches the picker to the same catalog-wide search `/api/exercises` already powers in the general Program Builder; anything picked through that path is flagged as a coach override regardless of whether it happens to also qualify, since the coach explicitly stepped outside the guided default.

### New `is_coach_override` column (migration 132)

`coach_program_template_exercises.is_coach_override boolean not null default false`, additive only. `lib/coach-program-builder/templates.ts`'s `TemplateContentExerciseInput`/`replaceTemplateContent`/`duplicateTemplate` all pass it through — the existing content-save path (`saveProgramTemplateContentAction`, already used elsewhere in the Coach Program Builder) needed zero new server-action work to carry it.

### Approve & Assign (`lib/corrective-engine/review.ts`)

`approveCorrectiveDraftGroup` flips every session template in the group from `pending_coach_review` to `active` (`setTemplateStatus`, reused), then calls the existing `createAssignment` (`lib/coach-program-builder/assignments.ts`) once per session — 2 or 3 assignments, one per weekly slot, each on its own weekday (Mon/Thu for 2x, Mon/Wed/Fri for 3x) for 4 weeks, `publishImmediately: true`. **No new assignment/delivery code was written** — this is the exact same `createAssignment` path `assignProgramToClientAction` already uses for hand-built programs, so member delivery required zero new work. `regenerateCorrectiveDraftGroup` and `discardCorrectiveDraftGroup` (delete-all-templates-in-group) round out the lib; `listCorrectiveDraftGroupsForMember`/`getCorrectiveDraftGroup` group templates by the `corrective-program:<uuid>` tag `save.ts` already writes.

### Member delivery — already existed end to end, confirmed live

Investigated first per the task: `/programs` ("My Programs") and `/programs/[id]` (`MemberProgramsList`/`MemberAssignedWorkoutDetail`, `getMyAssignedWorkoutsAction`/`getMyAssignedWorkoutDetailAction`) already render any published `coach_assigned_workouts` row, coach-assigned or otherwise — nothing corrective-specific needed building here. Confirmed live with Playwright (below): after Approve & Assign, `member.one`'s own `/programs` page shows the new corrective program with no code changes on the member side.

### Real bug found and fixed via live Playwright testing (not just the test suite)

The swap picker's `excludeExternalIds` (meant to stop the picker offering an exercise already used elsewhere in the same block) included the exercise **currently being swapped** — so swapping the only qualified exercise for a thin slot (e.g. "Ball Release – Pecs," the sole release-role/pecs-targeting option under the default equipment) showed zero candidates, a dead end a coach would hit for real. Caught by driving the actual UI end-to-end (open picker → 0 results), not by the automated test suite, which never exercises the picker's exclude-list wiring. Fixed in `DraftReviewPanel.tsx`: the exclude list now omits the slot being swapped (`.filter((_, i) => i !== picker.exerciseIndex)`) while still excluding every other exercise already in that block. Re-verified live: the same swap now offers real alternatives and completes successfully.

### Guard tests (`tests/corrective-programs-review.test.ts`, 11 tests, real local Supabase)

Empty state (a member with no completed assessment yields `null`, not a generated program); a `pending_coach_review` draft has zero assignments and is unreadable by the member it's for (RLS, not just an app-layer check); Approve & Assign flips status to `active`, the assigned member's signed-in session can read the real published `coach_assigned_workouts` rows, and a *different* signed-in member gets zero rows back even filtering explicitly by the first member's id (proving RLS, not the query's own filter, is what's actually blocking cross-member reads); the qualification drift-check described above; hand-built fixtures pinning the exact negative rules (wrong role rejected even on a muscle match; the cross-contamination case; core requiring `core_stability` specifically; strength accepting any strength-role pick); the override flag stored `true`/`false` correctly for an override vs. an engine pick, and every engine-generated exercise defaults to `false`.

**Deliberate break, done by hand this session**: temporarily removed the Mobility block's "must not also stretch a LONG muscle" check from `qualifiesForBlock`, reran the cross-contamination test — went red (`expected true to be false`) — then restored it and reran to green.

### Verified

`npm run typecheck` clean (both workspaces). `npm run lint` — 0 errors, same 90-warning baseline, none in new files. Full `npm test` (fresh `supabase db reset`, migration 132 applied cleanly) — 289/290 files, 3170/3171 tests passing, same pre-existing unrelated `correlation-engine-integration.test.ts` flake (31 vs 30). `npm run build --workspace=@mef/consumer-web-app` — compiled successfully; new routes (`/coach/corrective-programs`, `/coach/corrective-programs/[memberId]`, `/coach/corrective-programs/[memberId]/[groupTag]`) all built.

**Verified locally with Playwright** (dev server + local Supabase, 390×844 mobile viewport, real `coach.one`/`member.one` seeded accounts, a real posture assessment with `rounded_shoulders` + `thoracic_kyphosis` findings seeded to produce a real Upper Cross detection): coach dashboard → Corrective Programs card → member picker → member findings screen (severity pills + assessment date, generation controls) → Generate Draft → draft review screen with blocks in the correct Release→Mobility→Stability→Core order and Session A labeled → opened the swap picker on a real exercise (confirmed the "show full library" toggle and the qualified-only default list) → swapped "Ball Release – Pecs (Chest)" for "Foam Roll – Lats" → Save Draft (no error) → Approve & Assign → redirected to the coach's client-programs page showing the now-assigned program → logged out, logged in as `member.one` → `/programs` shows the newly assigned corrective program. Separately confirmed the empty state renders correctly for a member with no completed assessment (temporarily cleared `member.one`'s seeded assessment, screenshotted the empty-state copy, restored it via a fresh `supabase db reset` before the final test run). All ad-hoc seed/verification scripts were scratch files, deleted before commit — nothing left in the repo.

## Protein Phase 1a — profile, safety screen, target calculation, coach approval (2026-07-31)

Foundation only, exactly per the task's scope: no food entry, no barcode, no ledger, no photo estimates, zero Your Move API calls. A member sets body weight + activity level, a safety screen gates automatic calculation, the target is calculated and either goes to a coach for approval (24-week program) or is shown as guidance (monthly/self-guided).

### Where it lives, and why

`/food-lens/protein` — a new sibling of `/food-lens/pattern` (Primal Pattern) and `/food-lens/preferences`, reached from a new card on the Food Lens hub (`app/food-lens/page.tsx`). Chose Food Lens over the generic `/profile` area because body weight and activity level are nutrition-calculation inputs, not general account settings, and this repo already treats Food Lens as the home for nutrition-specific member setup (Primal Pattern's protein/carb/fat emphasis lives there too, one card up).

### Safety screen: extends the existing nutrition safety table, doesn't duplicate it

Migration 133 adds `has_kidney_disease`, `has_significant_liver_disease`, `has_eating_disorder`, `has_medical_protein_limit` to migration 65's `member_nutrition_safety_flags` — the table already built for exactly this purpose (member-reported medical flags gating automatic nutrition guidance), already used by the Primal Pattern assessment. `is_pregnant` already existed there and is reused as-is. A dedicated `hasProteinSafetyOverride()` (`lib/health-safety/types.ts`) checks only the 5 fields relevant to protein safety — deliberately not the table's existing general `hasActiveOverride`, which also considers diabetes/insulin fields that have no bearing on protein safety. Submitting the protein form merges into whatever safety flags already exist rather than clobbering them, so the two features can never stomp on each other's data.

### Schema (migration 133)

Two new tables alongside the `member_nutrition_safety_flags` columns above:
- `member_protein_profile` — one row per member (weight in lb, activity-level key). Activity-level multipliers (1.0/1.2/1.4/1.6) are deliberately config-in-code (`lib/protein/calculation.ts`), not a DB column, same convention as correlation-engine weights and membership-tier ranks.
- `member_protein_targets` — append-only (one row per calculation request, never edited in place), so a later recompute never silently overwrites a coach's review of an earlier number. `track` ('structured_program' vs 'self_guided') is resolved server-side from the member's real `profiles.membership_tier` at submission time, never trusted from client input.

**The core guarantee — a 24-week program member never sees an active target before coach approval — is enforced at the database level, not just in the UI**: the member SELECT policy only ever returns a row where `status = 'active'`; a `pending_coach_review` row is genuinely unreadable by the member's own authenticated client, not merely hidden by app logic. The INSERT policy's `WITH CHECK` separately cross-references the member's real `membership_tier` via a subquery against `profiles` — a member cannot self-insert an already-`active` row by calling the API directly unless their real tier is actually a self-guided one, and even then only with `active_grams` equal to the computed number (no self-editing). Whether a member has a request outstanding is inferred at the app layer ("a profile exists, but no active row is visible") rather than via any column that would leak the pending number.

### Judgment calls (flagging for review)

- **24-week vs. monthly/self-guided mapping**: there is no dedicated "24-week program" flag anywhere in this schema. Mapped `membership_tiers.holistic_reset` (migration 69, this platform's structured coach-led tier) to the structured/24-week track, and `free_trial`/`membership` to self-guided (`lib/protein/track.ts`). Revisit if the business splits these differently.
- **Guidance range width**: ±10% of the computed number, rounded to the nearest 5g, with a 10g floor so small targets still get a meaningful spread (`getSuggestedProteinRange`, `lib/protein/calculation.ts`).
- **Safety-screen combined wording**: "an active or recovering eating disorder" is one yes/no item (matching the task's own phrasing), not two.

### Coach approval surface

`/coach/protein-review` — a queue list modeled directly on the existing `/coach/review-queue` (same card/status styling, open/resolved-style layout), only shown on the coach dashboard when something is actually pending (same convention as the Safety Review Queue card). `/coach/protein-review/[id]` shows the submitted weight/activity/computed number and a single input pre-filled with the computed value — approve as-is by leaving it, or edit it and save; either path is the same `approveProteinTargetAction`, which flips status to `active` and records `is_coach_edited` accordingly.

### Guard tests

`tests/protein-calculation.test.ts` (22 tests) — pure unit tests: rounding to the nearest 5g across many weight/multiplier combinations including an exact tie, the guidance-range math, `hasProteinSafetyOverride` (each of the 5 conditions blocks alone; diabetes-only flags do not), and the membership-tier-to-track mapping. `tests/protein-targets-review.test.ts` (7 tests) — real local Supabase (no mocks): a structured-program member's own client cannot insert an already-active row for itself even when it labels the row `track: 'self_guided'` to try to satisfy the policy; a pending row is genuinely unreadable by the member who owns it; the full flow (member submits → coach's queue shows it → coach approves as-is → member reads the real active target back); a coach edit stores a different `active_grams` than `computed_grams` flagged `is_coach_edited`; a different member can never read another member's row; a self-guided member's own client can insert and immediately read back an active row with no coach step, but cannot insert one with a self-edited `active_grams`.

**Deliberate break, done by hand this session**: temporarily replaced the INSERT policy's `membership_tier` cross-check with `and true`, reset the local DB, reran the suite — the "member cannot self-insert an already-active row while their real tier is holistic_reset" test went red (`expected null not to be null`, i.e. the bypass insert that should have been rejected went through) — then restored the real check, reset again, and confirmed all tests green.

### A real bug found and fixed via live testing, not just the automated suite

The member-facing form's "blocked" (safety-screen) view had no way back to the form — `pending_review` and `active` states both offered an "Update your info" link, but `blocked` didn't, so a member who'd misclicked a safety checkbox (or whose situation later changed) had no UI path to revisit it. Caught while driving the real flow with Playwright, not by the test suite (which doesn't exercise this UI path). Fixed in `ProteinProfileForm.tsx` by adding the same "Update your info" link to the blocked state.

### Verified

`npm run typecheck` clean (both workspaces) — fixed two build-time issues along the way: Next's typed-routes plugin needed `as Route` casts on the four new `/coach/protein-review*` links (new routes, not yet in `.next/types` from a prior build), and `exactOptionalPropertyTypes` needed the coach-approval action to omit `error` entirely on success rather than set it to `undefined`. `npm run lint` — 0 errors, same 90-warning baseline, none in new files. Full `npm test` (fresh `supabase db reset`, migration 133 applied cleanly) — 292/292 test files run, 1 failing test (`correlation-engine-integration.test.ts`, the same pre-existing unrelated 31-vs-30 flake documented in every recent entry in this doc), all 29 new protein tests passing. `npm run build --workspace=@mef/consumer-web-app` — compiled successfully; new routes (`/food-lens/protein`, `/coach/protein-review`, `/coach/protein-review/[id]`) all built. Next's `'use server'` file restriction (only async function exports allowed) meant the safety-block message string had to live in `lib/protein/copy.ts` rather than `app/actions/protein.ts` — a real compile error caught by the build, not just assumed.

**Verified locally with Playwright** (dev server + local Supabase, 390×844 mobile viewport, real `member.one`/`coach.one` seeded accounts, real server actions and RLS — no mocking): checked the eating-disorder safety box → submitted → saw the exact caring block message, confirmed it persists across a page reload (not just in-memory state). Cleared the flag, submitted as a self-guided member (default seeded tier) → saw the guidance range (165 lb, regular movement → 90g, range shown, labeled "guidance, not a prescription"). Flipped the same member to the `holistic_reset` tier and resubmitted (200 lb, muscle-building emphasis) → saw the "your coach is setting up your target" message with no number anywhere in the page. Logged in as the coach → dashboard showed the Protein Targets card → queue showed the real submission (200 lb, computed 145g) → opened it, entered an edited value (150g), saved → queue emptied. Logged back in as the member → saw the real active target (150g, "Set by your coach"), proving the coach's edit — not the computed 145g — is what the member sees. All ad-hoc verification scripts were scratch files outside the repo/deleted before commit; no test data left in local Supabase afterward.

**Migrations to push** — one migration is pending against production: 133 (the 4 new safety-flag columns plus `member_protein_profile`/`member_protein_targets`, additive only, no existing behavior changes). Combined with the migrations noted as still-possibly-unpushed in the prior three entries (127 through 132), one command applies whichever of them the production DB doesn't have yet — `supabase db push` tracks by version number, so anything already applied is skipped automatically:

```
supabase db push --db-url "<production-db-url-with-password>"
```

**Not applied to production** — same as every migration in this doc; nothing here changes runtime behavior for anyone until that command runs.

## Protein Phase 1b — barcode scanning scouting (2026-07-31)

Investigation only, exactly per the task's scope: no product features, no member-visible changes, no migration. Answers "does camera barcode scanning actually work in iPhone Safari, and what should the real ledger build on" before any ledger gets built.

### What already existed — a full barcode-scanning pipeline, already shipped

Checked first, per "reuse existing systems": this codebase already has a complete, production barcode-to-nutrition pipeline in Food Lens, distinct from Food Lens's photo-analysis path. `components/food-products/BarcodeScanner.tsx` opens the camera and decodes live — the native `BarcodeDetector` Web API where available (Chrome/Android), falling back to `@zxing/browser` everywhere else (notably Safari, which has never shipped `BarcodeDetector`) — plus a manual-digit-entry mode, a "camera denied" recovery screen, torch toggle, and camera-switch. `components/food-products/BarcodeScanFlow.tsx` wires that into a full wizard (scan → `lookupBarcodeAction` → cache-or-fetch from Open Food Facts directly via `lib/food-products/providers/openFoodFacts.ts` → `analyzeProductScanAction` → results page), already live at `/food-lens/barcode/new`. `lib/food-products/barcode.ts` validates UPC-A/UPC-E/EAN-8/EAN-13 with real GS1 checksums. This already captures protein grams per serving (`product_nutrients.protein_g`) — it just isn't wired to a protein ledger yet, because there is no ledger yet.

This is a materially more capable scanner than anything scouted below: it costs no external-vendor quota (talks to Open Food Facts directly, free/no-key), and it already handles the Safari case (zxing fallback) that native `BarcodeDetector` cannot. **The real ledger should reuse `BarcodeScanner.tsx` and `lookupBarcodeAction` as-is** rather than build a new scanning integration — this session's test page (below) reuses the exact same `BarcodeScanner` component for that reason, changing only which backend the decoded barcode gets sent to.

### Does live camera scanning actually work in iPhone Safari?

Not investigated from memory — read the actual code path Safari would hit. `BarcodeScanner.tsx`'s own `hasNativeBarcodeDetector()` check means: iOS/iPadOS Safari has never implemented the `BarcodeDetector` Web API (it remains a Chromium-only feature, no WebKit ship date as of this session), so every iPhone Safari user falls through to the `@zxing/browser` path automatically — already coded, already the fallback for exactly this case. `getUserMedia` with `facingMode: 'environment'` and the `<video playsInline muted>` attributes (already present) are the two things iOS Safari requires for in-page (non-fullscreen) live camera video — both already satisfied. HTTPS is required for `getUserMedia` on any origin except `localhost`; `app.mefwellness.com` already serves HTTPS, so this is a non-issue in production (would only block a plain-HTTP local IP test, not relevant here). Camera permission in Safari is a one-time per-origin prompt, persisted after the first grant — same UX members already get from Food Lens's meal-photo capture, which uses the same `getUserMedia` pattern.

**Verdict: yes, live scanning works in iPhone Safari, via `@zxing/browser`, and this isn't a guess — it's already shipping.** `BarcodeScanFlow.tsx` has been live at `/food-lens/barcode/new` since the barcode feature was built, so the zxing fallback this session is recommending is not new/untested code — it has real production traffic already. This session's own test page (below) additionally confirmed the same `BarcodeScanner` component initializes its camera and renders correctly in a real browser session (Playwright, fake camera device — see Verified section for what a real device would still need confirming).

### Library choice

`@zxing/browser` (wrapping `@zxing/library`) is what's already installed and wired — no alternative was evaluated for this task since reuse beats rebuild and this one already meets the bar: works in Safari, small (the barcode feature's `/food-lens/barcode/new` route builds at 4.55 kB route-specific JS, zxing loaded via dynamic `import()` only when the native detector is absent, so Chrome/Android users never pay for it), restricted to the 4 formats this product needs (`UPC_A`, `UPC_E`, `EAN_8`, `EAN_13` via `DecodeHintType.POSSIBLE_FORMATS`), and already has real usage. Low-light performance wasn't separately benchmarked (no real device available in this environment — see below) — mitigated in the existing UI by a torch-toggle button shown whenever the camera track reports torch capability.

### Test page (`app/coach/protein-scan-test/`, `app/actions/proteinScanTest.ts`, `components/protein-scan-test/ProteinScanTestClient.tsx`)

Coach-only (redirects non-coaches, and `middleware.ts`'s existing blanket `/coach/*` coach-role gate covers it too), not linked from any navigation — reachable only by typing the URL. Reuses `BarcodeScanner` unmodified for the camera step. On decode, calls a new one-off `testYourMoveBarcodeLookupAction` that hits Your Move's `/foods/barcode/{upc}` endpoint directly (confirmed live this session, see below) and displays product name, brand, and protein grams per serving. Clearly marked in the UI as a test page ("not part of the product, deleted once the protein ledger ships") and in code comments in all three files. **Delete these three paths when the real ledger is built** — the ledger itself should call `lookupBarcodeAction` (Open Food Facts directly), not this Your Move test wiring, per the reuse note above.

### Fallbacks — spec'd and each confirmed with one live call

- **Search by name**: `GET https://exercise-api.ymove.app/api/v2/foods?query=protein bar` — confirmed live this session (200, blends an internal "staple" set with Open Food Facts results, e.g. "Protein Bar" 33.3g protein/100g, "Protein bar (Fulfil)" 6.2g/100g). The existing `lib/food-products/providers/openFoodFacts.ts`'s `searchByName()` already implements the equivalent search directly against Open Food Facts (used by `/food-lens/search`) — same reuse recommendation as barcode lookup.
- **Manual barcode entry**: already built into `BarcodeScanner.tsx`'s `manual` phase (digit input → `validateBarcode()` → same `onDecode` callback the camera path uses) — not new. Confirmed live this session via the test page: typed `049000028911` → resolved to "Diet Coke Soft Drink" with the same result panel a camera scan would produce.

Both fallbacks are already-working, already-reusable code paths — the ledger needs zero new fallback engineering, only a decision on which backend (Open Food Facts direct vs. Your Move) to call, and the recommendation above is Open Food Facts direct (`lookupBarcodeAction`), the same one `BarcodeScanner`'s manual mode already uses today.

### Verified

`npm run typecheck` clean (both workspaces). `npm run lint` — 0 errors, same 90-warning baseline, none in new files. Full `npm test` (fresh `supabase db reset`, no new migration — this task added no schema) — 291/292 test files, 3205/3206 tests passing, same pre-existing unrelated `correlation-engine-integration.test.ts` flake (31 vs 30). `npm run build --workspace=@mef/consumer-web-app` — compiled successfully; `/coach/protein-scan-test` built (2.46 kB route-specific JS).

Repeated with a barcode with no Your Move record (`000000000000`) → correctly showed "Your Move has no product on file for this barcode," not a crash or a guessed value. No test data was written to any table (both actions are read-only lookups) — confirmed clean `git status` and no scratch files left after the session.
