## Protein Ledger (Phase 1b) + app-wide white-label sweep (2026-07-31)

Prerequisite check passed: Phase 1a (profile/safety screen/target calculation/coach approval, migration 133) is live — verified `member_protein_profile`/`member_protein_targets` and `getMyProteinSetupState`'s four states all work exactly as documented above. The barcode-scouting verdict was treated as positive per the task (the scouting session above could not test a real iPhone camera — see its own "Honest limitation" note — but the user confirmed real-device scanning separately), so the ledger ships with scanning included.

### The big decision: no new ledger schema at all

Before building anything, checked for an existing system per this repo's standing rule — and found one. Migration 59's `member_food_log` + `food_products` + `product_nutrients` (already live, already used by Food Lens's barcode/search/manual-entry flows, edit/delete already built in `FoodLogList.tsx`) is exactly "a member's confirmed-grams food entries." Building a parallel `protein_entries` table would have duplicated working, tested, RLS-correct code for no reason. The Protein Ledger is a protein-focused *read* over that same table, plus one new lightweight write path (quick add) that reuses the same private-product pattern Food Lens's manual entry already established (`insertVerifiedFoodProductFromLabelScan`, `data_source = 'mef_verified'`, barcode null — never shared/deduped). Consequence worth knowing: because this is the same table, any food a member logs anywhere else in Food Lens via barcode/search/manual entry (not just via the new Ledger screen) also counts toward their protein tally automatically — one unified log, not two competing ones. Meal-photo entries (AI-estimated macros, `product_id` null) are explicitly excluded from every ledger query — confirmed-grams only, per the task.

New files: `lib/protein/ledger.ts` (pure grams/rounding/day-grouping/target-display logic, unit-tested), `app/actions/protein-ledger.ts` (today/history reads + log/quick-add/resolve-search-result actions — reuses `startFoodLensScanAction`, `lookupBarcodeAction`, `searchFoodsAction`, `insertFoodLogEntry`, `editFoodLogEntryAction`, `removeFoodLogEntryAction`, `insertVerifiedFoodProductFromLabelScan` as-is; deliberately skips `analyzeProductScanAction`'s rules-engine/Root-coaching pipeline since the ledger only needs a protein number, not a full nutrition-quality review), `components/protein-ledger/*` (progress card, entry lanes, entries list, 7-day history), `app/food-lens/protein/ledger/page.tsx`.

### Today view

`/food-lens/protein/ledger`, linked from a new card on the Food Lens hub showing a live "Xg of Yg today" (or "Xg logged today" pre-target) subtitle. Progress card branches on the same four `getMyProteinSetupState` stages Phase 1a's own summary card uses, via one new pure function (`resolveLedgerTargetDisplay`, unit-tested for exactly the task's two hard rules): active shows a real bar against `active_grams`; pending-review and blocked both show the tally with **no** target number and **no** setup link; blocked additionally gets no nudge at all (not even "not started" styling); not-started is the only state with a "set up your target" link. Entries list shows time + a lane label (Scanned/Searched/Quick add, inferred from what's already on the row — no new column) + grams, with edit (adjust servings, live-recalculates grams) and delete, both reusing the food log's existing actions verbatim.

**Day rollover**: member's device timezone, via `profiles.timezone` (already captured elsewhere in the app) and `lib/time/localDate.ts`'s `todaysLocalDate`/`localDateStringFor` — the same helper already used by check-ins, weekly reports, and coaching insights, so "today" means the same thing everywhere in the app. Writing this test exposed a real, pre-existing bug in that shared helper: its timezone conversion silently depended on the *host process's own* local timezone, not just the target timezone (it worked correctly only when the server process itself ran in UTC — true on Vercel, not true when running tests locally in a non-UTC timezone, which is exactly what caught it). Fixed via `Intl.DateTimeFormat.formatToParts` instead of a `toLocaleString`-then-reparse round trip — same function signatures, zero behavior change for any caller already running under TZ=UTC, now also correct everywhere else. Left `app/actions/checkin.ts`'s separate `resolveLocalDate` untouched (different implementation, tightly coupled to check-in's late-submission grace window, out of scope here).

### Entry lanes (all confirmed-grams only)

(a) **Scan** — `BarcodeScanner.tsx` + `startFoodLensScanAction('barcode')` + `lookupBarcodeAction` (exact scouting recommendation), then a confirm card (product/brand/serving, servings stepper, live grams) before anything is logged. (b) **Search** — `searchFoodsAction` (recent/frequent/cached/external, unchanged), picking a result resolves to product+nutrients via a new thin action (reusing `findCachedFoodProduct`/provider-chain lookup/`upsertFoodProductFromProvider` — no new fetch/cache logic), then the same confirm card. (c) **Quick add** — grams typed directly, name optional, its own confirm step (tapping "Add" is the confirmation). Nothing reaches `member_food_log` before that explicit tap in any lane — proved by test, see below.

### History

Simple last-7-days bars, each day's total vs. target, no trends/streaks (per task scope) — `lib/protein/ledger.ts`'s `buildDailyTotals`/`lastNLocalDates`, unit-tested across timezone boundaries in both directions (negative and positive UTC offset).

### White-label sweep (whole app)

Grepped every `.tsx`/`.ts` file for vendor names, then read each hit in context to separate real on-screen text from code/comments (which are allowed to keep vendor names per the task). Found and fixed three real on-screen violations:
- `components/food-products/FoodSearch.tsx` — search section/row label **"From Open Food Facts" → "From our nutrition database"**.
- `components/food-products/ProductHeader.tsx` — the exact "Source: X" badge the task called out by name: **"Source: Open Food Facts" / "Source: USDA FoodData Central" → "Source: MEF nutrition database"** (both data sources collapsed to the same neutral label; `mef_verified` already read "MEF Verified," untouched).
- `components/your-move-generation/GenerateProgramFlow.tsx` — coach-facing program-builder copy **"Your Move builds one repeating weekly split…" → "This builds one repeating weekly split…"**.

Everything else the grep matched (dozens of hits across exercise-library, body-assessment, `your-move-generation.ts`'s own error messages, the `/api/exercises/[id]/video-url` route) was already either a code comment/identifier or, in the video-url route's case, an error message the calling component (`ExerciseDetailView.tsx`) already discards and never renders — confirmed by reading the actual consumer, not assumed. `your-move-generation.ts`'s member/coach-facing error strings already say "the exercise provider" generically — already compliant, no change needed.

**Rounding bug found and fixed**: `components/food-products/NutritionFactsPanel.tsx` rendered every nutrient value raw with no `Math.round` — this is the literal "189.9999999999998 calories" bug from the scouting session's own barcode test (Diet Coke's real Open Food Facts response has floating-point rounding artifacts in its per-serving math). Fixed for calories, protein, and every other row (fat/carbs/fiber/sugar/sodium/potassium) — whole numbers throughout, matching how `AddToFoodLogSheet.tsx` already rounded its scaled-calories preview.

### Cleanup

Deleted `app/coach/protein-scan-test/page.tsx`, `app/actions/proteinScanTest.ts`, `components/protein-scan-test/ProteinScanTestClient.tsx` (confirmed no other file imports any of them). The task also asked to remove "the temporary coach dashboard link" — there wasn't one; the scouting entry above already states this page was "not linked from any navigation," and a grep of `app/coach/page.tsx` confirms it (the only protein-related things there are the real Phase 1a `/coach/protein-review` card). Noted for the user rather than silently assumed away.

### Guard tests

`tests/protein-ledger-logic.test.ts` (15 tests, pure) — rounding artifact collapse, entry-grams math (null nutrients stay null, never invented as 0), `resolveEntrySource` for all three lanes, `resolveLedgerTargetDisplay`'s two hard rules (pending-review never exposes `targetGrams`/active mode; blocked never sets `showSetupNudge`), day attribution across both a negative- and a positive-UTC-offset boundary, `buildDailyTotals` keeping empty days at 0g rather than omitting them. `tests/protein-ledger-integration.test.ts` (6 tests, real local Supabase) — a cached/looked-up product with no log entry never appears in any member's food log (confirmation gates the tally); an explicit insert does; memberTwo can neither read nor delete memberOne's entry (RLS, not just UI hiding); the three deleted file paths are confirmed gone via `fs.existsSync`; a white-label regression guard that greps every `.tsx` file under `app/`/`components/` (comments stripped first) for the known vendor-name patterns — deliberately broken (reintroduced "Open Food Facts" into `ProductHeader.tsx`, ran the test, watched it fail with the exact file/line/text) and reverted (test green again) before this was reported done, proving the guard actually catches a regression rather than trivially passing.

### Verified

`npm run typecheck` clean. `npm run lint` — 0 errors, same 90-warning baseline, no new warnings in new files. Full `npm test` (fresh `supabase db reset` — see below for why fresh, though this task added no migration) — 293/294 test files, 3226/3227 tests passing; the one failure is the same pre-existing unrelated `correlation-engine-integration.test.ts` flake (31 vs 30) documented in every recent entry in this doc. `npm run build --workspace=@mef/consumer-web-app` — compiled successfully; `/food-lens/protein/ledger` built (5.8 kB route-specific JS); `/coach/protein-scan-test` no longer appears in the route list at all.

**Verified locally with Playwright** (dev server + local Supabase, 390×844 mobile viewport, real `member.one` seeded account, real server actions/RLS, no mocking) — logged in, opened the ledger, ran all three lanes for real: quick add (typed grams, entry appeared, total updated correctly, delete removed it and the total dropped back down); search (typed a query, saw the member's own real recent-item history — no live external hits came back this session, see honest limitation below); scan (no camera in this sandboxed environment, so used the same manual-barcode-entry fallback the original scouting session used — typed Diet Coke's real UPC `049000028911`, got a real network response back from the packaged-food lookup showing "0g protein," confirmed it, and it landed correctly in the ledger as a real, non-fabricated zero). Editing an entry's servings and reloading the 7-day history were also exercised directly.

This actually caught two real bugs before they shipped, both fixed and re-verified:
1. **Total didn't refresh after editing/deleting an entry.** `ProteinLedgerEntries.tsx` updated its own list optimistically but never told the sibling progress card (a separate server-fed component) to recompute — so the "Xg of Yg" header could go stale the moment a member edited or deleted something. Fixed by calling `router.refresh()` after a successful edit/delete and syncing the list's local state from the refreshed server props via `useEffect`.
2. **Every entry mislabeled "Searched," even ones from a real barcode scan.** The scan lane created a real `food_lens_scans` row and looked the product up through it, but never actually passed that scan id into the log-entry action — so `resolveEntrySource` (which infers the lane from what's on the row) always fell through to "search." Fixed by threading `scanId` from the scan flow into `logProteinLedgerEntryAction`.

Also worth recording precisely because it was surprising: driving the app locally as the seeded member — just logging in and loading pages — triggered unrelated background writes (pattern-state/feed history rows) that then broke two *other* tests' fixture assumptions on the next full-suite run. Not a ledger bug; those tests assume a pristine seed and this session's own logins/navigations weren't isolated from that seed. Fixed by running a fresh `supabase db reset` before the final full-suite pass (confirmed clean afterward) rather than by cherry-picking rows to delete — flagging this for anyone else driving this app locally against the shared dev Supabase instance mid-test-cycle.

**Honest limitation**: no external search hits came back for "chicken" during this session's search-lane test — Open Food Facts' legacy `cgi/search.pl` endpoint returned an HTML page instead of JSON on this attempt (the barcode-lookup endpoint used by scan/quick-confirm worked fine and returned real JSON). `OpenFoodFactsProvider.searchByName()` already treats any such failure as "no external results" rather than crashing — matching its own documented behavior — so the lane degraded gracefully to just the member's recent/frequent history. This is the same, unmodified search backend the original `/food-lens/search` feature already uses; not a regression from this task, and not fixed here as out of scope.

**No migration to push** — this task added no schema. Everything it needs (`member_food_log`, `food_products`, `product_nutrients`) is migration 59, already in production since long before Phase 1a/1b.

## Protein Ledger: search race fix, generic-food ranking, numeral legibility (2026-07-31)

Targeted bug-fix pass on the Ledger's search lane and the Today's Protein card — no schema change, no rebuild of the ledger itself, per the task's own scope.

### 1. Search results vanishing (the reported bug)

Root cause confirmed exactly as the task suspected: `SearchLane`'s debounce (`components/protein-ledger/ProteinEntryLanes.tsx`) only throttled when a request was *dispatched* — it never guarded against one arriving out of order. A natural typing pause (e.g. after "chicken", before "breast") could let an earlier, shorter-query request stay in flight while a newer, longer-query request was also dispatched; whichever happened to resolve *last* won, so a correct, populated result set could be silently overwritten by a stale, often-empty response for an already-superseded partial query — exactly "briefly shows results, then reverts to Nothing found."

Fixed with a new pure guard, `shouldApplySearchResponse` (`lib/protein/ledger.ts`) — a response is only applied if its request id is still the latest one dispatched *and* its query still matches what's live in the box right now (checked via a ref, not the async closure's stale copy of `query`, which would otherwise always trivially match itself). `isSearching` now stays true from the moment the query changes until an *accepted* response lands, and "Nothing found" never renders while it's true — a "Searching…" state shows instead. True network-level cancellation (aborting the in-flight request) isn't available here since `searchFoodsAction` is a Next.js Server Action, not a raw `fetch` under this code's control — discard-on-resolve via the id/query check is the standard, correct substitute and matches "cancel in-flight requests" in effect: a stale response's network call still completes, but its result can never reach the screen.

Verified live (not just unit-tested): typed "chicken breast" one character at a time with real per-keystroke delays against a seeded cached product, polled the rendered text every 200ms for 2 seconds after typing stopped — "Nothing found" never appeared at any point, and the results, once shown, never disappeared again.

### 2. Generic foods ranked first

Investigated what's actually available to rank on. Open Food Facts' `cgi/search.pl` (what `OpenFoodFactsProvider.searchByName()` already uses) returns name/brand/image only — no popularity, category, or generic/branded flag. (A newer official Search-a-licious API does expose brand/category/popularity fields, but swapping the provider's HTTP endpoint is a bigger, separate change than this task's scope and wasn't made here — flagging it as a real option for a future session if richer ranking signals are wanted.) The app's own product cache (`food_products`) has no such flag either. Conclusion stated directly per the task's own fallback: **neither source can distinguish generic from branded**, so this ranks by closeness of the product name to what was actually typed instead — the task's own suggested fallback.

New `rankFoodSearchResults` (`lib/food-products/search.ts`): exact case-insensitive name match ranks first, then a name that starts with the query, then one that merely contains it, then everything else; ties within a tier broken by shorter name (a plain "Chicken Breast" outranks "Tyson Chargrilled Chicken Breast Tenders, Frozen"). Applied to both `searchCachedFoodProducts`'s and `searchFoodsAction`'s external-provider results — both now over-fetch a small candidate pool before ranking and slicing to the final shown count, so there's actually something to reorder among. This is shared by the original `/food-lens/search` page too (same `searchFoodsAction`), a positive side effect, not scope creep — it's the exact same shared function the Ledger's search lane already depended on.

Verified live: seeded a generic "Chicken Breast" (no brand) and a branded "Tyson Chargrilled Chicken Breast Tenders, Frozen" into the local cache (the sandbox's external OFF search was unreliable this session — see below — so this proved the ranking against real, working data instead), searched "chicken breast" in the Ledger, and the generic entry rendered first, branded second.

### 3. "0g" reading as "og"

Confirmed visually (screenshot, not assumed) — Cormorant Garamond's old-style figures render its zero nearly identical to a lowercase o at the Today's Protein card's display size. Fixed with `font-variant-numeric: lining-nums` on both of `ProteinLedgerProgress.tsx`'s number displays, verified by screenshotting before and after: the same serif display font, but the zero now has the flat top/bottom of a lining figure, unambiguous at a glance. Chose this over swapping to the body font (DM Sans) because it worked and keeps the design language fully intact — no font-family change needed anywhere in the card.

### Guard tests

`tests/protein-ledger-logic.test.ts` — 3 new `shouldApplySearchResponse` tests, including the literal out-of-order scenario the task asked for (a request for "c" dispatched first, resolving *after* a request for "chicken breast" that was dispatched second, is correctly discarded even though it completed successfully). `tests/food-products-search-ranking.test.ts` (new file, 5 tests) — exact match beats a longer branded name, starts-with beats contains, shorter name breaks ties, empty query leaves order untouched, a null product name never crashes or drops a result.

### Verified

`npm run typecheck` clean. `npm run lint` — 0 errors, same 90-warning baseline. Full `npm test` (fresh `supabase db reset` — same reason as last entry: driving the app locally to verify this pollutes two unrelated tests' fixtures every time, confirmed and cleaned the same way) — 294/295 test files, 3234/3235 tests passing; the one failure is the same pre-existing `correlation-engine-integration.test.ts` flake (31 vs 30). `npm run build --workspace=@mef/consumer-web-app` — compiled successfully; `/food-lens/protein/ledger` now 6.02 kB route-specific JS (was 5.8 kB).

**Verified locally with Playwright**, real member.one session, real server actions — see each fix's own "Verified live" note above for what was actually driven and screenshotted.

**Honest limitation, still true this session**: Open Food Facts' legacy search endpoint returned an HTML "temporarily unavailable" page rather than JSON again this session (both the legacy `cgi/search.pl` scraping endpoint currently used and the equally-legacy `/api/v2/search` REST endpoint failed the same way; the newer Search-a-licious API at `search.openfoodfacts.org/search` worked fine and returned real, well-structured JSON when tested directly). Since the user's own bug report describes seeing real branded results, this is most likely transient/sandbox-network-specific rather than a universal outage — the provider's endpoint was deliberately left unchanged this session (out of scope for a targeted fix), but if external search continues to come back empty in production, switching `OpenFoodFactsProvider.searchByName()` to the Search-a-licious endpoint is the recommended fix, and would also unlock real brand/category/popularity data for even better ranking than the exact-match heuristic used here.

**No migration to push** — no schema changed in this pass either.

## Food Lens page redesign: sections instead of a flat card stack (2026-07-31)

Restructured `/food-lens` from ~12 equal-weight stacked cards into clear sections. Layout/copy only — no destination page touched, no functionality added or removed, no new data query (the merged protein card actually removes one: see below).

### 1. Subheading swap

New headline "One number we count. Everything else, we coach." and supporting line, same Cormorant Garamond/DM Sans typography, same FOOD LENS eyebrow. Kept the exact locked subhead class (`text-[15px] leading-relaxed text-[#4F645A]`) so `tests/subhead-contrast-ratio.test.ts`'s "applied everywhere" grep still passes.

### 2. Log food section

Added a "Log food" section header. Scan a Meal is now a standalone full-width primary tile (forest-green gradient, gold camera icon, white text) at the top — the flagship action. The other four (Scan a Barcode, Scan a Label, Search, Manual Entry) became a 2x2 grid of compact icon+title tiles with their description sentences removed — those sentences were the actual bulk of the old page's height. All five hrefs unchanged.

### 3. Merged protein card

The old "Protein ledger" and "Protein target" cards are gone, replaced by one card reusing the existing `ProteinLedgerProgress` component (built in the Protein Ledger Phase 1b task) — it already encodes exactly the three states the task asked for (no target → setup nudge, pending coach review → clear pending message and no progress bar, target set → "Xg of Yg" with a fill bar), including the lining-numerals fix so "0g" never reads as "og." This let me *drop* a data fetch rather than add one: `getMyProteinSetupState()` was being called a second time just for the old separate "Protein target" card, and `getProteinLedgerTodayAction()` already calls it internally, so the page now fetches protein state exactly once. The card is wrapped in a link to the ledger whenever a target exists in any form (active/pending/blocked); when no target exists yet, the component's own internal "Set up your protein target" link is the only interactive element (avoids a nested `<a>`).

### 4. Primal Pattern: permanent card → dismissible banner

New `components/food-lens/PrimalPatternSetupBanner.tsx` (client component). Only rendered at all when the member has no active Primal Pattern profile (server-checked); closing its X hides it for the rest of the browser session via `sessionStorage` (not `localStorage` — deliberately not permanent), reappearing on a fresh session if still unset. Once a pattern is set, the banner — and the destination link that used to live in it — disappears entirely, per the task's explicit "must not occupy space for members who've completed it." This is a real reduction in reachability for that one destination once a pattern exists (no compact fallback link was added elsewhere on the page) — a deliberate reading of that instruction over the task's general "every destination stays reachable" framing; worth confirming this was the intended tradeoff.

### Verified

`npm run typecheck` clean. `npm run lint` — 0 errors, same pre-existing warning baseline (unrelated scripts/hooks). New `tests/food-lens-page-redesign.test.ts` (25 tests: subheading swap, Log food section structure, merged-card states via `resolveLedgerTargetDisplay`, banner session-scoping, unchanged section order) — all passing, plus the pre-existing `food-lens-navigation`/`subhead-contrast-ratio`/`protein-ledger-logic` suites re-run clean against the new file. Full `npm test` (fresh `supabase db reset` first) — 295/296 test files, 3259/3260 tests passing; the one failure is the same pre-existing `correlation-engine-integration.test.ts` flake documented in the entry above (confirmed by running it against `main` with this session's changes stashed — identical failure, so definitively not caused by this work). `npm run build` — compiled successfully, `/food-lens` route generated (3.56 kB route-specific JS).

**Verified locally with Playwright** (dev server + local Supabase, `member.one@example.test`, 390×844 mobile viewport): screenshotted the redesigned page in the no-target protein state (default seed) — headline/subhead copy, Log food section (primary tile + 2x2 grid), protein card, Primal Pattern banner, unchanged utility row/Week in Food/Recent scans all render in the correct order with zero console errors. Seeded a real `member_protein_targets` row (`active`, 120g) via direct SQL and re-screenshotted: "0g of 120g" with a real (empty) progress fill bar and guidance range, confirmed tapping the card navigates to `/food-lens/protein/ledger`. Updated that same row's status to `pending_coach_review` and re-screenshotted: the pending message renders with no progress bar, matching spec. Seeded a real `primal_pattern_profiles` row and confirmed the banner disappears entirely (element count 0), not just visually hidden. Drove the dismiss button directly: banner hides immediately, stays hidden after a same-session reload, and reappears in a brand-new browser context (fresh session). All seeded test rows deleted afterward and a full `supabase db reset` run before the final test pass, per this repo's own documented practice for local Playwright runs.

**Not deployed as of this entry** — build/tests verified locally; push and Vercel/domain verification to follow immediately after this entry.

**No migration to push** — no schema changed in this pass.
